# Decisions & Locked Assumptions

## Product / architecture decisions
| # | Decision | Choice |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Backend data strategy | Own DB — ingest tcgdex → Cloudflare D1, serve our own API |
| D2 | Accounts & persistence | Full accounts + auth; users own their decks |
| D2a | Auth methods | Email + password **and** social OAuth (Discord + Google) |
| D3 | Catalog scope | Full-history-capable schema, ingest SV-era first |
| D4 | Card images | Mirror into R2, serve from our domain |
| D5 | Card type | Unify — one canonical `Card`; builder/playmat are view projections |
| D6 | Online lobby server | In scope — real Durable Object (WebSocket) lobby |
| D7 | Game engine placement | Separate **`packages/engine`** (pure logic) — imported by both `apps/web` (local play) and `apps/api` (authoritative DO). *Why: the engine runs in two runtimes; an app can't import another app.* |
| D8 | Card-effect modeling | **Composable, data-driven op programs** over shared archetype templates (not per-card functions); engine emits **semantic events** consumed by the animation layer. *Why: cards are minor variations on a few archetypes; keeps authoring + animation cheap. Detail in `workstreams/simulator.md`.* |
| D9 | Image mirroring & ingest execution (refines D4/§3.4) | Images mirror into R2 **lazily** — a Worker route (`GET /assets/…`) serves from R2 and on miss fetches tcgdex origin, stores, streams; catalog rows keep tcgdex **origin** URLs. Card-data backfill runs as a **local Bun script** applying batched upsert SQL via `wrangler d1 execute`; a future cron does small in-Worker deltas only. *Why: a full SV backfill is ~10k images — over Worker subrequest limits in one shot and hours of CLI puts; cache-aside mirrors exactly what's viewed and stays within limits.* (2026-07-09) |
| D10 | M9 catalog search shape (refines D5/§6) | `CardBrief` **extended** with `types/hp/regulationMark/stage/trainerType/energyType` (card-table columns only — **no set join**, so no `setName`); `GET /cards` gains **repeatable `type`/`rarity`** (OR within a dimension) and **`sort=name\|hp-desc\|set`**; KV cache keys version-prefixed (`catalog:v2:`) so a shape change can't serve stale briefs. The builder consumes briefs through one adapter (`src/features/builder/catalog.ts` — accented `"Pokémon"`, stage/trainerType/energyType → the single `subtype`). *Why: the grid/filters/deck-math need those fields per tile; per-tile full-Card fetches or a fixture pool were the alternatives.* (2026-07-13) |
| D11 | M9 scope trims (pending P2) | **Kinds** (`subtypes`) filter stays a client-side per-page heuristic (server can't express OR across stage/trainerType; EX/Mega are name-regexes until structured data — P2 tasks 8-9). **Manual deck/folder reorder is session-only** (no position field in the schema — P2 task 17). **Folder delete keeps the lift-up UX client-side** (child PATCHes before DELETE) over the server's cascade semantics. **`RARITIES` is a hardcoded tcgdex vocabulary snapshot** in the rail. *Why: each would widen M9's already-large surface; all four are logged with their fixes in `workstreams/deck-builder.md`.* (2026-07-13) **Revised 2026-07-14 (P2-M1):** kinds now filter **server-side** (repeatable `stage`/`trainerType`/`energyType`) — only EX/Mega remain a client-side name-regex post-filter (tcgdex `suffix` not persisted; follow-up logged); hardcoded vocabularies replaced by **`GET /facets`**. Reorder (task 17) and lift-up remain as decided. **Revised again 2026-07-14 (task 17):** reorder is now persisted — see D13; only the lift-up trim still stands. |
| D12 | P2-M1 catalog surface & legality source of truth | `GET /cards` gains **repeatable `id`** (≤100, deck hydration in ONE call; chunked IN + in-memory ordering twin of the SQL sort), **`nameExact`** (case-insensitive whole-name equality), `serie`, `illustrator`, `text` (LIKE over ability/attack JSON), plus the kind params above; **`GET /facets`** serves the filter vocabularies (7 DISTINCTs, one db.batch, KV-cached). `CardBrief` += **`legal {standard,expanded}`**; KV namespace → **`catalog:v3:`**, with KV **bypassed** for >512-byte keys and any `?id=` request (per-deck id sets never recur — caching them is pure write waste). Web: **`Format.legalFlag`** keys BOTH the `legalOnly` filter (`legal=<flag>`) and `isLegalInFormat` (structured verdict beats printed mark; mark check only for legal-less fixtures) — one legality definition for grid + validator. Deck rules: **Radiant = 1 per deck total** (like ACE SPEC; official rule), Prism Star = 1 per name. *Why: filters needed server truth for correct totals; hydration was an N-request fan-out; legality had two disagreeing definitions.* (2026-07-14) |
| D13 | Manual-order persistence model (P2 task 17) | `position` INTEGER on `decks` and `folders` — **global per user, not per folder**: the web grid's flat array (position-ascending) IS the manual order, per-view filtering preserves relative order, and a nest move keeps the row's slot (folderId changes, position doesn't) — exactly the client's existing semantics. Creates take **min−1** (new rows prepend); migration 0003 backfills existing rows in the old name-order so nothing visibly moves. A reorder permutes the in-view rows **among their own slots' position values** (`applyReorder` returns the rewritten array + the changed `{id, position}` pairs) and persists via **`POST /decks/reorder` / `/folders/reorder`** — absolute assignments, ≤500 rows, applied atomically in one db.batch; unknown/foreign ids → 400 naming them; failure recovery is **resync**, not per-row rollback (the folder-delete precedent). Reorder deliberately does NOT bump `updated`. *Why: slot-permutation keeps payloads at the size of the move (not the scope), needs no renumbering pass ever, and one atomic endpoint beats N racing PATCHes.* (2026-07-14) |

| D14 | Engine core contract (P3-M1) | **`applyAction(state, action) → {ok:true, state, events} | {ok:false, error}`** — one reducer, never throws, never mutates. `GameState` is plain JSON (the mulberry32 `rngState` lives **inside** it, so same seed + same actions replay identically); card instances are **uids** (`"p1#0"`) over an injected `cardPool` carried in the state (keeps `applyAction` a pure 2-arg pair and snapshots self-contained). Seats **`p1`/`p2` are labels, not turn order** — the coin winner *chooses* first via an action, `firstPlayer` records it. **Choices are actions; mandatory steps auto-resolve** inside the reducer (mulligan redraws, prize setting, the turn-start draw), so a `Phase` exists only where a player owes a decision. **Events are the animation + wire contract** (semantic, serializable, payloads always copied — never aliasing state or the caller's action). **Wire-safety:** TS types are not input validation — P4 decodes actions from clients, so every index/seat/count an action carries is runtime-checked (three real card-destroying bugs came from exactly this: a JSON string `"1"` bench index). *Why: the engine runs in two runtimes (web local play + the authoritative DO), and an authoritative server needs a total, replayable, non-throwing function of (state, action).* (2026-07-14) |
| D15 | Mulligan compensation (rules correction) | Each player may draw 1 card **per opponent mulligan**, and **mulliganing yourself does not forfeit it** — so **both** seats can be owed at once (each off the other's count). `reference/ptcg-rules.md` §3.5 previously read "the non-mulliganing player", which the M1 engine implemented as an exemption that does not exist; the reference doc is corrected and the phase now carries a per-seat `owed`/`decided` map. The compensation draw is **clamped** so a player can never draw below the 6 cards they must still set aside as prizes. *Why: a wrong rule in the spec propagated straight into the engine — the reference doc is the source of truth and had to be fixed at the source.* (2026-07-14) |
| D16 | M2 staged turn tail, KO interrupts & damage modifiers (P3-M2) | The turn tail is a **`pending: PendingStage[]` queue** on `GameState`, drained by `advance()` (flow.ts): automatic stages run, decision stages either **park** (phase becomes the matching `ko:takePrizes`/`ko:promote` interrupt, prompt event emitted) or **auto-resolve** when only one legal resolution exists. `attack` seeds `takePrizes`+`promote` ahead of the shared `turnTail(seat)` triple. The parked phase mirrors `pending[0]` (dual encoding, kept deliberately for M2): handlers cross-check the two and reject with **`PHASE_DESYNC`** on mismatch. Win evaluation lives in ONE place — the shared `resolvePrizesAndResume`/`resolvePromotionAndResume` helpers used by both the action handlers and advance's auto-resolves. `gameOver` carries **`GameOutcome`** (`win {winner, reason}` \| `tie {reasons}`); sudden death is surfaced as `tie`, not played out. Printed weakness/resistance parse to a **`DamageModifier {op: multiply\|add\|subtract, amount}`** (×N / +N / −N; unknown garble keeps modern-era ×2/−30 defaults) applied weakness→resistance, floor 0 (§8.5); `DAMAGE_DEALT` carries the modifiers so animators can show the math. Data gaps never guess a KO: `hp ≤ 0` reads as the null data gap. *Why: KO resolution must pause mid-tail for player choices, M3's checkup KOs reuse the same interrupts, and additive legacy weakness ("+20", schema-documented) was being silently misread as ×2.* (2026-07-16) |
| D17 | Engine→playmat projection contract (P3) | **`projectGameState(state, viewerSeat) → GameProjection`** (`src/features/game/projection.ts`): `{ board, piles, pokemon, turn, activePlayer, waitingOn, pendingDecision, outcome }` — `board` is a drop-in playmat `BoardState` (`you` = viewer); the wrapper carries what BoardState can't (`piles` deckCount/discard, `pokemon` = per-uid `{damage, hp}`, `outcome` per-viewer win/tie). **`activePlayer` = turn owner; `waitingOn` = the seat that must act NOW** — the split exists because during `ko:promote` the actor is the NON-turn player (the KO'd defender), and a UI gating on turn ownership alone soft-locks. Hidden info is redacted structurally: opponent hand = positional backs (`opponent-hand-N`, no uids — a uid is stable across zones and would let a client track a hidden card), both prize piles = counts only, decks = counts. Public cards key off the engine **uid** so the Pixi layer animates a card as one object across projections. The phase mapping is an **exhaustive switch** (`satisfies never`) — a new engine phase is a compile error, not a silent guess. *Why: this is the same per-viewer filter P4's server will apply; building it client-side first pins the contract with tests.* (2026-07-16) **Revised 2026-07-17 (wire-up review):** during any `setup:*` phase the OPPONENT's active/bench also project as face-down backs (positional `-hidden` ids, no `pokemon` rows — a row would leak the uid key + printed HP); a hot-seat viewer flip at Ready was exposing the first player's face-down placements. Reveal re-keys positional→uid (remount, not flip) — the same trade as hand backs. |

| D18 | Local play page & engine card-pool sourcing (P3 wire-up) | **`/play`** is a hot-seat local match: `GamePage` owns `GameState` via `useLocalGame` (pure reducer over `applyAction`), renders `projectGameState` through the extracted presentational **`PlaymatView`** (the `/simulator` mock, retitled "Sandbox", is a thin wrapper driver), and the **viewer follows `waitingOn`** for the device flip. The engine `cardPool` is built client-side by **fanning out `GET /cards/:id`** (`src/features/game/cardPool.ts`, concurrency 8, module-level promise cache; 404s cache as stable `missing`, rejections self-evict) — NOT via the brief-returning batch route. *Why: `CardBrief` deliberately omits the combat blobs (attacks/weakness/retreat) the engine reads; the per-id route already serves the full canonical Card KV-cached, and this needed no api change while api deploys stay user-gated. A `?full` batch param is the logged follow-up to bundle with the next deploy.* (2026-07-17) |
| D19 | Drop-legality is a driver-supplied predicate (P3 wire-up review) | `AnimatedCardsLayer` takes an optional **`PlacementPredicate`** (defaults to the sandbox's `canPlace`, so the mock is untouched); `/play` supplies `gamePlacementPredicate(game, viewerSeat)` — a deliberately **coarse, gesture-class** gate (phase-aware: what CAN ever map to an action) kept in lockstep with `moveToAction` by a property test (every afforded drop translates to a non-null action). Fine-grained legality stays with `applyAction` + the error pill. Hand→hand reorder is rejected on /play (the engine owns hand order; affording it was a silent lie). *Why: the phase-unaware mock `canPlace` afforded gestures the engine could never accept (accept-animation → wrong-rule error pill), and M4 evolve/trainer drags can't be expressed through it — the seam had to move to the driver.* (2026-07-17) |
| D20 | Text-derived attack effects (P3-M3) | Status-applying attacks resolve through a **conservative exact-sentence deriver** (`packages/engine/src/effects.ts`): five anchored whole-effect patterns ("Your opponent's Active Pokémon is now {X}.", the coin-flip-gated variant, "This Pokémon is now {X}.", the Rest heal compound, the raised-poison Checkup variant) compile to a tiny **`EffectStep[]`** program (applyStatus/heal/coinFlipGate) executed after damage, before the KO check; ANY other text keeps today's loud `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED`. This is the first sliver of D8's op-program system — M4's id-keyed registry will sit ABOVE it (`programFor(cardId) ?? derive(text)`), and the interpreter is deliberately still attack-private until M4's ops force its real shape. **Self-`Paralyzed` is excluded from the self-pattern**: official wording clears paralysis only if "Paralyzed since the beginning of your last turn", so self-paralysis at your own turn's end must SURVIVE the immediate checkup — our blanket ended-seat clear (correct for every opponent-inflicted case) would wrongly erase it, so the deriver refuses the sentence until paralysis carries an applied-turn stamp; `reference/ptcg-rules.md` §12/§13.4 corrected at the source (the D15 precedent). Demo decks swapped in real status attackers (Numel sv03-031 ×3 Fire/Burn, Luvdisc sv02-047 ×3 Water/Asleep) so /play exercises the pipeline. *Why: statuses without an application path are dead code until M4; exact-sentence matching makes dozens of real cards fully simulated with zero guessing, and the apostrophe class (`['’]`) is the only tolerance so a re-ingest can't silently un-derive them.* (2026-07-17) |
| D21 | Battle state rides the CardModel (P3-M3 surface) | `CardModel` gains **`battle?: {damage, hp, conditions}`**, attached by the projection to in-play Pokémon only — the `statuses` side-table (`GameProjection.pokemon`, prop-drilled 5 hops to DamageChip) is DELETED; chips (damage + the new SLP/PAR/CNF/BRN/PSN pills) read the card they decorate. The playmat stays engine-free: `BattleConditions` is a structural restatement of the engine's `SpecialConditions`, pinned by a **two-way compile-time assignability assert** in `battleStateOf` (an added engine condition field fails the build instead of silently never rendering); `conditions` is REQUIRED (`NO_CONDITIONS` const for mocks) so a missing object can't read as "no conditions" downstream. The checkup itself runs entirely inside `advance` — **no new Phase members**; its KOs park on the existing ko:* interrupts, and the projection reports **`activeSeat: null` for between-turns parks** (discriminated by the pending queue: an in-turn KO still carries its endTurn/checkup tail, a checkup KO has drained it to bare startTurn). *Why: the side-table was the wire-up review's known wart; co-location kills the drilling, and the vocabulary stays out of the playmat per the zero-engine-imports rule — lift it to `@luminous/schema` when M4 markers force the shape to grow.* (2026-07-17) |

| D23 | Trainers + Abilities via a resumable effect interpreter (P3-M4 slice 2) | The real first appearance of D8's op-program system (D20's deriver was "the first sliver"). **One shared interpreter** (`packages/engine/src/interpreter.ts`) runs every op program — attacks, Trainers, Abilities alike; the M3 attack-private `runEffectSteps` is GONE. Attacks resolve `programFor(id)?.attack ?? deriveAttackEffect(text)` at the one call site (registry ABOVE the deriver, `registry.ts`); Trainers/Abilities have NO text deriver — an unauthored one loudly can't be played/used rather than guessing. **Mid-effect decisions PARK** via a new `effect:choose` Phase carrying a JSON continuation (`{pendingOp, rest, ctx}`) — replayable, and OUTSIDE the KO/win tail (no in-scope effect KOs or ends the game, so the interpreter never touches flow.ts). New actions: `playTrainer` (Item unbounded §7.1 / Supporter 1/turn §7.2 + the §4 first-turn Supporter ban; the card discards up front — none references itself mid-effect), `useAbility` (activated only; the once-per-turn per-Pokémon flag lives in `TurnAllowances.abilitiesUsed`, §9/§15.J), `resolveEffect` (the parked-decision resolver, every choice wire-checked against the prompt — the D14 never-throw contract extended to the effect layer). New ops: drawCards / discardHand / shuffleDeck / searchDeck→(bench|hand) / switchActive / gust / healChosen (+ M3's applyStatus/heal/coinFlipGate unified into `EffectOp`). **Passive abilities are a pipeline HOOK, not the interpreter** (Bouffalant "Bouffer": −20 damage AFTER Weakness/Resistance, read at the attack damage step; `DAMAGE_DEALT` gained an optional `reduction`). **Web:** Trainers/Abilities are HUD **buttons** (not board drags — the drag layer already no-ops on unknown phases, so `effect:choose` affords nothing), and `effect:choose` renders a chooseCards / choosePokemon dialog; §4/§7.2/§9 gating is mirrored on the buttons but the ENGINE owns fine legality (gust/switch with no legal target reject → the D19 pill). Representative set only — 6 Trainers (Professor's Research / Nest Ball / Boss's Orders / Switch / Potion / Energy Search) + 2 Abilities (Chien-Pao ex *Shivery Chill* activated, Bouffalant *Bouffer* passive), real sv01–sv03 ids in the demo decks; **broad SV-pool coverage is a later Workflow fan-out that just adds registry rows.** engineVersion 0.5.0. *Why: the M3 traps predicted exactly this — the registry sits above the deriver, and the interpreter had to be RESTRUCTURED (not extracted) to gain mid-effect parking + non-Active targets. Doing Trainers AND Abilities together proved the one interpreter serves both trigger models (play-a-card vs use-an-ability) and the passive-hook path, so the shape is validated before scale.* (2026-07-17) |
| D24 | Persistent-zone Trainers via a continuous-modifier pipeline (P3-M4 slice 3) | Stadiums (§7.3) and Pokémon Tools (§7.4) don't run op **programs** — their effects are **continuous**, so a new module **`packages/engine/src/continuous.ts`** aggregates them and is the ONE place a rule read consults them: `effectiveRetreatCost` (turn.ts retreat), `effectiveAttackCost` (attack.ts cost check), `effectiveMaxHp` (flow.ts `isLethallyDamaged` — the KO test — and the web `battle.hp`), and `passivesOf` (attack.ts damage: a defender's post-W/R reduction — now Bouffalant "Bouffer" **and** a defending Tool — plus an attacker's pre-W/R bonus). **Stadium** is a single shared **`GameState.stadium` {uid, owner}**: `playTrainer` routes Stadiums to `playStadium` (one/turn via a new `stadiumPlayed` allowance; replace-on-play discards the predecessor to **its owner's** pile; the same-**NAME** ban compares names not ids, so the sv02-171/sv03-229 "Artazon" reprints can't self-replace); allowed on turn 1 (§4 bans only Supporters + attacking). **Tool** is a new action **`attachTool`** (NOT playTrainer — it carries a target): one per Pokémon, uncapped per turn, no allowance consumed, no detach — it rides the stack through evolution and discards with a KO'd stack (the `tools` slot M1 reserved is finally populated). Both a Stadium and a Tool with NO authored `stadium`/`passive` row reject loudly (`TRAINER_NOT_SIMULATED`) — the coverage strategy, not a silent no-op. Representative set: Beach Court (sv01-167, Basic retreat −1) + Pokémon League HQ (sv03-192, Basic attacks +{C}) + Vitality Band (sv01-197, +10 before W/R) + Bravery Charm (sv02-173, Basic +50 HP). **Web:** Stadiums are BOTH a HUD button and a drag onto the shared stadium slot (`moveToAction`/`gamePlacementPredicate` gained the `owner:"global" zone:"stadium"` exception — the one non-own drop target); Tools attach by drag onto an own Pokémon (the energy-attach gesture). The projection shows the shared Stadium public (it can't exist during setup) and `battle.hp` reads the continuous max HP; HUD retreat/attack costs read the effective ones. engineVersion 0.6.0. **Deferred gap (documented, not silent):** evolution can now LOWER effective max HP (a Bravery Charm's Basic-only +50 dropping off), so a damaged charmed Basic that evolves below the new stage's HP should KO the instant it evolves (§8.1) — but `evolve` runs no KO check and the engine's KO flow is Active-only + turn-ending. An Active in that state is cleaned up at the next Checkup; a benched one is not (the same Active-only KO limit bench spread damage already carries). A mid-turn KO that RESUMES the actor's turn is new flow machinery, deferred with the bench-KO generalization; pinned by a characterization test. *Why: Stadium/Tool effects are continuous reads, not the interpreter's mid-effect programs, so a pipeline module — not registry op arrays — is the right seam, and it unifies the passive hook D23 introduced (Tools and printed passives feed one read site).* (2026-07-18) |
| D25 | Knock Out generalized off the Active (P3-M4 slice 4) | The KO flow was **Active-only + turn-ending**; two reaches needed it generalized. (1) **`knockOut(state, koedSeat, koedUid, prizeSeat, events)`** now KOs ANY in-play Pokémon, found by its stack-top **uid** (so a batch of KOs can't shift a later target's bench slot): a benched KO discards the whole stack, compacts the bench, and seeds ONLY a `takePrizes` stage — no promotion, since it leaves no empty Active spot. (2) **`collectKnockOuts(state, seats, events)`** is the ONE post-damage sweep — every lethally-damaged in-play Pokémon (Active + Bench), prizes to each KO'd side's opponent (`otherSeat`, universal per §8.1), stages grouped **prizes-first**, and the §14 **tie guard runs only when both seats lost a Pokémon** (a one-sided batch only ever wins for the one player) via the same speculative `evaluateWin`. It replaces the inline KO blocks in `attack.ts finishAttack` (now sweeps the defender's WHOLE board — the Active from base damage AND any sniped Bench) and `flow.ts runCheckup`, so §14 precedence stays single-sourced. (3) **A new `PendingStage {kind:"resumeTurn"; seat}` + `resolveMidTurnKnockOuts`**: a KO mid-turn (not from an attack) resolves its prize/promote stages then **RESUMES** the actor's turn (phase back to `turn:action`) instead of ending it — allowances live on `GameState`, untouched by the interrupt. This closes D24's **deferred evolve-below-HP gap**: `evolve` now runs the KO check, so a benched charmed Basic that evolves below the new stage's HP (a Bravery Charm's Basic-only +50 dropping off — the one effect that LOWERS effective max HP) is Knocked Out the instant it evolves, mid-turn, with the opponent taking the prize on the actor's turn. (4) **A `spreadDamage` op** (`{op:"spreadDamage", target:"opponentBench", amount}`) placed by the interpreter — flat damage to EACH opponent Benched Pokémon (no Weakness/Resistance on the Bench, §8.5); the interpreter only PLACES it (one `DAMAGE_DEALT` per benched Pokémon), the KO check staying in `finishAttack`, so the interpreter still never KOs or touches the flow tail. A sixth deriver sentence recognizes "This attack does N damage to each of your opponent's Benched Pokémon." (optional W/R clarifier). **Web:** the ONLY source change is `projection.ts koParkActiveSeat` treating `resumeTurn` as in-turn (a mid-turn KO park still reports the actor as `activePlayer`); the exhaustive `phaseViewOf` Phase switch is untouched (`resumeTurn` is a PendingStage, not a Phase), and the log/HUD need nothing — `DAMAGE_DEALT`/`KNOCKED_OUT` resolve a benched uid the same way, and the opponent's mid-turn prize pick rides the existing `waitingOn` viewer-follow. Representative demonstrator is a fixture spread attacker (Spread Shot); a live sv card lands with the coverage fan-out. engineVersion 0.7.0. *Why: bench spread/snipe damage (a large card class) and the evolve-below-HP correctness gap both needed the KO target generalized AND a mid-turn KO that resumes the turn — one sweep serving the attack, checkup and mid-turn paths keeps §14 single-sourced, and keeping the KO check out of the interpreter preserves the D23 rule that flow.ts owns every Knock Out.* (2026-07-18) |
| D27 | Triggered Abilities via event scan points (P3-M4 slice 6) | Triggered Abilities (§9) — the ones that FIRE on a game event, not player-activated — get a **`TriggeredAbility`** entry on `CardProgram.triggered` (`{ name; trigger; program; optional?; activeOnly? }`) run through the shared interpreter by a small **`triggers.ts`** wired at **three scan points**: **`onPlayToBench`** (turn.ts playBasicToBench — a turn play, NOT a setup placement), **`onEvolve`** (turn.ts evolve), **`betweenTurns`** (flow.ts runCheckup). Board triggers run on the controller's own turn, so a program needing a decision **PARKS on the existing `effect:choose`** and resumes to turn:action via `resolveEffect` — a parked trigger is byte-identical to a parked Trainer, so the **web needs no new dialog** (only two `logFromEvents` rows). `betweenTurns` triggers are **NON-PARKING by construction** and run synchronously inside the one Checkup reduction, AFTER the §13 condition ticks and BEFORE the KO sweep — a placed counter (Trevenant) KOs through that same shared sweep, and a lethal-poison + lethal-counter double KO rides the existing §14 tie guard. **Optional ("you may") triggers AUTO-FIRE** (every representative is pure upside; an "up to" search still lets the player take none) — a yes/no confirm for a downside trigger is the follow-up. New ops: **`healEach`** (heal N from EACH own Pokémon), **`damageActive`** (PUT N counters on the opponent's Active — a counter placement, so NO Weakness/Resistance AND **no damage-reduction passive**: "Bouffer" reduces *attack* damage, which an Ability's counter is not — the poison/burn-tick rule, distinct from spreadDamage which is attack damage); `healChosen.amount` widened to `number \| "all"`; a `byName` search filter. New event `ABILITY_TRIGGERED`; `COUNTERS_PLACED` gained source `"ability"`. Representative set (real sv01–03, verified vs local D1): **Flamigo "Insta-Flock"** (sv02-170/227), **Arboliva "Enriching Oil"** (sv01-023), **Garganacl "Blessed Salt"** (sv02-123), **Trevenant "Forest Miasma"** (sv03-012). **Deferred (documented):** `onKnockOut` (fires during the OPPONENT's turn mid-KO-sweep — needs a resume-the-tail continuation); parking mid-Checkup; the multi-target snipe + `settleRun`→`resolveMidTurnKnockOuts` reuse (an on-play/on-evolve *damaging* trigger that KOs mid-turn — Hawlucha/Meowscarada). Not added to the seed-tuned demo decks (an auto-firing trigger would perturb the demo hands) — covered by engine + web tests, the slice-4/5 precedent. engineVersion 0.8.0 → 0.9.0. *Why: triggered Abilities are a large multi-call-site surface (the slice-2 note flagged "a bigger surface best isolated"); doing the framework at the three scan points where the controller's-turn parks reuse effect:choose and the Checkup path reuses the existing KO sweep proves the mechanism with zero new web dialog code before the hairier on-KO / mid-turn-damage variants.* (2026-07-18) |
| D28 | Rare Candy — a dedicated evolve-skip action reusing a shared placement (P3-M4 slice 7) | Rare Candy (§7.1) evolves a Basic straight to a **Stage 2** from hand, skipping the Stage 1 ("counts as evolving"). It is NOT an interpreter op program — it evolves, and **flow.ts owns every Knock Out** (the interpreter never KOs) — so it is a dedicated **`rareCandy` action** `{seat, uid (the Rare Candy Item), target (the Basic in play), evolutionUid (the Stage 2 in hand)}` in cardplay.ts. The `evolve` tail was extracted into a shared **`placeEvolution`** (turn.ts) that BOTH `evolve` and `rareCandy` call, so Rare Candy inherits §10 carry-over (damage/energy/tools stay, conditions/markers clear, turnPlayed resets), the **on-evolve triggered Ability**, and the **evolve-below-HP mid-turn KO** (a Bravery Charm's Basic-only +50 falling off) for free. Timing mirrors `evolve` exactly (the §4 first-turn ban + the `turnPlayed >= turn` "played this turn" ban). **The Basic→Stage 2 link is resolved by BRIDGING**: the catalog stores only each card's IMMEDIATE `evolveFrom` (a Stage 2 names its Stage 1, never the Basic), so `stage2EvolvesFromBasic` (cardplay.ts) checks that some card in `state.cardPool` is a Stage 1 named `stage2.evolveFrom` whose own `evolveFrom` is the target Basic's name. **Documented gap:** a deck running Rare Candy + a Stage 2 but ZERO copies of the intervening Stage 1 can't resolve the link (the Stage 1 is absent from the pool) until the full evolution family is persisted at ingest — the same ingest-debt class as `prizeValueOf` = 1 and name-derived energy; the demo Ember deck runs Crocalor so it resolves (verified vs the live api: sv01-038 Skeledirge → sv01-037 Crocalor → sv01-036 Fuecoco). A registry **marker** `CardProgram.rareCandy?: true` (not a program) identifies it; `playTrainer` rejects a Rare Candy uid (`TRAINER_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`, the Tool-routing precedent). **Web:** an engine helper **`rareCandyOptions(state, seat)`** (the authority) lists the legal {Basic, matching Stage 2s} pairings; Rare Candy shows in the Trainers list but its button opens a bespoke **`RareCandyDialog`** (pick a Basic → pick a Stage 2 → dispatch `rareCandy`) rather than `playTrainer` — the one Trainer that carries a target isn't expressible through the generic button. The **projection needs NO change**: `rareCandy` → `placeEvolution` resolves to `turn:action` (done), a mid-turn KO park (`resumeTurn` — already in-turn per koParkActiveSeat), or an on-evolve trigger park (`effect:choose` — origin-agnostic), all already handled. New error code `RARE_CANDY_NO_STAGE2`. Demo: Rare Candy ×2 added to Ember (Fire energy 14→12; non-Basic-for-non-Basic keeps the mulligan rate). engineVersion 0.9.0 (unchanged — additive). *Why: Rare Candy sits at the intersection of Trainer-play + hand-inspection + evolve (stack/carry-over/timing) + mid-turn KO + on-evolve trigger — it does not fit the interpreter's "pure op, never KO" model, so a dedicated action that reuses the extracted evolve placement is cleaner and keeps flow.ts the single owner of Knock Outs; the bridge is the honest read of the data we persist, degrading gracefully with a documented gap.* (2026-07-18) |
| D29 | The mid-turn-damage snipe — a damaging op + a unified program-completion KO sweep (P3-M4 slice 8) | A damaging Ability that puts damage counters on CHOSEN opponent Benched Pokémon and can Knock one Out **mid-turn** (the actor keeps their turn). New op **`damageChosen`** (`{op, target:"opponentBench", amount, count}`, effects.ts/interpreter.ts): place `amount` HP of counters on `count` chosen opponent Benched Pokémon — **flat** (no Weakness/Resistance, no reduction passive; a counter placed by an Ability is not "damage from attacks" — the `damageActive`/poison-tick rule, **distinct from `spreadDamage`** which IS attack damage and does take Bouffer). PARKS on a NEW multi-Pokémon prompt **`choosePokemonMulti {candidates, max}`** / choice `pokemonMulti {refs}` (the first multi-select Pokémon decision; "you may" + "up to" → 0..max distinct refs) when the opponent has more than `count` Benched; forced (hits all) at ≤ count; no-op at 0. **The KO reaches flow.ts via a unified `settleProgram`** (flow.ts): the two IDENTICAL local settle helpers (triggers.ts `settleTrigger`, cardplay.ts `settleRun`) collapse into ONE that, on a completed program, returns the controller to turn:action then runs the shared **`resolveMidTurnKnockOuts`** (slice-4 machinery) over both boards — so a snipe that drops an opponent's Benched Pokémon resolves as a mid-turn KO that hands the turn back to the actor (the opponent's Pokémon prizes to the actor; a game-winning KO ends the game). It sweeps BOTH boards (a harmless superset — only the opponent's board can be freshly lethal from an in-scope effect, the same total scan runCheckup does) and is **behaviour-preserving for every non-damaging program** (the KO sweep is a no-op → straight back to turn:action, proven by the unchanged engine suite). The interpreter/triggers still never KO — completion is handed BACK to flow (the KO/tail owner), the intentional flow↔triggers cycle (both used only in function bodies). Meowscarada ex "Bouquet Magic" adds the engine's FIRST **ability activation cost** (`AbilityProgram.cost = {kind:"discardBasicEnergy", energyType}`): `useAbility` gates on the shared **`programPlayable`** (renamed from `trainerPlayable`, extended so the snipe needs an opponent Bench — a play that does nothing can't be made, so the cost is never spent for no effect) then pays the cost (discard a Basic {G} Energy from hand, `ABILITY_COST_PAID`) before running — an unpayable cost rejects **`ABILITY_COST_UNMET`** with nothing spent; the cost is paid once, before parking, so a parked snipe resolving via `resolveEffect` never re-charges. Representatives (verified vs the live api): **Hawlucha** (sv01-118, triggered onPlayToBench, 2×1 counter) and **Meowscarada ex** (sv02-015/256, activated, once/turn, not Active-only, 1×3 counters). NOT in the demo decks (auto-fire/contrived preconditions; test-covered — slice-4/5/6/7 precedent). **Web:** the projection is UNCHANGED (choosePokemonMulti rides the existing effect:choose; the mid-turn KO park is `resumeTurn` = in-turn, handled by koParkActiveSeat since slice 4); GameHud adds one `ChoosePokemonMultiDialog`; `logFromEvents` adds one `ABILITY_COST_PAID` row; Meowscarada's button uses the D19 afford-then-reject (no special gate). engineVersion 0.9.0 → 0.10.0 (additive). *Why: this is the deferred "multi-target snipe + settleRun→resolveMidTurnKnockOuts" tail from slices 4/6 — the last mid-turn-KO reach; unifying the two settle helpers is the natural home for the KO sweep (both already folded a program result to a phase), and the flat-counter rule keeps the poison/`damageActive` invariant that only *attack* damage takes Bouffer.* (2026-07-19) |
| D26 | Special Energy — provision as data, a wildcard cost matcher, and on-attach effects (P3-M4 slice 5) | Special Energies (§6.1) provisioned a conservative 1 Colorless (name-derived, cards.ts). Slice 5 models their rules text. **`EnergyProgram`** on `CardProgram.energy` (registry.ts): `{ provides: string[]; demoteWithOtherSpecial?: string[]; onAttach?: {kind:"switchIfBenched"} }`. **`providedEnergy(state, pokemon)`** (continuous.ts) is now the ONE provision read site — a basic energy gives its type, an AUTHORED special energy its EnergyProgram units (a concrete type, the `ANY_ENERGY` **wildcard**, or several — multi-unit is allowed), an UNAUTHORED special energy falls back to one Colorless (an energy always provides *something*, so it degrades gracefully rather than rejecting loudly like an unsimulated Trainer). Luminous Energy's "provides {C} instead if the holder has any OTHER Special Energy attached" is evaluated here against the Pokémon's other energies. **`costMet`** (attack.ts) upgraded for the wildcard: `ANY_ENERGY` fills a typed shortfall OR a Colorless slot — wildcards are allocated to the (pickier) typed needs first, then every remaining unit (surplus typed, leftover wildcards, Colorless-provided) pays the Colorless; backward-compatible with all-concrete provision. Both the engine's §8.2 cost check AND the HUD's payability preview call `providedEnergy` → **the D18 duplicated payability is gone** (they cannot drift). **On-attach**: `attachEnergy` (turn.ts) runs `energy.onAttach` — Jet Energy's `switchIfBenched` switches a benched holder to the Active Spot (reusing `switchInto`, now exported from interpreter.ts), firing ENERGY_ATTACHED then POKEMON_SWITCHED; nothing fires on an Active attach. Representative set: **Jet Energy (sv02-190)** + **Luminous Energy (sv02-191)** — the SV-era catalog has no Double Turbo (swsh-era), so the multi-provide case here is the wildcard kind. Demo decks run them **different per deck** (Ember: Jet, Tidal: Luminous — type-neutral, so the disjoint-energy rule keeps them split); both reachable in one Ember-vs-Tidal game. engineVersion 0.8.0. *Why: name-derived provision mis-simulated every special energy (a wildcard/multi-provider read as 1 Colorless), and a proper data model both fixes it and unifies the two payability copies (D18) behind one wildcard-aware matcher — the cost-assignment upgrade the M2 costMet comment reserved.* (2026-07-18) |
| D22 | Evolution is the first M4 slice (P3-M4) | M4 opens with **evolution** — a board action, NOT the effect registry (that waits for trainers/items). New `evolve` action (`packages/engine/src/turn.ts`, alongside playBasicToBench/retreat): places an Evolution card (`evolveFromOf(card) !== null`, cards.ts) on top of an own in-play Pokémon whose **top card name** equals the card's `evolveFrom`, carrying damage/energy/tools on the **stack** and clearing Special Conditions + markers (`POKEMON_EVOLVED` + a `STATUS_CLEARED` reason **"evolved"** naming the new top). Timing is TWO checks (§4/§10): the per-Pokémon **`turnPlayed >= state.turn`** ("not the turn it came into play, nor twice a turn" — evolving stamps turnPlayed to the current turn), plus the **first-turn ban** (`state.turn === (seat === firstPlayer ? 1 : 2)`) that turnPlayed can't express because setup Pokémon carry turnPlayed 0 by design. Every wire value is runtime-checked (BAD_TARGET/BAD_BENCH_INDEX/CARD_NOT_IN_HAND) — the D14 never-throw contract. No KO check (evolving never KOs; §10 devolution is the only shrink and is out of scope). **Web (drag):** a Pokémon dropped ONTO an own occupant is an evolve gesture — `moveToAction`/`gamePlacementPredicate` route pokemon→Active and pokemon→**occupied** bench index to `evolve`, pokemon→**free** bench (the index-less frame container, no index) stays `playBasicToBench` — the SAME gesture disambiguation as energy-attach (indexed = onto that Pokémon, container = the whole zone). Fine legality (name match/timing) stays the engine's, surfaced as the pill (D19). Demo decks gained real lines (Ember Fuecoco→Crocalor→Skeledirge, Tidal Buizel→Floatzel). *Why: evolution is foundational (Stage 1/2 were unplayable), self-contained, and reuses the `turnPlayed`/`stack`/`tools` fields M1 reserved for exactly this — it needs neither the effect-op interpreter restructure nor the id-keyed registry, so it lands cleanly before them.* (2026-07-17) |
| D30 | on-KO triggered Abilities — an inline prize guard + a resume-the-tail koTrigger stage (P3-M4 slice 9, closes M4) | Triggered Abilities that fire **"when this Pokémon is Knocked Out"** (§8.1) — the last M4 timing, firing during the OPPONENT's turn mid-KO-sweep (the inverse of the slice-8 snipe: the KO fires the trigger, not the trigger firing the KO). `TriggerTiming` gains **`"onKnockOut"`**; detection (`onKnockOutTrigger`, triggers.ts) resolves via `cardIdByUid → programFor`, valid AFTER the KO discarded the card (the id map is permanent). Two forms on `TriggeredAbility`, kept deliberately separate because they live in different layers: **(1) a coin-flip Prize GUARD** (`onKoPrizeGuard: "coinFlipPrevent"` — Glimmora "Shattering Crystal": heads → the opponent takes NO Prize for it) resolved **inline in the KO sweep** (flow.ts `planPrizes`, run UP FRONT — before the §14 tie-guard speculation — so the speculation and the real KO share the post-flip count and the rng advances ONCE; NOT the interpreter, which never touches the KO/prize tail, the D23 invariant); **(2) a general parking PROGRAM** run as a new **`koTrigger` PendingStage** folded through `settleProgram` with a new **`resumeTail`** flag — it PARKS on `effect:choose` (where `phase.seat` is the KO'd, NON-turn player) and the sweep RESUMES draining the remaining prize/promotion stages via `resolveEffect`. Batch stages group PRIZES-first, then koTriggers, then promotions; a §14 game-ending prize (finishGame clears `pending`) skips the koTrigger program, while the guard — resolved earlier — can itself DENY a game-winning prize. Representatives: **Glimmora** (sv02-126, real, verified vs the local catalog) + **fix-onko** (FIXTURE — no real SV on-KO Ability requires a decision, the fix-sniper demonstrator precedent). **Web:** the projection's `effect:choose` case reads the turn owner via **`koParkActiveSeat`** when `resumeTail` (so `activeSeat` ≠ `waitingSeat` — the KO'd player owes the decision mid-opponent-turn, exactly like the ko:* parks); two new log rows (`ABILITY_COIN_FLIP`, `PRIZE_PREVENTED`); the parked decision reuses the existing `chooseCards` dialog. engineVersion 0.10.0 → **0.11.0**. *Why: on-KO was the last deferred M4 mid-turn-KO timing; resolving the prize guard inline keeps §14 EXACT and honours "flow.ts owns every Knock Out, the interpreter never touches the tail", while the `koTrigger` stage + `resumeTail` is exactly the resume-the-tail continuation the slice-6/8 deferral called for. 2-agent Opus review (engine+rules / web+integration) BOTH CLEAN — no HIGH/MED/LOW correctness bugs; the §14 tie-guard×prize-denial interaction was enumerated over all four coin-flip combinations × 60 seeds and proven exact.* (2026-07-19) |

| D31 | Coverage expansion is a classify→verify fan-out that harvests existing-op cards + ranks a new-op backlog (P3-M5 pass #1) | The SV-pool coverage push (D8's "op programs across the pool") runs as a **Workflow fan-out**, but its FIRST job is CLASSIFICATION, not mass-authoring: agents judge each un-authored card against the current op vocabulary with the deriver doctrine (**exact map or flag** — a wrong-but-plausible program is a rules bug worse than a flag), an adversarial verifier re-checks every authorable draft, and the main loop **hand-verifies each program vs the local catalog before landing it** (this caught Poké Ball's shuffle placed outside its coin gate). Pass #1 over the 140 un-authored sv01–03 Trainers/Energy/Abilities found coverage is **op-bound, not authoring-bound** — only 5 expressible today, 113 needing new ops — so the primary deliverable is the **ranked new-op backlog** (`docs/reference/coverage-backlog.md`, cards-unlocked order) that turns the open-ended coverage problem into a prioritized sequence of well-scoped op-slices; the authorable subset lands as additive registry rows + fixtures + tests (no engine mechanism change → no 2-agent slice review, since classify+verify+hand-verify+tests already gate each program). New ops themselves are normal engine slices (interpreter case + review), each converting dozens of "needs-op" rows into data rows. *Why: authoring hundreds of card programs blind is high-risk and mostly blocked on missing ops anyway; classify-first maximizes coverage with the current engine, produces the roadmap, and keeps the human/main-loop in control of every codebase change.* (2026-07-19) |

| D32 | `attachEnergyFrom` — the energy-acceleration op (P3-M5 op-slice, top backlog unlock) | The first new op built off the D31 backlog. **`attachEnergyFrom`** (`{op, source:"hand"|"discard", energyType?}`, effects.ts/interpreter.ts) attaches ONE Basic Energy from the controller's hand or discard pile onto a CHOSEN own in-play Pokémon (§6 acceleration). Same-type Basic Energy is fungible, so the ONLY decision is the TARGET — it reuses the existing `parkOrForce` → `choosePokemon` prompt (no new prompt/event/dialog), re-finding the first matching energy at apply time (deterministic — while parked only the controller's `resolveEffect` runs). A source with no matching Basic Energy is a no-op, and `programPlayable` (cardplay.ts) blocks activating an Ability whose attach could only whiff (the gust/snipe precedent). It is NOT the §6.3 one-per-turn manual attach — no allowance is touched (it's the Ability's own effect); it reuses `ENERGY_ATTACHED` (host resolved by the energy uid). Representatives: **Quaquaval "Energy Carnival"** (sv01-054 — any Basic from hand, once/turn) and **Baxcalibur "Super Cold"** (sv02-060/210 — a {W} from hand, **REPEATABLE** `oncePerTurn:false`, so the HUD button never locks out), plus a FIXTURE **fix-attacher** for the discard-source branch (the clean real discard cards — Gardevoir/Koraidon — carry riders that need later ops). **Web:** UNCHANGED (reuses effect:choose/choosePokemon + ENERGY_ATTACHED); a `oncePerTurn:false` ability stays an enabled button. engineVersion 0.11.0 → **0.12.0**. 2-agent Opus review (engine+rules / web) BOTH CLEAN — no HIGH/MED/LOW bugs (no card duplication/loss, safe fungibility re-pick, wire-safety, discard order preserved, purity; the reuse claim verified through the real HUD). **Scope:** single-attach only — this authors 2 of the 11 `attachEnergyFrom` backlog cards; the other 9 layer RIDERS (multi / +counters / ends-turn / self-KO / deck-search / `lookAtTopN`) on this primitive in later slices. **One INFO (documented, unreachable today):** `programPlayable` runs BEFORE an ability cost is paid, so a future ability pairing a `cost` with a hand-source attach whose only match is the cost card could pass the gate then no-op (a wasted activation, never a corrupt state) — revisit when the discard-with-rider variants land. *Why: attachEnergyFrom was the single biggest backlog unlock (11 cards / the engine of most evolving-deck Abilities); building the clean single-attach primitive first — with the fungible-energy simplification collapsing it to a one-decision op — lands it cheaply and gives the riders a base to compose on.* (2026-07-19) |

| D33 | `attachEnergyFrom` riders — targetType / bonusCounters / notIfKO (P3-M5, Gardevoir ex) | The D32 attach op grows three composable RIDERS to author the marquee **Gardevoir ex "Psychic Embrace"** (sv01-086/228/245): **`targetType`** restricts the eligible targets to the controller's own Pokémon whose TOP card is that type ({P}); **`bonusCounters`** puts N HP of damage counters on the Pokémon just attached to (2 counters = 20), as `COUNTERS_PLACED` source "ability"; **`notIfKO`** excludes any target the bonusCounters would Knock Out (`damage + bonusCounters ≥ effectiveMaxHp` — the exact complement of the `isLethallyDamaged` KO test), so the placed counters are ALWAYS non-lethal and the op still never KOs. The candidate filter lives in a new exported **`attachEnergyTargets`** (interpreter.ts) shared by the park and by `programPlayable` (now also rejecting when NO eligible target remains — a play that does nothing can't be made). Gardevoir's program: `{attachEnergyFrom, source:"discard", energyType:"Psychic", targetType:"Psychic", bonusCounters:20, notIfKO:true}`, `oncePerTurn:false` (repeatable). **Web UNCHANGED** (reuses choosePokemon + ENERGY_ATTACHED + the COUNTERS_PLACED "ability" row + the damage chip for the self-counters). engineVersion 0.12.0 → **0.13.0**. 2-agent Opus review BOTH CLEAN — the non-lethal guarantee proven airtight (a basic-energy attach can't shift damage/effectiveMaxHp between park and apply — those read only the top card + tools, never energy; and settleProgram's DONE sweep is a safety net regardless), no base-op regression, wire-safety, purity; one LOW (a stale logFromEvents comment) fixed. **Scope:** authors Gardevoir ex — 3 of the op's 11 backlog cards; the remaining 8 still need multi-attach / deck-search source / `lookAtTopN` / "turn ends" / self-KO / onEvolve / special-energy-by-name. *Why: Gardevoir ex is the one attachEnergyFrom card whose riders compose cleanly on the single-attach primitive with no flow interaction (notIfKO keeps the self-counters non-lethal), and it is a defining SV deck — high value for three small, reusable riders.* (2026-07-19) |

| D34 | Multi-attach is N ops; the "your turn ends" ability rider is an endsTurn fold (P3-M5, Koraidon ex) | Authoring Koraidon ex "Dino Cry" ("attach up to 2 Basic {F} from discard to your Basic {F} Pokémon in any way; your turn ends"). **MULTI-attach needs NO new op** — "attach up to N in any way you like" is just **N `attachEnergyFrom` ops** in one program: the interpreter already parks on each op's target in turn, and a later op that finds no matching Energy no-ops, so the program attaches min(N, available) with an independently-chosen target per energy (the "may attach fewer when more are available" is auto-maxed — the pure-upside auto-take doctrine). Two small additions author the card: a **`basicOnly`** target-filter rider (the target must be `isBasicPokemon`, combined with `targetType`), and **`AbilityProgram.endsTurn`** — a flag that ENDS the controller's turn once the program completes, folded by **`settleProgram`** (flow.ts): on DONE it seeds `turnTail(controller)` and advances — byte-for-byte the shape of the normal `endTurn` action — instead of returning to turn:action; threaded across re-parks by a new `endsTurn?: true` field on the `effect:choose` Phase (parallel to `resumeTail`, mutually exclusive with it), passed by `useAbility`/`resolveEffect`. The endsTurn DONE branch skips the mid-turn KO sweep (Koraidon does not damage), but the folded Checkup would catch any KO regardless (a *damaging* endsTurn ability is future work). **Web UNCHANGED** (the two sequential parks + the turn-end transition ride the existing effect:choose/choosePokemon path and the viewer-follow, exactly like the on-KO `resumeTail` re-park). engineVersion 0.13.0 → **0.14.0**. 2-agent Opus review BOTH CLEAN — the endsTurn fold proven consistent with the normal turn end (no stale-phase leak), multi-attach has no double-attach/over-reject, basicOnly total; reuse verified through the real web layer. **Scope:** authors Koraidon ex — 4 of the op's 11 backlog cards; the remaining 7 still need a deck-search source / `lookAtTopN` / self-KO / onEvolve / can't-attack / special-energy-by-name. *Why: the interpreter's sequential parking makes multi-attach emergent (data, not code), so the real work was the two composable riders — and `endsTurn` is a reusable fold (Katy and other "your turn ends" cards) that mirrors the resumeTail pattern already proven.* (2026-07-19) |

| D35 | `discardPileRetrieval` — the recovery-Item op (P3-M5, next-biggest backlog unlock) | The discard-pile **mirror of `searchDeck`**, and the top new-op unlock after `attachEnergyFrom` (6 backlog cards). **`discardPileRetrieval`** (`{op, filter:CardFilter, dest:"hand"|"deck", max}`, effects.ts/interpreter.ts) retrieves **up to `max`** cards matching `filter` from the controller's DISCARD pile into their hand or deck. Like searchDeck it is "up to" (declinable) and PARKS on the **same `chooseCards` prompt** (no new prompt/dialog) unless the discard holds no candidate (a no-op, whiff-consistent with searchDeck); no bench-space clamp (hand/deck are uncapped). `dest:"deck"` appends to the deck end, so the printed "Shuffle … into your deck" is authored as **`[discardPileRetrieval(dest:deck), shuffleDeck]`** — the Nest Ball search-then-shuffle pattern, so a whiffed play still shuffles (self-affecting only, never an illegal state — the searchDeck whiff precedent). `dest:"hand"` (Energy Retrieval) has no trailing shuffle. Two new **`CardFilter`** kinds: **`supporter`** (Trainer + trainerType "Supporter") and **`pokemonOrBasicEnergy`** (category "Pokemon" OR a `energyType:"Normal"` basic Energy — Special Energy excluded); one new event **`DISCARD_RETRIEVED {seat, dest, uids}`** (count-only in the log — the discard pile is public, so no hidden-info concern). `retrieveMove` mirrors `searchMove` (dedup Set, only in-discard uids move, wire-checked by the existing `validateChoice`). Representatives (verified vs the local D1 catalog, all Items): **Energy Retrieval** (sv01-171 — 2 Basic Energy → hand), **Pal Pad** (sv01-182 — 2 Supporters → deck), **Super Rod** (sv02-188/276 — 3 Pokémon/Basic Energy → deck). **Web:** the shared `ChooseCardsDialog`'s accessible label changed **"Search your deck" → "Choose cards"** (it now serves both a deck search and a discard retrieval; the accurate text rides `prompt.note`, which reproduces the printed card text), the `chooseCards` prompt `dest` union widened to include `"deck"` (inert at every consumer — the dialog never reads `dest`), and one new `DISCARD_RETRIEVED` log row. engineVersion 0.14.0 → **0.15.0**. **Review (2 Opus agents — engine+rules / web+integration): BOTH CLEAN, no HIGH/MED correctness bugs.** The engine agent proved card conservation (symmetric remove/append, no clamp needed), filter semantics (Special Energy + Trainers excluded), park/resume with no deck-order leak (park is before the move; shuffle runs synchronously in the resume batch, never dropped), self-reference safety, and flow-tail safety; the web agent proved no hidden-info leak and an inert `dest` widening. Fixes applied: one MED (the purity test was freezing the pre-park state and never reaching `retrieveMove` — strengthened to freeze the parked state and drive `resolveEffect`); LOWs (pinned the `retrieveNote` strings, added the degenerate-ends + take-none-with-shuffle cases). **Forward-looking hazard documented (not triggered):** because `playTrainer` discards the played card up front, a FUTURE self-matching card (a Supporter authored on this op with `filter:supporter`) would offer itself as a candidate — flagged in the op's authoring note; the 3 Items here are all safe. **Scope:** authors 3 of the 6 `discardPileRetrieval` backlog cards; the other 3 need `discardFromHandCost` (Superior Energy Retrieval), a conditional draw (Miriam — "if you shuffled any, draw 3"), or an Ability (Pelipper). *Why: discardPileRetrieval was the next-biggest single new-op unlock and — unlike the remaining attachEnergyFrom cards, each still blocked on a second op — it lands 3 COMPLETE real cards cleanly as a near-exact mirror of the vetted searchDeck op, converting a whole recovery-Item family into data rows.* (2026-07-20) |
| D36 | `lookAtTopN` — the deck-top look op (P3-M5, Great Ball / Pokégear 3.0) | The top-of-deck twin of `searchDeck`. **`lookAtTopN`** (`{op, n, filter:CardFilter, max}`, effects.ts/interpreter.ts) looks at the top `n` cards of the controller's deck (index 0 = the top, per `drawToHand`) and puts **up to `max`** matching `filter` into their HAND. Like searchDeck it is "up to" (declinable) and PARKS on the **same `chooseCards` prompt** (no new prompt/dialog) — but the candidates are ONLY the `filter` matches among the **top `n`**, in deck order, never a deeper card (so `resolveEffect`/`validateChoice`'s "picks ⊆ candidates" already forbids reaching past the window — wire-safety is free). An empty top-`n` match is a no-op. "Shuffle the other cards back into your deck" is **NOT part of the op** — it is the trailing **`shuffleDeck`** op in the authored program (the Nest Ball search-then-shuffle pattern), so a whiff OR a decline still shuffles. New mover **`revealFromTop`** (mirrors `searchMove`/`retrieveMove`) + one new event **`DECK_TOP_REVEALED {seat, uids}`** (count-only in the log — honest "looked at the top" rather than DECK_SEARCHED's "searched"; the taken cards are revealed/public, the non-taken looked-at cards never enter any event). **No `CardFilter` addition** (Great Ball reuses `anyPokemon`, Pokégear reuses `supporter` from D35); **no `programPlayable` gate** (a look-at-top is always playable — you look and shuffle even on a whiff, so it never soft-locks). Representatives (verified vs the local D1 catalog, both Items): **Great Ball** (sv02-183 — top 7, reveal a Pokémon → hand), **Pokégear 3.0** (sv01-186 — top 7, reveal a Supporter → hand). **Web:** one `DECK_TOP_REVEALED` log row — the shared origin-agnostic `ChooseCardsDialog` (relabeled "Choose cards" in D35) renders the accurate `prompt.note` ("Look at the top 7 cards…"), and the projection forwards the `chooseCards`/`dest:"hand"` prompt with NO change (nothing switches on `dest`). engineVersion 0.15.0 → **0.16.0**. **Review (2 Opus agents — engine+rules / web+integration): BOTH CLEAN, no HIGH/MED bugs.** Engine agent (8-test suite + a 22-assertion probe): card conservation, candidate = exactly the top-`n` matches (the window boundary index-6-in / 7-out proven, plus shallow deck `n`>length and `n ≤ 0`), wire-safety (a deeper / duplicate / over-max pick rejected `BAD_EFFECT_CHOICE`), shuffle-always-fires (the trailing op lives in the continuation `rest`), no flow-tail/KO interaction, purity, program-vs-text. Web agent: log honesty + count-only + hot-seat seat attribution, the projection "unchanged" claim (no `dest` switch), the dialog serves the note, no demo regression. **No code fixes required.** **INFO (both agents, PRE-EXISTING P4 deferral, NOT this slice):** the parked `chooseCards` prompt carries the top-`n` candidate uids unredacted per-viewer — identical to `searchDeck`/`discardPileRetrieval`, unobservable in the local hot-seat (the dialog renders only for the acting viewer), the P4 server filter's job; a second INFO notes `revealFromTop` does not dedup its input (unreachable — validateChoice rejects duplicates first — and the identical contract as the vetted movers, kept consistent). **Scope:** authors 2 of the op's 5 backlog cards; the other 3 need a from-the-top **attach** variant (Electric Generator / Hydreigon — which also unblocks 2 of the remaining attachEnergyFrom cards) or an opponent-deck peek (Malamar). *Why: lookAtTopN was the next backlog op by unlock count; the reveal-to-hand core is a near-exact restriction of the vetted searchDeck (candidates limited to the top N + the same trailing-shuffle idiom, so wire-safety and the whiff/decline semantics come for free), landing 2 complete real cards cleanly and building the primitive the attach-from-top cluster composes on.* (2026-07-20) |
| D37 | `moveEnergy` — move Energy between your Pokémon; the compound-park op (P3-M5, Energy Switch / Poppy) | Move Energy own → own: **`moveEnergy`** (`{op, filter:CardFilter, max}`, effects.ts/interpreter.ts) moves up to `max` Energy from ONE of the controller's own in-play Pokémon to ANOTHER. It is the interpreter's **first op with two COUPLED decisions** (which Energy AND where), so — unlike every prior op, which parks on one existing prompt — it introduces a **new compound prompt** `moveEnergy` (`movable`: each takeable Energy uid tagged with its source `PokemonRef`; `destinations`: all own in-play refs; `max`; `note`) and a **new choice** `{kind:"moveEnergy", uids, dest}` answering both at once, applied **ATOMICALLY** by `moveEnergyApply` (strip the picks off their source, append to the dest in one `withSide` — a floating detached-Energy state never exists, which is why it is ONE compound park, not two sequential ones). **"Up to"/declinable** (move none is legal) and it always parks when playable (never auto-resolves), the searchDeck/lookAtTopN family. **`validateChoice`** enforces the couplings a compound decision needs: each uid an offered `movable` (distinct, non-string rejected), ALL picks share ONE source Pokémon ("from 1 of your Pokémon"), dest an offered ref, **dest ≠ source** (no moving a Pokémon's Energy onto itself) — so a crafted P4 payload can't apply an illegal move. `moveEnergyApply` **conserves cards** (each uid removed from exactly one non-dest Pokémon, appended once to the dest; the dest is never stripped → no self-move/dup; stale/dest-resident uids skipped) and is pure like `searchMove`. New **`programPlayable` gate** (`moveEnergyPlayable`): ≥1 Energy the filter admits AND **≥2 of your Pokémon** in play — the EXACT complement of stepOp's no-op, so no wasted card and no soft-lock (≥2 Pokémon ⇒ a legal source≠dest pair). One new `CardFilter` kind **`anyEnergy`** (Basic OR Special — Poppy moves either; Energy Switch stays `basicEnergy`); one new event **`ENERGY_MOVED {seat, uids, from, to}`** (`from`/`to` are `PokemonTarget` copies; no bench compaction, so both resolve post-state). Representatives (verified vs the local D1 catalog): **Energy Switch** (sv01-173, Item — a Basic Energy, max 1), **Poppy** (sv03-193/220/227, Supporter — up to 2 any Energy, max 2). **Web (a new dialog):** `MoveEnergyDialog` — a staged pick like Rare Candy (source → its Energy + a distinct destination → Confirm, or **Move none** to decline), which can only ever dispatch a `validateChoice`-accepted choice (dest drawn from destinations-minus-source; picks capped at `max`, all from the one selected source); + one `ENERGY_MOVED` log row. **Hidden info: this prompt leaks NOTHING** — `movable`/`destinations` are entirely the controller's OWN face-up in-play Energy/Pokémon (public table facts), so — uniquely among the parking ops — no P4 redaction is even needed for it (contrast searchDeck/lookAtTopN, whose deck-candidate uids are the standing P4-deferral INFO). engineVersion 0.16.0 → **0.17.0**. **Review (adversarial engine+rules + web+integration passes, plus an independent Opus pass): CLEAN, no HIGH/MED bugs** — card conservation under dest==source / dest-resident / stale-uid / two-source / bench-shift, every illegal wire payload rejected, gate ⇔ no-op equivalence, purity, rules accuracy (Poppy moves Special too; Energy Switch basic-only; same-source enforced; no §6.3 attach-allowance touched), and the dialog can't dispatch a rejected choice. **One action:** closed a DOM coverage gap (the multi-source source-picker stage was untested → added a two-source Poppy DOM test). **INFO (not a bug):** Energy Switch's text is not "you may", so allowing a decline is a minor documented deviation (harmless), consistent with the "up to"/declinable search family. **Scope:** authors 2 of the op's 5 backlog cards (own→own); the other 3 layer MORE ops — **Armarouge** "Fire Off" (a {R} Energy benched→Active: fixed-endpoint + provided-type filter, an Ability), **Mismagius** "Magical Flick" (moves the OPPONENT's Energy on evolve: opponent-side + onEvolve trigger), **Exp. Share** (an on-KO Tool trigger). *Why: `moveEnergy` was the next backlog op by unlock count (5), and its two clean own→own Trainers land now; the compound-park shape (a genuinely two-decision op applied atomically) is the honest model — reusing one existing prompt can't express a two-endpoint move, and a two-park split would leak a floating-Energy intermediate — and it is the primitive the fixed-endpoint / opponent-side / on-KO movers compose on.* (2026-07-20) |
| D38 | `handRefresh` — shuffle your hand into your deck, then draw; the hand-refresh Supporter family (P3-M5, Youngster / Judge / Brassius / Katy) | New op **`handRefresh`** (`{op, who:"you"|"both", draw:HandRefreshDraw}`, effects.ts/interpreter.ts) — the Iono/Judge/Marnie family. It is the FIRST op in a new class: **fully AUTOMATIC** (no decision → it NEVER parks, like drawCards/discardHand/shuffleDeck) AND it can affect the **opponent** (`who:"both"`, Judge — the first op to reach the OPPONENT's hand/deck). Per affected seat, in a fixed order (controller first, then — for `who:"both"` — the opponent), it captures the PRE-shuffle hand size, moves the whole hand into the deck, shuffles the combined pile (`shuffle([...deck, ...hand])` — one rng advance; append-then-shuffle = "shuffle into"), then draws per **`HandRefreshDraw`**: `{kind:"fixed", count}` (Youngster 5, Judge 4, Katy 8) or `{kind:"handPlus", delta}` (Brassius "draw that many cards plus 1" = the pre-shuffle hand size + 1). **Card conservation** is a pure multiset move (`combined = [...deck, ...hand]`, then `drawToHand` slices top `drawCount` → hand, rest → deck; invariant under empty-hand / empty-deck / hand==whole-deck / both-empty, all guarded); the Supporter is discarded UP FRONT by `playTrainer`, so it is never shuffled back. `rngState` is read fresh each iteration and written back into `next`, so `who:"both"` threads the two shuffles with no stale reuse / no dropped advance (reuses rng.ts's single vetted Fisher–Yates). **Always playable** (no `programPlayable` gate — you may always shuffle-and-draw, even from an empty hand); never damages/KOs. **Katy "your turn ends"** lands the `endsTurn` fold on the TRAINER path: a new CardProgram flag **`trainerEndsTurn?: true`** (parallel to `rareCandy`) that `playTrainer` passes to `settleProgram`, folded **byte-for-byte like `AbilityProgram.endsTurn`** (the D34 Koraidon precedent — DONE seeds `turnTail(controller)`); every non-Katy trainer passes `endsTurn:false` and stays byte-identical to the old no-opts call (no regression). This closes the `endTurn` backlog row (Katy joins Koraidon). One new event **`HAND_SHUFFLED_INTO_DECK {seat, count}`** (count-only — a shuffled hand card is hidden; the deck reorder is intrinsic, no separate `SHUFFLE` event, which has no engine-state consumer). Representatives (verified vs the local D1 catalog, all Supporters): **Youngster** (sv01-198, draw 5), **Judge** (sv01-176, both draw 4), **Brassius** (sv03-187, draw hand+1), **Katy** (sv01-177/237, draw 8 + turn ends). **Web: one log row, no dialog** — handRefresh never parks, so there is NO new `EffectPrompt`/dialog (GameHud untouched); `logFromEvents` gains one count-only `HAND_SHUFFLED_INTO_DECK` row, the draw riding the existing count-only `CARDS_DRAWN`. **Hidden info (Judge — the first op to touch the opponent's hand): leaks NOTHING new** — the opponent's `CARDS_DRAWN` uids are the standard `drawToHand` contract every draw uses; the log's CARDS_DRAWN + HAND_SHUFFLED_INTO_DECK arms are both count-only, and the per-viewer projection already redacts the opponent's hand to uid-less `hiddenHand` backs. Katy's turn-end needs no web change (rides the existing phase→seat viewer-follow). engineVersion 0.17.0 → **0.18.0**. **Review (adversarial engine+rules + web+integration passes): BOTH CLEAN, no HIGH/MED, NO code fixes** — card conservation over all four degenerate zone states, correct two-shuffle rng threading, handPlus timing (no off-by-one), the Katy endsTurn fold consistent with Koraidon (full 374-test engine suite passes — no regression), purity against a recursive freeze, all four texts byte-matching the catalog (no missed reprint; Iono/Marnie correctly deferred), and the Judge hidden-info safe on both the log and projection paths. **INFO (both, not bugs):** an empty hand emits `count:0` (honest, non-leaky — the `HAND_DISCARDED "(0 cards)"` precedent); the no-separate-`SHUFFLE`-event choice is cosmetic with no downstream consumer. **Scope:** authors 4 COMPLETE Supporters — the whole `shuffleHandIntoDeck` row (Youngster/Brassius/Katy) plus Judge, and closes the `endTurn` row (Katy); Iono/Marnie layer on later (bottom-of-deck move + a `prizeCount` draw). *Why: the hand-refresh family was the highest-value clean unlock left — high-play-rate staples that land COMPLETE as a fully-automatic op (no compound park, no new dialog, no hidden-info surface beyond public counts), chosen over the from-the-top-attach compound (higher risk) and the conditionalPlay family (each card still needs a second effect op); and it lands the reusable "affect the opponent's hand/deck" reach + the Trainer-path `endsTurn` fold cheaply.* (2026-07-20) |
| D39 | Iono — the `handRefresh` riders that close the hand-refresh family (P3-M5) | **Iono** (sv02-185 + reprints sv02-254/269 — "Each player shuffles their hand and puts it on the bottom of their deck. If either player put any cards on the bottom of their deck in this way, each player draws a card for each of their remaining Prize cards.") needs **no new op** — three composable riders on the D38 `handRefresh`: **`toBottom?: true`** (the PLACEMENT: shuffle the **hand alone** and append it UNDER the deck — `[...deck, ...shuffle(hand)]` — instead of shuffling it into the deck, so the deck's existing order and therefore the next draws SURVIVE; that difference is the whole strategic point of Iono over Judge and is pinned by a test asserting the drawn cards are exactly the pre-play deck top, in order); **`draw: {kind:"prizeCount"}`** (each affected player draws their OWN remaining Prize count — the player behind draws more; a Prize count is public, so the draw size leaks nothing); **`onlyIfAnyMoved?: true`** (the draw clause fires only if ≥1 affected player actually moved ≥1 card — an **all-or-nothing gate ACROSS the seats**, "if EITHER player…", not a per-seat one). That cross-seat gate is why `handRefresh` now runs **two passes** over the affected seats (all placements, then all draws) instead of D38's interleaved move-then-draw: **behaviour-preserving** for Youngster/Judge/Brassius/Katy (zones are per-seat disjoint and `drawToHand` consumes NO rng, so final states + rng threading are byte-identical — verified by reconstructing the old loop and diffing at 3 seeds each), the only change being Judge's event INTERLEAVING (SHUFFLED p1, SHUFFLED p2, DRAWN p1, DRAWN p2 — which also reads truer to "each player shuffles … and draws"). One new event **`HAND_TO_BOTTOM_OF_DECK {seat, count}`** — count-only like its sibling, but a **distinct** event because the public fact differs (the deck's order is untouched, which both players must be able to read off the log). `shuffle` consumes no randomness for a length ≤ 1 input, so an Iono into two empty hands advances the rng ZERO times and reorders nothing. **Web: one log row, no dialog** (the op still never parks; no projection change) — "shuffled their hand (N cards) and put it on the bottom of their deck": the row must say **both** verbs, since the hand IS randomized on its way under an unshuffled deck. engineVersion 0.18.0 → **0.19.0**. **Deliberate deviation (the reviewers asked for it in writing):** Iono played when EVERY affected hand is empty is a total no-op (nothing moves, nothing draws, no rng) yet stays **legal** — no `programPlayable` gate, unlike every other could-only-whiff op (gust/switch/attach/moveEnergy). Reasons: the digital clients allow it; it is self-inflicted, cannot soft-lock, and only burns the player's own Supporter allowance; and gating it would have to reach into "playTrainer discards the played card up front" (the controller's hand still holds Iono when the gate runs) — the exact leaky coupling flagged in D35. **Review (adversarial engine+rules + web+integration): no HIGH; 2 MED, both fixed.** Engine MED was pure test-adequacy found by MUTATION testing: deleting the hand shuffle left all 379 engine tests green (every bottom assertion was order-insensitive and nothing asserted `rngState`) → added `.not.toEqual(handOrder)` + `rngState` assertions on both the shuffled and the zero-randomness paths, and re-verified the mutant now dies. Web MED (found independently by both agents): the log row said only "put their hand … on the bottom", implying — against its sibling row — that Iono does NOT shuffle, so a player who had seen that hand would think the deck's tail was known → copy fixed to say both. LOWs applied: an Iono degenerate-deck test (deck shallower than the Prize count short-draws, does not deck out), the describe-title rename, and a real end-to-end log test pinning the 5-row grouped batch (the two-pass restructure was unpinned on the web side). Verified clean: card conservation over every degenerate zone combination on both sides, exact rng advance counts, "Iono itself is never bottomed", gate semantics (`every`/per-seat/opponent-prizes mutants all die), the projection leaking nothing (zero opponent uids serialized), no exhaustive-switch fall-through, HUD playability, and no demo regression. **Scope: the hand-refresh family is CLOSED** — the sv01–03 catalog has no Marnie. **Carried forward (pre-existing, not this slice):** every authored Supporter (Iono, Judge, Katy, Youngster, Professor's Research) has `legal_standard=0` in the local ingest, so they don't surface in the builder's default standard-legal search; and `GameLog` announces only the last entry to screen readers, so a 5-row batch is mostly silent. *Why: Iono is the highest-play-rate Supporter in the format and the last row of a family we had already built and vetted — it lands as three small riders on a reviewed op rather than a new mechanism, and the one structural change it forces (moves before draws) is provably behaviour-preserving for the four cards already on the op.* (2026-07-21) |

| D40 | Board conditions — one `BoardCondition` vocabulary with two consumers (P3-M5, Fighting Au Lait / Falkner / Grusha) | The `conditionalPlay` backlog family, built as ONE primitive rather than a per-card gate. **`BoardCondition`** (effects.ts) is a tiny predicate union evaluated for one seat — **`morePrizesThanOpponent`** (strict `>`, i.e. you are BEHIND on Prizes taken; also what Defiance Band / Luxray / Reversal Energy read), **`yourStadiumInPlay`**, **`noEnergyOnYourPokemon`** (Active AND Bench, Basic or Special) — with a single evaluator **`conditionHolds`** (interpreter.ts) and a printed-text describer `conditionNote`. **INVARIANT: every member reads only PUBLIC state** (Prize COUNTS, the Stadium zone's owner, face-up Energy), which is precisely why a gate **emits no event** — unlike `coinFlipGate`, whose flip IS new information, a board condition is a fact both players can already read off the table, and the branch's own ops emit whatever actually happened. **Consumer A — the printed PLAY GATE: `CardProgram.trainerPlayableIf`**, checked by `playTrainer` BEFORE the card leaves hand (so a failed gate costs the player nothing) and rejecting with a new `ErrorCode` **`PLAY_CONDITION_NOT_MET`**. It is categorically distinct from `programPlayable`'s would-only-whiff heuristics: this is a rule PRINTED on the card, not the engine's judgement about ops that would do nothing. **Consumer B — the mid-program branch: the `conditionGate` op** `{cond, then, otherwise?}`, spliced into runProgram's work queue exactly like `coinFlipGate` (so an op inside a branch can still park, and a branch may hold further gates), evaluated against the board AS OF the gate. One-armed is the common shape (Falkner); `otherwise` exists for the printed "…**instead**", a genuine if/ELSE (Grusha). Plus one tiny fully-automatic op **`drawUntilHandSize`** — it DRAWS to a floor and never TRIMS a bigger hand, and at a count ≤ 0 it no-ops with **no event** (no misleading "drew 0 cards" row). Representatives (verified vs the local D1 catalog): **Fighting Au Lait** (sv02-181, Item — the gate + `healChosen 60`), **Falkner** (sv02-180/251, Supporter — `[drawCards 2, conditionGate(yourStadiumInPlay) → [drawCards 2]]`, two ops so the log shows the two printed draw instructions separately), **Grusha** (sv02-184/253/268, Supporter — the `otherwise` arm). **Web: zero new events, prompts, dialogs or projection fields.** The only change is the HUD greying out a gated Trainer with the engine's own `conditionHolds`, carrying `conditionNote` as the row's `title` + an `sr-only` line — because disabling the button REMOVES the reject pill that used to explain the printed rule, and that rule is text the HUD never shows. engineVersion 0.19.0 → **0.20.0**. **`yourStadiumInPlay` is OWNERSHIP, not presence** — the slice's highest-risk rules call, verified by the reviewer against the Japanese printing of ハヤト (「場に**自分の**スタジアムが出ているなら」) and Bulbapedia's Stadium article: an opponent's Stadium must NOT grant Falkner's +2. **Recorded deviation (deliberate):** the HUD now greys out a printed `trainerPlayableIf` gate while `programPlayable`'s whiff gates (Boss's Orders into an empty Bench) stay live and explain themselves via the reject pill. That mixed standard is intentional — a rule printed on the card is knowable before clicking, an engine whiff-judgement is not — and the tooltip is what makes it coherent rather than a silent grey row. **Naming:** the field is `trainerPlayableIf`, not `playableIf`, because it is honoured on EXACTLY one path (playTrainer's Item/Supporter branch); `playStadium`, `rareCandy`, `attachTool` and `useAbility` all return before it and would ignore it SILENTLY — the `trainerEndsTurn` precedent, and the reason Luxray's Ability-level gate must not be authored here. **Review (adversarial engine+rules + web+integration): NO HIGH from either.** The engine agent ran **24 mutants, 19 killed, 5 survived (1 equivalent)**; all four real survivors were test-adequacy, now fixed and re-verified dead via two new fixtures — **fix-gateorder** (an attach BEFORE the gate that flips the condition, a two-op arm, and an op AFTER the gate: one ordered `CARDS_DRAWN` assertion kills the evaluate-against-entry-state, push-instead-of-unshift and dropped-tail mutants at once) and **fix-gatedsup** (the only gated SUPPORTER, pinning that a rejected gate does not burn the §7.2 allowance) — plus a never-trims test and the first assertions on `conditionNote`'s player-facing wording. The web agent's four MEDs: the `engineVersion` bump was missing; the log rows were unpinned (three tests added, incl. Falkner's PAIR of draw rows and Grusha's zero-row case); the export's "a P4 client may evaluate one locally" claim was **self-defeating** (see below); and the HUD's opponent-`prizes` read. **P4 item recorded, not fixed:** `conditionHolds` takes a `GameState`, so only a raw-state host can run it — the projection does NOT carry `stadium.owner` and redacts the prize arrays. A redacted prize array still has a `.length`, so `morePrizesThanOpponent` would fail **SILENTLY** rather than loudly if shipped online unchanged; the doc comments now say so instead of claiming client-safety. **Carried forward (pre-existing, not this slice):** Fighting Au Lait is playable with nothing damaged anywhere (the Potion shape — `programPlayable` treats heal as "playable enough"), which this slice makes conspicuous by enforcing the card's printed legality line; and `GameLog` announces only the batch's LAST row to screen readers, which a Falkner drawing 4 now makes sharper (two identical "drew 2 cards" rows — and an unchanged live region announces nothing at all). **Scope:** 3 of the `conditionalPlay` row's 5 cards' worth of primitive, landing 2 cards from that row's neighbours instead; the remaining gated cards each need a SECOND missing piece — Dendra (a choose-from-hand-to-bottom park + `drawUntilHandSize`), Letter of Encouragement (a "were any of your Pokémon KO'd during your opponent's last turn" state fact), Luxray (an in-hand Ability surface), Lunatone (Stadium-effect prevention). *Why: the board-condition gate was the biggest remaining cluster, and building it as one vocabulary with two consumers — rather than a bespoke flag per card — is what makes it a primitive: the same three predicates already have five more readers waiting in sv01–03 (Defiance Band's conditional passive is the natural third consumer), and the play-gate/branch split mirrors the distinction the rules themselves draw between "you can't use this card" and "if X, do more".* (2026-07-21) |
