| D41 | `discardEnergy` — the energy-removal "hammer" family, and the coin-gate playability bug it exposed (P3-M5, Crushing Hammer / Giacomo / Mawile) | New op **`discardEnergy`** (`{op, from, filter}`, effects.ts/interpreter.ts) — the §15 family that takes Energy off the **OPPONENT's** in-play Pokémon. `filter` narrows which ATTACHED Energy may be taken (`anyEnergy` / the new **`specialEnergy`** CardFilter, the exact complement of `basicEnergy` inside `anyEnergy`), and **`from` names both the eligible Pokémon and how many are hit**, spelled the way the cards are: **`opponentActive`** (Mawile — their Active alone), **`opponentChosen`** (Crushing Hammer — one Energy off ONE of their Pokémon) and **`opponentEach`** (Giacomo — one off EVERY one of their Pokémon that has a match; a Pokémon with none is SKIPPED, the "do as much as you can" rule — Compendium 2064/Megaton Blower is the near-exact analogue). **Exactly one Energy per affected Pokémon: no `count` field**, because every printed card in scope takes one and every "discard 2 / discard all" variant is an ATTACK (blocked — see below). **`opponentChosen` is a ONE-step decision, not two**: "from 1 of your opponent's Pokémon" reads like pick-a-Pokémon-then-its-Energy, but since exactly one Energy comes off, picking the Energy IS picking the Pokémon — so the prompt is a single flat candidate list with none of the source/destination coupling that made `moveEnergy` (D37) a compound park. **The engine's FIRST mandatory park:** every previous parking op was "up to" (searchDeck / discardPileRetrieval / lookAtTopN / moveEnergy) or exactly-one (choosePokemon); here the only question is WHICH Energy, never whether, so `validateChoice` enforces an EXACT count and an empty pick is an ERROR, not a decline. New prompt `{kind:"discardEnergy", discardable:{uid,from}[], scope:"one"|"each", note}` + choice `{kind:"discardEnergy", uids}`; the two scopes fall out of **two rules plus a count** — every uid offered and distinct, no Pokémon gives up two, and the total is 1 or the number of distinct sources (from which "one each, none skipped" follows by pigeonhole). It parks only on a real choice (`forcedDiscards` auto-resolves the M1 no-choice cases); an empty candidate set is a no-op; the discarded card goes to its **OWNER's** pile (the §8.1 KO-cleanup rule applied to one Energy); it never damages, so it never KOs. One new event **`ENERGY_DISCARDED {seat, uids, from}`**, one **per affected Pokémon** (a Giacomo sweep emits several), `seat` naming the VICTIM as DAMAGE_DEALT's does. Representatives (verified verbatim vs the local D1 catalog): **Crushing Hammer** (sv01-168), **Giacomo** (sv02-182/252/267), **Mawile** (sv03-143, an onPlayToBench TRIGGERED Ability) — all three `from` arms land with a real printed card, no speculative vocabulary; `fix-hammer` is the one fixture (the `opponentChosen` arm without the coin gate). **Web: one dialog, one log row, NO projection change** — every candidate is Energy sitting face up on the table. engineVersion 0.20.0 → **0.21.0**. **THE REVIEW REVERSED A DOCUMENTED DECISION OF MINE — recorded loudly because it is the interesting part.** The slice originally shipped Crushing Hammer as playable into an Energy-LESS board, reasoning that `programPlayable` scans top-level ops only and "the flip happens either way, which is how the printed card plays". That is **wrong**: §7 "you cannot play a card if it has no valid target" (Compendium [ruling/906](https://compendium.pokegym.net/ruling/906/), **reconfirmed by the TPCi Rules Team 2025-06-26**) covers it, because **a coin flip is PROCEDURE, not effect** (the rulebook's own attack sequence separates "flip a coin" from applying effects). The apparent counter-precedent — Unown-E's "Hidden Power" forcing every flip to tails, yet flip Trainers staying playable (PUI Rules Team 2008-01-24) — is the OTHER branch of [ruling/284](https://compendium.pokegym.net/ruling/284/): *"playing a card for no effect happens when **the game state itself** prevents any effect from taking place"*. Blocked by an effect in play → playable; blocked by the board → not. So **`programPlayable` now DESCENDS into `coinFlipGate.then`** (a coin gate has no `otherwise`, so a whiff-only branch is a whiff-only card). Caveat stated plainly: no card-specific ruling exists for any coin-flip Trainer — this is derivation from the general rule, which is why the check is one named predicate rather than scattered per-card logic. **This also fixes a PRE-EXISTING bug the slice merely inherited:** Pokémon Catcher (sv01-187, `coinFlipGate → [gust]`, M5 pass #1) was likewise playable into an empty opponent Bench — verified by the reviewer's probe, pinned by a new test now. **Poké Ball is deliberately untouched**: `searchDeck` stays "playable enough" because a deck is NOT public knowledge, so "this search will find nothing" is never a fact the game state establishes — exactly the ruling's own limit. **NOT extended to `conditionGate`** (deliberate asymmetry, recorded): no printed sv01–03 card puts a whiff-able op inside one, and deciding it properly means EVALUATING the condition and scanning only the arm that will run — a bigger claim than the coin gate's pure implication. **Recorded, NOT fixed (MED):** Mawile is the first "you **may**" trigger that cannot be declined at all, because `discardEnergy` is the first MANDATORY parking op — every earlier auto-fired optional trigger left an escape hatch through its own declinable op (the interpreter's stated doctrine: "the forced branch AUTO-TAKES rather than offering the 'you may' decline — only the parked branch does"). Left as-is and pinned by a test: the auto-fire doctrine's justification is that every optional trigger in scope is PURE UPSIDE, and this one is unambiguously so (no sv01–03 Special Energy's loss helps its owner), so the "trap" costs a click and nothing else; the real fix is the trigger-level yes/no confirm the doctrine already contemplates. **Review (adversarial engine+rules + web+integration): no HIGH in the slice's own logic from either.** The engine agent ran **45 mutants, 41 killed, 4 equivalent**; the 5 real survivors were all test-adequacy and are now dead — `refKeyOf` dropping the bench index (two benched holders would FUSE into one group and park instead of auto-resolving), `!==` → `<` on the required count (in scope "one" that would accept two Energy off two DIFFERENT Pokémon — the per-Pokémon rule cannot catch it, only the exact count), the `opponentActive` arm never parking in any test, and unpinned bench-iteration / discard-pile-append order. It also proved the `movableEnergies` → `attachedEnergies` generalization byte-identical for moveEnergy (three forcing probes all killed). The web agent found no soft-lock, no dispatchable-but-rejected choice and no leak, and fixed three MEDs: the log row rendered the Energy card and its host identically (now tinted apart, the ENERGY_ATTACHED precedent), the grouped rows had no programmatic host association (now `fieldset`/`legend`, the only grouped dialog in the HUD), and six missing tests. Two UI calls taken on its report: **forced groups come PRE-ANSWERED** in `scope: "each"` (a single-candidate group is not a question — without seeding the player re-clicks every forced group before Confirm lights up; the MoveEnergyDialog sole-source precedent), and **the group legend carries the SPOT** ("· Bench 1"), because two copies of one Basic would otherwise give identical legends over identical "Basic Energy" rows and for Crushing Hammer WHICH host loses the Energy is the whole decision. **P4 item surfaced (pre-existing, not this slice):** `projection.ts` returns `pendingDecision` for BOTH viewers ungated on `phase.seat` — harmless for this prompt (face-up Energy), but the sibling `chooseCards` prompt built by `searchDeck` carries the controller's DECK uids, which a remote non-decider must not receive. **Scope: 3 of the row's 4 cards** (Klawf ex needs `onDamagedByAttack`, a different mechanism). *Why: `discardOpponentEnergy` was the resume point's #1 and the last cheap op-slice on the ranked backlog; building it as ONE op whose `from` member is the extension seam — rather than three per-card shapes — is what lets the self-discard attacks join later as a member rather than a rewrite. The far more valuable outcome was diagnostic: scoping the family found ~13 more sv01–03 cards on the same idea blocked not by the op but by `attack.ts` discarding a parked result, which is now the top backlog row.* (2026-07-21) |

| D42 | Attack effect programs can PARK — the `attackEpilogue` stage, and the §8 self-discard attack cost (P3-M5, engine 0.22.0) | **The structural unlock D41's scoping diagnosed, built as one STAGE rather than one op.** `attack.ts` ran an attack's effect program as `runProgram(...).state`, which silently DROPPED a parked prompt and every op behind it — so no attack effect could hold a decision, blocking ~13 sv01–03 cards whose op vocabulary already existed. The fix inverts the order: **queue the tail first, then run the program.** New `PendingStage` **`{kind:"attackEpilogue", seat, koedSeat}`**; **`finishAttack` MOVED from attack.ts into flow.ts** and exported (the `advance` case must reach it, and attack.ts already imports flow, so the handler cannot sit on the far side of that edge); `attack()` seeds the stage and folds through **`settleProgram(..., {resumeTail:true})`**. A DONE program drains straight into the stage — identical to the old inline call, one hop later; a PARKED one leaves it in `pending` until `resolveEffect` finishes the program (across any number of re-parks) and drains it then. **`resumeTail` is REDEFINED, deliberately:** it meant "this program is mid-KO-sweep (an on-KO Ability)" and now means "this program is mid-TAIL — the stages that finish the job are already queued behind it, so DONE means keep draining, not fold back to a turn". Both callers want exactly that, and neither wants the mid-turn KO sweep. The one place they differ is the projection: an on-KO park has `phase.seat` ≠ the turn owner, an attack park has `phase.seat` = the turn owner — `koParkActiveSeat` resolves both off the pending queue, so `attackEpilogue` joined `endTurn`/`checkup`/`resumeTurn` as an in-turn marker. **The family that proves it is DERIVED, not authored:** `discardEnergy` gained a **`yourActive`** arm (the controller's own Active — for an attack, the printed "this Pokémon", §8) and a **`count: "all"`** rider, and two anchored whole-sentence deriver rules cover **12 catalog rows with ZERO registry rows** — "Discard an Energy from this Pokémon." (Houndoom sv01-034/sv03-133, Charcadet, Staraptor, Paldean Tauros, Dedenne ex ×2, Bombirdier) and "Discard all Energy from this Pokémon." (Pawmot ×2, Raichu ×2). Deliberately NOT derived: the numeric counts ("Discard 2/3 Energy" — needs an exact-N park) and the typed ones (Kilowattrel's "a {L} Energy" — needs an energy-of-type filter, and `{L}` means an Energy that PROVIDES {L}, so Special Energy counts). **Third idea, and the one the review sharpened most: INTERCHANGEABLE CANDIDATES COLLAPSE.** `interchangeableCandidates` keeps one representative per (host, energy identity) before the forced/park decision, so a Houndoom attacking with three {R} attached asks nothing — the M1 "a choice with no choice in it is not a choice" doctrine applied to card identity, via the `attachEnergyFrom` fungibility precedent. WHICH POKÉMON loses the Energy is never fungible, so the key includes the host. **The identity is NOT the catalog id** — the review caught that the real catalog prints Basic Fire Energy under three ids (sve-002/010/018), so an id key would have parked a real deck on a non-choice and broken the slice's own headline claim; a Basic keys on what it PROVIDES, everything else on its id (the two spaces prefixed apart, so a Basic {R} and a Special providing {R} never merge). This CHANGED shipped behaviour for D41's hammer arms too (two copies of one print on one Pokémon now auto-resolve), which is the improvement — three D41 tests moved to two DISTINCT prints to keep testing a park. **`ENERGY_DISCARDED` gained `actor`**: victim and actor are the SAME seat for a self-discard, so the log's `otherSeat(seat)` inference would have credited Houndoom's own attack cost to its opponent — an inference the log should never have been making. **Review (adversarial engine+rules + web+integration): NO HIGH from either.** 56 mutants between them, 9 survivors, ALL now dead. The engine agent proved the epilogue sound (no stuck game, no lost KO, no double-run, purity and card conservation across re-parks), verified both fixture cards byte-for-byte against the local D1 ingest, and confirmed the two regexes match exactly those 12 rows across all 890 catalog rows with zero false positives and zero misses. Fixes applied from the reports: the id-vs-provision key above; a **`PHASE_DESYNC` guard** on `resolveEffect` for a `resumeTail` park with an EMPTY queue (that fold is the only one that writes no phase, so it would loop forever — the ko:* handlers' crafted-snapshot guard, applied to the case that cannot self-recover); `finishAttack` now **COMPOSES** with the queue instead of overwriting it, which makes the epilogue case's `popStage` load-bearing rather than redundant; the log row now names the host's **OWNER** (a mirror match printed an identical row for both boards, and a possessive pronoun cannot fix it — the `who` chip is viewer-relative while the segments are fixed text, so the only wording true from every seat is the player's name); the dialog legend carries the same `opponent` marker its ChoosePokemonDialog sibling uses; `count:"all"` rows fold repeats into "×N"; and the effect:choose dialog is keyed on the PROMPT so two parks in one turn cannot inherit a stale pick. **Recorded, NOT fixed (LOW, loudly commented):** the epilogue sweeps ONE seat, so a KO an attack inflicts on the ATTACKER's own board is missed — unreachable today, but sv01-038 Skeledirge's "This Pokémon also does 30 damage to itself." is one deriver sentence away, and widening it to `SEATS` makes the §14 double-KO tie guard reachable from the attack path for the first time, so it wants its own slice. *Why: this was the top backlog row and the biggest remaining structural unlock — every future attack effect may now hold a decision. Building it as a queued STAGE rather than a bespoke mid-attack phase is what made it fall out of machinery that already existed (`resumeTail` needed a broader definition, not a sibling), and deriving the first family from printed text rather than authoring it is what turned one mechanism into 12 cards with no data rows at all.* (2026-07-21) |

| D43 | The `discardEnergy` ATTACK arms — the opponent-side twins + the exact-N `count` (P3-M5, engine 0.23.0) | **No new op: two extensions of D41/D42's `discardEnergy`, both DERIVED from printed text, landing 11 catalog rows with ZERO registry rows.** (1) The hammer family's **attack TWINS** — `"Discard an Energy from your opponent's Active Pokémon."` bare and behind a flip — run the EXISTING `opponentActive` arm with an `anyEnergy` filter (Pincurchin sv02-072, Gumshoos sv03-177, Dragonite V swsh10.5-049/-076; Dedenne sv01-094, Maschiff sv01-136, Tympole sv03-050, Yveltal sv06.5-035). They are the first ops to park a decision about the **OPPONENT's** board mid-attack, and they needed NO new machinery — D42's `attackEpilogue` carries the tail and `coinFlipGate` already splices its branch into the work queue. (2) The **exact-N self-discard**: `count` widens from `"all"` to any number (Corviknight sv02-148 / Slither Wing sv06.5-026 "discard 2", Koraidon sv01-124 "discard 3"). Three consequences: the prompt's `scope` becomes **`{kind:"total",count} \| {kind:"each"}`** (the old `"one"` was always `{total,1}`); the D42 interchangeable collapse is **CAPPED at `count` per class rather than flattened to 1** — flattening would erase a genuinely distinguishable answer ("discard both Fighting" on two {F} + a filler), while the cap still removes every copy that could ONLY duplicate (four identical {F} with `count:3` remains forced, no prompt); and **`forcedDiscards` folds the SHORT board into the same rule** — "auto-resolve when the collapsed offer is ≤ count" IS simultaneously the M1 no-choice rule and §8.6 "do as much as you can", which this slice added to `reference/ptcg-rules.md` (corroborated on-card by Sandaconda's "If you discarded any Energy in this way"). `validateChoice`'s one-per-Pokémon rule becomes **`"each"`-only** (an exact-N takes all N off ONE Pokémon — precisely what that rule forbade); `opponentEach` + a numeric count is made **unrepresentable** (`count?: never` on a split union arm) rather than silently ignored. **Web:** only `DiscardEnergyDialog` changes — `"total"` is a flat multi-select of `count`, and a click at the cap is **REFUSED** (the ChooseCards/ChoosePokemonMulti rule) rather than evicting the oldest, which would silently un-press a row the user is not looking at now that identical rows can legitimately be offered; the count line became an `<output>` live region and refused rows carry `aria-disabled`. **Anchoring is load-bearing beyond any earlier deriver slice:** Krookodile (sv01-117) prints "Flip a coin **until** you get tails. For each heads, discard an Energy from your opponent's Active Pokémon." — it ENDS in the twin's exact words, so an unanchored regex would silently discard once for a repeat-count attack. engineVersion 0.22.0 → **0.23.0**; 1250 → **1268** tests. **2-agent adversarial review (engine+rules with 48 mutants / web+integration): NO HIGH in the slice's own logic from either.** The engine agent verified all five regexes against all 978 catalog rows (11 new matches, zero false positives, zero misses) and brute-forced the capped-collapse math over 7770 class-size configurations (zero false shortfalls, fake parks, or unreachable answers). The web agent found **one HIGH beside the slice**: `ENERGY_DISCARDED` named its host by SPOT, and an attack twin strips a defender its own damage Knocked Out in the SAME batch — so the log re-read an empty slot and printed "from Tide's the Active spot". The event now carries **`host`** (the top-card uid, the KNOCKED_OUT precedent). Of 10 surviving mutants, 1 was a real hole (the **`each`-scope cap of 1** was entirely unpinned — a `cap = 2` mutant changed shipped Giacomo behaviour with all 1266 tests green), 4 were the unpinned regex anchors, 1 was pre-existing outside the slice (`moveEnergy`'s duplicate-uid check), and 4 were equivalent; all are now dead. Also corrected: three of four new fixtures carried wrong HP/retreat under a header claiming catalog verification. *Why: D42 built a mechanism (attack effects can park) whose value depends on attacks actually printing decisions — these are the ones they print, and both extensions are data, not code. Capping the collapse rather than flattening it is the whole design insight: the M1 "a choice with no choice in it is not a choice" doctrine generalizes to a count as "keep exactly as many copies as the pick could want", which turns the no-choice rule and the do-as-much-as-you-can rule into one comparison.* (2026-07-21) |

| D44 | The `providesEnergy` CardFilter — "an Energy that PROVIDES {X}", read by two ops (P3-M5, engine 0.24.0) | **The energy-removal family's last missing piece, and the first filter a CARD alone cannot answer.** Two claims carry it. (1) **PROVISION, NOT PRINT:** "{L} Energy" is not "a Basic Lightning Energy card" — a Special providing {L} IS one, a Special providing only {C} is not whatever its art, and a WILDCARD provider (Luminous, "provides every type of Energy but only 1 at a time") is an Energy of EVERY type. (2) **HOST, NOT CARD:** provision is a while-ATTACHED property, and Luminous's own clause makes it depend on the holder's OTHER attachments ("provides {C} instead if the Pokémon this card is attached to has any other Special Energy attached"), so the identical physical card is a {L} Energy on one board and not on another. `attachedEnergies` therefore answers the filter per Energy **on its host** (`matchesAttached`), and the card-only pile scanner `matchesFilter` answers **false** — a card in a deck/hand/discard is attached to nothing, so authoring this filter into a pile-scanning op is a LOUD NO-OP rather than a wrong card (guessing "a Basic Energy of that type" there would take the wrong print; Decidueye's "Basic {G} Energy **card** from your hand" is the row that really wants `basicEnergy`, and it says CARD for exactly this reason). Both claims are written into `reference/ptcg-rules.md` as **§6.5**. **ONE provision rule, two readers:** `continuous.ts` was SPLIT rather than copied — `providedEnergy` (the §8.2 cost matcher's source) and the new `unitsProvidedBy`/`providesEnergyType` share one `unitsOf`, which buys the invariant that **an Energy that can PAY a {L} cost is exactly one a "{L} Energy" effect may take** (Kilowattrel pays Thunder Blast's {L} with a Luminous and then discards that same Luminous; a drift would make the board either unattackable or a silent no-op). **TWO CONSUMERS, one vocabulary — the D40 shape, which is the whole justification for a filter rather than a field on `discardEnergy`.** (a) The **typed self-discard**, DERIVED in both printed notations — the modern `{R}` and the swsh-era spelled-out `Fire` — from one `ENERGY_TYPE_BY_CODE` map, landing **5 catalog rows with ZERO registry rows** (Kilowattrel sv01-079, Arcanine ex sv01-032/-224, Charmeleon swsh10.5-009, Charizard swsh10.5-010). The map's values are pinned to be exactly `BASIC_ENERGY_TYPES`, and `energyType` is typed **`BasicEnergyType`** so the one value the design forbids is UNREPRESENTABLE rather than merely commented against (the D43 `count?: never` precedent): Colorless is the conservative provision FALLBACK for every unauthored Special Energy, so a `{C}` filter would quietly match cards nobody meant. The typed **`"all"`** is what proves the filter works in the APPLY and not just the prompt — it never asks a question, so it is the only thing between Charizard's "all Fire Energy" and a board stripped bare. (b) **`moveEnergy`'s `route: "benchToActive"`** — the fixed-endpoint rider that slice deferred — authoring **Armarouge ×2** (sv01-041/-203) "Fire Off", whose three flags each read off the printed words (`oncePerTurn:false` = "as often as you like"; `activeOnly:false` = the card never says "this Pokémon", so a BENCHED Armarouge may move its own {R}; the filter for "{R}"). One decision survives (WHICH Energy), so it parks on the SAME compound prompt with a single-entry `destinations` — dialog and wire validator both read the prompt, so neither knows a route exists. **Web:** one behavioural change — `MoveEnergyDialog` treats a SOLE destination as already chosen (the rule its source list already followed), because the route makes that the normal case rather than an edge one. engineVersion 0.23.0 → **0.24.0**; 1268 → **1301** tests. **2-agent adversarial review (engine+rules with 49 mutants / web+integration): NO HIGH from either.** The engine agent ran the deriver over all **1108 text surfaces** of all 978 catalog rows (exactly the 5 intended rows, zero false positives, zero misses), threw **36 hostile `resolveEffect` inputs** at both new prompts (zero throws, zero mutation), confirmed purity under `deepFreeze`, verified all five new fixtures byte-for-byte against the catalog, and killed 36 of 49 mutants. Fixes applied from the two reports: **`ENERGY_MOVED.to` no longer spreads the client's object** (a hostile `dest` smuggled arbitrary keys into an event handed to the animator, the log and P4's broadcast — the state was always safe, but a copy of attacker-controlled data is not a copy of the engine's own value); `energyType` tightened to `BasicEnergyType`; the route's "an Active exists" precondition **collapsed from three copies to one** shared `moveEndpoints` (all three were untested, and one mutation left an unrecoverable `effect:choose`); a **`fix-blend` fixture** for a Special providing TWO distinct types, since both authored Special Energies are single-valued and the predicate's "is the type AMONG the units" was indistinguishable from "is it the FIRST unit"; `moveNote`'s article ("Move a Energy"); the Armarouge JSDoc, which a fixture insertion had orphaned; the web `promptKey` (the move key ignored the `destinations` its `dest` now derives from) and a `<output>` live region; plus DOM tests for the **multi-destination pick** and **`reset()` on source change**, both of which the web agent proved unpinned by mutations that passed all 1297 tests. **Recorded, NOT fixed:** the typed candidate set is SNAPSHOTTED, so discarding one Special cannot un-demote a survivor (unreachable — a wildcard is only a candidate when it is the sole Special, so removing it un-demotes nothing; it activates with the first typed Special Energy); Charizard's printed Ability **"Burn Brightly"** ("Each basic Fire Energy attached to your Pokémon provides {R}{R}") is a PROVISION modifier and `unitsOf` has no hook for a Pokémon Ability that changes provision, so the "one provision rule" is print+registry only; and the Abilities row is the only HUD button family with no playability gate, so an as-often-as-you-like Ability's button never greys (pre-existing for Baxcalibur/Gardevoir ex, made routine here — the fix wants the engine-exported preview predicates P4 needs anyway). *Why: the family was op-bound on exactly one predicate, and the predicate turned out to be more interesting than a parameter — reading provision instead of print forced the filter to be answered on the HOST rather than the card, which is what made it worth sharing with a second op instead of bolting onto the first.* (2026-07-21) |

| D45 | `attachFromTop` — the from-the-top ATTACH, and the play gate that had to be REMOVED (P3-M5, engine 0.25.0) | **One new op: look at the top N of your own deck and attach the Energy you find there straight onto your Pokémon, "in any way you like".** The variant the `lookAtTopN` slice explicitly deferred. Two consumers that differ in EVERY rider the op has, which is what made each rider worth having: **Electric Generator** (sv01-170, Item — top 5, up to 2 Basic {L} Energy CARDS, onto your BENCHED {L} Pokémon, leftovers shuffled back) and **Hydreigon "Tri Howl"** (sv02-140, Ability, once per turn — top 3, ANY number of Energy cards, onto ANY of your Pokémon, leftovers DISCARDED). **"In any way you like" is why it is its own op:** `attachEnergyFrom` attaches one fungible card and asks only WHERE; `moveEnergy` takes several Energy onto ONE destination; here each attached card names its OWN destination, so the answer is a **MAP** (card → Pokémon) — the new `attachCards` prompt and `{kind:"attachCards"; assignments}` choice, the first whose answer pairs each pick with a target of its own. Any split is legal, **including all of them on one Pokémon** (pinned by its own test — a per-target-once rule would look plausible and pass every single-card test). **The leftovers clause is the design point.** "Shuffle the other cards back into your deck" is the trailing `shuffleDeck` op (the Nest Ball pattern): those cards never left the deck, and a shuffle destroys order anyway, so a step that knows nothing about the window is exactly right. "**Discard** the other cards" cannot be expressed that way — it must name exactly the looked-at cards that were NOT attached, and after k are attached the window holds n − k, so a "discard the top 3" step would eat cards deeper in the deck. Hence `discardRest` ON the op. And it is charged on EVERY path — the whiff, the decline, and the no-eligible-target board — so every non-parking branch routes through the SAME apply with an empty assignment list rather than returning `state`; for Electric Generator that is invisible, for Tri Howl it is the difference between the Ability costing three cards and costing nothing. One new event `DECK_TOP_DISCARDED` (the pile is public; the attached cards are named by their own `ENERGY_ATTACHED`). **The filter reads the PRINT** — `basicEnergy` + `energyType` for "Basic {L} Energy CARDS", never `providesEnergy`: these cards sit in a DECK attached to nothing, so D44's §6.5 rule applies from the other side (and authoring the wrong one is a loud no-op, not a wrong card). **`attachEnergyTargets` was WIDENED, not copied** — it takes a shared `AttachTargetRiders` (`targetType`/`basicOnly`/**`benchOnly`**/`bonusCounters`/`notIfKO`), so "is this Pokémon an eligible attach target" is one rule with two op-shaped readers. **THE REVERSAL WORTH RECORDING: the first draft GATED the play and that was the slice's one HIGH.** `attachFromTopPlayable` refused Electric Generator when the controller had no Benched {L} Pokémon, by `attachEnergyFrom`'s "an attach with no eligible target can only whiff" rule. Wrong, because **`programPlayable` is an AND over a program's ops**: vetoing on this op vetoes the CARD, including its second printed sentence, which always resolves. The ruling cardplay.ts already quotes (ruling/284 — "playing a card for no effect happens when THE GAME STATE ITSELF prevents any effect") draws exactly that line, shuffling a known-bad deck top is an effect players buy, and two in-repo precedents agree (Great Ball / Pokégear are unconditionally playable; so is Nest Ball into a full Bench). The op is now **ungated**, the no-target board is a live path, and the general shape of the rule is recorded for the first card that needs it: a gate wants to be "no op in this program can do anything", not a per-op veto. **Web:** one new dialog — a two-step LOOP (pick a card, pick where it goes, repeat) rather than MoveEnergyDialog's wizard, where clicking an assigned card is the only undo; plus one log row. Rules doc extended at **§15.E** (the "in any way you like" distribution rule, the leftovers clause, and what does/does not become public). engineVersion 0.24.0 → **0.25.0**; 1301 → **1331** tests. **2-agent adversarial review (engine+rules with 50 mutants / web+integration), 65 mutations between them:** the HIGH above, plus `max` clamped to `min(printed, candidates.length)` (both agents, independently — a prompt saying "0/2" over one row invites a pick that does not exist), the prompt keeping the printed "you find there" / "in any way you like", four engine test holes each proved by a mutation that passed all 1323 tests (the cap was never the rule that rejected; attaching onto an Active that already held Energy; append-vs-prepend into a discard pile that was empty at every seed; and the apply's four wire guards, unreachable through the action API and now driven through `applyChoice` — one of them, the bench-index check, DESTROYS a card if removed), and five web fixes (a pending pick is `aria-current`, not `aria-pressed`, because Confirm does not dispatch it; the assigned-row readback carries the SPOT, since two benched Pokémon may share a name; releasing one card no longer cancels another's pending pick; the at-cap text says what you CAN do; and a dispatch-time filter against the offer, because the failure mode of a stale prompt key is not a bad attach but an UNESCAPABLE dialog — no row to release, every dispatch rejected, no state change to remount on). **Recorded, NOT fixed:** the P4 `pendingDecision` leak (pre-existing, D36) now carries a prompt whose unattached candidates go BACK into the deck and never become public by any other route; `GameLog`'s last-row-only announcement makes this the first slice where the only spoken row is a pure COST; and Tri Howl on an empty deck burns its once-per-turn silently. *Why: the deferred variant turned out not to be a rider on either op it sits between — "in any way you like" is a genuinely different answer SHAPE, and the leftovers clause is genuinely two different mechanisms wearing one printed sentence. The gate reversal is the more valuable half of the entry: it is the first time this engine's whiff heuristic met a card with two printed clauses, and the fix was to stop asking the question rather than to answer it better.* (2026-07-21) |

| D46 | `discardFromHandCost` — the printed hand COST that PARKS, and the field it absorbed (P3-M5, engine 0.26.0) | **The first op that is not an effect but the PRICE of one.** Printed in two wordings that are one mechanism, and the wording tracks **where the paying card sits**: TRAINERS say "You can use this card only if you discard 2 **other** cards from your hand" (Ultra Ball sv01-196; Earthen Vessel sv06.5-096, "**another** card") because the card is in the very hand it pays out of; ABILITIES say "You **must** discard a card from your hand **in order to use** this Ability" and **never** say "other" (Tinkaton sv02-105 "a card"; Revavroom sv01-142 "an Energy card"; Meowscarada ex "a Basic {G} Energy card"; Radiant Blastoise swsh10.5-018 "a Water Energy card") because the card is on the board. The correlation is exact across the catalog (3/3 Trainers, 7/7 Abilities), so **"other" is not a field on the op** — it is the played uid excluded in the GATE, the one place that runs while the Trainer is still in hand. The apply never needs it: `playTrainer` moves the played card to the discard before the program runs. Rules doc **§7.5**. **IT IS AN OP, AND THE FIRST ONE, BECAUSE IT PARKS — which is what let it ABSORB an existing field.** The review's reframing of the slice: `AbilityProgram.cost` (`{kind:"discardBasicEnergy", energyType}`, paid by `findAbilityCostCard` before `runProgram`) was **the same printed sentence**, and it could only ever express the FUNGIBLE case — Basic {G} Energy copies are interchangeable, so "the first match" was as good as any. Tinkaton's bare "a card" is the general one. So the field, its finder and the `ABILITY_COST_PAID` event are **GONE**, Meowscarada migrated onto the op, and the row went from 4 cards to **9 catalog rows** (+2 unmapped Meowscarada reprints the slice also picked up). Widen, don't copy — D44's `continuous.ts` split and D45's `attachEnergyTargets` widening, applied to a field this time. Behaviour is identical: the collapse leaves one candidate, so it never parks. **ORDER IS OBSERVABLE, and Revavroom is the witness:** pay 1 out of a hand of 5, THEN "draw cards until you have 6" draws **two**; paying afterwards would draw one. **MANDATORY, so `chooseCards` grew a required `min`** (0 at every printed "up to" park site, `count` here — the only mandatory card park there has ever been) plus a `"discard"` dest; `validateChoice` enforces the floor and the dialog disables Confirm below it. It parks only on a real question: `energyIdentity` generalized to **`cardIdentity`** (basic Energy by provided type, everything else by catalog id) and `handCostCandidates` keeps ≤ `count` per class. ONE collapsed set serves both prompt and gate, which the review proved sound in both directions — `kept = Σ min(nᵢ,count)`, so `kept ≥ count ⟺ actual ≥ count`. **THE GATE VETOES — the opposite call from D45, one slice later, and the text is what decides.** `attachFromTop` is ungated because a per-op veto inside `programPlayable`'s AND would kill a card whose other printed clause always resolves (the engine guessing a whiff); this one vetoes the whole card on the card's own printed authority ("only if"). So it lives beside `trainerPlayableIf` — printed RULES — not among the whiff heuristics, and it needs the played uid, which `programPlayable` structurally cannot see. **2-agent adversarial review (engine+rules, 39 mutants / web+integration, 28): NO HIGH from the engine agent; ONE HIGH from the web agent — the HUD did not mirror the new gate.** `playableTrainers` greyed out `trainerPlayableIf` but not this, so Ultra Ball on a hand of `[itself, one other card]` — the ordinary late-game state, and the moment the card is most often dead — rendered fully enabled and rejected on click, against the doctrine the same file states and the suite already pins **for the identical error code**; `handCostUnmet`/`handCostPhrase` were not even exported, so the HUD could not have mirrored it. Both are exported now (safer than `conditionHolds`: they read only the viewer's own hand) and the row greys with the printed reason. Two review findings changed the slice's story rather than just fixing it: **the `searchNote` bug was LIVE on a shipped card, not latent** — Earthen Vessel is the first `searchDeck` for SEVERAL of something whose noun takes an article, but **Chien-Pao ex "Shivery Chill"** (four prints) has read "Search your deck for up to 2 **a Basic Water Energy**" since it was authored, surviving only because nothing asserted any search note; and **the Radiant Blastoise filter is a documented DEVIATION, not a settled reading** — the print says "a **Water** Energy card" where Meowscarada says "a **Basic** {G} Energy card", and mapping both to `basicEnergy` erases a printed distinction §6.5 says should matter, so the comment now says `basicEnergy` is the conservative approximation (it fails by refusing the Ability, never by discarding a wrong card) pending a pile-side "would provide" predicate. Also fixed: the a11y pattern was brought into line with its mandatory sibling `DiscardEnergyDialog` (the counter is an `<output>` that stays MOUNTED through short→satisfied, since that transition is when Confirm becomes reachable and a region that unmounts announces nothing; over-cap rows are `aria-disabled` rather than silently inert — and one of the slice's own assertions had PINNED that silence; Confirm carries the denominator); the untested count-1 branch, which four of the six cards use and where the mutant `min > 0` → `min > 1` survived all 1357 tests; a two-park `promptKey` test; and four comments that claimed more than they proved (`handCostUnmet` is **not** compositional across two costs in one program — latent, now scoped explicitly). **Recorded, NOT fixed:** the P4 `pendingDecision` leak is now first to carry **hand** uids, and the op's own justification does not cover it — with Ultra Ball three uids are named and only two ever become public, so the third's identity is given away permanently, and `candidates.length` against the public hand-back count additionally leaks the hand's **duplicate structure**; **"choose 1" is not "up to 1"** — `choosePokemonMulti` has no `min`, so Meowscarada/Radiant Blastoise can pay the cost and then decline the placement (pre-existing and unchanged here, but this op makes the payment irreversible — the fix is the same widening this slice gave `chooseCards`); `GameLog` announces only a batch's LAST row and a cost is by construction never last; and `playTrainer` discards the played card BEFORE the cost, so the pile reads `[Ultra Ball, paid1, paid2]` where paper produces the reverse — cosmetic today, but exactly the ordering Superior Energy Retrieval will care about. **Deferred with a named reason:** **Superior Energy Retrieval** (sv02-189/-277), whose "(You can't choose a card you discarded with the effect of this card.)" needs the cost's uids to reach the following retrieval's candidate set — op→op data flow this engine has never needed; authored naively the card becomes strictly better than printed (pay 2 Basic Energy, take those same 2 back plus 2 more). engineVersion 0.25.0 → **0.26.0**; 1331 → **1363** tests. *Why: the resume point called this a new op, and it was really a promotion — the engine already had this cost, as a field that could only handle the case where the answer did not matter. Making it park is what turned it into one vocabulary, and the gate is the D45 question asked again with the printed text pointing the other way.* (2026-07-21) |

| D47 | The printed number is a FLOOR, and the hand cost that is not a discard (P3-M5, engine 0.27.0) | **Two mandatory-exactly-N fixes, one on each prompt that could still say "up to" — the resume point's #1, which D46's own review found.** (1) **`choosePokemonMulti` gained `min`/`max` + `declinable`.** A printed number in a *choose* clause is EXACT: a card prints the words "up to" when a partial answer is legal, and no consumer of `damageChosen` does. The prompt carried only `max`, so **Meowscarada ex / Radiant Blastoise could pay their hand cost — irreversible, already spent — and then place nothing**. Written into `reference/ptcg-rules.md` as **§9.1**, the general form of §7.5's "exactly the printed number", with §8.6 as its only softening (fewer targets than the number ⇒ take as many as you can). `declinable` is a THIRD field rather than `min: 0`, because Hawlucha's printed "**you may** choose 2" is ALL-OR-NOTHING — 2 or none, never 1 of 2 — and `min: 0` would have bought that decline by legalizing a half-answer the card does not offer. The decline has nowhere else to live: `triggers.ts` AUTO-FIRES optional triggers (the pure-upside doctrine), so the pick it governs is the only place a player can say no. **The review reversed the first draft on WHERE that decline exists:** a mandatory snipe over a Bench of `count` or fewer auto-takes (no decision left, the M1 rule), but a DECLINABLE one has the same decision at every Bench size, so it now parks at every non-empty Bench — the draft honoured "you may" only with 3+ Benched Pokémon, i.e. exactly where declining matters least. (2) **`discardFromHandCost` became `payFromHand` with a required `to: "discard" \| "deckBottom"`**, landing **Dendra ×3** (sv02-179/-250/-266): "Put a card from your hand on the bottom of your deck. **If you do**, draw cards until you have 5 cards in your hand. (If you have no **other** cards in your hand, you can't use this card.)" Nothing is discarded and the word "cost" never appears, yet all three clauses of D46's mechanism are printed on it — a mandatory exact pick out of hand, a benefit conditional on it, and the same play gate written as its contrapositive, "other" included — so this is one field wider, not a second op (WIDEN, DON'T COPY: D44's `continuous.ts` split, D45's `attachEnergyTargets`, D46's absorbed `AbilityProgram.cost`). The rename follows the widening: the op PAYS from hand and the destination says where. **The event follows the destination's VISIBILITY** — `HAND_COST_PAID` gained `to` rather than splitting, because cards paid to the discard become public where they land while cards put under the deck go hidden → hidden and are never seen again; the log speaks in counts either way but prints a different verb, and P4's per-seat filter has the destination to key on. The **prompt** carries `dest: "deckBottom"` as its own member for the same reason the review gave in the other direction: the first draft parked with `dest: "deck"` and let the dialog infer the verb from `min > 0`, which is the guess `min` exists to prevent (`discardPileRetrieval` also says `"deck"`, and there it means SHUFFLED IN). Order stays observable: Dendra + 3 others pays 1 and draws **three**, and the bottom of a SHORT deck is not out of reach — with 2 cards left the payment is drawn straight back, which is what paper does. **Skwovet ×2** (sv01-151/-222, "Nest Stash") reaches the same destination with the WHOLE hand and needed **no new op at all** — `handRefresh` with Iono's `toBottom` + `onlyIfAnyMoved` in a `oncePerTurn`/`activeOnly:false` AbilityProgram, the first `handRefresh` on the Ability path. **2-agent adversarial review in ISOLATED worktrees (86 + 67 mutations): no HIGH engine-side; ONE HIGH web-side — `usableAbilities` never mirrored the §7.5 gate**, so Meowscarada ex with no Basic {G} in hand rendered lit and was refused on click with no reason shown; recorded as "deliberate per D29" a slice earlier, indefensible once the Trainer row and the Ability row share one helper. Ability rows now carry the printed reason, name WHERE the Pokémon sits (Skwovet ×2 is the first board with two identical Ability labels), and the multi dialog refuses at the cap with `aria-disabled`. Eight test holes closed, each proved by a surviving mutation — most tellingly the **reprint rows** (deleting 3 of the slice's 5 catalog ids left the suite green, and every prior slice ships that test) and a **PADDED multi-choice** (two refs pass the new floor, one is not a candidate, and `placeSnipeCounters` would silently drop it — the shape that defeats all-or-nothing). engineVersion 0.26.0 → **0.27.0**; 1363 → **1395** tests. **Recorded, NOT fixed:** the P4 `pendingDecision` leak got worse in kind — the opponent's client now receives the parked player's HAND uids for a pick whose cards go to a HIDDEN destination (runtime-confirmed by the web agent); `payFromHand` does not itself enforce "if you do" (the ops after it run even if nothing was paid — unreachable through Dendra because the gate refuses the play, and the general fix is the same `EffectContext` op→op flow Superior Energy Retrieval wants); the two play paths order their gates differently (`PLAY_CONDITION_NOT_MET` vs `NO_LEGAL_TARGET` on a doubly-illegal state); and Skwovet on an empty hand still logs "shuffled their hand (0 cards) and put it on the bottom of their deck" (inherited from Iono). *Why: a rules FIX and a card slice turned out to be the same claim — that the printed number and the printed verb are the engine's to state, not the caller's to infer. The floor is that claim about a count, the destination about a zone, and both were being guessed downstream: one by a prompt that only said "at most", one by a dialog reading "deck" and hoping.* (2026-07-21) |

| D48 | A clause can read what an earlier clause DID — the op→op record (P3-M5, engine 0.28.0) | **The engine's first op→op data flow, and the mechanism two card families had been waiting on.** Every op before this could be decided from `GameState` plus the op itself; some printed clauses cannot, because they ask what *this card's own earlier sentence* actually did, and the board after an effect looks the same whether a card got there by that effect or was already sitting there. Written into `reference/ptcg-rules.md` as **§9.2**. Three printed wordings, one mechanism: Miriam's "if you shuffled any cards into your deck **in this way**", Dendra's "**if you do**", and Superior Energy Retrieval's "(You can't choose a card you discarded with **the effect of this card**.)". **THE SHAPE:** an op files the uids it ACTUALLY moved under a named **`EffectSlot`** (`recordAs`), and later ops read them — **TWO readers, one record** (the D40 two-consumers shape): a new **`recordGate {slot, then, otherwise?}`**, the third and last gate, spliced into runProgram's work queue exactly like `coinFlipGate`/`conditionGate` so an op inside a branch can still park; and **`discardPileRetrieval.exclude`**, which drops them from a candidate set. SER is what makes the second one load-bearing rather than cosmetic — its cost pays INTO the very discard pile its next clause reads, so without the exclusion a player discards 2 Basic Energy and takes the same two straight back, the cost refunds itself, and the parenthetical the printer added to stop exactly that means nothing. **THE RECORD RIDES `EffectContinuation`, NOT `EffectContext`** — a deliberate deviation from what the backlog named for three slices. The context is WHO the program runs for: one seat, constant for the whole program, read by ~30 helpers that would then hold a value going stale under them mid-run. This is the opposite, an accumulator changing at nearly every op, so its sibling is **`rest`** ("the ops still to run"), not `seat`; it is threaded like `events` and is ABSENT when empty, so every program predating §9.2 parks into a byte-identical phase. **KEYED BY SLOT, NOT BY POSITION.** "Read the previous op's result" is the obvious cheaper design and it is wrong: Janine's Secret Art prints a whole sentence between the action and the clause about it ("Then, shuffle your deck. If you attached Energy … in this way, …"), and Miriam — whose two sentences ARE adjacent on the card — stops being adjacent under the natural decomposition of "shuffle N into your deck" (a move plus a trailing `shuffleDeck`, the Pal Pad pattern). A closed 2-member union makes a mistyped slot a COMPILE error, which matters because an unrecorded slot reads as "nothing moved" — indistinguishable from a real empty answer, so a typo would silently cost Miriam its draw. Keyed by **uid**, not card id: a second copy of the identical print already in the pile stays choosable, which is the printed sentence read literally. Lands **Miriam ×3** (sv01-179/-238/-251) and **Superior Energy Retrieval ×2** (sv02-189/-277), and lets **Dendra** finally state its own "if you do" in the program rather than resting on a gate in another file. **Review (2 adversarial agents in ISOLATED worktrees): NO HIGH engine-side** — 49 mutations, 42 killed, 7 argued equivalent, with D14 replay verified through a JSON round-trip at every park, deep-freeze purity, and conservation across five degenerate zone states; its three MEDIUMs were all test holes, the sharpest being that **the slice's headline claim was never exercised** (`resumeProgram`'s record-seeding could be deleted with all 12 original tests green, because neither shipped card READS `cont.record` — both record and read inside one `runProgram`, so the assertions pinned storage, not carry). **ONE HIGH web-side, and it is a design gap: the prompt described half the card.** Miriam's dialog said "Shuffle up to 5 Pokémon…" over a button reading "Take none", and nothing anywhere mentioned the 3 cards — so declining silently forfeited the draw AND the turn's only Supporter, with the gate's false arm emitting no event to say so. Every prompt note before §9.2 was the whole printed effect because an op WAS the whole effect; a recording op is the first that is not. Fixed in the ENGINE, not the web: a parking op that records now looks DOWN THE QUEUE for the gate reading it and appends the printed conditional (`withConsequence`), so Dendra's cost prompt is now its printed sentence verbatim — with a deliberately tiny describer that is **silent on anything it has no phrase for**, because a partial consequence is worse than none. Three further web MEDIUMs were **false claims in this slice's own prose**, all corrected at the source: "Miriam prints retrieve → shuffle → in this way" (it prints two adjacent sentences — the gap is the engine's), "drawing first would deal cards off a deck top the player has seen" (runtime-disproved: `retrieveMove` appends to the deck's END, and the real reason is the returned cards would sit at the BOTTOM in pick order, which "shuffle" denies), and `discardPileRetrieval`'s self-match tripwire, whose stated reason ("every card here is an Item…") this very slice rotted by putting a Supporter on the op. engineVersion 0.27.0 → **0.28.0**; 1395 → **1446** tests. **Recorded, NOT fixed:** Miriam is playable into an empty discard (spending the turn's only Supporter for a bare shuffle) and SER into a discard with no Basic Energy (2 cards for nothing) — both follow shipped doctrine, but Miriam is the first SUPPORTER on this op, where a whiff costs the turn rather than a card, so the prompt is the mitigation and not the gate; `promptKey`'s `chooseCards` bounds are unpinned, now load-bearing since SER is the first card to park twice on one prompt kind. *Why: the printed text refers to itself, and nothing in the interpreter could hear it. A flag per card would have covered these five rows and nothing else; threading the op's RESULT through the continuation covers the clause-shape, which is what "in this way", "if you do" and "with the effect of this card" all are.* (2026-07-22) |

| D49 | The deck-SEARCH attach, and a §9.2 gate that asks WHERE (P3-M5, engine 0.29.0) | **One new op, `attachFromDeck`** — search your own deck for Energy matching a filter and attach it straight onto your own Pokémon, then shuffle (the printed "Then, shuffle your deck" stays a trailing `shuffleDeck`, the Nest Ball pattern). It is the deck-search **sibling of `attachFromTop`** and reuses that op's `attachCards` MAP prompt, because the printed clause is the same one — several cards, several places, chosen together; every difference between them falls out of the SOURCE. Two matter. (1) The candidate set is the WHOLE deck, so it is **COLLAPSED to `max` representatives per interchangeable class** (D42's `cardIdentity` — a Basic Energy keys on what it PROVIDES, since the catalog prints Basic Darkness Energy under four ids); a real list holds a dozen, and twelve rows for a pick of three is one question wearing twelve hats. Unlike the discard collapse the key carries **no host**, because a card in a pile is attached to nothing — while WHICH Pokémon loses an Energy is always a real choice. (2) There is **no leftovers clause** (nothing ever left the deck), so no `discardRest` and no routing of the empty paths through the apply. **TWO PRINTED TARGET RULES, one new field:** "in any way you like" (**Charizard ex** ×4, sv03-125/-215/-223/-228, "Infernal Reign" — an onEvolve trigger, and the first registry row reached through Rare Candy's shared `placeEvolution` too) is no rider at all; "**for each of those Pokémon** … attach it to THAT Pokémon" (**Janine's Secret Art** ×2, sv06.5-059/-088, Supporter) is **`maxPerTarget: 1`** — one apiece onto distinct Pokémon, enforced in `validateChoice` alone, since the apply re-derives nothing. A card prints "in any way you like" exactly when the split is free, so the absence of the phrase is not silence but the OTHER rule (`ptcg-rules.md` §15.E). **Janine parks ONCE, and that is a claim about the card:** her printed first sentence ("choose up to 2 of your {D} Pokémon") is not a second decision, because same-type Basic Energy is fungible — so "one Energy each onto 2 chosen Pokémon" and "2 Energy onto distinct Pokémon" are the same answer, and the map prompt already says it. (Which is why this slice does NOT consume §9.1's `min: 0` end; Saguaro still wants it.) **§9.2 gained `recordGate.contains: "yourActive"`** for her last sentence — a gate asking not "did the earlier op do anything" but "did any of it reach the ACTIVE": two Energy onto the Bench records two uids and poisons nothing, which is the player's choice to make. The narrowing **reads the BOARD, not the record**, so the record's values stay one kind of thing everywhere (the cards an op moved) and "where did it end up" is answered where every other board question is; the two readings are NOT interchangeable in general — `switchActive`, `moveEnergy` (own→own) and `discardEnergy`'s `yourActive` arm each separate them — but no printed card sequences any of those between an attach and its "in this way" clause, and Janine runs attach → shuffle → gate, so this is **safe as authored and unsafe as a general rule**; the op's doc names all three and records the likely answer for the card that needs one (file the HOST rather than widen the gate). `withConsequence` had to learn the CONDITION as well as the consequence — its hardcoded "If you do" is right for Miriam and **quietly wrong** here, since a player reading "If you do, it is now Poisoned" would put both Energy on the Bench expecting poison. **Ungated**, per `attachFromTop`/ruling/284 (the shuffle always resolves) — and this is the sharpest form of that call yet, because Janine is a SUPPORTER (§7.2) whose whiff costs the turn rather than a card; recorded, with the prompt as the mitigation. **Web: one field.** `AttachCardsDialog` refuses a target already holding its one Energy, and `promptKey` carries `maxPerTarget` (an assignment gathered under "in any way you like" is not a legal answer to a one-apiece prompt); the one case designed rather than translated is ONE eligible {D} Pokémon, where "up to 2" is a hard 1 — folded into the dialog's `atCap` so the second pick is refused UP FRONT instead of stranding the player against a list of rows that all refuse it. No new events, no new log rows. Deferred with named blockers: **Pawmot** ("to THIS Pokémon" — `EffectContext` carries no source uid), **Forretress ex** (a §9.2 gate into a KO by effect, which no op can do), **Geeta** ("your Pokémon can't attack this turn" — no turn-scoped restriction exists). engineVersion 0.28.0 → **0.29.0**; 1446 → **1526** tests. **Review (2 adversarial agents in ISOLATED worktrees, 69 + 46 mutations): ONE HIGH EACH, and both were false CLAIMS rather than wrong behaviour** — the pattern worth carrying, because each claim had papered over real missing coverage. The engine's was the `contains` equivalence above, which I had caught mid-session; the review found **four** divergences where I found one, and the one that matters most is a false POSITIVE the detach-only framing misses entirely (`moveEnergy benchToActive`: attach to the Bench, move it up, get poisoned for an attach that never touched the Active). Its three MEDIUMs were all real and all fixed: **the prompt's `max` lied** whenever `maxPerTarget × targets` was the true ceiling — Janine on the ordinary one-{D}-Pokémon board printed "up to 2" over a board where 1 was the maximum, the exact dishonesty `attachFromTop`'s clamp exists to prevent (the review read it as deliberate; it was not, and the clamp is now a third ceiling in the same `min`, taken BEFORE the collapse); **the whiff filed nothing** where its two sibling recording ops file `[]`, so with three recording ops the third disagreed by accident; and the Charizard fixture sat under a "verified verbatim" banner with three undeclared deviations, the sharp one being that **Charizard ex is a {D} Pokémon** that merely attacks with {R}. 15 of 69 mutants survived the suite as first written; 33 new tests kill 11, three of them serious (a mutant that DUPLICATES a card, one that THROWS out of `applyAction` — a D14 breach — and one filing *picked* rather than *attached* uids). **The web's two HIGHs were both in the new test file's own header:** `maxPerTarget` rides `promptKey` was listed as a case "below" and had none (deleting the field left all 1524 tests green; the failure mode is an assignment gathered under "in any way you like" surviving into a one-apiece prompt and dispatching an answer the engine rejects with a pill that cannot say which pairing was wrong), and the log test's `texts()` helper **discarded the seat entirely** under a test named "names BOTH attaches" that only checked "some row mentions Energy" — so a self-poison filed under the OPPONENT's seat survived it, and Janine is the first `STATUS_APPLIED` in the game filed under the controller's own seat. *Why: the resume point named Janine's Secret Art as one card needing a per-target deck search; the catalog showed five cards printing that sentence, differing only in the target rule and a drawback — so the op is the family's shape, not the card's, and the two target rules are what it exists to tell apart.* (2026-07-22) |
| D50 | The printed "this Pokémon" — a source uid on the effect context (P3-M5, engine 0.30.0) | **`EffectContext` gained `sourceUid?: string`** — the top-card uid of the Pokémon whose effect is running, a FIELD rather than an op, and the cheapest structural row the board had (three places documented the wall in so many words: `discardEnergy`'s `yourActive` arm, Pawmot, Forretress ex). It is set at **every run site that knows it**: `useAbility` (the uid was already in scope for the once-per-turn stamp — threading it was the whole job), `runBoardTrigger`/`runCheckupTriggers` (the uid already rode `ABILITY_TRIGGERED`), `runKoTrigger` (naming a card that has **left play** — honest, since the program is that card's own printed sentence, and a source-reading op then finds no board top and whiffs, which is what "this Pokémon" means after a KO), and the attack path (the attacker **by uid**, not "the Active", so a program that moves its own attacker cannot stale it). **ABSENT for a Trainer program** — a Trainer has no "this Pokémon" — and absence is structural: the parked context serializes byte-identical to the pre-slice `{seat}` (the §9.2 absent-when-empty promise, applied again). Constant for the whole run like `seat` (the accumulator shape lives on `EffectRecord`, per D48's reasoning), rides `EffectContinuation` across parks (JSON, D14). **First reader: `attachFromDeck.toSelf`**, the third printed target rule — the sole target is the source, resolved by **board TOP uid** (a source evolved over is a DIFFERENT Pokémon: the stack is one unit, but the printed "this Pokémon" of the old top no longer exists once evolved over; gone-from-play likewise), with the empty resolutions falling into the op's standing whiff path (the trailing shuffle still runs). Lands **Pawmot ×2** (sv01-076/-209, "Electrogenesis": "Once during your turn, you may search your deck for a Basic {L} Energy card and attach it to this Pokémon. Then, shuffle your deck.") — `oncePerTurn`, **`activeOnly: false`**, and that is the card's point: a **benched Pawmot feeds itself**, the exact sentence no seat-relative target rule (`attachEnergyTargets`) could say. The park stays declinable — a search of a hidden zone may be **failed on purpose** (`ptcg-rules.md` §9.1), so the printed bare article ("a Basic {L} Energy card") does not make the pick mandatory. Written into `ptcg-rules.md` as **§9.3**, including the LATENT doctrine gap: `sourceRef` would RE-ACQUIRE a source that left play and returned as a board top mid-program — unreachable at HEAD (full op-vocabulary audit: nothing returns an in-play card to the board mid-program), but "a returned Pokémon is a NEW Pokémon", so the first revival/self-recovery op must revisit it. **Web: no new anything — and one deliberate behaviour change from review.** A sole-target `attachCards` offer (every `toSelf` park, and Janine over a lone {D} Pokémon) violated the house **"a list of one is not a decision"** rule (D44, `MoveEnergyDialog`): the dialog demanded a tap on a target list of one. `AttachCardsDialog` now **commits the attach on the card tap** when the offer holds a single target; the target list never mounts, and the `<output>` copy is mode-aware. engineVersion 0.29.0 → **0.30.0**; 1526 → **1561** tests. **Review (2 adversarial agents in ISOLATED worktrees, 43 + 33 mutations): ONE HIGH engine-side, and it is the second slice running where the HIGH is a false coverage CLAIM rather than wrong behaviour (D48's sharpest MEDIUM was the same shape)** — the "board TOP, not stack membership — deliberately" doc and the shipped evolved-over test claimed the discrimination, but the stale-uid test sat its uid in the DECK, so **three wrong implementations** (Active-by-stack-membership, bench-by-stack-membership, bench-by-base-uid) passed the entire suite; under any of them a source evolved over mid-continuation attaches to the EVOLUTION. Closed by evolved-over tests on both arms. Its MED is the same lesson at the next altitude: every shipped board benched ONE Pawmot at slot 0, so hard-coded-index-0 and match-by-catalog-id (a two-Pawmot board resolves the WRONG copy) both survived — the two-Pawmot board that closes them also pins the per-uid once-per-turn stamp. LOWs: `toSelf`'s "mutually exclusive" doc claimed a rule the code doesn't enforce (reworded to the honest precedence — ignores `targetType`/`benchOnly`, `maxPerTarget` would still cap; authoring it alone is the rule, the doc is the enforcement); the cross-id collapse test was seed-vacuous-able (both prints could have left the deck — now pinned present). 43 mutants: 41 killed (34 by the pre-existing suite, 7 only by the review's 11 new tests), 2 equivalent — one being `runCheckupTriggers`' threading, which nothing can observe (no betweenTurns program parks or reads the source). **Web: NO HIGH**; besides the sole-target fix, two untested mirrors — `usableAbilities`' `activeOnly` gate could be deleted with all 1545 tests green (the exact lit-row-refused-click HIGH of D47, this time on a benched Chien-Pao; pinned both directions), and `promptKey`'s attachCards `targets`/`max` segments were comment-enforced only (pinned via the belt technique). The fixture's "verified verbatim, zero deviations" claim was independently re-verified TRUE against D1, both ids, every surface — the D49 banner lesson, held. *Why: the resume point ranked it the cheapest structural unlock — `useAbility` already knew the uid, so the wall was never information but plumbing; and it unblocks a FAMILY (any "this Pokémon" effect on a bench-usable Ability), not a card. Forretress ex stays blocked on the OTHER half of its sentence (a §9.2 gate into a KO by effect), Geeta on turn-scoped restrictions — the target rule was never their blocker.* (2026-07-22) |
| D51 | The printed "up to N … each" heal — §9.1's `min: 0` end lands (P3-M5, engine 0.31.0) | **`healChosen` gained `upTo?: number`** — the printed "up to N … each" (Saguaro ×3, sv02-187/-255/-270, Supporter: "Choose up to 2 of your Pokémon and heal 50 damage from each of them", identical on all three prints, verified verbatim vs D1). The **FIRST consumer of `choosePokemonMulti` with `min ≠ max`** — the range the prompt has carried since D47 with every consumer at `min === max`: the park is `{min: 0, max: min(upTo, in play), declinable: false}` (min: 0 IS the printed decline, so the "you may" flag stays unset — the prompt-doc rule), with §8.6 clamping the ASK and the heading with it (`healUpToNote` says "Choose up to 1 …" over a lone Active). **The field is `upTo`, not `count`**, because `damageChosen.count` means EXACT (§9.1's headline — "choose 1" is not "up to 1"); reusing `count` would have made the field's meaning depend on the op, and an exact multi-heal, if one ever prints, gets its own field rather than a silent reinterpretation. The `upTo` arm parks at EVERY non-empty board (the D47 declinable-snipe rule — the take-fewer right is the same decision over one candidate as over six); the exact-1 single arm (Potion / Fighting Au Lait / Arboliva's `"all"`) is **byte-untouched**, still `parkOrForce` onto `choosePokemon`, still auto-resolving at one candidate. **Candidates are ALL your Pokémon, damaged or not** (the printed choose clause has no damaged restriction; an undamaged pick heals 0 and emits nothing — the single-arm rule since M4), and **the play is UNGATED** (`programPlayable` has never had a heal branch) — the recorded paper divergence: under TPCi's state-change doctrine a fully-healed board cannot play it; ours can, spending the turn's Supporter on nothing, and the wart is now THREE cards wide (Potion, Fighting Au Lait, Saguaro; the honest "Take none" is the mitigation). The `applyChoice` arm folds the picked refs through the single-target helper — one HEALED per damaged pick, each clamped to its own damage, `[]` a legal no-op resolve that still spends the §7.2 allowance. **Web: ZERO source changes** — `ChoosePokemonMultiDialog` was written for this day (Confirm never disabled at min 0, "Take none" at zero picks, "Confirm N" with no denominator for partials — nothing is owed), but every `min ≠ max` branch was an untested dead path; two DOM tests now drive the Saguaro park end-to-end, the partial confirm pinning WHICH ref is dispatched (a dialog sending the first N candidates fails it). engineVersion 0.30.0 → **0.31.0**; 1561 → **1576** tests. **Review (2 adversarial agents in ISOLATED worktrees, 34 + 8 mutations): NO HIGH from either — and engine-side no false coverage claim AT ALL, the first such slice in three.** Engine: 32/34 mutants killed; the 2 survivors argued equivalent (`healUpToNote`'s `"all"` arm is dead code with no printed author — declared in its own doc; the own-seat guard sits behind pre-validated refs at all three call sites, the runCheckupTriggers precedent). A 13-shape garbage probe all rejected with the state byte-identical (D14); all three reprint rows independently load-bearing. **Web: its MED is the recurring shape** — `promptKey`'s `pokemonMulti` arm was entirely UNPINNED (gutting the key to the bare string survived every test); LATENT, since no live path parks this prompt twice with no render between, but the key's own doc claims the discrimination — closed with the attachCards belt technique over all four segments (min/max/declinable/candidates), verified to kill both surviving mutants. The web table also proved the two new DOM tests are the SOLE killers of the "up to" surfaces (the live region's phrasing; the denominator-free Confirm). LOWs kept: the batch announcer speaks only a batch's LAST row (7th slice — a 2-pick resolve announces one HEALED; a decline announces nothing after "played Saguaro", which covers three distinct outcomes); Saguaro's HUD row is lit exactly when the engine accepts (scratch-verified, incl. the undamaged board — not the recurring lit-row HIGH). *Why: the resume point's #1 for three slices — the cheapest reader of a range the vocabulary already carried, landing a real Supporter with one field and zero web source changes; and naming the field after the printed words keeps "choose 1 is not up to 1" structural rather than conventional.* (2026-07-22) |

| D52 | The reveal-and-bottom family, and the engine's first NON-CONTROLLER decision (P3-M5) | **Ortega** ×2 (sv03-190/-219) + **Greavard** "Underworld Stroll" (sv01-105, DERIVED), on two new ops. (1) **`bottomFromOpponentHand`** — reveal the opponent's whole hand, the controller picks one card matching an optional filter, it goes under the OPPONENT's deck. Three calls worth stating. The **reveal is TOTAL and public** (new `HAND_REVEALED` naming every uid — the one hand event in the file that is not count-only, because a reveal is precisely the moment those cards stop being hidden), emitted as the op's FIRST act on both the parking and the auto-resolving path. The **bottomed card is NAMED** (`CARD_TO_BOTTOM_OF_DECK`, with an `actor`) where Dendra's identical destination is count-only: the event follows the move's VISIBILITY, and this one happened face up at both ends, so which card sits on the bottom is real public information. And the offer **COLLAPSES to one representative per interchangeable class** — sound here exactly where it is not in `payFromHand`, whose collapse over a still-hidden hand leaks the hand's duplicate structure; this hand was just shown in full, so the offer's shape has nothing left to give away. (2) **`opponentMayDraw`** — the printed "your opponent **may** draw a card", and **the first decision in the engine answered by the seat that did not make the play**. The park carries a `decider`, the phase files an **`answerer` beside its controller `seat`** (absent when they agree, so every pre-D52 park serializes byte-identically), and `resolveEffect` admits only that seat: the controller cannot consent for their opponent, and the turn does not move while they think. Rules doc **§9.4**. Greavard derives from attack TEXT rather than a registry row because `CardProgram.attack` is per-CARD and its second attack (Sharp Fang) must stay plain. `programPlayable` gained the empty-opponent-hand gate — a PUBLIC count, so the Catcher/Hammer class under ruling/906+284, and it reads hand LENGTH never the op's filter, since whether a non-empty hidden hand holds a match is exactly what the game state does not establish — and is now **EXPORTED**, so the HUD greys a dead row with the predicate that rejects it. engineVersion 0.31.0 → **0.32.0**; 1576 → **1627** tests. **Review (2 adversarial agents in ISOLATED worktrees, 87 + 44 mutations): one HIGH each, and both were FALSE CLAIMS over untested guards — the pattern, now four slices running.** The engine's: the new deriver rule's test said it pinned the ANCHORS, and both its negative cases were refused by the pattern BODY instead — dropping `^`, `$` or both passed all 722 tests, where the same mutation on two sibling rules dies immediately (`attackDiscards.test.ts` states the convention verbatim). Closed with two strings that contain the printed sentence and add text on one side. Its MEDIUMs were all real and all test-adequacy: the apply's wire guard could be deleted and **duplicate a card** off a crafted snapshot; the record could file *picked* rather than *moved* uids and park a decision for an event that did not happen; the gate's `=== 0` boundary was never driven at a hand of ONE; "reads length, never the filter" — the promise the export rests on — was unpinned; and the reveal-first ordering held on the parking path only. **The web's HIGH was a `promptKey` arm that could not be observed at all**: its comment and its test's name claimed the count prevented a stale button label, but the dialog is STATELESS (both buttons dispatch on the tap, every label derives from the live prompt), so gutting the arm to a constant passed all 1612 tests — the arm is deleted and the reasoning recorded, since `kind:note` already gives it a per-count identity. Its second HIGH: the mirror was claimed "for Trainers AND Abilities" with zero Ability tests, over a change that newly greys six shipped Ability programs. Three MEDIUMs changed the code rather than only its tests: the dialog **never named the deciding player** although its comment said it did — and the handoff banner that normally does sits OUTSIDE the modal, hence inert, so a hot-seat device said "You may draw a card" to whoever was holding it; **Escape-swallowing was untested and breaking it soft-locks the game** (HudDialog only calls `showModal()` on mount, so a dismissed dialog never returns while the phase stays parked); and `pendingDecision` shipped to both viewers, which was a tautology until this slice made "who may see the prompt" a real question — now withheld from a non-answerer, deliberately NARROWLY (reversing D17's contract for every other park is a named P4 job, not this slice's to do silently). Focus was also moved off Decline, since Escape is swallowed and Enter would otherwise forfeit a card irreversibly. **One finding was a pre-existing engine contradiction this slice made user-visible**: `programPlayable`'s `discardEnergy` arm claimed "Crushing Hammer's coin-GATED discard stays playable either way" while the `coinFlipGate` branch six lines above descends into `then` for exactly that card (D41) — the descent wins, measured, and the stale comment is corrected, because since 0.32.0 the disagreement renders to a player as an unclickable row. *Why: the row three earlier slices left open, and the only one that needed a mechanism rather than a field — a card can put a decision on the player who did not play it, and modelling that as a second decider beside the controller (rather than collapsing them either way) is what keeps both "the turn is still yours" and "this choice is not" true at once.* (2026-07-22) |

| D53 | `damageSelf` + the two-seat attack epilogue — the D42 debt paid (P3-M5, engine 0.33.0) | The recoil clause **"This Pokémon also does N damage to itself"** — **Skeledirge** sv01-038 "Blazing Shout" (190 to the defender, then 30 to itself; effect text verbatim vs D1, the × hexdump-checked), the engine's **FIRST attack that damages its OWN board**. Two moving parts. (1) **`damageSelf`**, a flat self-damage op emitted as **COUNTERS_PLACED source `"self"`** — the confusion self-hit's own model, OUTSIDE the §8.5 W/R pipeline: you have no Weakness to your own attack (§8.5 W/R apply to the Defending Pokémon only), and no reduction either — Bouffalant's "Bouffer" reduces the *opponent's* attack, not your recoil. It lands on the attacker's own **Active** (the source of a §8 attack), consistent with the sibling `damageActive` targeting a seat's Active, and like every op it only RAISES damage and never KOs. (2) **finishAttack's KO sweep widened from the single defender seat to BOTH**, in **`[defender, attacker]` order** so §8.1's "the player whose turn it is takes their Prizes first" holds: the attacker's prize (for the defender's KO) queues ahead of the defender's (for the self-KO), and a self-KO **prizes to the DEFENDER** exactly as the confusion self-hit's does. `collectKnockOuts` already carried every piece — prizes-first grouping in ref order, on-KO triggers, promotions, and the §14 double-KO **tie** guard — so the widening is one argument and the `koedSeat` field on the `attackEpilogue` stage is now **dead and dropped** (the sweep reads both boards off the post-effect state, so the KO'd seats need not be predeclared). This makes the **§14 double-KO tie reachable from the attack path for the first time**, and the **§14.2 "you self-KO'd your last Pokémon → you lose (`noPokemon`)"** loss reachable through an attack — both now tested. Skeledirge derives from TEXT (its other attack, Passionate Singing's discard-pile attach, has no rule and stays on the loud `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` path — the card's real attack list is its own). engineVersion 0.32.0 → **0.33.0**; 1627 → **1636** tests. **Review (2 adversarial agents, mutation-proven): ONE HIGH engine-side, the recurring FALSE-COVERAGE pattern** — the deriver's `^`-anchor "load-bearing, both of them" test used a lowercase-"this" leading negative that the case-sensitive regex refuses *regardless of the anchor*, so dropping `^` left all 738 tests green (agent ran the mutation). Closed with a capital-"This" leading killer; **both anchor drops now fail the test, each verified by running the mutation.** The MED — the newly-reachable §14.2 self-KO loss — was pinned proactively (added before the review returned). Two LOWs kept and declared: `damageSelf` reads `ctx.seat`'s Active rather than threading `sourceUid` (harmless — no derivable attack relocates the source before a lone recoil op, and it matches `damageActive`'s pattern; declared in the op doc), and the op's `amount <= 0` guard is defensive/untested (mirrors `damageActive`). **Web: NO HIGH** — the `COUNTERS_PLACED "self"` row reads in the attacker's own voice ("Skeledirge did 30 damage to itself", `who: "p1"`, a SEAT row not a system one, since the attacker chose the attack), asserted on both text and `who` so gutting the arm to the default fails. *Why: the item three earlier resume points flagged as "the cheapest thing standing between the engine and a real bug rather than a latent one", owed before any self-damaging attack text — and the honest reading of §8.1 is that a KO is a KO on either board, so the fix is to widen the sweep the mechanism already had, not to special-case recoil; modelling the recoil itself as the confusion self-hit's flat-placement twin keeps the "damage vs counters" and "inside vs outside §8.5" doctrines single-sourced.* (2026-07-23) |
| D54 | Count-scaled attack damage — the additive "+" family lands (P3-M5, engine 0.34.0) | The printed **"This attack does N more damage for each damage counter on this Pokémon."** is now SIMULATED — the first reader to turn the dead `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` damage-modifier path (`cards.ts` `parseAttackDamage` splits `{base, modifier}`; nothing consumed the modifier) into a simulated one. A new reader **`deriveAttackDamageBonus`** (effects.ts, beside `deriveAttackEffect` but DISJOINT from it) parses the anchored whole-sentence into `{per, count: {kind: "damageCountersOnSelf"}}`, and **attack.ts folds `per × Math.floor(attacker.damage/10)` into the printed base BEFORE the §8.5 W/R pipeline** (`base + scaled + bonus`, alongside the continuous Vitality Band bonus). It is **PRE-damage math the caller computes, NOT an `EffectOp`** the interpreter runs — every `deriveAttackEffect` op runs AFTER damage, so the two derivers are disjoint and a card carries at most one (the whole-sentence anchor guarantees a scaling sentence never also derives an op). Lands **Paldean Tauros** sv02-028 "Raging Horns" (20+, verbatim vs the real card) and simulates **`fix-attacker` "Rage"** (the default test attacker, 10+, identical text). The skip condition is rewritten around **`effectSimulated`/`modifierSimulated`** so the "+"/text is flagged loudly ONLY when genuinely unsimulated — for any card with `damageBonus === null` it reduces ALGEBRAICALLY to the old condition (verified by the engine reviewer, and pinned by the zero-regression suite).
