New **`DAMAGE_DEALT.scaled`** field carries the added HP (present only when >0, the `bonus`/`reduction` pattern), and the web log gains a **`· scaled +N`** breadcrumb reading ahead of `· boosted +N` in §8.5 order. **Charizard ex** sv03-125 "Burning Darkness" (180+, "…for each Prize card your opponent has TAKEN" — a DIFFERENT count source) is DELIBERATELY left on the `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` path, the **anchor-guard witness** proving the deriver is narrow; the two "+"-skip witnesses (checkup / logFromEvents) migrated from Rage to it, and `count` is a union so the Prize-card reader lands as a MEMBER later, not a second interface. engineVersion 0.33.0 → **0.34.0**; 1636 → **1647** tests. **Review (2 adversarial agents in isolated worktrees, 17 engine mutations): NO HIGH.** The anchor-killers, the pre-W/R fold ordering (a 40-vs-30 test), the "right Pokémon at declaration time" read, and the `scaled`-honesty all genuinely die. **Three false-coverage MED/LOWs found AND FIXED, each mutation re-run to confirm the kill:** (1) the fold's `per` operand was untested — every folded fixture prints per=10, so `* bonus.per → * 10` survived the whole suite; (2) the widened `base + scaled > 0` guard was untested (latent — no base-0 scaler); both closed with ONE synthetic **base-0, per-20** scaler ("Fury", fix-attacker index 4) whose fold test asserts `{base:0, scaled:60, dealt:60}` — the `* 10` mutation gives 30 and the `base > 0` revert emits no DAMAGE_DEALT at all; (3) the `· scaled +N` log breadcrumb was unpinned (deleting it survived) — closed with a rendered-string assertion, the `· boosted +N` sibling's technique. A stale "stays on the ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED path" comment on the Raging Horns fixture (now false) was corrected. **Recorded, not fixed:** no fixture exercises a card with BOTH a derived post-damage program AND a "+" modifier, so the skip arm that still fires with `effect: null, damageModifier: "+"` is correct-by-analysis only (pre-existing shape). *Why: the count-scaled family was resume item #3, the natural continuation of the recoil/self family `damageSelf` (D53) unblocked; the additive "+" reader is the smallest honest step (one real card + the default attacker), and leaving Charizard's Prize-source scaling unsimulated is not a gap but a NARROWNESS witness the false-coverage review pattern needs. The slice's originally-flagged #1 (Sandaconda "rider clause") was FALSIFIED during exploration — its real text ("your opponent shuffles their Active Pokémon and all attached cards into their deck") needs a brand-new op plus a persistent-marker subsystem (`markers[]` is a never-read stub), not the `recordAs` the resume note assumed.* (2026-07-23) |

| D55 | The count union's SECOND member — Charizard ex "Burning Darkness" (`opponentPrizesTaken`) (P3-M5, engine 0.35.0) | The D54 `DamageCountSource` union gained its second member, **`opponentPrizesTaken`**, landing **Charizard ex** sv03-125 "Burning Darkness" (180+, "This attack does 30 more damage for each Prize card your opponent has taken." — verbatim vs the local D1 catalog, all 4 prints identical). One new anchored regex **`OPPONENT_PRIZES_SCALE`** (effects.ts) beside `SELF_COUNTER_SCALE`, and one new evaluator arm in **`scaledAttackDamage`** (attack.ts), whose signature **widened from `(attacker, bonus)` to `(attacker, bonus, state, defenderSeat)`** to reach the opponent's board: `(PRIZE_COUNT − state.players[defenderSeat].prizes.length) × per`. **The word "more" is load-bearing:** it is what separates this ADDITIVE "+" family from the **"×"/multiply Prize family** ("This attack does N damage for each Prize card your opponent has taken." — Pecharunt ex sv06.5-039, Annihilape sv01-109, Wo-Chien ex sv02-027, no "more"), which folds nothing into a printed base and stays on the loud `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` path (a future reader, a DIFFERENT shape — not a `count` member). Taken = **6 − remaining** (each player draws from their OWN Prizes on a KO, §8.1), read at **declaration** before this attack's own KO awards a Prize (§8.5 — the "+X before Weakness" base modifier). **Simulating Charizard RETIRED it as D54's anchor-guard witness, so the unsimulated-"+" witness MIGRATED to a real replacement — Entei sv03-030 "Blaze Ball"** (60+, "…for each {R} Energy attached to this Pokémon.", an attached-Energy count source no reader simulates) — across all three witnesses (scaledDamage discrimination, checkup, logFromEvents). `SCALED_DAMAGE_DECK` gained 4 Entei with `fix-fire-energy` trimmed 20→16 to hold exactly 60. **No web change** — `DAMAGE_DEALT.scaled` + the `· scaled +N` breadcrumb render generically over both count sources. engineVersion 0.34.0 → **0.35.0**; 1647 → **1653** tests, `bun run check` + `vite build` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer + an in-tree mutation self-audit): NO HIGH** — rules correctness (opponent = defender, 6−remaining, declaration timing), regex narrowness (grepped over every catalog "has taken"/"more damage for each" attack — matches ONLY the 4 Charizard prints, refuses all "×" and self-scalers), deck integrity (=60), the Entei fixture (verbatim), and the two-case switch exhaustiveness all confirmed correct. **ONE MED false-coverage FOUND AND FIXED, mutation-proven:** every apply-level Prize test used Charizard's `per: 30`, so a `* bonus.per → * 30` literal (the value a careless author would most plausibly hard-code, and thus the sharpest survivor — my "captured per" test ruled out only `* 10`) survived the whole suite. Closed with a synthetic **per-50 probe** ("Bounty", fix-attacker index 5, base 40 → `{base:40, scaled:100, dealt:140}` at 2 Prizes taken) — the Prize-source twin of D54's "Fury" probe; the `* 30` mutant now dies, and `attack.test.ts`'s out-of-range-index witness bumped 5→6. *Why: resume item #1, the cheapest continuation — it confirms the union-of-two shape the D54 interface was deliberately built for, lands a real in-tree headline card, and the anchor-guard witness is PRESERVED by migration (to a card that stays loud) rather than lost. The "×"/multiply family (resume #2) is now the named next step, its refusal already witnessed.* (2026-07-23) |
| D56 | The "×"/multiply damage family — Pecharunt ex / Annihilape (P3-M5, engine 0.36.0) | The sibling of D54–D55's additive "+" family. **"This attack does N damage for each Prize card your opponent has taken."** (NO "more") is now SIMULATED. Unlike the additive fold, the printed "N×" is the **PER-unit**, so `per × count` is the **WHOLE damage with the printed base DROPPED** (count 0 → 0 damage) — a **DISTINCT fold, not a `count` member**, exactly as D55 predicted. A new reader **`deriveAttackDamageMultiplier`** (effects.ts) + regex **`OPPONENT_PRIZES_MULTIPLY`** (`^This attack does (\d+) damage for each Prize card your opponent has taken\.$`, no "more") parse into the SAME `{per, count: {kind: "opponentPrizesTaken"}}` shape and **reuse D55's count evaluator UNCHANGED** — the two families differ ONLY in attack.ts's fold. attack.ts reads BOTH scaling derivers (`damageMultiplier` only when `damageBonus === null` — the regexes are mutually exclusive on "more", so at most one fires) and **`scaledBase = damageMultiplier !== null ? 0 : base`** drops the base for a multiply while the additive family keeps it; the §8.5 pipeline guard (`scaledBase + scaled > 0`) and `DAMAGE_DEALT.base` both use `scaledBase`. Lands **Pecharunt ex** sv06.5-039 "Irritated Outburst" (60×, {D}{D}) and **Annihilape** sv01-109 "Rage Fist" (70×, {F}) — two pers on one count source. **The bigger "×" siblings stay LOUD, the multiply reader's anchor-guard witnesses:** **Wo-Chien ex** sv02-027 "Covetous Ivy" (a benched-TARGET multiply — "…to 1 of your opponent's Benched Pokémon for each Prize…", refused by the mid-sentence text the anchors exclude) and **Chien-Pao ex** "Hail Blade" (a DISCARDED-Energy count — "…for each card you discarded in this way.", refused by the tail). New `MULTIPLY_DAMAGE_DECK` (Darkness/Fighting Energy pay {D}{D}/{F}; Fire cannot). **No web change** — `DAMAGE_DEALT.base:0, scaled:N` renders through the generic `· scaled +N` breadcrumb. engineVersion 0.35.0 → **0.36.0**; 1653 → **1661** tests, `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH, NO MED, no surviving mutants.** Catalog narrowness proven EXHAUSTIVELY (of the 4 cards printing "for each Prize card your opponent has taken", the multiply regex matches ONLY Pecharunt/Annihilape; refuses Wo-Chien benched and Charizard's additive "more"); the base-drop is pinned two ways (a count-0 "no DAMAGE_DEALT" test — a kept base would deal 60 — and a count-2 `{base:0, scaled:120, dealt:120}` test — a kept base would give 180); the captured per is pinned by Annihilape's 70 ≠ Pecharunt's 60. **ONE LOW FOUND AND FIXED:** the Pecharunt fixture's "Verbatim vs the local D1 catalog" comment omitted the card's real "Subjugating Chains" Ability — corrected to an honest "printed STATS + attack row verbatim; Ability OMITTED (unexercised, no registry row)", the false-verbatim defect this repo names explicitly (the Charizard fixture comment). **Recorded, NOT fixed:** the multiply W/R ordering is not INDEPENDENTLY pinned by a weak-defender test (every multiply test uses fix-bigbody, no weakness) — but the fold is the SAME shared pipeline line as the additive family, already pinned by the "folds BEFORE weakness" test, and with `scaledBase` forced to 0 the multiply arm only supplies `scaled`, so it cannot break the order alone (reviewer LOW — the same call D55 made for its Prize arm). *Why: resume item #1, the cheapest continuation — it confirms the "distinct fold" shape D55 deliberately deferred, reuses the count evaluator with zero change, and lands two real headline cards while PRESERVING the anchor-guard discipline (the harder "×" siblings become the new loud witnesses). Kept as sibling nominal readers rather than folding into one deriver-with-a-flag, matching how the additive family already reads two count sources with two anchored regexes.* (2026-07-23) |
| D57 | The "×" family's benched-TARGET sibling — Wo-Chien ex "Covetous Ivy" (P3-M5, engine 0.37.0) | The last of the four "for each Prize card your opponent has taken" cards, and a genuinely new mechanism. **"This attack does 60 damage to 1 of your opponent's Benched Pokémon for each Prize card your opponent has taken. (Don't apply Weakness and Resistance for Benched Pokémon.)"** now SIMULATES. Its target is one **CHOSEN Benched** Pokémon, not the Defender, so it is NOT the D56 pre-W/R fold but a **`damageChosen`** op (count 1) — the put-counter snipe (Hawlucha / Meowscarada) extended with two card-driven flags: **`deals`** (the hit is ATTACK DAMAGE — a `DAMAGE_DEALT` with the benched reduction passive applied, exactly `spreadDamage`'s model, where the default snipe emits a flat `COUNTERS_PLACED` counter; a Benched target still takes NO Weakness/Resistance either way, §8.5) and **`perTakenPrize`** (the amount is `amount × the opponent's TAKEN Prizes`, read from state in the interpreter's new **`snipeAmount`** at BOTH the run site and the pick — provably equal, since no prize moves in the `effect:choose` window and the attack's own KOs defer to `finishAttack`; 0 taken → 0 damage, so no park and no event). A new anchored derive **`BENCHED_SNIPE_TAKEN_PRIZES`** (effects.ts) tells it from `SPREAD_EACH_BENCH` by "1 of" vs "each of"; the "6 − remaining" Prize formula was extracted to a shared **`takenPrizes(state, seat)`** helper (types.ts) used by BOTH this and the D55/D56 fold. `placeSnipeCounters` → **`placeSnipe`** (a `deals` branch); `snipeNote` gained "damage" vs "counters" wording. Lands **Wo-Chien ex** sv02-027 (+ reprints -232/-257; Basic, 230 HP, Grass, ×2 Fire), verbatim vs the local D1 catalog. **This RETIRES Wo-Chien as D56's anchor-guard witness**, leaving **Chien-Pao ex** "Hail Blade" (a DISCARDED-Energy count) the sole remaining "×" witness — the "for each Prize card your opponent has taken" family is now COMPLETE. New `COVETOUS_IVY_DECK`. **No web change** — a `DAMAGE_DEALT` on a benched uid renders generically, as `spreadDamage`'s already does. engineVersion 0.36.0 → **0.37.0**; 1661 → **1673** tests, `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH.** Recompute-at-resolve safety, the 0-guard, the `deals`/`spreadDamage` parity, the benched-KO-through-`finishAttack` turn-END (distinct from the Ability snipe's mid-turn resume), regex narrowness (matches ONLY Wo-Chien of ~20 catalog "to 1 … Benched" attacks — the "also does N" riders all stay loud), and the `takenPrizes` refactor's value-identity all CONFIRMED. **ONE MED false-coverage FOUND AND FIXED, plus 2 LOWs:** the "reduction passive" test benched a passive-LESS body (fix-bigbody, reduction 0), so `dealt = amount − reduction` and the `reduction` field went unpinned (`dealt = amount` and `reduction: undefined` survived the suite) — closed by benching real **Bouffalant sv03-174 "Bouffer"** (−20), asserting `{base:60, reduction:20, dealt:40}`; the purity test moved from the park path (which never reaches `placeSnipe`) to the state-rewriting AUTO-TAKE path; the non-discriminating `weakness:null` was left as-is (the `deals` branch hard-codes null — no realistic mutation reaches it). The captured `amount` is pinned by a per-40 `fix-bramble` probe (Wo-Chien always prints 60). *Why: resume item #1, and it CLOSES the Prize-count family cleanly — reusing the existing `damageChosen` park (count-1, mandatory) rather than a new op, and extending it with two honest card-driven flags rather than overloading the counter semantics. The `takenPrizes` extraction removes the one formula the D55 fold and this op would otherwise duplicate. The two harder "×" cousins are deliberately deferred: Chien-Pao needs a pre-damage park (inverts the damage-then-effect order) and belongs to its own session; the flat "also does N to 1 Benched" rider family (~15 cards) is now cheap — the same op minus `perTakenPrize` — and is the high-yield next step.* (2026-07-23) |
| D58 | The flat benched-snipe RIDER family (P3-M5, engine 0.38.0) | The high-yield cheap step D57's catalog scan named. **"This attack ALSO does N damage to 1 of your opponent's Benched Pokémon. (Don't apply Weakness and Resistance for Benched Pokémon.)"** — a SECONDARY snipe AFTER the main damage (which rides the attack's own `damage` field) — now SIMULATES. It is the SAME **`damageChosen`** op D57 built (count 1, `deals: true`) but WITHOUT `perTakenPrize` (a FIXED N, not Prize-scaled), so it is **ZERO new op or interpreter code** — just a new anchored derive **`ALSO_BENCHED_SNIPE`** (effects.ts) and one case in `deriveAttackEffect`, producing `[{op:"damageChosen", target:"opponentBench", amount:N, count:1, deals:true}]`. The main hit and the rider coexist by the normal attack flow: attack.ts deals the `damage`-field base to the Defender (through W/R) FIRST, then the effect program runs the benched snipe (parks on a count-1 choice, or auto-takes a Bench of one, or whiffs on an empty Bench). Lands ~15 prints across **Miraidon** sv01-080 "Lightning Laser" (90 + 30), **Tadbulb** sv03-076 "Shake and Discharge" (20 + 10) — both authored as fixtures — plus Pawmot, Bellibolt, Decidueye ex, Jumpluff, Orthworm, Palafin, Pyroar, Dartrix (same shape, any N). **Stays LOUD (different shapes):** Flamigo "Synchronized Feathers" ("If Flamigo is on your Bench, …" — a board-condition gate, off the `^This` anchor), the "each of" also-SPREAD rider, "2 of" (count ≠ 1), "1 of YOUR Benched" (self-target), and "does N to 1 of your opponent's POKÉMON" (any target). New `ALSO_SNIPE_DECK` (BOTH setup Actives pinned to `fix-bigbody`, 32/60 dominant, so the Defender is a deterministic 200 HP body with an EMPTY Bench — no seed-dependent displaced body pollutes the benched targets). **No web change** — a `DAMAGE_DEALT` on a benched uid renders generically. engineVersion 0.37.0 → **0.38.0**; 1673 → **1683** tests, `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer, mutation-driven): NO real defects.** Every load-bearing field is genuinely pinned — a `count:2` injection fails 3 tests, a `perTakenPrize:true` injection fails 6, the captured amount is pinned by Miraidon 30 ≠ Tadbulb 10, `deals` by DAMAGE_DEALT + `not COUNTERS_PLACED`, main+rider coexistence + ordering by the two bases (90 then 30). Regex narrowness proven empirically over 18 edge cases + the whole catalog. **ONE comment nit FIXED:** the code claimed the word "also" separates this from Covetous Ivy, but the reviewer proved the whole-sentence anchor does (Covetous Ivy's clause continues "… Benched Pokémon FOR EACH Prize …", so the `\.$` after "Pokémon" blocks it; making "also" optional survives the suite, harmlessly) — corrected to name the anchor as load-bearing. *Why: resume item #3, and the cheapest high-yield step in the whole milestone — the D57 op was deliberately built to generalize, and this is the proof: ~15 real cards for a ~26-line diff, no new mechanism. It also OPENS the benched-snipe rider family for more cheap shapes (each-of spread, count-2, any-target), each a small extension of the same op.* (2026-07-23) |
| D59 | The ANY-target snipe family, with conditional Weakness/Resistance (P3-M5, engine 0.39.0) | **"This attack does N damage to 1 of your opponent's Pokémon. (Don't apply Weakness and Resistance for Benched Pokémon.)"** — the WHOLE attack (no `damage` field), a chosen hit on the opponent's **Active OR a Bench** — now SIMULATES. `damageChosen`'s `target` widened from `"opponentBench"` to `"opponentBench" | "opponentAny"`; a new regex **`CHOSEN_ANY_TARGET`** derives `[{op:"damageChosen", target:"opponentAny", amount:N, count:1, deals:true}]`. In the interpreter, **`oppAnyRefs`** offers the opponent's Active (always present during an attack, so the set is never empty) plus the whole Bench, and **`placeSnipe` gained an Active arm `snipeActive`** that runs the FULL §8.5 pipeline — base + the attacker's continuous pre-W/R bonus → ×Weakness → −Resistance → −reduction, floored — because **Weakness/Resistance apply ONLY to the Active** (rules §8.5), so a snipe that lands on the Active is real attack damage while a Benched pick stays flat. The Bench arm is UNCHANGED (still flat, still `next`-threaded), so the put-counter snipe (Hawlucha / Meowscarada) and the benched-`deals` families (Wo-Chien, riders) do not regress. Lands **Fezandipiti ex** sv06.5-038 "Cruel Arrow" (100), **Rotom** sv01-069 "Linear Attack" (20), Paldean Tauros "Aqua Dive" (60), Bellibolt ex / Kilowattrel (50). **Stays LOUD:** Umbreon "Feint Attack" ("This attack's damage isn't affected by Weakness or Resistance" — a different tail, and a no-W/R-EVEN-on-the-Active flag for later), Pawmot ex "Levin Strike" (a leading discard cost), Magikarp "Jump" (a leading coin flip). New `ANY_SNIPE_DECK` (both setup Actives pinned to a `fix-lightning-weak` body — ×2 Lightning, 130 HP — the deterministic W/R target). **No web change.** engineVersion 0.38.0 → **0.39.0**; 1683 → **1693** tests, `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH, and NO regression to the shared machinery** — the `placeSnipe` `next` threading is byte-identical for every opponentBench consumer (their candidates are Bench-only and `validateChoice` enforces picks ⊆ candidates, so `refs.some(active)` is always false → `next === state`); the `if (targeted.size === 0) return next` change is the essential fix (returning `state` there would have discarded an auto-taken Active snipe); `validateChoice` is unchanged and still rejects an Active pick on a Bench-only prompt (snipe.test.ts green). **ONE MED FOUND AND FIXED:** `snipeActive` dropped the attacker's `damageBonusBeforeWR` (Vitality Band's "+10 to your opponent's Active, before W/R") — which DOES apply to an Active snipe (that is precisely the case the bonus targets); my draft comment "consistent with the Bench arm, which also omits it" was a false equivalence (the Bench correctly omits it because Vitality Band never boosts Bench). Since these cards have no main `damage` field, the bonus was applied NOWHERE — Fezandipiti + Vitality Band on the Active would deal 100, rules 110. Fixed by adding the bonus into the pre-W/R term and emitting the `bonus` event field, pinned by a new Vitality-Band test asserting `(20 + 10) × 2 = 60` (the bonus inside the weakness multiply). **Recorded, NOT fixed (reviewer LOW):** `snipeActive`'s Resistance application, the `− reduction` term, and the two `Math.max(0, …)` floors are not INDEPENDENTLY pinned (no test gives the Active a Resistance or a reduction passive, none drives the total negative), and the Active-snipe KO → prize flow is untested — all a verbatim copy of attack.ts's own tested pipeline (Weakness order is pinned) and the benched arm's tested reduction, so a real bug is unlikely; left analysis-only rather than adding three fixtures for a LOW. *Why: resume item #2, the highest-yield remaining single mechanism (~6 cards) and the natural generalization of the benched-snipe op — the conditional-W/R is the one genuinely new idea, and the rules made it unambiguous (§8.5). Extended the existing `damageChosen` + `placeSnipe` rather than a parallel op, keeping the four other snipe families on the same machinery. `snipeActive` duplicates attack.ts's §8.5 math rather than sharing a helper — a small faithful copy, acceptable until a third consumer justifies extraction.* (2026-07-23) |
| D60 | The authoritative per-viewer redactor + wire + read-only online board (P4 online, increment 1c, engine 0.40.0) | The match now CROSSES THE WIRE and RENDERS. The engine gains the PURE **`redactGame(state, seat) → RedactedGame`** (`packages/engine/src/redact.ts`): the per-viewer hidden-information filter the lobby DO runs server-side, ONCE PER CONNECTED SOCKET, so **no full `GameState` (opponent hand / deck order / face-down prizes / face-down setup placements) ever crosses the network**. **`RedactedGame`** is a `@luminous/schema` wire type (`match/redacted.ts`), Zod SOURCE-OF-TRUTH (types inferred, the catalog `Card` pattern — no hand-written twin to drift), **viewer-relative** ("you"/"opponent", so a payload only ever carries the recipient's full side + the opponent's redacted side): opponent hand a COUNT of positional-id backs (id `opponent-hand-N`, `cardId:"hidden"` — never the engine uid, which is stable across zones and would let a client track a hidden card), prizes + decks COUNTS, discards + Stadium PUBLIC (§2/§7.3), in-play redacted to anonymous backs during setup (**no `battle` row → no printed-HP leak**), and the mid-effect **prompt STRIPPED** (`RedactedDecision.effectChoose` carries counts only). The engine RESTATES the web app's playmat redaction against this neutral shape (no image URLs — the client builds them from `cardId`, keeping the asset scheme client-side; no engine imports on the far side). Its subtle phase derivation — the exhaustive `phaseViewOf` switch (turn owner / who-must-act / parked interrupt / outcome) + `koParkActiveSeat`/`turnOwnerOf`/`preferViewer`/`PendingDecision`/`inSetup` — was **MOVED out of the web projection into the engine** (`phaseView.ts`) so the projection AND the redactor share ONE source (the one drift-prone piece; the board redaction stays restated per surface — playmat vs wire — each independently tested). The DO broadcasts each socket its own view on the **in-game FLIP** (`broadcastMatch`, a per-viewer fan-out gated on the countdown→in-game transition so no later in-match alarm re-broadcasts) and on **RECONNECT** (`sendMatchIfInGame` in the three hello branches — a reseat preserves in-game so the record survives `abandonsMatch`), keyed by the pure `matchSeatOf(snapshot, playerId)` (`slotOf` → `SEAT_OF_SLOT`: host→p1, guest→p2). `{kind:"match", game}` joins `LobbyMessage`/`LobbyServerMessage` + `lobbyServerMessageSchema` with the exactness proof. Client: `useLobby` gains a `match` state (set on the frame; CLEARED on any `state` with `phase !== "in-game"` so a rematch in the same lobby can't show a stale board); a new **`OnlineMatch`** feeds the SAME `PlaymatView` a `projectionFromRedacted(game)` (the wire→`GameProjection` adapter, in `projection.ts`) in place of `MatchPlaceholder` — **READ-ONLY** (no-op drag/pass, deny-all placement, empty log) until increment 2 drives turns. **The wire round-trip is PINNED to reconstruct EXACTLY the local `projectGameState` board** (`projectionFromRedacted(redactGame(state, seat))` `toEqual` `projectGameState(state, seat)` for turn:action + setup) — the guard against the two redactors drifting. engineVersion 0.39.0 → **0.40.0**; 1702 → **1719** tests, `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH — the reviewer could construct no input that leaks opponent-private data or crashes the redactor;** `phaseViewOf` verified byte-identical to the pre-move code, the DO fan-out targets the correct seat per socket (unseated sockets skipped), the flip-gate fires exactly once, reconnect preserves the record, and `redactedGameSchema` matches the engine output field-for-field. **THREE LOWs RECORDED, not fixed:** (L1) the client channel does no Zod on inbound frames — a malformed `match` frame would throw at render; NOT opponent-exploitable (only the trusted DO sends server frames, and it always builds schema-valid frames) and consistent with how `state`/`reject` are already trusted — validating only `match` would be inconsistent; (L2) seat identity is bearer-only (a socket that knows another seat's `playerId` receives that seat's view) — the PRE-EXISTING lobby auth model, unchanged by this diff, and the redaction is correct for the bound identity; (L3) `OnlineMatch` lights the turn glow on "you" during setup (activePlayer null) — cosmetic, the single-viewer analog of LocalMatch's per-seat glow. *Why: the redactor is the load-bearing new code P4 needs — the only prior redactor was client-side + playmat-coupled, unusable by a DO. Sharing `phaseViewOf` (rather than duplicating the ~90-line phase switch) removes the one genuinely drift-prone piece, and the round-trip equality test makes the remaining board-redaction duplication safe by construction. Viewer-relative (not an absolute-seat map) makes the leak-free property structural — a payload has nowhere to put both sides' hidden zones. The effect prompt + interactive actions are deliberately increment 2's job; 1c delivers the read surface, not a rule change.* (2026-07-23) |

| D61 | The authoritative action transport + the online setup flow (P4 online, increment 2a, engine 0.40.0) | The online match becomes INTERACTIVE — setup + basic turn drags + pass now DRIVE the authoritative server game. A new **`{kind:"action", action}`** client→server frame (schema, added to `LobbyMessage`/`lobbyClientMessageSchema`) carries an engine `GameAction`; its `wireActionSchema` is deliberately **`z.looseObject({type})`** (loosely typed, KEEPING the action's fields) because the DO does NOT trust it — a full `GameAction` Zod mirror would duplicate the engine's single source of truth, and `applyAction`'s never-throw totality (D14, the wire-decode contract) is the real validator; the one `GameAction → WireAction` cast at the client send boundary is the honest cost of that looseness. The DO's new **`case "action"`** handler binds the action to the SOCKET's own seat via `matchSeatOf` (host→p1/guest→p2) — **never the client-claimed `action.seat`, so a client can't act for its opponent** — gated to a seated player in an in-game lobby with a persisted record, runs the pure **`applyMatchAction(record, seat, action)`** (persist-BEFORE-broadcast, crash-safe), and re-fans each socket its own redacted view (reuse `broadcastMatch`). `applyMatchAction` gates to **`SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS`** — the 2a-drivable subset (setup + the moveToAction turn drags + endTurn) — so a crafted attack/decision frame can't push the match into a `ko:*`/`effect:choose` phase the 2a HUD renders no control for (the review's soft-lock LOW, fixed; with the fixed basic-only decks this blocks nothing a legit client sends). **`RedactedGame` gained a redacted `phase`** (the action-routing discriminant: setup:chooseFirst / drawExtra`{owed}` / place`{ready}` / turn:action / ko`{count}` / effect:choose / gameOver — only NON-sensitive fields, the effect prompt still stripped, `owed` the viewer's OWN); `pendingDecision` was FOLDED INTO it and derived client-side (`decisionFromPhase`), preserving the round-trip equality with `projectGameState`. **`moveToAction` + `placement.ts` were refactored to a transport-agnostic context** (`phaseKind` + `isViewerTurn` + `isReady`) so local play (full `Phase`) AND online play (redacted phase) drive ONE tested drag router / drop gate — behavior-identical (the placement.test.ts lockstep unchanged, verified via the passing suite). Client: **`useLobby.sendAction`**; a new **`OnlineHud`** (chooseFirst / mulligan-draw / place-ready panels, gated on `waitingOn === "you"`, dispatching setup actions with the client's own seat); **`OnlineMatch`** now wires drags → `moveToAction` → `sendAction`, pass → `endTurn`, and the shared placement predicate, in place of the 1c read-only stubs. engine UNCHANGED (0.40.0 — a transport/read surface, not a rule change); 1719 → **1731** tests, `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH, NO MED** — the seat-binding blocks acting-for-opponent (forge test), the three gates block unseated / not-in-game / no-record, `applyAction`'s totality holds against hostile payloads (every wire-reachable handler defensively validates the fields it reads), persist-before-broadcast + the DO input gate prevent desync, the redacted phase leaks nothing, and the refactors are logically identical. **TWO LOWs:** (L1, recorded) the DO `case "action"` boundary has no direct test — only the pure `applyMatchAction` does (the DO-shell-untested pattern; the boundary mirrors the tested `intent` handler); (L2, FIXED) the `SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS` allowlist above. *Why: the transport is the load-bearing new mechanism P4 needs, and setup is the first REACHABLE surface (the game starts at setup:chooseFirst — without it the online board froze there in 1c). Sharing `moveToAction`/`placementPredicateFor` rather than a parallel online router keeps the drag semantics in ONE tested place; the loose wire action + total `applyAction` is the engine's own intended design (it built the never-throw contract FOR the wire). The turn:action HUD (attacks/abilities/trainers/retreat) + the ko/effect dialogs are 2b — they need full-state playability data the projection deliberately doesn't carry, plus the redacted effect prompt.* (2026-07-23) |
| D62 | The online attack + KO loop; splitting 2b (P4 online, increment 2b-i, engine 0.40.0) | **2b was too big for one increment** — the local turn HUD reads the full `GameState` (attack payability, retreat cost, ability/trainer legality), all unsafe over a projection — so it's **SPLIT**: 2b-i lands the attack/KO loop (the online match now plays TO A WIN with the fixed basic decks); 2b-ii is retreat + abilities/trainers; 2b-iii is the effect:choose prompt+dialog + the per-recipient event log + the rejection pill. **Wire (2b-i):** `RedactedPhase.turn:action` gained an **`attacks`** field — a new `@luminous/schema` **`RedactedAttack`** (`index`/`name`/`cost`/`damage`/`playable`), the Zod source-of-truth. The engine's `redactPhase` fills it via a new **`redactedAttacksOf(state, viewerSeat, turnSeat)`**: the viewer's OWN Active's printed attacks, each with a server-computed **`playable`** that folds §8.2 payability (under the Stadium's continuous cost — `effectiveAttackCost` + `providedEnergy` + `costMet`), the §4 first-turn ban and the §12 immobilize gate into ONE boolean — EXACTLY the local `TurnPanel`'s `disabled`, so the online client adds no engine logic. Populated **only for the turn owner** (`viewerSeat === phase.seat`; the opponent's view of the same `turn:action` carries `[]`), **own Active only** (a face-up public zone), so **nothing hidden leaves the DO** — the payability is derived from information the opponent could compute anyway, but is withheld from them regardless. **API DO:** `SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS` grew by **`attack`/`takePrizes`/`promote`** — with the fixed **pure-damage** decks (no status/abilities) the loop attack → ko:takePrizes → ko:promote never reaches `effect:choose`, so no soft-lock; `resolveEffect`/`retreat`/`useAbility` stay blocked (2b-ii/iii grow the set as their HUD lands). **Client:** `OnlineHud` gained an **AttackPanel** (bottom-right, mirroring the local `TurnPanel`) and the **ko:takePrizes / ko:promote dialogs** (centered overlays) — the ko dialogs need **NO new wire data** (the prize count rides `RedactedPhase.ko:takePrizes.count`, the viewer's own prizes-count/Bench ride the already-public board), the same reason the LOCAL PrizeDialog/PromoteDialog read them straight off the state. **`EnergyDots` extracted** to a shared `src/features/game/EnergyDots.tsx` (GameHud + OnlineHud, one source, so the cost pips can't drift). The `turn:action` round-trip equality (`projectionFromRedacted(redactGame(…)) ≡ projectGameState(…)`) still holds — `attacks` never enters the `GameProjection` (`decisionFromPhase` keeps `turn:action`→null). engine UNCHANGED (0.40.0 — a transport/read surface, not a rule change); 1731 → **1740** tests (4 engine attack-payability cases pinning the ban/immobilize/opponent-empty folds, 5 OnlineHud DOM cases), `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH, NO MED** — the server-side payability faithfully mirrors the local `TurnPanel`'s `disabled`, leaks nothing (turn-owner + own-Active + viewer-public), the action shapes match the engine handlers, the prize/bench indices align 1:1 with the index-stable redaction, and the round-trip equality holds. **FIVE LOWs — 2 FIXED** (an orphaned EnergyDots doc comment in GameHud; the ko `Dialog` is now a native `<dialog>` — top-layer + focus-trap, matching the local HudDialog), **3 RECORDED** (deliberate): the OnlineHud ko/attack panels restate GameHud's markup — the SAME restate-per-surface choice as D60's board redactor (the two read different shapes, full `GameState` vs the redacted wire, so only the shape-neutral `EnergyDots` was extracted); `AttackPanel` omits the §12 immobilize/confusion notes (harmless with the pure-damage fixed decks — 2b-ii's immobilize surface, where the wire already carries `battle.conditions`); and the allow-`attack` no-soft-lock guarantee is PLAN ORDERING not structural (the SEQUENCING CONSTRAINT is now loud in `match.ts` — real decks/increment 3 must not precede the 2b-iii effect dialog). **Still unverified:** the DO `case "action"` boundary has no direct test for the new types (only the pure `applyMatchAction`/allowlist do); a two-client browser run of the loop. *Why: the attack + KO loop is the smallest sub-slice of 2b that makes the online game PLAYABLE TO A WIN with the current fixed decks — the natural "prove the whole authoritative loop end-to-end" milestone — and it needs nothing the effect system carries, so it lands cleanly ahead of the heavier retreat/ability/trainer/effect surfaces. Computing `playable` server-side (rather than shipping payability primitives to the client) keeps the engine's cost/immobilize/ban rules in ONE place; the ko dialogs falling out for free (no new wire) confirms the redacted phase + board already carry what a decision needs. Splitting 2b follows the same "was Slice 1, now split" pattern the plan already used, keeping each increment one reviewable commit.* (2026-07-23) |
| D63 | The online retreat; scoping 2b-ii to retreat alone (P4 online, increment 2b-ii, engine 0.40.0) | The online turn HUD can now RETREAT — the match plays attack → KO-loop → retreat with the fixed basic decks. **2b-ii was SCOPED TO RETREAT ALONE** (the plan grouped it as "retreat + abilities/trainers"): the fixed decks (Sneasel/Mareep + Energy) have no abilities and no non-Stadium trainers, so a wire surface for those can't be exercised end-to-end against a real online game — building it now would be speculative and untested, against the "only wire what's driven" discipline the redactor has followed since D60. So **abilities + non-Stadium trainers are CUT and DEFERRED to increment 3** (real deck selection), where a real deck can drive them. **Wire:** `RedactedPhase.turn:action` gained a **`retreat`** field — a new `@luminous/schema` **`RedactedRetreat`** (`cost`/`can`), Zod source-of-truth, NULLABLE (null for the non-acting viewer, mirroring 2b-i's `attacks: []`). The engine's `redactPhase` fills it via a new **`redactedRetreatOf(state, viewerSeat, turnSeat)`**: the viewer's OWN Active's CONTINUOUS retreat cost (§7.3 — Beach Court discounts Basics) + a server-computed **`can`** folding the §12 immobilize gate, the once-per-turn `retreated` allowance, a non-empty Bench (something to promote to) and enough attached energy to pay the cost into ONE boolean — EXACTLY the local `TurnPanel`'s `canRetreat` (§11), so the online client adds no engine logic. Populated **only for the turn owner** (`viewerSeat === phase.seat`; null for the non-acting viewer), **own Active only** — a face-up public zone — so **nothing hidden leaves the DO** (the energies to discard and the Bench to promote to were already public on the board; only the cost was server-derived). **API DO:** `SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS` grew by **`retreat`** — and unlike `attack`, retreat carries NO sequencing caveat: the engine's `retreat` handler (`turn.ts:333`) only discards energy + promotes a Bench Pokémon, returning `ok(next)` with the phase UNCHANGED, so it can never park at `effect:choose` and is soft-lock-safe with ANY deck (verified by reading the handler); it also validates every field defensively (`discardEnergy` an array of exactly `cost` attached uids, `isBenchIndex(promoteBenchIndex)` blocking the string-concat bench-wipe, attachment membership), so a crafted frame rejects, never crashes/soft-locks. **Client:** `OnlineHud`'s AttackPanel gained a **Retreat (cost) button** (disabled off `retreat.can`) and a **`RetreatDialog`** — the local HUD's RetreatDialog restated over the redacted wire shape (pick `cost` attached energies + a Bench Pokémon → dispatch `{type:"retreat", seat, discardEnergy, promoteBenchIndex}`), the SAME restate-per-surface choice as D60/D62 (the two read different shapes, full `GameState` vs the redacted wire). The `Dialog` helper gained an optional `onDismiss` so retreat is DISMISSABLE (Escape/Cancel close it — retreat is optional) while the ko:* decisions stay mandatory (Escape swallowed). The `turn:action` round-trip equality (`projectionFromRedacted(redactGame(…)) ≡ projectGameState(…)`) still holds — `retreat` never enters the `GameProjection` (`decisionFromPhase` keeps `turn:action`→null). engine UNCHANGED (0.40.0 — a transport/read surface, not a rule change); 1740 → **1747** tests (5 engine retreat-gate cases pinning the immobilize/allowance/bench/energy folds + the non-actor null, 2 OnlineHud DOM cases for the dialog dispatch + the disabled gate), `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO defects at HIGH, MED, or LOW** — `can` is field-for-field identical to the local `canRetreat`, `redactedRetreatOf` is null for the non-actor (no leak), the handler is soft-lock-safe (phase-unchanged) and totally validating, the dispatched `discardEnergy` uids are engine uids (`redactedCardOf.id`) matching the handler's `active.energy.includes` check, the bench indices align 1:1 with the index-stable redaction, and the free-retreat (cost 0) path is accepted by both `can` and the handler; the reviewer independently ran the 91 affected tests green. *Why: retreat is the one turn:action beyond attack the fixed decks can actually reach and test, so it lands cleanly now; abilities/trainers need cards the fixed decks lack, so wiring them now would be untested speculation — deferring them to real decks keeps each increment driven-and-verified. Transient, unlike the terminal `{kind:"reject", to, reason}`: it aims at the socket that sent the action (no `to`) and is not terminal. **API:** `applyMatchAction` now returns a discriminated **`MatchActionOutcome`** (`{ok:true,record} | {ok:false,reason}`) instead of `MatchRecord | null` — the reason is a generic string for an off-allowlist type (no engine message to quote) and the ENGINE's own `error.message` for an illegal move, which is safe to surface because it describes the ACTOR's own move/phase, never the opponent's hidden zones (`match.test.ts` updated to the new shape + a reason assertion). `OnlineMatch` renders the SHARED **`TransientErrorPill`** (the one the /decks library and /play page already use) with the nonce+TTL dismiss logic lifted verbatim from `GamePage`. engine UNCHANGED; 1774 → **1777** tests (wire schema accept/refuse for the new frame, `applyMatchAction` reason, 3 OnlineMatch DOM pill cases — render under `MemoryRouter` since PlaymatView's BackToMenu navigates), `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH** — the info-leak check is clean: I traced every engine `err(...)` site; each message that interpolates a card NAME first confirms the uid is in the ACTOR's own hand or names a public in-play Pokémon (attacker/defender/evolve target), none resolves a name from an opponent's hand/deck/prize; `validateChoice` echoes only the client-supplied uid, never the legal-candidate set; `resolveEffect`'s `WRONG_SEAT` gate (names a seat, not data) precedes candidate validation; and delivery is actor-only (`this.send(ws, …)`, no broadcast — an unseated/observer socket returns before `applyMatchAction`). **1 MED — FIXED:** a nonce collision — `useLobby` restarts its per-error nonce at 1 after clearing `actionError` on an accepted `match`, but `OnlineMatch`'s `dismissedNonce` never cleared, so the sequence reject→dismiss→accept→reject-again collided (`nonce === dismissedNonce`) and the second pill silently didn't render (the very silent-drop this slice closes); Returning a `reason` from `applyMatchAction` (rather than a bare null) is the minimum needed to give the pill something to say, and the engine's own message is both the most useful text and safe (it is about the actor's own action). So there is nothing to redact, only to RELABEL, and the relabel is a pure client render step over the viewer's own absolute `game.seat`. **Wire:** the `{kind:"match"}` frame grew **`log: SeatLogEntry[]`** (`LobbyMessage` in types.ts + the `matchMessageSchema` Zod mirror; the `…MatchesType` exactness proofs still hold). It rides the FRAME, NOT `RedactedGame`, and deliberately: the log ACCUMULATES across the whole game and cannot be rebuilt from a single snapshot's `state` (which `redactGame(state, seat)` is a pure function of), so putting it on `RedactedGame` would give that type a field its own builder can't fill — so **`RedactedGame` / `redactGame` / the `projectionFromRedacted ≡ projectGameState` round-trip pins are UNTOUCHED** (this is a transport/read surface, no engine rule change). **API:** `MatchRecord` grew **`startedAt`** (epoch ms — the "+MM:SS" elapsed origin), **`names`** (host→p1 / guest→p2 display names for the log's system rows, from the lobby snapshot at handoff) and **`log`**; **`applyMatchAction` takes a `now` arg** — the pure reducer stays CLOCKLESS (the `useLocalGame` hostReducer pattern: the impurity, the wall clock, rides IN on the call), and the DO stamps `Date.now()` once per action — and APPENDS the accepted events' rows (append-only; seed/startedAt/names ride over). The DO's `handoffToMatch(snapshot)` seeds startedAt/names + the initial log from createGame's own events at +00:00; `broadcastMatch` sends `record.log` whole to every socket (so a reconnect gets the full backlog for free). **Client:** `useLobby` gained a `matchLog` state (set with `match` on the frame, cleared with it on leaving in-game — the abandon/rematch reset); `OnlineMatch` renders `viewLogEntries(log, game.seat)` into PlaymatView, replacing `NO_LOG`. engine RULES UNCHANGED (0.40.0 — a transport/read surface); 1777 → **1782** tests (wire log accept/refuse/round-trip, a `match.test` log-append + elapsed-stamp + reject-leaves-log-untouched, the engine log suite moved with its viewer-flip case rewritten to a seat-keyed assertion, a web `viewLog` relabel test, an OnlineMatch DOM log-render case), `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH, NO MED** — the headline LEAK property (one identical `record.log` broadcast to both sockets, no per-viewer redaction) HOLDS under a per-arm classification of `formatEvent`: every arm names nothing / a seat label / a COUNT for a hidden zone / a genuinely-PUBLIC in-play card, and the two deliberately-naming events (`HAND_REVEALED`, `CARD_TO_BOTTOM_OF_DECK`) fire only after a public hand reveal (verified at their emit sites), with face-down setup benching staying `POKEMON_PLACED`-unnamed vs mid-game `POKEMON_BENCHED`-named. Seat-keying (`game.seat` is the viewer's own absolute seat, opposite for the two clients), record lifecycle (drop on abandon, no in-game→different-guest path, reconnect resends the leak-safe backlog), purity (fresh log array, `now` threaded, never mutates — the prefix-unchanged test) and the exactness proof / round-trip (RedactedGame untouched) all held. **2 LOWs, both RECORDED (not fixed):** (1) a `MatchRecord` written by pre-slice code (`{seed,state}`) read across THIS deploy lacks `startedAt`/`names`/`log` → NaN elapsed + a `names[…]` TypeError on a system row, and `log:undefined` → a client render throw; NARROW (a match in-flight across a single deploy, dev-only fixed decks), self-heals on abandon+rematch — the acknowledged no-DO-storage-migration gap (carried from 1b), so deferred to increment 3's storage/reconnect hardening rather than bolted on against the "no migration contract yet" posture. (2) the whole log re-broadcasts on every accepted action (O(n²) bandwidth) — the deliberate simplicity tradeoff (a reconnect gets the full backlog for free), flagged for a later delta/paginate pass. **Verifiable in a browser (unlike D64–D67):** the fixed decks emit a real log (shuffles / coin flip / draws / attacks / KOs), so a two-client browser run would exercise the online log end-to-end — the first online surface the fixed decks can drive to completion — but that is not yet done (test-only). *Why: moving `logFromEvents` into the engine (rather than duplicating it, or building rows on the client from redacted events) is the only way the DO and the /play page share ONE formatter — and the engine, not the web, is its home now that it runs server-side (the D60 redactor precedent). Broadcasting the log seat-keyed + identical to both — rather than redacting it per viewer like the board — is correct BECAUSE the log is already leak-safe: a per-viewer log would be pointless work and would falsely imply the log carries hidden info. Putting it on the FRAME rather than `RedactedGame` keeps the redactor a pure function of state and its round-trip pins intact. Threading `now` into `applyMatchAction` keeps the authoritative reducer pure/replayable (the D14 contract) — the DO owns the clock, as it owns the seed. Keeping the render `LogEntry` in the playmat preserves its zero-import isolation; only the tiny wire `SeatLogEntry` is shared.* (2026-07-24) |
| D69 | Real deck selection + authenticating the lobby WS + seat authentication; splitting increment 3 (P4 online, increment 3a + 3a-sec) | The lobby → engine handoff now loads each player's OWN chosen deck from D1 instead of the hardcoded `FIXED_MATCH_DECKS` — the first authenticated surface in the otherwise account-less lobby — and the review's one MED (a bearer-only-seat HAND leak) was closed in the same session as 3a-sec. **Increment 3 was too big for one slice, so it is SPLIT** (the 2b→i/ii/iii discipline): **3a** = real deck resolution + the match-start failure surface (this); **3a-sec** = seat authentication (folded in — the security follow-up to 3a); **3b** = the online ability + non-Stadium trainer HUD (the affordances real decks make reachable); **3c+** = reconnect/timeouts, concede/rematch, spectate. **The auth boundary — chosen with the user ("authenticate the WS", over "DO reads by deckId unauthenticated" and "signed deck reference"):** the lobby stays account-less for SEAT identity (players are anonymous browser-minted ids), but the deck read must be OWNER-SCOPED or a crafted `select-deck` naming another user's deck id would let the DO load — and, via the redacted views, let a player infer — someone else's private deck. The account-less lobby had NO authenticated identity to scope against (the WS forwarded the `session` cookie but the DO ignored it), so **the DO now resolves that forwarded cookie at the `/ws` upgrade** — new read-only **`resolveUserIdFromCookie`** (unlike `requireUser` it 401s nothing, clears no cookie, deletes no expired row — connecting a lobby socket must not mutate auth state; anonymous / no-cookie is a D1-free null) — and stashes the userId on the **socket attachment** (`attachUserId`, set before the hello, each of playerId/userId preserving the other). **userId is DO-only** — it never joins the broadcast `LobbySnapshot` (`PlayerState` has no such field), so it can't leak to the opponent. At handoff **`loadPlayerDeck(db, deckId, userId)`** reads `WHERE decks.id = <client deckId> AND decks.userId = <the socket's authenticated userId>` (via `userIdOf(playerId)` off the live socket), so someone else's deck reads the same as a missing one (the deck-routes 404-as-missing, no existence leak) and a null userId (anonymous) is refused before any query; the pure `deckIdsFromRows` flattens the compressed `(cardId, count)` rows to the flat 60-id list `createGame` wants. **Contents come from D1, never the client** (only the opaque `deckId` crosses the wire), so a client cannot forge an illegal deck; `startMatch`/`createGame` stays the authoritative validator (60 + a Basic — the persistence layer enforces neither). **The failure surface (the load-failure wedge from 1b, finally closed):** `handoffToMatch` now returns **`{ok} | {ok:false, reason}`**, the alarm flips to in-game ONLY on success, and on failure **`resetAfterFailedStart`** un-readies both seats → back to `selecting` (un-readying is what BREAKS the countdown re-fire loop; leaving it in `countdown` past its deadline would re-fire the failing handoff every alarm) and broadcasts a new **`{kind:"match-error", reason}`** server frame (added to `LobbyMessage` + `lobbyServerMessageSchema`, the exactness proof holds; server-only) to BOTH players — no MatchRecord was written, so a retry re-attempts cleanly. Client: **`useLobby.matchError`** shows it on the versus screen, cleared by the next lobby state change (it arrives right AFTER the reset-to-selecting `state` frame, so it survives that frame's clear). engine UNCHANGED (a transport/auth surface); 1782 → **1787** tests (`deckIdsFromRows` + the `loadPlayerDeck` auth guards proven to short-circuit before any query, the attachment userId round-trip / set-order, `resetAfterFailedStart`, the match-error wire accept/refuse; the `FIXED_MATCH_DECKS` tests removed), `bun run check` green. **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH** — all five ranked properties HOLD: deck-ownership auth (a socket's `userId` only ever comes from a valid `session` cookie, so the owner-scoped read can only load a deck of the account whose token you hold — a crafted `deckId` reads as missing, anonymous/null-deck refused pre-query), userId confidentiality (grep-confirmed absent from every schema, read only in `userIdOf`, never sent), session resolution (read-only, rejects forged/expired/no-cookie), the failure surface (in-game unreachable without a MatchRecord — the 1b wedge is closed — no alarm re-fire loop, retry unblocked), and no regressions (no dangling `FIXED_MATCH_*`). **1 MED — FIXED in-session as 3a-sec (a PRE-EXISTING leak 3a ELEVATED): the bearer-only-seat HAND leak.** Every `state` frame broadcasts the seats' PUBLIC `playerId`s (the D60 L2 gap); a socket that replayed one was reseated by `helloDecision` (no account check) and handed that seat's redacted HAND via `sendMatchIfInGame` (the deck stays a count, so the decklist itself did NOT leak). Harmless with the fixed decks; a real competitive leak once the hand is from a real deck. **Fixed** by a new pure **`seatClaimAllowed(boundUserId, userId)`** (`= boundUserId === null || boundUserId === userId`) + a DO-only **`SEAT_USERS_KEY`** (`playerId → account`, off the wire): the `hello` handler binds a playerId to its account on the FIRST authenticated claim, and refuses any later hello replaying that playerId whose socket `userId` doesn't match — enforcing ONLY once the seat is account-bound, so an anonymous seat stays bearer-only (safe — it can't load a real deck, so never reaches an in-game hand) and the anonymous→authenticated upgrade isn't self-locked. **A second read-only review (scoped to the fix) confirmed the leak CLOSED** — no `hello` path reaches a match emitter for a seat bound to a different account, the other emitters redact per `attachedPlayer` (set only by a gated hello), and an in-game seat is ALWAYS bound (handoff requires both authed) — **with no legit flow broken** (ordinary reconnect + the anonymous→authenticated upgrade both pass; `deleteAll` clears the map on empty-lobby). **3 LOWs, all BY-DESIGN, recorded:** a player who LOSES their session mid-lobby can't rejoin their own bound seat (the intended boundary; low-probability — 30-day sessions, and a cookie that drops on reconnect was absent on the initial claim too, so the seat was never bound); a same-tab account SWITCH is locked out for the lobby's lifetime (recoverable in a new tab, fresh playerId); `SEAT_USERS_KEY` grows unpruned across repeated guest-takeovers (bounded, TTL-cleared). **2 LOWs — RECORDED:** (a) `userIdOf` returns the first same-`playerId` live socket regardless of userId, so two live sockets with different userIds (a mid-lobby login change) can pick the account that doesn't own the `deckId` → a spurious `match-error` (recoverable; fix = prefer a non-null-userId socket); (b) `match-error` broadcasts the actor-specific reason to BOTH players — a trivial sign-in-state disclosure (the name is already public; fix = actor-only specific reason, generic notice to the other). **Non-defects confirmed:** cookie forwarding preserves headers to the DO (verify LIVE — a non-surviving cookie would resolve every authed user anonymous and block ALL matches); attachment set-order/hibernation; no alarm socket-set race (DO input-gating); empty/oversized decks fail safe through `startMatch`. **3b GAP (recorded, loud):** with real decks, abilities + non-Stadium trainers (Supporters/Items) are now REACHABLE, but the online HUD has no affordance for them (`useAbility` stays off `SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS`; only the Stadium DRAG sends `playTrainer`), so a deck that NEEDS one to progress can STALL — a missing affordance, not an engine soft-lock, and the whole of 3b. **Still unverified:** a real browser two-client run confirming the SameSite=None `session` cookie reaches the DO on the cross-origin WS handshake (the code forwards `c.req.raw`; browser behavior is standard but untested here). *Why: owner-scoping the read is the only safe way to turn a client-supplied `deckId` into cards, and the account-less lobby had nothing to scope against — so authenticating the WS (additively: anonymous lobbies still work, a userId just attaches when a valid cookie rides the upgrade) is the minimum that makes the read correct, and resolving the FORWARDED cookie in the DO (rather than reconstructing the upgrade request with a trusted header, or a signed deck reference) keeps the change to one read at /ws with no new token machinery. Reading contents from D1 (not the client) is what makes the deck server-authoritative — a client sends only an id it can't parlay into an illegal list. Returning a result from `handoffToMatch` and only flipping on success finally closes the 1b wedge with a real player-facing reason instead of a silent console.error. Splitting 3 keeps 3a a single reviewable commit and defers the ability/trainer HUD — which needs its own wire surface — to 3b, exactly the "was too big, now split" discipline.* (2026-07-24) |

| D70 | The online ability + non-Stadium trainer HUD (P4 online, increment 3b, engine 0.40.0) | The online turn HUD now offers activated Abilities + non-Stadium Trainers (Items/Supporters), closing the loud 3b GAP D69 recorded: real decks (3a) made both reachable, but the online HUD had no button for either (`useAbility` off the allowlist; only the Stadium DRAG sent `playTrainer`), so a deck needing one could STALL. **The pattern is 2b-i/ii's, unchanged:** the local HUD reads playability off the FULL `GameState` (the acknowledged P4 blocker — `programPlayable`/`conditionHolds`/`handCostUnmet` read the Stadium OWNER, prize counts and the own hand, none of which the wire carries whole), so rather than recompute client-side over the redacted view (which the D69/index.ts caveat says can't see the Stadium owner and lacks the fine card facets), the redactor computes the whole thing SERVER-SIDE and ships only the result — exactly as `redactedAttacksOf`/`redactedRetreatOf` fold payability into a `playable`/`can` boolean. **Wire:** `RedactedPhase.turn:action` gained **`abilities: RedactedAbility[]`** (`target` the seatless `PokemonTarget` `useAbility` dispatches back / `abilityName` / `label` / `disabled` / `reason: string \| null`) and **`trainers: RedactedTrainer[]`** (`uid` the `playTrainer` dispatches back / `name` / `disabled` / `reason`). `RedactedAbility.target` reuses a NEW standalone **`redactedPokemonTargetSchema`** (the seatless spot union = the engine's `PokemonTarget` = `PokemonRef.spot`), kept separate from `RedactedPokemonRef` rather than shared because extracting the union and referencing it FROM `RedactedPokemonRef.spot` broke Zod's discriminated-union inference for that ref (the 2b-iii round-trip machinery depends on it) — a low-risk duplication over disturbing a working type. **Engine:** `redactPhase`'s `turn:action` arm fills them via new **`redactedAbilitiesOf(state, viewerSeat, turnSeat)`** and **`redactedTrainersOf(...)`** (redact.ts), which mirror GameHud's `usableAbilities`/`playableTrainers` field-for-field — the §9 Active-only + the §9/§15.J once-per-turn `abilitiesUsed` gate; the §4 first-turn + §7.2 one-Supporter timing; the printed `trainerPlayableIf` board gate (`conditionHolds`); the §7.5 hand cost (`handCostUnmet`, the played uid excluded as the printed "OTHER cards"); and the would-only-whiff `programPlayable` gate — folded into ONE `disabled` boolean with the greyed-row `reason` (the printed clause via `conditionNote`/`handCostAction`, else "No legal target", else null when self-evident/enabled). **The leak posture:** both are emitted to the ACTING viewer ONLY (`viewerSeat !== turnSeat` ⟹ [], like attacks), and everything the folds read is the actor's OWN public board + OWN hand (already face-up on their own wire), so nothing hidden leaves the DO and the opponent's view of the same turn:action carries none — pinned by "non-acting viewer gets []" tests for both. **SCOPED — two exclusions from `redactedTrainersOf`:** STADIUMS (they play by the 2a `moveToAction` board drag onto the shared slot — a button here would double the affordance) and **RARE CANDY (DEFERRED)** — it is a Trainer that EVOLVES via its own `rareCandy` action + a two-step Basic/Stage-2 dialog reading `rareCandyOptions` off the full state, its own wire surface, so it stays a follow-up sub-slice; omitting it from the wire list means no dead button is offered, and a Rare-Candy-dependent deck can still stall on that ONE play (a narrower, documented version of the gap 3b closed). **Round-trip untouched:** neither list enters the playmat projection (`decisionFromPhase` keeps `turn:action`→null, `projectionFromRedacted` reads only board/piles/phase-decision), so `projectionFromRedacted ≡ projectGameState` still holds — pinned with a POPULATED turn:action (a real Chien-Pao ex ability board), the same guard 2b-i/ii used for attacks/retreat. **Client:** `OnlineHud`'s AttackPanel gained the Abilities + Trainers sections restated from GameHud over the wire shape (the D60/D62/D63 restate-per-surface choice — the reason on the LI `title` + an sr-only twin since a disabled button leaves the tab order, the disabled greying), dispatching `useAbility`/`playTrainer`. **API DO:** `SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS` += `useAbility` (`playTrainer` was already on for the Stadium drag); soft-lock-safe like `attack`/`resolveEffect` — a crafted `useAbility`/`playTrainer` off its phase is rejected by the engine's own `turnGate`, and one that PARKS at effect:choose drives to completion because every prompt kind is dialoged (the 2b-iii invariant, the reason admitting a parking play is safe). Rare Candy stays OFF the set. engine RULES UNCHANGED (0.40.0 — a transport/read surface, not a rule change); 1790 → **1803** tests (redact abilities/trainers passthrough + actor-gate + activeOnly-bench greying + dedup + §4 supporter-ban + Stadium/RareCandy exclusion, a populated-turn:action round-trip for both viewers, 3 OnlineHud DOM dispatch/disabled/reason cases, the match.test allowlist update — useAbility now supported + soft-lock-safe off-phase, rareCandy still refused), `bun run check` green. **Still unverified:** a two-client browser run with an ability/trainer deck — the surface is test-only (the prior fixed decks had neither; a real-deck lobby would exercise it end-to-end). **Review (1 read-only adversarial reviewer): NO HIGH, NO MED** — all six scrutinized properties HOLD: (LEAK) both folds actor-gate (`viewerSeat !== turnSeat ⟹ []`) and, past that gate, read ONLY `state.players[viewerSeat]` + public counts (`programPlayable`'s only opponent reads are bench/hand LENGTHS; `conditionHolds` reads prize counts + the Stadium owner + own energy; `handCostUnmet` reads the own hand alone), so no hidden opponent content enters any boolean and the opponent's view of the same turn:action carries none — leak posture identical to `redactedAttacksOf`; (FIDELITY) both folds are character-for-character the local `usableAbilities`/`playableTrainers` `disabled`/`reason` expressions, so no greyed-while-legal or lit-while-illegal drift, and the Stadium/RareCandy/Tool exclusions can't drop a plain Item/Supporter; (SOFT-LOCK) the `useAbility` handler rejects off-phase/garbled-target via `turnGate`/`isPokemonTarget`/`resolveOwnPokemon` without throwing, and a parking play resolves since `resolveEffect` is on the set and all 7 prompt kinds are dialoged (exhaustive `EffectChooseDialog` switch); (ROUND-TRIP) `decisionFromPhase` returns null for turn:action and the projection never reads the lists; (DISPATCH) the two dispatches match `UseAbilityAction`/`PlayTrainerAction` + the local dispatches verbatim, the seatless target is structurally the engine `PokemonTarget` re-validated by `isPokemonTarget`, and the DO rebinds the seat; (PURITY) fresh `out` arrays, no state write. **1 LOW — FIXED:** a card with TWO activated abilities shared one `target` literal across its rows (harmless — never mutated, JSON-serialized — but a footgun); now a fresh per-row rebuild (the `redactedRefOf` precedent). **1 LOW — RECORDED (residual, not a defect):** an Ability can be the FIRST online play to reach the `moveEnergy`/`discardEnergy`/`choosePokemonMulti` parks, so any latent bug in those specific dialogs would surface now — the reviewer confirmed all three have components + the switch is exhaustive, so no break, just newly-reachable surface (the browser-run caveat). *Why: computing playability server-side and shipping the boolean is the ONLY safe way to offer these online — the predicates read the Stadium owner + prize arrays + the own hand the wire deliberately withholds, so a client recompute would be under-informed (D69/index.ts said as much), and it is the exact `redactedAttacksOf`/`redactedRetreatOf` precedent so no new mechanism is introduced. Gating to the actor keeps the actor's hand-card names off the opponent's snapshot (they were already the actor's public own-hand, but the surface must not become a second path). Excluding Stadiums avoids doubling the drag affordance; deferring Rare Candy keeps 3b a single reviewable commit — its rareCandyOptions wire surface + two-step dialog is a distinct piece, the "was too big, now split" discipline again. Duplicating the seatless target union rather than sharing it into RedactedPokemonRef trades three lines for not disturbing the working 2b-iii round-trip inference. Keeping the lists off the projection preserves the redactor's round-trip pin.* (2026-07-25) |
| D71 | The online Rare Candy — the last deferred turn affordance (P4 online, increment 3b-ii, engine 0.40.0) | Rare Candy (§7.1) now plays online, closing the ONE narrow gap D70 deliberately carried: `redactedTrainersOf` omitted it (so no dead button was offered) and `rareCandy` stayed off `SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS`, so a Rare-Candy-dependent deck could still stall on that single play. It was deferred because it is the one hand Trainer that is NOT a plain button: it EVOLVES rather than running a program, and its decision is a PAIR (which Basic in play, which Stage 2 in hand) that no single dispatch can carry — the engine cannot express it as an `effect:choose` prompt either (a prompt picks from ONE candidate list), which is exactly why the local HUD gives it a bespoke two-step dialog. **Wire (the shape choice):** `RedactedTrainer` gained **`rareCandy: boolean`** — the row is still a Trainer row, just flagged as the one whose click OPENS A DIALOG — and `RedactedPhase.turn:action` gained **`rareCandy: RedactedRareCandyOption[]`** (`target` the seatless `PokemonTarget` the action dispatches back / `basicUid` / `basicName` / `stage2: {uid, name}[]`). The pairings sit on the PHASE, not on the row, for the same reason the local dialog calls `rareCandyOptions` itself: they do not depend on WHICH copy of Rare Candy is played (the row supplies only the `uid`), so two printings would otherwise duplicate one list. This mirrors the local structure 1:1 (`TrainerOption.rareCandy` boolean + a separately-computed options list), which is what makes the fidelity review a field-for-field comparison. **Engine:** `redactedTrainersOf` stopped skipping Rare Candy and now mirrors the local `playableTrainers` exactly, including its three deliberate scope-outs — `trainerPlayableIf` / the §7.5 hand cost / the would-only-whiff `programPlayable` gate are evaluated ONLY on the Item/Supporter path, because `cardplay.ts` reads them only in `playTrainer`'s Item/Supporter branch and the `rareCandy` action consults none of them (greying on one here would DISAGREE with the engine) — and its §7.1 gate is the lazily-computed `rareCandyOptions(state, seat).length > 0` (the cardPool scan is only worth paying when a Rare Candy is actually in hand), also the local's shape. Rare Candy is also exempt from the `program.trainer === undefined` skip: its registry program is the bare marker `{rareCandy: true}` with no ops. New **`redactedRareCandyOf(state, viewerSeat, turnSeat)`** maps `rareCandyOptions` to the wire shape — the ENGINE stays the legality authority (it folds the §4/§7.1 first-turn ban, the Basic-in-play + came-into-play-this-turn checks, and the Basic→Stage 1→Stage 2 chain BRIDGE, a `state.cardPool` scan the client could never run), so a listed pairing always resolves to a legal action and the client adds no rule logic; the `target` literal is rebuilt fresh (the `redactedRefOf` no-alias purity contract, and D70's LOW fix). **The consistency invariant:** both are computed in the SAME `redactPhase` call on the same immutable state, so a LIT row always has ≥1 pairing and a greyed row always has [] — no dead dialog is reachable. **Leak posture:** identical to the rest of 3b — actor-gated (`viewerSeat !== turnSeat ⟹ []`), and everything named is the actor's own in-play stack top (public) + own hand (already face-up on their own wire). **Client:** `OnlineHud`'s trainer row branches on the flag (`setRareCandyUid` vs `playTrainer`), and a new `RareCandyDialog` restates the local one over the wire shape — the Basic pick pre-selected when there is only one option, the Stage-2 pick, Cancel + a Back that appears only when the Basic pick was a real choice — dispatching `{type:"rareCandy", seat, uid, target, evolutionUid}`. **API DO:** `SUPPORTED_MATCH_ACTIONS` += **`rareCandy`**. Soft-lock-safe on any deck: `turnGate` rejects it off a turn:action / wrong seat without throwing, the handler re-validates every wire value (the target shape + bench index, both hand uids distinct and in hand, the Basic-and-chain link), and on accept it evolves through the SHARED `placeEvolution` — whose only parks are an on-evolve triggered Ability's `effect:choose` (all 7 kinds dialoged since 2b-iii-c) and the evolve-below-HP mid-turn KO (ko:takePrizes/ko:promote, dialoged since 2b-i). Note this introduces NO new park surface: plain `evolve` has been on the allowlist since 2a and reaches the identical `placeEvolution` tail. engine RULES UNCHANGED (0.40.0 — a transport/read surface); 1803 → **1812** tests (a flagged-row + pairing-list passthrough, the bench-index option, the §4 first-turn empty-list-with-greyed-row consistency pin, the actor gate, a target-copy purity pin, a populated-`rareCandy` round-trip, 3 OnlineHud DOM cases — two-step dispatch / sole-Basic pre-select / greyed-row-no-dialog — and the match.test allowlist flip to supported + soft-lock-safe off-phase), `bun run check` green. **With this, every turn affordance the local HUD offers has an online counterpart** — what remains for P4 is the match LIFECYCLE (3c+: reconnect/timeouts, concede/rematch, spectate) + the DO-storage-migration hardening. **Still unverified:** a two-client browser run (the surface is test-only, like every increment since 3a). **RECORDED (pre-existing engine gap, not introduced here):** `stage2EvolvesFromBasic` resolves the bridge Stage 1 against the SHARED `state.cardPool` (both decks' cards), so in the narrow case where a player's own deck runs NO copy of the bridge Stage 1 but the OPPONENT's does, their dialog lights up when it otherwise would not — a faint inference about the opponent's decklist. It is the documented ingest-debt gap in `cardplay.ts` (the catalog stores only each card's IMMEDIATE `evolveFrom`), identical in local hot-seat play, and fixing it is an engine/ingest change, not a transport one. *Why: flagging the row rather than inventing a separate affordance keeps Rare Candy where the player looks for it (its hand-Trainer row) and keeps `redactedTrainersOf` a faithful mirror of the local list — the property every 3b review checked. Putting the pairings on the phase rather than the row matches the local decomposition and avoids duplicating a copy-independent list. Keeping the engine as the legality authority (shipping its options rather than the facts to recompute) is the 2b-i/ii/3b server-compute-then-ship pattern once more, and here it is not merely convenient: the chain bridge needs the card POOL, which the wire has no business carrying. Admitting `rareCandy` to the allowlist is safe on the same argument `evolve` already is — same placement tail, same parks, all dialoged.* (2026-07-26) |
| D72 | Version the persisted MatchRecord, and retire what this build can't read (P4 online, increment 3c-i) | The DO has never had storage migrations — an acknowledged gap since 1b, and the 2b-iii-d-ii review turned it into a concrete LOW: a `MatchRecord` written by pre-slice code (`{seed, state}`) and read across a deploy stamps NaN elapsed, throws on `names[seat]` for a system log row, and broadcasts `log: undefined` into a client render throw. This closes it, and does so FIRST because reconnect/timeouts (3c-ii+) will change the record's shape again — the ordering the 2b-iii-d-ii note asked for. **The shape: a version, not defaults.** `MatchRecord` gained **`version`**, stamped from a new **`MATCH_RECORD_VERSION`** constant, and every read goes through a new PURE **`readMatchRecord(raw): MatchRecord | null`** (match.ts) that refuses anything whose version isn't this build's. Defaulting the missing fields on read — the fix the LOW originally proposed — was REJECTED: it repairs only the fields we happen to know went missing, and says nothing about the far more dangerous case, an embedded engine `GameState` written by a build whose RULES have since moved, which reads back "fine" and then plays subtly wrong. A version covers both, and it is what makes "no migrations" a loud, recoverable condition instead of a silent hazard. Past the version gate the checks are deliberately SHALLOW (seed/startedAt numbers, state a non-null object, log an array, names carrying both seats) — just enough that the DO's own reads can't throw on a truncated blob; it does NOT re-validate the `GameState`, because a same-version state is by definition one this build wrote, and a deeper check would drift into a second, weaker copy of the engine's invariants (pinned by a test that a same-version record with a nonsense state still reads back). **The DO side:** one door onto the key — a private **`peekMatch()`** returning a discriminated `MatchLoad` (`none` / `ok` / `stale`) — and a **`retireStaleMatch()`** that drops the record, takes the lobby out of in-game, and tells both players (`{kind:"match-error"}`, reusing 3a's load-failure surface verbatim: to a player the two are the same event — the match can't continue, re-pick and ready up). The four call sites differ ON PURPOSE: the action handler and `sendMatchIfInGame` RETIRE (they are the paths a real player hits mid-match — without this the lobby wedges in-game around an unreadable record, reconnects sit on the placeholder forever and every action is silently dropped); the alarm's countdown→in-game flip only SKIPS (the record was just written at the current version, so `stale` is unreachable there, and retiring inside the alarm would commit a second snapshot mid-flip); and the handoff's idempotency guard treats `stale` as "no live match" so the fresh write simply replaces it (the lobby is starting a match right now — nothing to reset). **A REAL BUG caught while building it, worth recording because the shape of it is a trap:** the first draft reused 3a's `resetAfterFailedStart` to leave in-game, which does not work — `recomputePhase` returns an `in-game` snapshot UNTOUCHED (the invariant that stops a mid-match ready-toggle from tearing the game down), so un-readying an in-game lobby leaves the phase exactly where it was, i.e. wedged in-game around the record just deleted: precisely the failure this slice exists to close. Fixed by a new **`endMatch(snapshot)`** (logic.ts) that clears the phase FIRST and then re-derives (`selecting` with a guest, `waiting` without) — explicitly, because leaving in-game must be a decision a caller makes, never a side effect. `abandonsMatch` reports the transition, so `commit` drops the record with it. `endMatch` is the primitive **concede / rematch / timed-out-player (3c-ii+) all need**, so it is a shared seam, not a one-off. 1814 → **1825** tests (`readMatchRecord` accept / the exact pre-slice `{seed,state}` shape / version older AND newer — a rollback must not read the deploy ahead of it / field-by-field truncation / non-objects / the deliberate no-deep-validation; `applyMatchAction` PRESERVES the version — pinned because the applied record goes straight back to storage, so dropping it would retire a live match after its first action; `endMatch` leaves in-game, un-readies, keeps deck picks, falls back to waiting with no guest, and can't re-enter countdown; plus the trap itself — `resetAfterFailedStart` leaves an in-game lobby in-game). `bun run check` green. Engine untouched. **Not verified in a browser** (the retirement needs a deploy boundary to fire); the DO shell stays untested per the standing pattern, with all new logic pure and covered. *Why: versioning is the smallest honest answer to "we have no migrations" — it converts an unbounded set of silent corruptions into one explicit, recoverable event, and it costs one integer. Bumping it is a deliberate act at the next shape change, so ordinary deploys don't disturb live matches. Reusing the 3a match-error surface avoids a second recovery vocabulary for what players experience as the same interruption. And extracting `endMatch` rather than bending `resetAfterFailedStart` keeps the two intents separate: one is "the match never started", the other is "the match is over" — they differ in exactly the phase guard that made the first draft wrong.* (2026-07-26) |
| D73 | Concede, and the abandonment forfeit that ends a stuck online match (P4 online, increment 3c-ii, engine 0.41.0) | Closes the last way an online match could never resolve: before this, one player closing their tab left the other staring at a board FOREVER. Nothing bounded it — the 5s `DISCONNECT_GRACE_MS` only greys the seat's connection dot, and the empty-lobby TTL cannot help because it fires only when NOBODY is connected, and the abandoned player is. **The engine gains a fourth way a game ends.** `GameOverReason` += **`conceded`**, and a new **`concede(state, action)`** (flow.ts, beside `finishGame` — the one GAME_OVER emitter) hands the win to the opponent. Unlike every other action it is legal in **ANY phase but `gameOver`**, which is the whole point: a concede gated on your own turn would be useless in exactly the situations it exists for — an opponent who walked away mid-turn, or an effect park nobody is left to answer — and it is the ONE action that can always end a match that is otherwise stuck. Conceding mid-park is safe because `finishGame` clears `pending` wholesale, so no queued stage resumes into a finished game. **ONE reason for two triggers** (a player pressing concede, and the server forfeiting for an abandoner) because to the opponent they are the same event, a forfeit — and the engine has no business knowing about sockets; the prose everywhere says "forfeited", which is true of both. This IS a rules-surface change, hence engine **0.41.0**. **`MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` deliberately NOT bumped:** widening an enum is backwards-compatible — a record written before it holds only older reasons, which this build still reads — and the D72 test is "could the previous deploy's record be MISREAD by this one", not "did any type change"; that reasoning is now written into the constant's doc as the worked example. **The DO side (3c-ii's actual feature):** a second, much longer clock. New **`ABANDON_GRACE_MS` = 90s** and an `ABANDON_KEY` list reusing the SAME `{playerId, deadline}` shape and the same pure helpers as the disconnect grace, but with a different consequence: the 5s decides whether a seat LOOKS disconnected, the 90s decides whether they LOSE. It is armed in the alarm's disconnect-grace branch (the first moment we know they didn't come straight back, with the blips already absorbed) and only while the lobby is `in-game`; it is cleared by **`clearDisconnectTimers`**, which replaced `clearPendingDisconnect` and now cancels BOTH timers from every hello branch — folded together precisely so a future branch can't cancel one and leave the other armed, which would forfeit a player who HAD come back. When it fires, **`forfeitAbandoned`** applies the engine's own `concede` for the vanished seat through the ordinary `applyMatchAction` → persist → `broadcastMatch` path, so a forfeited match ends exactly like any other: a real `GameOutcome`, the winner's overlay, a log row, and the lobby staying in-game with a finished game (as it does after a normal win).
