| D91 | Defiance Band — a CONDITIONAL passive damage bonus, the D40 `BoardCondition` vocabulary's third consumer (P3-M5, engine 0.42.0) | The `conditionalDamageBonus` backlog row's first card, and the first PASSIVE reader of the D40 vocabulary — the conditional twin of Vitality Band ("If you have more Prize cards remaining than your opponent, the attacks of the Pokémon this card is attached to do 30 more damage to your opponent's Active Pokémon (before applying Weakness and Resistance)", sv01-169). **A card row + one new field, not a new primitive**, because it REUSES the existing `morePrizesThanOpponent` condition (added at D40 and already read by Fighting Au Lait's play gate + a branch op — this is the same predicate as a passive). `PassiveEffects` gains **`damageBonusBeforeWRIf: { amount, cond: BoardCondition }`**. **The load-bearing design call: WHERE the condition is evaluated.** continuous.ts's `passivesOf` is the single continuous-modifier aggregation point but is SEAT-FREE (it takes only `state` + the Pokémon), and a `BoardCondition` is seat-relative (whose Prizes?). Folding the evaluation into `passivesOf` would have needed `conditionHolds` from interpreter.ts, and interpreter.ts already imports `passivesOf` — a MODULE CYCLE. So `passivesOf` stays pure and seat-free, now collecting the gated bonuses RAW in a new `conditionalDamageBonusBeforeWR: {amount,cond}[]` (aggregation is its job); the FOLD lives in a new **`interpreter.ts` `attackerPreWRBonus(state, pokemon, seat)`** (evaluation beside the sole `conditionHolds`, its job), shared by the ONLY two pre-W/R read sites — attack.ts's main hit (`action.seat`) and `snipeActive` (`ctx.seat`) — the only places that know the attacker's seat. Kept OUT of the unconditional `damageBonusBeforeWR` so the defender-reduction and HP reads (which never look at either) can never be credited an attacker's conditional bonus. **snipeActive parity is free**: it now calls the shared helper, so a snipe landing on the opponent's Active gets Defiance Band's +30 exactly as it already got Vitality Band's — verified by the existing Vitality Band snipe coverage still passing after the refactor. **Test-deck choice (a small but real call):** the new tests use a DEDICATED `DEFIANCE_DECK` fixture rather than adding sv01-169 to the shared `STADIUM_TOOL_DECK` — the latter is a fixed 60 (setup.ts enforces `DECK_SIZE`), so any card swapped in reshuffles every seed and broke a fragile opening-hand test (fix-energy-in-hand + fix-tool-in-deck) shared across 5 suites; a dedicated all-Basic deck is mulligan-free and seed-independent (cards pulled post-setup by id). **No web/API/DB/schema touch** — damage bonuses are engine-internal, surfaced only via the `DAMAGE_DEALT` log event (no projection field), confirmed by an Explore map. engineVersion 0.41.0 → **0.42.0**; 1941 → **1945** tests (registration + applies-when-behind + no-op-when-level + the (30+30)×2=120 before-W/R pin). `bun run check` green (137 files / 1945 tests). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** (a cheap, contained slice; the mechanism mirrors Vitality Band, already reviewed). **Remaining on the row: Choice Belt** (sv02-176) — a TARGET-TYPE gate ("…Active Pokémon V"), a different shape (not a `BoardCondition`, so not this field), its own slice. *Why: the resume point named this "the last cheap D40 reader"; it is cheap precisely because the hard part (the condition vocabulary) already exists — the only new engineering is deciding where a seat-relative predicate gets evaluated within a seat-free aggregation pipeline, resolved by splitting aggregation (continuous.ts, unchanged responsibility) from evaluation (interpreter.ts, beside the one evaluator) rather than threading a seat through six call sites or duplicating `conditionHolds`.* (2026-07-27) |
| D92 | Choice Belt — a TARGET-gated passive damage bonus, and the rule-box suffix read off the NAME (P3-M5, engine 0.43.0) | The `conditionalDamageBonus` row's **second and last card, closing it 2/2** — the target-gated twin of Vitality Band: "The attacks of the Pokémon this card is attached to do 30 more damage to your opponent's Active Pokémon **V** (before applying Weakness and Resistance)" (sv02-176). **Two calls carry it.** **(1) A SIBLING field, not the D40 vocabulary.** `PassiveEffects` gains **`damageBonusBeforeWRIfTarget: { amount, targetSuffix }`** beside D91's `damageBonusBeforeWRIf`, because the two gates ask different questions: D91's reads the ATTACKER's seat-relative board (a `BoardCondition`), this one reads the card being HIT. Folding it into the `BoardCondition` union would have meant inventing a condition whose subject is the defender — a different kind of predicate wearing the same name. The plumbing is D91's, unchanged in shape: the seat-free `passivesOf` collects these bonuses RAW into a new `targetConditionalDamageBonusBeforeWR` list (aggregation), and the FOLD stays in `interpreter.ts`'s `attackerPreWRBonus`, which now also takes the **`defenderCard`** — free, because both of the only two pre-W/R read sites already had it in hand (attack.ts's main hit had `defenderCard`, `snipeActive` had `target.card`), so **snipeActive parity is again free** and the module-cycle argument that put the fold in interpreter.ts still holds. **(2) The "is it a V" test needed NO schema, ingest, DB or API change.** tcgdex's dedicated `suffix` field is dropped at ingest — but the marker is ALSO part of the printed card NAME (a Pokémon V is literally printed "<Species> V"), and `name` IS persisted, so the datum survives *encoded in a column we already have*. A new **`cards.ts pokemonSuffixOf(card): "ex"|"V"|"VMAX"|"VSTAR"|"GX"|null`** derives it, matching each suffix as a WHOLE marker: "V" does not match "VMAX"/"VSTAR" (neither is a space-then-"V"), which is exactly the distinction the card draws — Choice Belt boosts a V, never a VMAX. The alternative (an ingest change + a migration + a backfill to persist `suffix`) would have been a multi-layer slice to recover a datum already in hand. It is a derivation, not a guess: the suffix is part of the printed name by the game's own naming rule. **Known and accepted: the gate is DORMANT against the live catalog.** The ingested SV-era pool contains no Pokémon V at all, so today Choice Belt is registered, correct, and never fires in a real game; it starts firing the moment a prior-era set is ingested, with no engine change. That is the right trade — the card is in the catalog, and the alternative is a registry hole that reads as "unsimulated". **A follow-up it unlocks (recorded, NOT taken):** `prizeValueOf` still returns 1 for every KO, its comment blaming the lost suffix; `pokemonSuffixOf` now supplies exactly the classification it wants, so 2-prize ex/V and 3-prize VMAX become a ONE-LINE change. Deliberately not taken here — prize counts ripple through the win condition and almost every KO test, which is its own slice, not a rider on a Tool. **No web/API/DB/schema touch** (damage bonuses are engine-internal, surfaced only via the `DAMAGE_DEALT` log event). engineVersion 0.42.0 → **0.43.0**; 1945 → **1952** tests — 5 behavioural (registration, +30 vs a V, no-op vs a plain body, **no-op vs a VMAX**, and the (30+30)×2=120 before-W/R pin against a Fire-weak V) on a dedicated `CHOICE_DECK` fixture per D91's test-deck reasoning, plus 2 `pokemonSuffixOf` unit tests in `cards.test.ts` (its conventional home) pinning the literal match and the null cases. `bun run check` green (137 files / 1952 tests). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — a contained slice reusing D91's just-reviewed mechanism. *Why: the row's last card looked like it needed persisted card metadata we threw away at ingest; it turned out the metadata is redundantly encoded in the name, so the whole slice collapsed to one derivation function and one passive field.* (2026-07-27) |
| D93 | Prize value — a KO owes its rule box's real Prize count, off `pokemonSuffixOf` (P3-M5, engine 0.44.0) | The follow-up D92 recorded-but-did-not-take. `prizeValueOf` stops returning 1 for every KO: **3 Prizes for a VMAX, 2 for any other rule box (ex / V / VSTAR / GX), 1 for a plain body** — `suffix === null ? 1 : suffix === "VMAX" ? 3 : 2`, the class read off D92's **`pokemonSuffixOf`** (the printed NAME suffix, so — like Choice Belt — **no schema / ingest / DB / API / web change** was needed to value an ex at its two Prizes). This is the first D92-family change that actually FIRES against the live catalog, which is full of ex Pokémon (Choice Belt stays dormant for want of a Pokémon V; prize value does not). **The load-bearing call was NOT the code — it was measuring the blast radius instead of trusting the fear.** D92's own comment (and the `prizeValueOf` doc) warned the change "ripples through the win condition and almost every KO test," which is why three prior sessions deferred it. So rather than guess, this session **applied the one-line change and ran the FULL suite: all 1952 tests stayed green.** The reason is structural — every existing KO test knocks out a PLAIN body (the `fix-victim` / `fix-attacker` fixtures), and `prizeValueOf(plain) === 1` is unchanged; the ex/V/VMAX fixtures exist only as ATTACKERS or as Choice Belt's damage TARGET, never as a KO'd body a test counts Prizes for. The feared radius was real in principle and empty in fact. **No flow change needed either:** the `count` these feed was ALWAYS a variable at both prize read sites (flow.ts's `takePrizes` stage at :92/:107 and the §14 tie-guard speculation at :175-:205), and both already clamp to the Prizes that remain (the handler's `Math.min(stage.count, side.prizes.length)`, the tie-guard's `prizes.slice(count)`), so a 2-Prize KO with one Prize left takes the last one and WINS (§14.1) rather than over-drawing — `prizeValueOf` simply never fed those paths a value >1 before. **Tests:** a dedicated **`prizeValue.test.ts`** — 4 behavioural (V→2, VMAX→3, plain→1 as the contrast, and the clamp-to-win) driving P1's `fix-attacker` Bite through a pre-damaged rule-box Active on a new mulligan-free **`PRIZE_DECK`** (D91's dedicated-deck reasoning: never mutate a shared fixed-60), plus 2 `prizeValueOf` unit cases in `cards.test.ts`. The stale `attack.test.ts` ex-flow-pick comment ("the catalog cannot mark ex yet") was corrected — that test crafts a 2-Prize decision directly to pin the PICK HANDLER in isolation, which is still the right split now that `prizeValue.test.ts` owns the KO→count wiring. **No web/API/DB/schema touch** (the web HUD renders whatever Prize row the engine reports; a 2-Prize KO just removes two). engineVersion 0.43.0 → **0.44.0**; 1952 → **1958** tests (137 → **138** files). `bun run check` green. **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — a one-function slice reusing D92's just-reviewed `pokemonSuffixOf`. *Why: the datum and the classifier already existed (D92); the only thing standing between them and correct Prize counts was an unmeasured fear of test churn, and measuring it dissolved the fear — a good reminder that "wide blast radius" is a hypothesis to test, not a reason to defer indefinitely.* (2026-07-27) |
| D94 | Spread "also" rider — one optional token, and a deviation from the resume point's ordering toward the candidate with real yield (P3-M5, engine 0.45.0) | The each-of-spread also-rider: "This attack **also** does N damage to each of your opponent's Benched Pokémon." (Cetoddle sv02-053 "Avalanche" — 30 to the Active, then 10 to each Bench). **A one-token regex relaxation, ZERO new op/interpreter/registry code:** the word "also" is made OPTIONAL in the existing `SPREAD_EACH_BENCH` deriver (`^This attack (?:also )?does …`), so the rider derives to the **same** `spreadDamage` op as the bare "This attack does N …" form. **Design call — one rule, not a twin.** The snipe family has separate anchored regexes per shape because "1 of" vs "each of" pick genuinely different ops (`damageChosen` vs `spreadDamage`); here the string AFTER "does" is identical, and the engine already treats even the bare spread as a SECONDARY hit (the main damage rides the attack's own `damage` field — comment predates this slice), so "also" is pure emphasis of a fact the `damage` field already carries. A separate `ALSO_SPREAD_EACH_BENCH` regex + dispatch case would be a redundant twin producing a byte-identical op, so it was rejected in favour of the optional token. **A test moved, not just added:** `alsoBenchedSnipe.test.ts` listed this exact "each of" text as a `toBeNull()` near-miss — but it was null only because NO reader accepted it; now `SPREAD_EACH_BENCH` correctly claims it, so the assertion flipped from "refused" to "derives to `spreadDamage`", preserving the test's real intent (the single-target `damageChosen` snipe reader must never grab a spread). Pinned by a derive assertion on the verbatim Cetoddle text in `knockout.test.ts`; the full §8.5 spread flow (bench W/R, Bouffer reduction, bench KO) is already covered by `fix-sniper`'s Spread Shot, so re-driving an identical op through a Cetoddle deck would be redundant. **The DEVIATION worth recording:** the resume point ranked "count-2" (a benched/any-target snipe to "2 of your opponent's Pokémon") FIRST as cheapest. But there is no committed card corpus (attack text is fetched from tcgdex at runtime), and the backlog names **no concrete SV card for count-2** while it names Cetoddle for the spread rider. A live tcgdex query CONFIRMED Cetoddle sv02-053 prints this text verbatim; count-2 remains a "family-completeness, dormant, no known consumer" relaxation. Per conventions ("prefer the first unless the code tells you otherwise"), the corpus reality — one candidate authors a real live card, the other zero verifiable cards — is exactly that signal, so this session took the spread rider. count-2 stays on the backlog, honestly labelled as dormant/unverified. **No web/API/DB/schema change** (a deriver-only slice; `spreadDamage` is engine-internal, surfaced via `DAMAGE_DEALT`). engineVersion 0.44.0 → **0.45.0**; 1958 → **1960** tests (138 files). `bun run check` green. **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — a one-token relaxation of a just-verified regex. *Why: the two open candidates were equal in cost but not in value — verifying the actual catalog (rather than trusting the "cheapest first" label) showed one fires against a real card and the other against none, so "better and green" meant taking the one with yield.* (2026-07-27) |
| D95 | Umbreon "Feint Attack" — the any-target snipe that ignores Weakness/Resistance AND effects on the target (P3-M5, engine 0.46.0) | The resume point's first-named candidate, and the any-target snipe family's (D59) first rider that CHANGES the op rather than adding a card. Umbreon **sv03-130** (Obsidian Flames, in the live SV pool — tcgdex-confirmed this session, VERBATIM): "This attack does 50 damage to 1 of your opponent's Pokémon. This attack's damage isn't affected by Weakness or Resistance, or by any effects on that Pokémon." **A FIELD on the existing `damageChosen` op (`ignoreWR?: true`), not a new op** — threaded through `placeSnipe`/`snipeActive` as one boolean, mirroring the existing `deals` param. **The load-bearing discovery: the real SV text is LONGER than the backlog shorthand.** The backlog and prior resume points named this "Umbreon's no-W/R-on-Active flag" — an `ignoreWR` on the W/R math alone. But a live tcgdex query showed the printed sentence carries a SECOND clause, "…, or by any effects on that Pokémon.", which in the engine's snipe pipeline means the target's `damageReductionAfterWR` passive (Copperajah's Bronze Body, Bouffalant's Bouffer — the only target-side effect a snipe models). So the flag nulls Weakness, Resistance AND that reduction, on BOTH snipe arms (a Feint that lands on a benched reduction body skips it too). **What it KEEPS is the discriminating call:** the attacker's own pre-W/R bonus (`attackerPreWRBonus` — Vitality Band, Defiance Band, Choice Belt) STILL applies, because those are effects on the ATTACKER, not on "that Pokémon" (the target) — so a Feint + Vitality Band lands 50+10=60 FLAT (bonus added, not doubled inside a nulled Weakness). Modeling only the W/R half (matching the shorthand) would have DERIVED the card yet under-honored its own printed sentence against every reduction body — a latent bug — so the honest slice is the full sentence. **The deriver: one regex, not a twin.** `CHOSEN_ANY_TARGET`'s optional tail became an ALTERNATION — the existing Benched-clarifier parenthetical (no op change) OR a captured group for the Feint sentence (sets `ignoreWR`); the ", or by any effects on that Pokémon" clause is itself optional so the bare classic wording derives the same flag. A `toBeNull()` near-miss in `anyTargetSnipe.test.ts` (it listed Umbreon's short form as REFUSED "— a DIFFERENT tail") flipped to a positive derive — the D94 "a test moved, not just added" pattern. **Test-deck choice (the D91 reasoning, via a mid-slice course-correction):** the runtime tests first grew the shared `ANY_SNIPE_DECK`, which is a FIXED 60 — adding 6 cards overflowed it, and rebalancing the dominant `fix-lightning-weak` 30→24 reshuffled the seed-sensitive opening-hand pins and broke an EXISTING snipe test, so a dedicated mulligan-free `FEINT_DECK` was cut instead, leaving all 20+ existing snipe tests byte-identical. `board`/`anySnipe` gained an optional `deck` param (default unchanged). A synthetic {L} `fix-feint` (the card's VERBATIM SV text, but typed {L} so `fix-lightning-weak`'s Weakness fires and can be shown NULLED) is the runtime witness; the real card is pinned by the verbatim-text derive assertion. **No web/API/DB/schema change** (the snipe's damage is engine-internal, surfaced via `DAMAGE_DEALT`; `ignoreWR` just nulls two modifiers already in the event). engineVersion 0.45.0 → **0.46.0**; 1960 → **1964** tests (138 files). `bun run check` green. **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — a one-field slice on the just-reviewed D59 snipe pipeline; two research sub-agents (a tcgdex verification + an Explore code-map) scoped it. *Why: the resume point named it first as a small "W/R flag"; verifying the actual print (rather than trusting the shorthand) turned it into a faithful "ignore all target-side modifiers" slice — the same D94 lesson that the catalog, not the backlog label, decides the real shape of the work.* (2026-07-27) |
| D96 | Chien-Pao ex "Hail Blade" — the variable-count discard whose damage scales off it, and the engine's FIRST registry-authored attack (P3-M5, engine 0.47.0) | The resume point's first-named candidate and the damage family's hardest card. Chien-Pao ex **sv02-061** (Paldea Evolved, live SV pool — tcgdex-confirmed VERBATIM this session): "Hail Blade" ({W}{W}, "60×") = "You may discard **any amount** of {W} Energy from **your Pokémon**. This attack does 60 damage for each card you discarded in this way." **The D95 lesson held again:** the real print is broader than the backlog shorthand ("discard from this Pokémon") — it is "from **your** Pokémon" (the whole own board) and "for each **card**" (count-scaled). **Three gaps, closed together, in one coherent slice, reusing the most existing machinery: Model A (post-damage, §9.2 record-driven).** §8 applies damage BEFORE the effect program, so an attack whose number depends on a mid-attack choice cannot use the pre-W/R `AttackDamageBonus` fold (read off the board at declaration) — the honest model is base 0 + a program op that re-enters the W/R math AFTER the discard. (1) `discardEnergy` gained a **`from: "yours"`** arm — the CONTROLLER's whole board, the printed "your Pokémon", distinct from `yourActive`'s "this Pokémon"; the candidate scan (`attachedEnergies` whole-board) and apply (`discardEnergyApply` strips active+bench) already supported it, so it was `discardVictimSeat` + a from-arm, not new enumeration. (2) A **`count: "any"`** mode — the one DECLINABLE discard (0..all), a new `DiscardScope {kind:"upTo", max}` whose pick MAY be empty (every other discard scope is mandatory/exact), never collapsed by interchangeability (the COUNT is what the damage reads, so N identical {W} must offer N rows) and never forced (a real decision at every non-empty board). (3) A §9.2 **`recordAs`** on `discardEnergy` filing the discarded uids under a NEW `discarded` `EffectSlot` — "for each card you discarded **in this way**" is literally an op→op record read (the Miriam/Superior-Energy-Retrieval shape), so `discardEnergyApply` now returns `{state, discarded}` and `recordMoved` fires at every discard site (a no-op for the 5 pre-existing discard cards, which set no `recordAs`). The reader is a **new `damageDefender` op** `{per, count: EffectSlot}` that deals `per × recorded(slot).length` to the DEFENDER through the EXISTING **`snipeActive`** — the same full §8.5 pipeline Umbreon/Wo-Chien use to hit the Active (Weakness DOUBLES, Resistance/reduction subtract, the attacker's continuous pre-W/R bonus ADDS) — so it is genuine attack damage, NOT the flat put-counter model. **Because the program owns the damage, the printed "60×" base is SUPPRESSED** (attack.ts: a `programDamage` check — a program containing `damageDefender` drops the base + marks the "×" modifier simulated, exactly as the multiply family drops its own base; otherwise a 0-discard Hail Blade would deal 60 flat + double-count, and the "×" would flag `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED`). **The FIRST registry-authored attack** (the `programFor(id)?.attack` seam, unused until now): CHIEN_PAO's existing Shivery-Chill entry gained the `attack` program, because the variable-count sentence is one the deriver (`deriveAttackEffect` + the multiply/additive readers) deliberately refuses — so `deriveAttackEffect(verbatim)` STILL returns null and the providesEnergy/scaledDamage near-miss tests stay green. **A client change too** (no schema/API/DB): since M9 the web app runs on the real engine, so `GameHud.tsx`'s discard dialog gained the upTo/declinable branch (a capped multi-select, "Discard none"/"Discard N", always-ready Confirm). **HONESTY NOTE — landed under a hard autoloop landing without its own behavioural tests.** engineVersion 0.46.0 → **0.47.0**; `bun run typecheck` green and the **engine suite unchanged-green (929 tests)** — proving the implementation compiles and breaks nothing existing — but the dedicated `hailBlade.test.ts` (damage scaling / decline-is-0 / whole-board / Weakness / ex-KO prizes) was being written by a sub-agent STOPPED at the hard-landing before it produced the file, so those behaviours are code-reviewed-by-eye only. **The resume point makes writing them the immediate next slice, then `/code-review`.** *Why: the resume point named it first and as "its own session"; the code-map confirmed a three-gap slice, but each gap reused an existing seam (whole-board apply, the snipe W/R pipeline, the §9.2 record), so the honest whole-sentence implementation was tractable in one pass — the missing piece is verification, deferred (not skipped) with a loud resume point.* **FOLLOW-UP (build #81, 2026-07-27) — the deferred verification LANDED and the honesty note is now CLOSED:** `packages/engine/src/hailBlade.test.ts` (10 tests) pins all seven named behaviours — registry-authored + deriver-still-refuses, the whole-board `upTo` park with no interchangeable collapse and no non-{W} offered, 60×k to the defender with the self-discard event and exact ordering, the DECLINE dealing zero (the base-suppression proof), a benched Pokémon's {W} as fuel while the Active's cost Energy stays on, Weakness DOUBLING the hit (the proof it re-enters §8.5 rather than putting flat counters), and an ex KO owing 2 Prizes (D93). Two fixtures added (`fix-water-weak`, `HAIL_BLADE_DECK`). **The implementation needed no behavioural fix** — the design above survived verification unchanged. **What DID surface: `bun run check` had been RED since this decision landed** — #80 ran only `typecheck` + the engine suite, so the lint gate never ran (`attack.ts` `useOptionalChain`, fixed behaviour-identically; a stale comment there denying any registry-authored attack corrected). *Lesson recorded: after a hard mid-flight landing, re-run the FULL gate, not the subset the interrupted session reached.* engineVersion unchanged at 0.47.0; 139 files / 1974 tests green. `/code-review` remains NOT run (user-triggered). (2026-07-27) |
| D97 | Mewtwo VSTAR "Psy Purge" — the CAPPED Hail Blade shape, and the authored-attack seam keyed by attack INDEX (P3-M5, engine 0.48.0) | The resume point's flagged "cheap adjacent option now that the machinery exists". Mewtwo VSTAR **swsh10.5-031** (Pokémon GO / Crown Zenith, live in the D1 catalog — tcgdex-confirmed VERBATIM, identical across all three prints -031/-079/-086): "Psy Purge" ({P}{C}, "90×") = "Discard up to 3 Psychic Energy from your Pokémon. This attack does 90 damage for each card you discarded in this way." — the SAME declinable whole-board discard → `damageDefender` D96 built, but the count is capped at 3 rather than "any amount". **Two small changes, both reusing D96's machinery.** **(1) The CAP — one optional field, not a new count mode.** `discardEnergy` gained **`cap?: number`**, meaningful only alongside `count: "any"`: it bounds the declinable up-to BELOW the whole board (the printed "up to N"), so the park's `{kind:"upTo", max}` becomes `min(cap, discardable.length)` instead of the full offer, and `discardNote` prints "up to 3 …" instead of "any amount of …". Rejected the alternative of a new `count` shape (`{upTo:N}`): the "any" branch already parks exactly right and the wire validator (`cardplay.ts` — "discard at most N Energy", `BAD_EFFECT_CHOICE`) was ALREADY cap-agnostic (it reads `scope.max`), so a narrower `max` needed zero wire change — the cap is purely a smaller ceiling on the existing declinable mode. **(2) The authored-attack seam is now keyed by ATTACK INDEX.** D96's `CardProgram.attack` was a single `EffectOp[]` bound to the card id — fine for single-attack Chien-Pao, but Mewtwo VSTAR has TWO attacks (Psy Purge idx 0, "Star Raid" the VSTAR-Power spread idx 1), and `programFor(id)?.attack` would have applied Psy Purge's discard-and-scale program to BOTH — Star Raid would wrongly park on a discard. So `attack` became **`{ readonly [attackIndex: number]: EffectOp[] }`** and `attack.ts` reads `programFor(id)?.attack?.[index]`: a card authors only the attacks whose sentence the deriver refuses; Star Raid (idx 1) finds no program, the deriver refuses its text, and it is honestly flagged `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` — no discard, no damage. Chien-Pao migrated to `{ 0: [...] }` (its two pinned `hailBlade.test.ts` assertions updated to `?.attack?.[0]`); the only functional reader was `attack.ts:216`, so the blast radius was one line + the type + two test assertions. Binding by index (not by attack name) matches how the `attack` action already selects the attack. **A stale comment corrected, made reachable by this slice.** `cards.ts pokemonSuffixOf`'s doc claimed "The current SV-era catalog holds no V/VMAX/VSTAR at all, so Choice Belt's gate is dormant" — but the live catalog's Pokémon GO set (swsh10.5) IS an ingested prior-era set holding **13 Pokémon V, 2 VMAX, 5 VSTAR** (queried this session), so Choice Belt's V-gate is LIVE, and Mewtwo VSTAR is the first authored VSTAR. Comment-only fix (no behaviour). **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/psyPurge.test.ts` (11 tests) — authored-at-idx-0-with-cap-3 + idx-1-undefined + all three prints; the CAP (park max 3 not 5, "up to 3" caption, a 4-pick rejected by the validator); 90×k scaling (3→270 survives a 280 HP Mewtwo defender, 1→90, decline→0); **Star Raid idx 1 stays unsimulated** (the index-keying proof — no park, no damage, `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED`); Weakness DOUBLING (90×2=180 vs a ×2 Psychic body); and a VSTAR KO owing **2 Prizes** (D93 — the first VSTAR the authored pool KOs). New fixtures `fix-psychic-weak`, the `swsh10.5-031` Mewtwo VSTAR (both attacks, VERBATIM), and `PSY_PURGE_DECK` (mirrors `HAIL_BLADE_DECK`, {P}-flavoured). **Every test passed on first run — no behavioural fix.** No web/API/DB/schema change (swsh10.5 already ingested; the HUD's existing upTo dialog carries the cap via the smaller `scope.max`). engineVersion 0.47.0 → **0.48.0**; 139 → **140** files, 1974 → **1985** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (tsc + lint + vitest — the D96 lesson: ran the FULL gate, twice, including after the comment edit). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — a contained slice extending D96's just-verified machinery; one Explore sub-agent mapped the seam and verified the catalog, and the exact print was re-confirmed against the live D1 before authoring. `/code-review` still NOT run (user-triggered; D96 + this slice both await it). *Why: the resume point flagged it cheap because the discard/record/damageDefender machinery already existed — the real work was the two seams the shorthand hid (a cap on the up-to, and index-keying so a multi-attack card doesn't misfire), each a small honest change, and the D95/D96 "verify the verbatim print" lesson held a third time (the text matched the shorthand here, but the catalog query is what confirmed the card, its ids, and the stale V/VSTAR comment).* (2026-07-27) |

| D98 | `damageAttacker` — the reactive counters-on-attacker recoil, landed as a defender-side PASSIVE rather than the `onDamagedByAttack` trigger the backlog named (P3-M5, engine 0.49.0) | The resume point named `onDamagedByAttack` first (4 cards) with `damageAttacker` (3 cards) as "the natural pair", each a "full new-op slice … scope for a whole session". **Two research sub-agents flipped the framing** (the D95/D96/D97 "verify before scoping" lesson, applied to the ARCHITECTURE, not just the text): a verbatim D1 query + an Explore code-map showed the `damageAttacker` subset (Cacnea/Cacturne "Counterattack Quills" 30, Stunfisk "Custom Trap" 50) is NOT a trigger at all — "if this Pokémon is damaged by an attack, put N damage counters on the Attacking Pokémon" is a **reactive continuous read, the exact shape of `damageReductionAfterWR`** (also read at damage time, seat-free). So it landed as ONE new `PassiveEffects` field `damageAttacker?: {amount, requiresTool?}` — **no new op, TriggerTiming, action, or phase.** continuous.ts sums it seat-free, evaluating Custom Trap's `requiresTool` against the holder's board (so the read site stays a plain number); attack.ts folds it right after `DAMAGE_DEALT`, placing flat counters (outside the §8.5 W/R pipeline — the confusion-self-hit / `damageSelf` model, `COUNTERS_PLACED source:"ability"`) on the attacker's Active when `dealt > 0`. **"Even if this Pokémon is Knocked Out" is FREE:** the placement precedes finishAttack's both-board §8.1 sweep (present in BOTH the program branch's `attackEpilogue` and the direct call), which then KOs a lethally-retaliated attacker and prizes it to the defender — the confusion-self-KO path proves the sweep already handles a mid-turn attacker KO. **The "in the Active Spot" clause needs no `activeOnly` flag** — it is enforced by the READ SITE: only the main-hit Active defender retaliates, so a benched holder hit by spread naturally does not (which is exactly what the clause says). Cacnea/Cacturne share one const (byte-identical). **Deliberate deviation, with a trail:** the general `onDamagedByAttack` trigger framework (a new TriggerTiming + scan point + a reactive program that PARKS an opponent choice) is genuinely larger and is still needed by the OTHER two cards on that row — **Armarouge** sv03-044 (Burn the attacker, an `applyStatus`) and **Klawf ex** sv03-120 (discard an Energy from the attacker, an opponent-side parking discard) — so it stays the documented follow-up, now the whole of the (annotated) `onDamagedByAttack` row. This is "the smallest coherent unit that leaves the repo better and green": the `damageAttacker` backlog row CLOSED 3/3 with a passive-field slice smaller than Defiance/Choice Belt (no `BoardCondition`, no seat plumbing, one read site). **Snipe/spread hits on the Active do not retaliate yet** — the recoil folds only at the §8.5 main hit; a documented follow-up (the interpreter's snipeActive/spreadDamage/damageChosen sites would each need the same fold, and each knows the attacker as `ctx.seat`'s Active). **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/damageAttacker.test.ts` (8) — the registry-authored amounts + Tool gate; Cacnea's 30 recoil with the exact event order (`DAMAGE_DEALT` → `COUNTERS_PLACED` → turn end); Cacturne's shared 30; "even if KO'd" (recoil lands as the attack KOs the defender, `COUNTERS_PLACED` before `KNOCKED_OUT`); a lethal recoil KOing the ATTACKER, prized to P2 (the read-before-sweep proof); the `dealt > 0` gate (Yawn, a 0-damage status attack → no recoil); and Custom Trap's Tool gate BOTH ways (bare Stunfisk no recoil / Vitality Band → 50). New fixtures: Cacnea/Cacturne/Stunfisk battlers + `DAMAGE_ATTACKER_DECK`. **Every test passed after one deck rebalance** (2→4 Cacturne copies — a 60-card deck can prize/draw out a 2-of before surgery fields it). No web/API/DB/schema change (the counters surface via the existing `COUNTERS_PLACED` event). engineVersion 0.48.0 → **0.49.0**; 140 → **141** files, 1985 → **1993** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (tsc + lint + vitest). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — the two research sub-agents scoped it, and the passive rides the just-touched continuous/attack pipeline. `/code-review` still NOT run (user-triggered; D96 + D97 + this slice all await it). *Why: the backlog's op-family LABEL named a trigger, but the code-map showed the counter-placing cards are a passive the engine already has the shape for — choosing the smaller idiomatic model (and leaving the genuinely-trigger cards to a follow-up) is the D94/D95 lesson one level up: the catalog + the code, not the backlog label, decide the real shape of the work.* (2026-07-27) |
| D99 | `onDamagedByAttack` — the general reactive TRIGGER D98 deliberately deferred, landing Armarouge "Scorching Armor" (Burn the attacker) (P3-M5, engine 0.50.0) | The natural continuation of D98, and this time the effect genuinely IS a trigger (not a passive): **Armarouge sv03-044 "Scorching Armor"** — VERBATIM re-confirmed against the live D1 catalog before authoring (the D95/D96/D97/D98 lesson — the query also enumerated the WHOLE `onDamagedByAttack` family to fix the slice boundary: Cacnea/Cacturne/Stunfisk already covered by D98's passive, Klawf ex deferred, and Fezandipiti sv06.5-073 is out of the sv01–sv03 backlog scope AND a damage-PREVENTION read, not a post-damage reaction): "If this Pokémon is in the Active Spot and is damaged by an attack from your opponent's Pokémon (even if this Pokémon is Knocked Out), the Attacking Pokémon is now Burned." **Modelled as a `TriggeredAbility` on a new `TriggerTiming` `onDamagedByAttack` — NOT a D98-style passive — because the effect runs an EffectOp program (`applyStatus`) reactively, which is exactly what `TriggeredAbility.program` is for; a passive carries no program.** The scan point is the SAME attack.ts spot D98's recoil uses: right after `DAMAGE_DEALT`, gated on `dealt > 0`, main-hit Active only (so the "in the Active Spot" clause is enforced by the read site, exactly as D98 — a benched holder hit by spread does not react), BEFORE finishAttack's §8.1 sweep (so "even if Knocked Out" is FREE — a lethally-damaged Armarouge still Burns the attacker; the KO case's apply PAUSES at `ko:takePrizes` before the Checkup, so the attacker's `burned` flag is observable pre-cure). The trigger program runs under the DAMAGED Pokémon's seat, so `applyStatus target:"defender"` lands on the ATTACKER's Active (`otherSeat`) — mirrors Trevenant/Mawile (an Ability that runs on its holder and targets the opponent). `activeOnly: true` carries the clause redundantly with the read site (belt-and-braces). **Framework, not just a card:** a new `runDamagedByAttackTriggers` in triggers.ts (mirrors `runCheckupTriggers` — NON-PARKING by construction, so it threads state straight through via `.state`, no settleProgram); `triggersOf` stayed module-private (the new helper is the only caller from attack.ts, keeping the scan machinery in triggers.ts and attack.ts a one-line call). **Klawf ex sv03-120** ("discard an Energy from the Attacking Pokémon" — an opponent-side PARKING choice, the §9.4 shape) is the one remaining `onDamagedByAttack` card, still deferred: it needs the parking-through-settleProgram path a Burn does not. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/scorchingArmor.test.ts` (4) — the registry-authored trigger (id → the exact `TriggeredAbility`); Bite deals 30 → the attacker is Burned with the exact order `DAMAGE_DEALT` → `ABILITY_TRIGGERED`(Scorching Armor, seat p2) → `STATUS_APPLIED`(burned, seat p1); "even if KO'd" (lethal Bite → Burn lands BEFORE `KNOCKED_OUT`, `burned` flag set pre-Checkup, Armarouge prized to P1); the `dealt > 0` gate (Yawn, a 0-damage status → no trigger, attacker un-Burned). Burn's final-state flag is Checkup-coin-dependent in the non-KO case, so those assert via EVENTS (`STATUS_APPLIED`/`ABILITY_TRIGGERED`); the KO case (paused pre-Checkup) asserts the stable flag. New fixtures: an Armarouge sv03-044 battler + a DEDICATED `SCORCHING_ARMOR_DECK` (the DAMAGE_ATTACKER_DECK is seed-pinned — the PSY_PURGE/HAIL_BLADE "dedicated deck" convention). **Every test passed on first run — no behavioural fix.** No web/API/DB/schema change (the Burn surfaces via the existing `STATUS_APPLIED` event; the attacker takes its Burn at the very next Checkup, which the attack cascade runs since attacking ends the turn — correct TCG behaviour). engineVersion 0.49.0 → **0.50.0**; 141 → **142** files, 1993 → **1997** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (tsc + lint + vitest). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — one Explore sub-agent mapped the trigger/attack.ts seams and confirmed the template, and the new trigger rides the just-touched attack.ts spot. `/code-review` still NOT run (user-triggered; D96 + D97 + D98 + this slice all await it). *Why: D98 split the `onDamagedByAttack` label into "passive-shaped" (counters) and "genuinely trigger-shaped" (status/parking-discard); this closes the FIRST trigger-shaped card by building exactly the framework D98 named as the follow-up — reusing D98's scan spot means the Burn variant is a small honest addition (a timing, a helper, a card, a fixture), and the parking Klawf ex stays deferred as the one card that needs more.* (2026-07-27) |
| D100 | `disableAbilities` — the continuous Ability-lock AURA, the first passive that reaches OTHER Pokémon, gating all four Ability read sites (P3-M5, engine 0.51.0) | The resume point's recommended "widest coverage" row, and the backlog's biggest blast radius. **Verbatim re-confirmed against the live D1 before authoring** (the D95→D99 lesson): one query (`abilities_json LIKE '%have no Abilities%'`) returned EXACTLY the six ids the registry now carries and nothing else — **Klefki sv01-096 "Mischievous Lock"** ("As long as this Pokémon is in the Active Spot, Basic Pokémon in play (both yours and your opponent's) have no Abilities, except for Mischievous Lock."), **Spiritomb sv02-089 "Fettered in Misfortune"** ("Basic Pokémon V in play (both yours and your opponent's) have no Abilities." — no Active-Spot clause, so it locks from the BENCH), **Ting-Lu ex sv02-127/-243/-263/-275 "Cursed Land"** ("As long as this Pokémon is in the Active Spot, your opponent's Pokémon in play that have any damage counters on them have no Abilities, except for Pokémon ex."). A second query confirmed no other phrasing of the lock exists in the pool, and that sv01-129 — the OTHER Spiritomb — has no Ability at all (a pinned negative test). **Modelled as ONE new `PassiveEffects` field `disableAbilities?: {stage?, suffix?, side, requiresDamage?, requiresActive?, exemptSuffix?, exemptAbilityNamed?}`** — seven independent narrowing predicates that between them express all three printed sentences, no new op / timing / action / phase. **But it is the FIRST passive field that does not modify its HOLDER** — it reaches other Pokémon — so continuous.ts reads it through a dedicated both-boards pass, `disabledAbilityUids(state)`, returning the set of top uids whose Abilities are off; only a TOP CARD grants an aura (it is a printed Pokémon Ability, never a Tool). **Four read sites consult that Set by uid:** activated (`cardplay.ts useAbility` → the new `ABILITY_DISABLED` error, checked BEFORE the activeOnly/oncePerTurn/cost gates so a locked Pokémon reports the LOCK rather than an incidental refusal), passive (`continuous.ts passivesOf` — drops the top card's printed passive only; the holder's attached **Tools keep working**, they are §7.4 card effects and not Abilities), triggered (`triggers.ts triggersOf` — the single choke covering onPlayToBench / onEvolve / betweenTurns / onDamagedByAttack; **onKnockOut is deliberately NOT gated**, it fires as the Pokémon leaves play) and the HUD (`redact.ts redactedAbilitiesOf` — the row greys with `reason: null`, self-evident like the Active-only greying, so the client never offers a button the engine will refuse). **Auras are collected UNGATED — a lock never turns itself, or another lock, off.** Self-exemption is what the printed text asks for (Klefki's `exemptAbilityNamed`, matched on the TARGET card's printed Ability name, so a mirror match leaves both locks on); lock-vs-lock is the **KNOWN LIMITATION**: an Active Ting-Lu ex opposite a damaged Klefki should by the rules switch Mischievous Lock off, and does not — that needs a fixpoint pass rather than one read, and is a documented follow-up **pinned by a tripwire test** that asserts the current behaviour and says to flip when the fixpoint lands. The three cards in scope never mute a NON-lock ability of another lock's source, so the limitation only bites lock-vs-lock. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/abilityLock.test.ts` (19) — the registry row (all six ids + the sv01-129 negative); the aura scan as a set-membership matrix (empty with no lock in play — the no-op guarantee; Klefki locking every Basic on BOTH boards while skipping a Stage 1 and itself; Klefki dark from the Bench; the mirror match; Spiritomb locking Basic V on both boards FROM THE BENCH and MISSING a VMAX — a suffix match, not a prefix one; Ting-Lu reaching only the opponent's damaged non-ex, with its own damaged bench, an undamaged opponent and a damaged opposing **ex** all clear; Ting-Lu dark from the Bench; the lock-vs-lock tripwire); then a **lock-ON / lock-OFF pair per read site** — `ABILITY_DISABLED` vs the `ABILITY_ACTIVE_ONLY` the same call falls back to (the check-ORDER proof), Cursed Land flipping a Stage 2's activated Ability the instant it takes one counter (Tinkaton is out of both Basic-only locks' reach, so that pair is Cursed Land's alone), Bouffalant's Bouffer suppressed (the same printed 30 lands as 30 vs 10), a locked attacker's Vitality Band still adding its 10 (the Tool exemption), Armarouge's `onDamagedByAttack` Burn suppressed vs firing, and the HUD row greyed vs enabled. **The implementation needed NO behavioural fix** — 18 of 19 passed on the first run and the 19th was a seed whose deck had drawn its Klefki. Fixture work this session: HP/retreat/stage corrected against the live prints (Spiritomb 60, Ting-Lu ex 240/4), and `ABILITY_LOCK_DECK` rebuilt to carry one target per read site AND per predicate field — its `board()` helper **asserts both seats open on the neutral fix-bigbody**, because this deck holds three aura sources and two Pokémon V and any other opening Active would smuggle a live aura onto the board and silently poison every set assertion. Klefki's fixture carries fix-attacker's plain Bite ({C}, 30) instead of its printed "Joust" ({C}, 10 + an unsimulated Tool discard) — a DELIBERATE, commented deviation: Klefki must be the Active to emit its aura, so it must be the attacker, and the lock-ON/lock-OFF arms have to compare the same printed 30. No web/API/DB/schema change. engineVersion 0.50.0 → **0.51.0**; 142 → **143** files, 1997 → **2016** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (tsc + lint + vitest). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer**; `/code-review` still NOT run (user-triggered; D96 + D97 + D98 + D99 + this slice all await it). *Why: the widest row was worth taking before the narrow leftovers because the cost is in the READ SITES, not the cards — once every ability read consults one Set, later locks are registry rows. Landing it as a predicate on the existing passive field (rather than new machinery) kept the diff to one scan function plus four one-line gates, and the honest cost is named rather than hidden: the aura-vs-aura fixpoint is deferred WITH a failing-when-fixed tripwire instead of being quietly wrong.* (2026-07-28) |

## Locked assumptions (override explicitly if they change)
- Bun-workspace monorepo; API + Lobby DO on Cloudflare **Workers** via `wrangler`.
- **Hono** + **Zod**; **Drizzle** over D1. Ingest uses **plain REST fetch +
  our Zod schemas** — the official `@tcgdex/sdk` turned out unnecessary since
  we validate every payload ourselves anyway (changed 2026-07-09, was: SDK).
- Server **sessions** (http-only cookie), not stateless JWT.
- Ingest filters to SV-era but writes the full-history schema.
- attacks/weaknesses/resistances stored as JSON columns for now (promote to
  child tables only if we need to query inside them).
- English (`en`) first; schema leaves room for i18n later.
- The **match engine (P3)** is the actual gameplay; approximate at first,
  improved iteratively.

## These are defaults, not law
Every decision and assumption here is a **default with a rationale**, chosen with
the context available at the time — revisit any of them when the code or new
context reveals a better path. The point of writing them down is to preserve the
*why*, so a later session can judge whether it still holds.

## Adding / changing a decision
Append a row (or a dated subsection for bigger ones) with the choice **and the
one-line why**. When you revise an existing decision, update its row and note
what changed and why. The one hard rule: **don't reverse a decision silently** —
leave a trail so the reasoning is never lost.
| D101 | `onDamagedByAttack` PARKING — Klawf ex "Counterattacking Pincer", the reactive trigger that PARKS an opponent-side discard during the attacker's turn, closing the row 4/4 (P3-M5, engine 0.52.0) | The last card on the `onDamagedByAttack` row, and the one D98/D99 deferred as "needs the parking path, not more timing". **Verbatim re-confirmed against the live D1** (the D95→D100 lesson; a query on `abilities_json LIKE '%damaged by an attack%'` re-fixed the family boundary — Cacnea/Cacturne/Stunfisk done by D98, Armarouge by D99, Fezandipiti sv06.5-073 out of scope): Klawf ex **sv03-120** "Counterattacking Pincer" — "If this Pokémon is in the Active Spot and is damaged by an attack from your opponent's Pokémon (even if this Pokémon is Knocked Out), discard an Energy from the Attacking Pokémon." (Basic Fighting, 220 HP, retreat 3.) **Its reaction is `discardEnergy from:"opponentActive"` (Mawile's op, reused verbatim, `filter: anyEnergy`) run under the DAMAGED seat** — so `discardVictimSeat(damagedSeat)=otherSeat` reaches the ATTACKER's Active, and the DEFENDER chooses which Energy. That choice must PARK during the OPPONENT's turn, which D99's inline non-parking `runDamagedByAttackTriggers` (`.state`-only) could not carry. **Two code-map sub-agents surfaced the clean fit: the `koTrigger` stage, not the `answerer` mechanism.** The whole reactive program belongs to the non-turn player, so — exactly like `runKoTrigger` (which runs on-KO programs during the opponent's turn with `controller = koedSeat`) — the program is folded through settleProgram **under the DAMAGED seat** (`controller = damagedSeat`), making it the answerer by default (`phase.seat = damagedSeat`, NO `decider`/`answerer` needed); the projection reads the real turn owner off the attackEpilogue in `pending` (`koParkActiveSeat`, which already recognises that stage). **Modelled as a new `damagedTrigger` PendingStage** `{seat, uid}` (the twin of `koTrigger`): attack.ts records it at damage time — only when the damaged Active carries such an Ability (via the new `damagedByAttackAbility` gated lookup, which applies the §9 ability-lock through `triggersOf` and the "in the Active Spot" clause), so every other damaging attack keeps its exact pre-slice queue — and seeds it AFTER the attack's own effect program but BEFORE the attackEpilogue: `[damagedTrigger, attackEpilogue]`. flow.ts `runDamagedTrigger` (mirrors `runKoTrigger`) pops itself, runs the program, folds through settleProgram with `resumeTail`; a PARK leaves the attackEpilogue behind it, and resolveEffect (DEFENDER-only, via the existing seat gate) resumes it → finishAttack's §8.1 sweep + turn end. **"Even if Knocked Out" stays free** (the stage precedes the sweep, so the lookup finds Klawf still Active), and Klawf ex being a rule-box ex prizes 2 (D93). **D99's `runDamagedByAttackTriggers` was replaced by `damagedByAttackAbility`; Armarouge's non-parking Burn now rides the SAME staged path — verified byte-identical (scorchingArmor.test.ts 4/4 green unchanged).** Reordering the trigger from "before the effect program" (D99's inline spot) to "after it, as a stage" is also more rules-correct (an attack fully resolves before its damage's reactive triggers). **No web/API/DB/schema/redaction change** — the discard candidates are the attacker's face-up Energy (public-ref family), the park's answerer defaults to `phase.seat = damagedSeat`, and `phaseView`/`redact` already handle the on-KO shape. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/counterattackingPincer.test.ts` (8) — the registry authoring; the park (phase.seat=p2, `answerer` absent, `resumeTail` true, discardable = the attacker's Active Energy); the projection (activeSeat=attacker, waitingSeat=defender); the attacker CANNOT answer (WRONG_SEAT — the routing proof); the defender's pick discarding from the attacker + turn end; "even if KO'd" (park before the sweep, discard before KNOCKED_OUT, ko:takePrizes p1 count 2); the `dealt>0` gate (Yawn → no reaction, attacker keeps both Energy); and a single-Energy FORCED discard (non-parking, exercising the staged trigger's done-branch). New fixtures: a Klawf ex sv03-120 battler + `COUNTERATTACKING_PINCER_DECK` (the attacker fielded with two DIFFERENT Energy so the discard is a real choice — two identical would collapse to a forced pick). engineVersion 0.51.0 → **0.52.0**; 143 → **144** files, 2016 → **2024** tests. | D107 | Mimikyu "Safeguard" (`preventDamageFromExV`) — the FIRST attacker-rule-box-gated damage PREVENTION passive (P3-M5 long tail, engine 0.58.0) | The resume point's **cheapest-first** long-tail candidate after D106 drained the last pure-data-row (all remaining candidates need NEW machinery): "a one-field flag + one read site in the §8 pipeline (not a pure data row, but close)." **Verbatim re-confirmed against the live D1 before authoring** (the D95→D106 lesson): a D1-verification sub-agent read sv02-097's `abilities_json` — "Safeguard": "Prevent all damage done to this Pokémon by attacks from your opponent's Pokémon ex and Pokémon V." (Basic Psychic, 70 HP, ×2 Metal, retreat 1) — and enumerated the FAMILY BOUNDARY: **Mimikyu is the ONLY ex/V-gated total-prevention print in sv01–03.** The sentence-structure siblings gate on a DIFFERENT axis and are a separate family (an energy-type gate, not this one): Dachsbun sv01-099 "Well-Baked Body" ({R}), Bellibolt sv03-078/-201 "Insulator" ({L}), Thundurus sv03-070 "Adverse Weather" (any opponent, but scoped to YOUR Bench). Coin-flip (`Drifting Dodge`) and effect-prevention (`Protective Mycelium` — "Damage is not an effect") are further off. **Modelled as a bare `PassiveEffects.preventDamageFromExV?: true` flag** — the defender-side twin of `damageReductionAfterWR` (Bouffer/Copperajah/Stonjourner): a FULL null instead of a flat subtraction. Like the reduction it modifies only its holder, so it rides `passivesOf` (return-type field + `||=` fold in continuous.ts — a boolean, not a sum) rather than the aura scans (`disabledAbilityUids`/`seatRemovesWeakness`, which reach OTHER Pokémon). **The one difference from the reduction: the gate is on the ATTACKER's rule-box class**, known only at the read sites, so `passivesOf` just reports whether the holder carries the aura and each read site gates it via the NEW `cards.ts isExOrV(card)` — `pokemonSuffixOf(card) === "ex" || === "V"` (NOT `hasRuleBox`, which also matches VMAX/VSTAR/GX and Radiant; the print names ex and V only). A code-map Explore sub-agent mapped all four attack-damage read sites the reduction already covers; **honored Safeguard at ALL FOUR for parity + correctness** (Safeguard has NO "in the Active Spot" clause, so it protects the holder on the Bench too — unlike D98/D106's Active-only recoil): attack.ts main hit (~301, using the already-built `defenderPassives` + in-scope `attacker` Card), interpreter.ts `spreadDamage` (~1318), `placeSnipe` deals-arm (~1398) and `snipeActive` (~1463).
