| D112 | The retreat-LOCK seam — `InPlayPokemon.retreatBlocked`, the bare `preventRetreat` op, and §13.4's paralysis clock reused verbatim as the lifetime (P3-M5, engine 0.63.0) | The biggest single family left unbuilt, and the first effect that is a **CONDITION on a Pokémon with a turn-scoped lifetime** rather than a continuous modifier. **Verbatim re-confirmed against the local D1 before authoring** (the D95→D111 lesson), and the census corrected the shorthand **three** times. (1) **The local pool is 978 cards over SIX sets, not 890 over five** — `sv01`/`sv02`/`sv03`/`sv06.5`/`sve` **plus `swsh10.5`** (Pokémon GO, 88 cards, reg-mark F, `legal_standard=0`), which every prior census silently omitted. (2) The seam is **three sentence shapes, 17 printings / 15 names**, not one shape and 15: **L1** the bare rider `"During your opponent's next turn, the Defending Pokémon can't retreat."` (15 printings / 13 names — and the 15th is **swsh10.5-036 Onix**, missed by every earlier list); **L2** Paldean Clodsire `sv03-128` "Poison Ring", a TWO-clause effect whose lock sentence says `"that Pokémon"`, bound by anaphora to the Poison in front of it; **L3** Snorlax `swsh10.5-055` "Block", a continuous **Ability** aura (`"As long as this Pokémon is in the Active Spot, your opponent's Active Pokémon can't retreat."`) with no turn duration — a different mechanism, left unbuilt. (3) The alternate-wording sweep (`cannot retreat`, `can't be retreated`, `unable to retreat`, `must stay`, `may not retreat`, `can't switch`, `retreating`, U+2019 variants) returns **zero** new hits; `switch this` returns 15, all benign own-side self-switches. **L1 + L2 are built; the slice is derive-only — ZERO registry rows.** Two anchored regexes in effects.ts turn 16 printings into programs, exactly the D110 economics. **The state lives on `InPlayPokemon.retreatBlocked`, deliberately NOT in `SpecialConditions`** — that struct is pinned field-by-field by `RedactedConditions` (`@luminous/schema`) and `BattleConditions` (the playmat), and its members render as status chips, tick at the Checkup and gate ATTACKING; a retreat block does none of those. Putting it there would have forced a wire + playmat diff to model something that is not a §12 condition. The cost of keeping it out is that the three "an effect of an attack ends here" sites (`clearOnLeavingActive`, evolve, the interpreter's switch) each clear one more field by hand — accepted, and each is covered by a test. **The op is a bare `{ op: "preventRetreat" }`** with no `target` (D104's minimal-shape rule): every print in the pool locks the DEFENDER. It is **not** an `applyStatus` variant, because `StatusName` is a closed five-value union pinned by two exhaustive `Record`s plus the wire schema. **The LIFETIME is the §13.4 paralysis clear, reused with no new machinery** — one more `endedSeat`-only block in `runCheckup`. Follow the seats and it is exactly the printed duration: applied during the ATTACKER's turn, it survives the Checkup that immediately follows (`endedSeat` is then the attacker), holds through the locked player's whole next turn, and lifts at the Checkup that ends it. It inherits paralysis's one caveat — a self-applied block would lift a turn early — which no print can reach, since both derived shapes target the defender. **Read sites: three `&&` terms, no new plumbing** — a new `RETREAT_PREVENTED` error code at turn.ts's retreat gate (its own code, not `STATUS_PREVENTS_RETREAT`: a client showing "Asleep/Paralyzed" there would be lying), one term in redact.ts's `can` (**no new wire field** — the client needs to know it cannot retreat, not why) and one in the web `GameHud.tsx`. New `RETREAT_BLOCKED`/`RETREAT_BLOCK_ENDED` events with their own log rows; the early clears stay SILENT, announced by their own POKEMON_SWITCHED/POKEMON_EVOLVED row. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/retreatLock.test.ts` (**16**) on a DEDICATED `RETREAT_LOCK_DECK` (every retreat-COST deck is seed-pinned) — both derivations; the typographic-apostrophe twin; four anchor guards, including **Clodsire's lock clause alone deriving to null** (matching it loosely would have swallowed the Poison in front of it); the zero-registry-row claim; a fixture-text-verbatim guard (the sentence is load-bearing on the deriver path); defender-only scoping with both benches asserted clean; the **not-a-§12-condition** assertion (no `STATUS_APPLIED`, `conditions` untouched); Poison Ring's two clauses in printed order; the index-keyed negative (Muddy Hammer locks nothing); the gate refusing **the very retreat the same board otherwise allows**; the block NOT preventing attacking; the wire `can` closing with the cost unchanged; the full lifetime cycle (survives the attacker's Checkup → refused on the locked turn → `RETREAT_BLOCK_ENDED` at the next Checkup → the same retreat then succeeds); the one-clear-per-lock count; and both early clears (a **Switch dodges the lock** — the rules refuse retreating, not being switched — and evolution sheds it). New fixtures: a faithful Tarountula `sv02-016` + Paldean Clodsire `sv03-128` + the deck. engineVersion 0.62.0 → **0.63.0**; 154 → **155** files, 2121 → **2137** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (exit 0). Zero NEW formatter churn (verified by stashing: every file this touches that `biome format` flags was already flagged at HEAD; the new test file is format-clean). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer**; two sub-agents scoped it (one D1 verification, one code-map Explore). `/code-review` still NOT run (D96–D112 all await it). *Why: the seam needed a turn-scoped per-Pokémon flag and the engine had none — but it already had one in disguise. Paralysis's `endedSeat`-only Checkup clear IS "until the end of your opponent's next turn"; recognizing that turned the hard part of the slice into four lines. The transferable lesson is the one that decided where the flag lives: **a struct that something else pins field-by-field is not a free place to put a new field.** `SpecialConditions` looked like the obvious home and would have dragged the wire schema and the playmat into an engine-only slice; the honest read of "is this a §12 condition?" was no, and the shape followed. Also: the census correction is the third in a row, and this one found a whole SET (`swsh10.5`) that earlier sweeps had never looked at — so "the ingested pool" must be re-derived from the DB, never quoted from these docs.* (2026-08-01) |
| D113 | `preventOpponentActiveRetreat` — Snorlax "Block", the 5th aura scan: the retreat-LOCK seam CLOSES at 3/3 shapes (P3-M5 long tail, engine 0.64.0) | D112's resume point named this first, and it landed as the smallest aura slice yet. **Verbatim re-confirmed against the local D1 before authoring** (the D95→D112 lesson): a D1 sub-agent read the row — `swsh10.5-055` Snorlax, Basic Colorless, 150 HP, retreat 4, ×2 Fighting, reg mark F; Ability "Block" = "As long as this Pokémon is in the Active Spot, your opponent's Active Pokémon can't retreat."; attack "Collapse" {C}{C}{C}{C} 150 "This Pokémon is now Asleep." — and re-ran the whole-pool lock census with COALESCE on every text column: **it is the ONLY continuous-Ability retreat lock in the ingested 978 cards, and has no reprint** (`name LIKE '%Snorlax%'` → exactly one row). The sweep also settled the apostrophe question pool-wide: **zero rows anywhere carry U+2019** — all 459 apostrophe-bearing rows are ASCII, so lock matching never needs the typographic twin. **The `legal_standard=0` caveat D112 raised was a NON-ISSUE and is now retired**: 851 of 978 ingested cards are `legal_standard=0`, including **every card in sv01/sv02/sv03** (the flag tracks the post-G rotation; only sv06.5's 96 reg-mark H cards and most blank-mark Energies are Standard). The engine has simulated non-Standard cards since D1 and has no legality concept; format legality is a deck-builder (P2) concern. **Pick engine slices by mechanism, never by `legal_standard`.** **Modelled as a bare `PassiveEffects.preventOpponentActiveRetreat?: true`** (D104's minimal-shape rule: one print, unconditional once both ends are Active, nothing left to parameterise), read through a new continuous.ts scan **`opposingRetreatBlocked(state, pokemon)`** — the **fifth** member of the aura-scan family and the BOOLEAN cross-board twin of D111's `opposingRetreatSurcharge`, sharing its derived-seat trick and its §9 `disabledAbilityUids` gate. **The two differ in exactly one line, and the printed text is the whole reason:** Trap Territory's sentence scopes only the TARGET ("your opponent's **Active** Pokémon's Retreat Cost"), so a benched Spidops ex still imposes it; this print OPENS with "As long as this Pokémon is in the Active Spot", which scopes the SOURCE too — so the source list is the opponent's Active **alone**, and the clause is enforced by the SCAN rather than by a new `activeOnly` field (the `damageAttacker` precedent). **It rides NEITHER D112's `retreatBlocked` flag NOR the `preventRetreat` op**: a continuous aura has no turn duration, so there is nothing to apply and nothing to clear — it is read live and lapses the instant either end leaves the Active Spot. **Read at the RETREAT GATE, never folded into `effectiveRetreatCost` — a block is not a cost**, so the printed/modified cost stands and there is simply no legal retreat to spend it on (the wire still reports the real `cost` beside `can: false`). The three D112 read sites take one more `&&` term each (turn.ts's gate, redact.ts's `can`, the web `GameHud.tsx`) and nothing else: **no new events** (a continuous aura announces nothing), **no new error code** — `RETREAT_PREVENTED` is reused, with a different *message* only, because "an attack effect" would be a lie for an Ability aura — no new op, no wire field, no API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. The two provenances are two independent guards at one gate, which is what makes them compose for free. **The family's FIRST §9 POSITIVE.** Klefki `sv01-096` "Mischievous Lock" = `{ stage: "Basic", side: "both", requiresActive: true, exemptAbilityNamed: "Mischievous Lock" }` — it silences BASIC sources only, which is why Florges, Clefable ex and Spidops ex (all Stage 1/2) could record only the negative. **Snorlax is a Basic**, so an Active Klefki across the board really does take Block down — and the Pokémon thereby freed to retreat is the Klefki itself. §9 reachability in this family is a function of the source's STAGE. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/block.test.ts` (**12**) on a DEDICATED `BLOCK_DECK` (every retreat-cost and retreat-lock deck is seed-pinned) — the bare-flag registry row; a fixture-text-verbatim guard (HP/retreat/stage/weakness/both text rows, ASCII apostrophes asserted); the block applying, with the pre-surgery control; the **SOURCE clause** (a BENCHED Snorlax blocks nothing — Trap Territory's exact inverse, located by card id so the guard is not vacuous); the target clause (a benched target reads false); own-board scoping asserted in BOTH directions off one board (P1's own Snorlax frees P1 and blocks P2); the §9 positive plus its lock-free control; the cost unchanged under the block; the wire `{cost: 2, can: false}` against `{cost: 2, can: true}`; the gate refusing **the very retreat the same board otherwise allows**; the block NOT preventing attacking and writing nothing onto the Pokémon (no `STATUS_APPLIED`, `conditions` untouched, `retreatBlocked` still false — it is neither a §12 condition nor D112's flag); and the composition with D112's flag (both at once, then each provenance alone, all `RETREAT_PREVENTED`). New fixtures: a faithful Snorlax `swsh10.5-055` (the first `swsh10.5` fixture carrying an Ability) + the deck, which needed a 4th body (`fix-attacker`) the spec had not foreseen because the "does not block attacking" case needs the *blocked* Pokémon to have an attack, and it cannot be the Klefki. The suite pins BOTH Active spots before each surgery: `BLOCK_DECK` is Basic-dense, so an unpinned starter could itself be an aura source or a §9 lock and every control would be seed-luck. engineVersion 0.63.0 → **0.64.0**; 155 → **156** files, 2137 → **2149** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (exit 0: tsc + biome lint + vitest). Zero new formatter churn (57 files flagged before and after — note the real pre-existing count is **57**, not the "~45" D111 recorded, which was the default 20-diagnostic cap misread). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer**; two sub-agents scoped it (one D1 verification, one code-map Explore) and one implemented it. `/code-review` still NOT run (D96–D113 all await it). *Why: the transferable lesson is the one D111 half-found and this slice sharpened — **each Active/Basic clause in a printed sentence scopes exactly one end, and you read them off one end at a time.** Two scans that are otherwise the same walk differ only in which end they gate, and getting that backwards is silent: a benched-source bug produces a board that simply never blocks, with no failing assertion anywhere. Second: the §9 gate's reachability is not a property of the gate, it is the source's STAGE against the lock's predicate — worth checking per slice instead of copying the previous suite's "structurally unreachable" comment. Third, the meta-lesson: a resume point's caveat is a hypothesis, not a finding. "`legal_standard=0`, decide whether the engine cares" cost one query to disprove, and would have cost a whole slice to route around.* (2026-08-01) |
| D114 | `noRetreatCostSelf` — Wimpod "Punk Out", the 6th scan and the first SELF-only one: the retreat-COST seam CLOSES at 10/10 (P3-M5 long tail, engine 0.65.0) | D113's resume point named this first, as "the last known open print on the retreat-COST seam", and it was. **Verbatim re-confirmed against the local D1 before authoring** (the D95→D113 lesson): a D1 sub-agent read the row — `swsh10.5-025` Wimpod, Basic Water, 70 HP, **retreat 3**, ×2 Lightning, no resistance, reg mark F, Common, `legal_standard=0`/`legal_expanded=1`; Ability "Punk Out" = "If your opponent has any Pokémon V in play, this Pokémon has no Retreat Cost."; attack "Gnaw" {W} 10, no effect key — **no reprint** (one Wimpod row pool-wide) — and re-ran the cost census with COALESCE on every text column: `%retreat cost%` returns **exactly 10 rows**, the other nine all built (D108–D111), and `%no retreat cost%` returns exactly **2** (Clefable ex + this). The card's rules text contains **no apostrophe at all**, neither ASCII nor U+2019, so the anchor question does not arise; `Pokémon` is a real U+00E9 and the weakness value a real U+00D7. **The shape had no precedent: a SELF-only set-to-zero with a CROSS-BOARD predicate.** Every retreat modifier before it either reaches OTHER Pokémon through a dedicated aura scan or modifies its holder through the seat-free `passivesOf` fold; this one modifies nobody but its holder yet must look across the table to decide. **Modelled as `PassiveEffects.noRetreatCostSelf?: { requiresOpponentSuffixInPlay?: PokemonSuffix }`**, read through a new continuous.ts scan **`hasFreeRetreatSelf(state, pokemon)`** — the **sixth** member of the aura-scan family. **Deliberately NOT a refinement of `noRetreatCostAura`**: that field frees ALL OF YOUR Pokémon satisfying its clause, this one frees exactly its holder, and conflating them behind one field would have silently freed the whole board the moment a Wimpod hit the Bench. **The predicate is a bespoke optional `PokemonSuffix` field, not a `BoardCondition` member**, for two reasons: the direct precedent is `damageBonusBeforeWRIfTarget: { targetSuffix: PokemonSuffix }` in the same interface (when the gate is a printed SUFFIX rather than a seat-relative board fact, `PassiveEffects` carries the suffix directly), and structurally it is forced — `conditionHolds` lives in **interpreter.ts, which imports continuous.ts**, so reading a `BoardCondition` from a continuous scan would close an import cycle. The refinement is OPTIONAL per D104's minimal-shape rule: a future unconditional "this Pokémon has no Retreat Cost" print just omits it. `pokemonSuffixOf` matches `" VMAX"`/`" VSTAR"` before the bare `" V"` arm, so `=== "V"` is **exactly** the printed "Pokémon V" by construction. **"In play" means Active + Bench** — the sentence carries no Active clause at *either* end, so a BENCHED opposing V satisfies it, and reading it as Active-only would have been silent (the D113 one-end-at-a-time rule, applied to a sentence where the answer is "neither end"). **Zero call-site churn:** the seat is derived exactly as the five sibling scans derive theirs (find the side holding this top uid) purely so "your opponent" resolves, which keeps `effectiveRetreatCost(state, pokemon)`'s signature and ALL FOUR of its readers (turn.ts's retreat gate, redact.ts's `redactedRetreatOf`, attack.ts's `scaledAttackDamage`, the web `GameHud.tsx`) untouched. `effectiveRetreatCost`'s set-to-zero tier now has TWO members (`hasFreeRetreatAura(…) \|\| hasFreeRetreatSelf(…)`), either one alone zeroing the cost ahead of every ± delta. **No new op, event, error code or wire field; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff.** The scan reads only the TOP CARD's passive, not attached Tools (unlike `passivesOf`) — no Tool in the ingested pool grants free retreat, and a future Air Balloon would fold the tool list in here, un-§9-gated, a Tool being no Ability. **The family's SECOND §9 POSITIVE, and this time source and beneficiary are the same card:** Wimpod is a **Basic**, so an Active Klefki `sv01-096` "Mischievous Lock" silences Punk Out and the printed 3 snaps back — D113's rule (reachability follows the SOURCE's stage) confirmed on a second card. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/punkOut.test.ts` (**16**) on a DEDICATED `PUNK_OUT_DECK` (every other retreat-cost and retreat-lock deck is seed-pinned) — the registry row; the "only row carrying the field" census claim (swept over `FIXTURE_POOL` plus the nine other seam ids, since `REGISTRY` is not exported); a fixture-text-verbatim guard (ability + attack + hp/retreat/stage/types/weakness, and the byte facts: no U+0027, no U+2019, the `é` present); no V opposite → 3; an opposing ACTIVE V → 0; an opposing **BENCHED** V → 0 with the Active asserted non-V; own-side V frees nothing while the same V opposite frees; the whole thing mirrored onto P2's board; VMAX opposite → 3 and ex opposite → 3 (the literal reading of "Pokémon V"); **the SELF-only test** — on the very board where Wimpod reads 0, a `fix-retreat2` teammate still reads 2, the case that would catch modelling it as an aura; the §9 positive with its lock-free control; the Beach Court composition (3 → 2 alone, → 0 with the V, the set-to-zero beating the ± delta); the real RETREAT action (free retreat succeeds, `RETREATED` emitted, Energy NOT discarded — and without the V the same action is `RETREAT_COST_MISMATCH`); and the wire (`{cost: 3, can: false}` → `{cost: 0, can: true}`, no new field). New fixtures: a faithful Wimpod `swsh10.5-025` + the deck; the `board()` helper **throws if any seed opens a Pokémon V on either side**, so no control can go vacuous. engineVersion 0.64.0 → **0.65.0**; 156 → **157** files, 2149 → **2165** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (exit 0: tsc + biome lint + vitest); clean diff (4 engine files + 1 new test), no formatter churn. **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer**; two sub-agents scoped it (one D1 verification, one code-map Explore) and one implemented it. `/code-review` still NOT run (D96–D114 all await it). *Why: the transferable lesson is that **"needs a dedicated scan" and "is an aura" are different questions**, and the family's name had been quietly conflating them. The rule that actually decides between a scan and the seat-free `passivesOf` fold is **does the effect need to know a seat?** — reach is only the most common reason it might. Second: a printed SUFFIX gate belongs on the `PassiveEffects` field, not in `BoardCondition`, and the import direction (interpreter → continuous) makes that more than a style preference — if a continuous scan ever genuinely needs the shared condition vocabulary, move `conditionHolds` down to a module both can import rather than adding the back-edge. Third, on scope: this closes the cost seam at 10/10 **against the ingested 978 cards**, and the live remote D1's ~54 `%retreat cost%` printings are the honest measure of how provisional that is.* (2026-08-01) |
| D115 | The CONDITIONAL flat attack-damage bonus — "If \<clause\>, this attack does N more damage.", modelled as a fourth `DamageCountSource` that is a 0-or-1 INDICATOR (P3-M5 long tail, engine 0.66.0) | **Both leads the D114 resume point named were costed first, and BOTH were corrected — the first fatally.** (1) **Slither Wing `sv06.5-026` is DEAD**: the resume point's own caveat ("check first whether the catalog even carries a Future subtype field") resolved to **no**. `PRAGMA table_info(cards)` has no subtype/tag column and no column that could hold one; the upstream tcgdex `suffix` field IS parsed (`packages/schema/src/tcgdex/card.ts`) but **dropped before the D1 insert**, and it carries the rule-box suffix (`ex`/`V`) anyway, never the Ancient/Future tag. "Future Pokémon" is therefore a **name list, not a predicate**, and in the ingested pool it is a **1-card list** — only Iron Moth `sv06.5-009` could ever satisfy it — that rots the moment sv04/sv05 ingest. Not built; recorded as blocked on an ingest change. (2) **The "Stadium in play" sub-shape is a SINGLETON, not 5 printings**: the resume point grouped `sv02-096` Palossand with `sv02-180`/`-251` Falkner and `sv02-130`/`-244` Paldean Clodsire ex, but Falkner is **already built** (D40's `conditionGate`), and Clodsire ex's sentence is on an **Ability**, is *presence* not ownership ("if **a** Stadium is in play", not "if **you have** a Stadium in play"), and belongs to a different seam. Only Palossand is new, and only 1 printing. **The census that mattered was the one neither lead asked for.** Sweeping the whole 978-card pool for the *skeleton* rather than the *predicate* found that "If \<clause\>, this attack does N more damage." is **32 distinct sentences / 43 printings** (excluding the 26 coin-flip riders, a different mechanism) — the largest unbuilt attack-text family in the pool, and one where the MECHANISM is shared and each predicate is an independent one-row extension. Also settled by the sweep: the pool holds **no** trailing "… more damage if …", **no** "If …, this attack does N LESS damage." twin, and **no** sentence combining a leading `If` with a trailing "for each" — so this bucket and the three built scaling buckets are **disjoint by construction**, which is what makes an anchored `^If` regex safe. **The design: a fourth `DamageCountSource` member `{ kind: "boardCondition"; cond: BoardCondition }`, honestly degenerate — a 0-or-1 INDICATOR.** `per × count` is then the printed flat bonus when the clause holds and 0 when it does not, so the whole family reuses the additive `AttackDamageBonus` fold **whole**: no new fold in attack.ts, no new branch in the §8.5 pipeline, no new event, no new op, no registry row, no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. The only structural change is that `scaledAttackDamage` gains the **ATTACKER's** seat — it knew only `defenderSeat`, and a `BoardCondition` is relative to whose board it reads. This is D110's "a member, not a mechanism" rule applied a second time, and the D110 payoff repeats: `effectSimulated`/`modifierSimulated` both flip purely because `scaling !== null`, so the printed `+` and the sentence stop being flagged **whether or not the condition holds** — which is correct, and is asserted in both directions. **The clause table is a `Map<string, BoardCondition>`, not an object literal** — the key is arbitrary PRINTED text, and an object lookup on arbitrary text can hit `Object.prototype` (`"constructor"`, `"toString"`) and return something that is not a `BoardCondition` at all. **An UNMAPPED clause falls through to `null` and stays LOUD** (`ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED`): silently scoring an unrecognised condition at 0 would be a wrong number with no failure, where the loud path is a visible gap. Four clauses mapped, **11 printings / 11 card ids / 7 distinct cards, ZERO registry rows** — every card derives straight off its printed sentence. **Two of the four reuse `BoardCondition` members that already existed** (`yourStadiumInPlay` → Palossand `sv02-096`; `morePrizesThanOpponent` → Gyarados `sv02-043`), making this family the D40 vocabulary's **fourth consumer** and the first to reach it through printed ATTACK text rather than a registry row — the vocabulary paid off five years of slices later with two cards for zero new predicate code. Two members are new: `opponentActiveDamaged` (Meowscarada ex `sv02-015/-231/-256/-271` +120, Gyarados ex `sv01-045/-225` +180 — 6 printings, the biggest single clause) and `opponentActiveIsEvolution` (Seviper `sv02-137` +50, Houndoom `sv03-133` +70, Toxicroak `sv06.5-024` +90), the latter reusing **D105's `evolveFromOf(card) !== null`** verbatim so the Evolution reading cannot drift from the `evolutionPokemon` CardFilter's. **The printed word "already" is a TIMING word, not a predicate refinement**: `opponentActiveDamaged` is read at DECLARATION, before this attack's own damage lands, so an undamaged defender cannot bootstrap the bonus with the very hit it is scaling — enforced by the read site, asserted explicitly (undamaged → 180, not 360). `conditionNote` deliberately drops the "already" (a tooltip fragment is read in the present tense). `conditionalDamage.test.ts` (**28**) on a DEDICATED `CONDITIONAL_DAMAGE_DECK`: all four derivations; the anchor guards (the coin-flip rider, a lowercase `if`, a missing period, appended trailing text, and a well-formed but **UNMAPPED** real clause — Venoshock's `sv01-128`/`sv03-129` "is Poisoned" — proving an unrecognised condition stays loud); both derivers' mutual disjointness with `deriveAttackEffect` and `deriveAttackDamageMultiplier`; each of the four board effects with its control; the Stadium **OWNERSHIP** case (the opponent's Beach Court in the same shared zone does NOT satisfy it); the "already" timing case; the Basic-vs-Evolution pair; the §8.5 fold order (the bonus lands BEFORE Weakness — Seviper into Palossand is `(50+50)×2 = 200`); no `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` in **either** the true or the false case; `conditionHolds`/`conditionNote` for both new members incl. the null-Active edge and both seats; and a frozen-board purity check. New fixtures: faithful Palossand `sv02-096` (one of the few rows with a non-empty resistance, and it mixes an int-damage attack with a string-damage one), Gyarados `sv02-043`, Gyarados ex `sv01-045` and Seviper `sv02-137` (a **Basic**, so it fields in one surgery) + the deck. engineVersion 0.65.0 → **0.66.0**; `bun run check` **GREEN (exit 0): 157 → 158 files, 2165 → 2193 tests**. |
| D116 | The SELF-SCOPED clause — a printed pronoun resolves in the CLAUSE TABLE, not in `conditionHolds`; + 4 more conditional-damage clauses, 7 printings / 6 cards on ZERO registry rows (P3-M5 long tail, engine 0.67.0) | D115's resume point flagged one real design question and this slice answers it. "If **this Pokémon** has any damage counters on it" (Talonflame `sv02-030` +90, Darmanitan `sv03-035` +60) is the family's first clause whose subject is the card itself, and `BoardCondition` is **shared** — `trainerPlayableIf` (a Trainer play gate) and the HUD's grey-out read the same union, and a Trainer has no self. **Resolution: name the member for what it READS, and resolve the pronoun in the `CONDITIONAL_DAMAGE_CLAUSES` table.** The member is `yourActiveDamaged` — a plain seat-relative board fact, meaningful on every consumer — and the table row maps the printed "this Pokémon" onto it. That reading is EXACT, not an approximation: §8 lets only the Active declare an attack, so on the one path this sentence can arrive by, "this Pokémon" IS "your Active". **No second union, no `context` parameter threaded through `conditionHolds`, no split evaluator** — the alternative (a self-scoped kind that means "the attacker" and is nonsense at the play gate) would have put a member in the vocabulary that two of its five consumers must never author. Reusable form: *when a printed sentence's subject is context-dependent, resolve it at the site that knows the context and keep the shared vocabulary context-free.* Three more clauses ride along, all reading fields already on the model: **`yourBenchDamaged`** (Tyranitar ex `sv03-066`/`-211` "Lightning Rampage" +100 — the printed plural "your Benched Pokémon have any damage counters on them" is the game's usual collective "any of them", the Active is deliberately excluded, an empty Bench is false), **`opponentActivePoisoned`** (Seviper `sv01-128` and Paldean Clodsire `sv03-129`, both printing "Venoshock" +120 — Poison is a counter AMOUNT on the model, so "is Poisoned" is `poisonDamage > 0`, exactly as the Checkup reads it) and **`opponentActiveHasSpecialCondition`** (Sableye `sv02-136` "Unseen Claw" +70, the pool's only printing — reuses `presentStatuses` verbatim so "affected" cannot drift from the status chips). The table goes 4 → 8 entries and covers **18 of the pool's 43** leading-`If` printings. Census re-derived against the local D1 before authoring and it **confirmed D115's shorthand exactly** (43 printings / 32 distinct sentences / **26 distinct clauses** — the clause count was new); the clause strings were re-read char-for-char, and the sweep turned up the one real trap: three damage-PLACEMENT sentences contain "has any damage counters on it" (Yveltal `sv06.5-035`, Vespiquen ex `sv03-096`/`-212`) which a substring matcher would have swallowed — the whole-sentence anchor does not. The "already" in the opponent-facing twin is the **sole lexical discriminator** between the two damage-counter clauses. Cost: 4 union members, 4 `conditionHolds` arms, 4 `conditionNote` arms, 4 table rows — **no new op, event, error code, registry row or wire field, no fold change, no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff.** `conditionClause.test.ts` (46) on a dedicated `CONDITION_CLAUSE_DECK`; the "unmapped stays LOUD" witness moved off Venoshock (now mapped) onto Ceruledge `sv02-098`. |
| D117 | `yourActivePoisoned` + `opponentActiveIsExOrV` — the clause table's THIRD wave: the SELF-Poison twin and the rule-box clause, 4 printings / 2 cards on ZERO registry rows (P3-M5 long tail, engine 0.68.0) | D116's resume point named this slice first ("best printings-per-unit-of-work left, near-zero design risk") and it was exactly that: **2 union members, 2 `conditionHolds` arms, 2 `conditionNote` arms, 2 table rows, no new op, event, error code, registry row or wire field, no fold change, no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff.** The table goes **8 → 10 entries, 18 → 22 of the pool's 43 leading-`If` printings**. **Verbatim re-confirmed against the local D1 before authoring** (the D95→D116 lesson): all three Okidogi ex rows (`sv06.5-036`/`-082`/`-090`) are **byte-identical in rules text** (one distinct `hex(attacks_json)` across the three; `abilities_json` NULL on all three — only id/local id/rarity/illustrator differ), so **one fixture serves three ids**; Ceruledge `sv02-098` is the pool's only printing of its clause. A COALESCE'd sweep for `is Poisoned` returns **exactly 5 printings, all now mapped**, split 3/2 between the SELF and CROSS-BOARD readings — the two clauses are **one possessive apart and read opposite boards**, which is the whole argument for keying the table on whole printed clauses and never substrings. **`yourActivePoisoned` is D116's pronoun rule applied a second time**: the member is named for the board fact it READS (your Active), the CLAUSE TABLE resolves the printed "this Pokémon", `conditionHolds` stays context-free and shared with the play gate and the HUD. The rule has now been used twice and can be considered settled. **`opponentActiveIsExOrV` reuses D107's `isExOrV` verbatim** — ex and V ONLY, never VMAX/VSTAR/GX — so "Pokémon ex or Pokémon V" reads exactly as Mimikyu "Safeguard" reads it and the two cannot drift; deliberately **NOT `hasRuleBox`**, and the VMAX case is the test that catches conflating them. | D122 | The POKÉMON TOOL clause — `yourActiveHasToolAttached`, a BARE TAG on a LITERAL row; 1 printing / 1 card / 1 clause on ZERO registry rows, and the LAST clause in the family that reads a field the model already carries (P3-M5, engine 0.73.0) | Greedent `sv01-152` "Enhanced Fang" ("If this Pokémon has a Pokémon Tool attached", +80) now SIMULATES — the slice D121's resume point named first, and it was exactly the cheapest thing left. **The clause table goes 16 → 17 literal rows + 2 templates, covering 21 of the pool's 26 distinct clauses / 37 of its 43 printings**, and with it the seam of "clauses that read state the engine already has" **CLOSES**: every one of the five clauses left needs new per-turn state (Lokix's evolved-this-turn, Pikachu's Supporter-this-turn, Revavroom ex/Golisopod's promoted-this-turn), an INGEST change (Slither Wing's Future tag) or the coin-flip mechanism. **The cheapness is structural, not luck: `InPlayPokemon.tools` is already a uid array, and every uid in it reached it through `attachTool` (cardplay.ts), which refuses anything whose `trainerType` is not "Tool" before it can land.** So — unlike D118's Energy clauses, where an Energy card can pay for a type it is not named after and the predicate must therefore be PROVISION — **there is no reading question here at all and no continuous.ts helper: a Tool is a Tool, and the predicate is a length test.** The one real design call is **`> 0` rather than `=== 1`**: §7.4 caps a Pokémon at one Tool, but that cap is enforced **at the attach gate**, and Revavroom ex `sv03-156`'s "Tune-Up" Ability raises it to 4 — a predicate written to the cap would answer FALSE on a board the printed sentence plainly describes. The reusable form: **a rule enforced at a GATE is not an invariant of the state the gate writes into; read the state, not the rule.** D116's pronoun rule, FOURTH use, and it matters more here than usual because **two OTHER cards print this clause's exact words under a different consequent** — Genesect `sv06.5-040`'s "ACE Nullifier" is **byte-identical through the comma** and Stunfisk `sv03-112`'s "Custom Trap" carries the same words as a **middle conjunct**; both are ABILITIES, and the whole-sentence anchor is the only thing keeping a damage bonus off them. **Verbatim re-confirmed against the local D1 before authoring** and the census re-confirmed **43 printings / 32 distinct (clause, N) pairs / 26 distinct clauses** row by row, plus: no reprint of `sv01-152` (the only "Greedent" in the catalog, and its `attacks_json` is unique); the effect string is **77 code points / 79 UTF-8 bytes** with no apostrophe of either kind; **`for each Pokémon Tool` occurs ZERO times in 978 cards**, so there is no per-Tool scaling family to anticipate; and — a fact worth carrying — the ENTIRE text pool contains exactly **two** distinct non-ASCII code points, U+00D7 `×` and U+00E9 `é`, with **no U+2019 anywhere**. **Cost: 1 union member, 1 `conditionHolds` arm, 1 `conditionNote` arm, 1 table row, 1 test-surgery helper. No new op, event, error code, registry row, wire field or fold change; no continuous.ts diff; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff.** The note ROUND-TRIPS to the printed clause with only the pronoun swapped. **The TEST design turns on a harness fact: `attachTool` refuses UNAUTHORED Tools (`TRAINER_NOT_SIMULATED`), so no Tool in the pool attaches successfully while doing nothing** — the clause cannot be armed by an inert card through the real action. Two real Tools are used instead and they are the suite's sharpest pair: **Bravery Charm `sv02-173` is the SILENT arm** (`basicHpBonus: 50`, and Greedent is a **Stage 1**, so `effectiveMaxHp` is asserted still 120 and every point of the 80-point swing is the clause's), while **Vitality Band `sv01-197` is the LOUD arm** (`damageBonusBeforeWR: 10`, arming the clause AND adding its own bonus in the SAME pre-W/R step, so 170 against the Charm's 160 — a fold that read the Tool's passive AS the clause would score the two identically). `toolClause.test.ts` (**28**) on a dedicated `TOOL_CLAUSE_DECK`: the verbatim + byte guard (77/79, the `bytes = codePoints + count("Pokémon")` invariant, precomposed é, no apostrophe) with the Genesect sentence as a **non-ASCII-and-apostrophe-bearing COMPANION** so the claim is about this string rather than the module; the derivation, N-from-the-sentence and the printed-0 guard; the bare-tag proof (`Object.keys(cond)` is exactly `["kind"]`); **both REAL near-miss Abilities refused on all three derivers** plus eight constructed rewrites (plural, "attached to it", "any", **de-accented**, opponent-scoped, bench-scoped, Tool-by-name) and the outer anchor guards; deriver disjointness and the **index-keyed negative** (index 0 "Bite" carries NO `effect` key at all on the D1 row); ZERO registry rows for `sv01-152` asserted three ways, with the two Tools' programs asserted PRESENT so the board cases are not vacuous; control 80 / Charm 160 / Band 170; **armed BY THE GAME** through a real §7.4 `attachTool` (`TOOL_ATTACHED` then 160); **a Tool on your own BENCH arming nothing** — the same board as the control with one Tool moved one spot, which is the case that catches "any of your Pokémon"; **seat asymmetry from BOTH seats, each paired with the same Tool on the attacker's own Active** so neither half can pass for want of a Tool anywhere; the §8.5 fold order ((80+80)×2 = **320**, control 160) against a synthetic `fix-colorless-weak`; no `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` in EITHER direction; direct `conditionHolds`/`conditionNote` incl. the **TWO-Tool case** (the `> 0` proof), the null-Active edge and the benched-only edge; and a frozen-board purity pair. New: faithful Greedent `sv01-152`, synthetic `fix-colorless-weak` (the `fix-darkness-weak` precedent), `TOOL_CLAUSE_DECK`, and **`attachToolFromDeck` — the harness's first Tool surgery** (the real action is turn-gated and hand-scoped, so it cannot reach the OPPONENT's board, and it deliberately does not enforce the §7.4 cap the Tune-Up Ability lifts). `conditionalDamage.test.ts`'s loud witness was **RE-POINTED, not deleted** → **Pikachu `swsh10.5-027`** "you played a Supporter card from your hand during this turn", chosen because it needs NEW PER-TURN STATE and so cannot be mapped out from under the case by a one-row extension the way its last three occupants were. engineVersion 0.72.0 → **0.73.0**; `bun run check` **GREEN (exit 0): 164 → 165 files, 2457 → 2485 tests**. Zero new formatter churn, verified with `git stash -u` (46 files flagged in `packages/engine/src` before and after — note the earlier `git stash` without `-u` left the new untracked test on disk and made the comparison meaningless). |
| D123 | The SUPPORTER-PLAYED clause — `youPlayedSupporterThisTurn`, a BARE TAG on a LITERAL row costing ZERO new state, and the SEAT GUARD a single-bag per-turn allowance forces; 1 printing / 1 card / 1 clause on ZERO registry rows (P3-M5 clause family, engine 0.74.0) | Pikachu `swsh10.5-027` "Buddy Bolt" ("If you played a Supporter card from your hand during this turn", +30) is a **HAPAX** in the ingested 978 cards. D122 declared the cheap era over — every remaining clause buying new state, an ingest change or a new mechanism — and that was true of the **BOARD**, but this clause reads a **TURN EVENT**, and the fact it wants **already existed**: §7.2's one-Supporter-per-turn cap is `state.allowances.supporterPlayed`, set by `playTrainer` and cleared by `freshAllowances()` on every `startTurn`. The printed "during this turn" is therefore **not a new clock — it is exactly the lifetime the allowances bag already has**. Zero new state: no `TurnAllowances` field, no `PlayerSide` field, no serializer change, no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump, no `redact.ts` diff. **The generalisation worth keeping: before costing a per-turn clause as "new state", check the RULE-ENFORCEMENT flags — the engine already records a per-turn fact for every once-per-turn cap it enforces, and a "did you do X this turn" clause where X is capped is the same bookkeeping seen from the other side.** **THE ONE REAL CALL IS THE SEAT GUARD, and it is D122's gate lesson's twin.** `GameState.allowances` is a **single bag describing whoever owns the current turn**, not a per-seat record, while `conditionHolds(state, seat, cond)` is **seat-relative** and has consumers where `seat` is NOT the turn owner (a `conditionGate` under a `koTrigger`/`damagedTrigger` stage evaluates during the OPPONENT's turn — the `resumeTail` case on `effect:choose`). Read unguarded it would answer *"your OPPONENT played a Supporter"* as *"you played a Supporter"*. So the arm proves ownership first — `phaseViewOf(state, seat).activeSeat === seat && state.allowances.supporterPlayed` — taking the owner from **THE one exhaustive switch over `Phase`**, never from turn parity, which `phaseView.ts`'s own header forbids for precisely the case that breaks it. **Rule: a state field scoped to the TURN is a fact about the TURN OWNER, not about a seat; a seat-relative predicate over it must first prove the seat owns the turn.** A checkup-origin `ko:*` park has `activeSeat` null and reads FALSE for both seats ("during this turn" is vacuous between turns); an attack-origin park still names the attacker and reads TRUE — the suite pins both, which is what proves the guard reads *ownership* rather than refusing every `ko:*`. Also the family's **first full round-trip**: the printed subject is already "you", so D116's pronoun rule does not apply and `conditionNote` returns the sentence byte for byte. Census (whole effect string, all 978 cards): the only other `you played` anywhere is Crobat `sv06.5-029`'s "Shadowy Envoy" **Ability**, which names a SPECIFIC card *and* drops the "during" — refused on either difference alone. The clause string is also the family's **first pure-ASCII** row (96 chars / 96 bytes, no `é`). Table: **17 → 18 literal rows + 2 templates, 22 of 26 distinct clauses / 38 of 43 printings.** The `conditionalDamage.test.ts` loud witness was re-pointed (occupant #4) to Revavroom ex `sv06.5-015` — 3 printings, and per-Pokémon per-turn state no field carries in any form. |
| D124 | The PROMOTED-THIS-TURN clause — `yourActivePromotedThisTurn`, the family's FIRST slice to buy NEW STATE, and a TURN STAMP rather than a flag; 3 printings / 2 cards / 1 clause on ZERO registry rows (P3-M5 clause family, engine 0.75.0) | 2026-08-01 | Revavroom ex `sv06.5-015`/`-081` "Accelerator Flash" (20+, **+120**) and Golisopod `swsh10.5-026` "First Impression" (20+, **+90**) now SIMULATE: *"If this Pokémon moved from your Bench to the Active Spot this turn, this attack does N more damage."* — **THREE printings on ONE literal row**, the best printings-per-clause ratio in the whole family and the per-turn cluster's anchor. Table: **18 → 19 literal rows + 2 templates, 23 of 26 distinct clauses / 41 of 43 printings.** **ONE ROW AND NOT A TEMPLATE even though the pool prints two amounts:** the amount is captured by the OUTER skeleton (`this attack does (\d+) more damage`), so the clause itself is byte-identical across all three printings and there is nothing inside it to parameterise — D118's rule read from the other end. **THIS IS THE FIRST CLAUSE IN THE FAMILY THAT BUYS NEW STATE, and D123's shortcut is now spent:** D123 got its turn event free by reading a rule-enforcement flag, but no rule caps how often a Pokémon may move up, so there was no bookkeeping to borrow. The fact is **per-Pokémon AND per-turn at once** and nothing on the model held it — `turnPlayed` records when the CARD entered play, `markers` is a write-only string bag never read in production, `TurnAllowances` is per-SEAT — so `InPlayPokemon` gained `promotedTurn: number \| null`. **THE DECISION IS THE TURN STAMP, NOT A FLAG, and it pays three times.** (1) **No turn-boundary clear**: `promotedTurn === state.turn` expires by arithmetic, so `startTurn` needs no walk over in-play Pokémon (it has none) and no future slice can forget to reset it. (2) **No seat guard** — the exact inverse of D123, and the generalisation to carry: **D123's guard was owed to seat-AMBIGUOUS STATE (`allowances` is one bag owned by the turn's owner), NOT to the words "this turn".** A fact stamped on a Pokémon is unambiguous — the Pokémon knows whose it is — so the predicate is total over seats, consumers and phases, and the suite proves it on ONE board where the seat answering TRUE is *not* the turn owner (a Boss's Orders drag). (3) **§8.1 falls out free**: a KO promotion runs during the OPPONENT's turn and stamps THEIR turn number, which can never equal `state.turn` once the controller's own turn begins — no clear, no special case, and without it every KO would hand the defender a free +120. **All three bench→Active routes stamp it and all three COUNT**, because the printed verb is "moved", not "retreated": `retreat` (turn.ts), `switchInto` (interpreter.ts — Switch/Escape Rope, Boss's Orders, Jet Energy's on-attach switch) and `resolvePromotion` after a KO (flow.ts). Setup placement is not a route (a card from the hand never came from the Bench), so `makeInPlay` stamps `null`. **It SURVIVES EVOLUTION** — `placeEvolution` spreads the Pokémon and clears only `conditions`/`retreatBlocked`/`markers`; §10 sheds the effects of ATTACKS, and having moved is a fact about the turn, not an effect on the card (retreat a Varoom in, evolve it into Revavroom ex the same turn, and the clause is still armed — the only line on which the card is good). **MATCH_RECORD_VERSION 1 → 2**: a record written by the previous deploy has in-play Pokémon with NO `promotedTurn` at all — a **missing required field, not a widening**, which is the one case that constant's doc block says must bump. It would read back *benignly* (`undefined === state.turn` is false), and that is exactly the trap the version exists to refuse. Census (whole effect string, all 978 cards, re-confirmed): `Active Spot this turn` in exactly 5 cards, `moved from` in exactly 3, and **zero U+2019 anywhere in the pool** (only `é` U+00E9 and `×` U+00D7 are non-ASCII). Two near-misses stay LOUD and neither is a table near-miss: Palafin `sv03-062`/`-200` "Justice Kick" (*"If this Pokémon **didn't move from the Bench** to the Active Spot this turn, this attack **does nothing**"* — a different consequent, so the OUTER anchor refuses it; a `conditionGate`-shaped slice on the same state whenever someone wants it) and Weavile `sv02-134`'s "Assaulting Hunt", which reads the same transition as a **`when` TRIGGER**, not a per-turn query. The `conditionalDamage.test.ts` loud witness moved to occupant **#5**, Lokix `sv02-021` — now SHARED with `clauseTable.test.ts`, and that sharing is a fact about the pool: only **three** unmapped clauses remain in 978 cards against four suites that each need a witness. |
| D125 | The REQUIREMENT skeleton — "If \<clause\>, this attack does nothing.", the clause family's SECOND CONSEQUENT, mapped on ZERO new vocabulary; 3 printings / 2 cards / 2 clauses (P3-M5, engine 0.76.0) | 2026-08-01 | Palafin `sv03-062`/`-200` "Justice Kick" (`{W}{W}`, **210**) and Lycanroc `sv03-117` "Finishing Fang" (`{F}`, **90**) now SIMULATE: the attack is **CANCELLED** when its printed requirement is unmet. D124's resume point named Palafin as the cheapest thing left and asked for a sweep first — the sweep is what set the slice's size: the pool holds **19 "does nothing" printings across 6 distinct clauses**, so the unit of work was the CONSEQUENT, not the card. **THE HEADLINE IS WHAT IT DID NOT COST.** The literal clause table is UNTOUCHED at 19 rows + 2 templates (23 of 26 clauses / 41 of 43 printings), because both new rows reuse a `BoardCondition` member the vocabulary already carried — `yourActivePromotedThisTurn` (D124) and `opponentActiveDamaged` (D115). Zero union members, zero `conditionHolds`/`conditionNote` arms, zero ops, zero state fields, zero registry rows, zero `continuous.ts` diff, zero web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. **A NEW CONSEQUENT IS A NEW SKELETON, NOT A NEW VOCABULARY** — the generalisation to carry: before costing a new sentence family, check whether the *condition* half is already spelled; the expensive half is usually the consequent and the two are independent. **THE RESUME POINT'S FORECAST WAS WRONG IN THE MOST USEFUL WAY.** It called this "a `conditionGate`-shaped slice", and it cannot be one: an effect PROGRAM runs at `attack.ts`'s tail, strictly AFTER the §8.5 damage pipeline, and no op can retract damage already dealt. **A derived shape is classified by WHERE IN RESOLUTION IT LANDS, not by how its sentence reads** — anything that must precede damage cannot be an `EffectOp`, however naturally the sentence maps onto one. The gate the engine already had for exactly *"no damage, no effects, and the turn still ends"* is the **§8 step-3 CONFUSION path**, so D125 is that path minus the self-damage: push `ATTACK_FAILED` and `return finishAttack(...)`, which keeps the §8.1 both-board sweep and the turn end, because a cancelled attack is an attack that was USED. **THE ONE REAL DESIGN CALL IS POLARITY, AND IT BELONGS TO THE SKELETON RATHER THAN THE VOCABULARY.** No `{ kind: "not", cond }` member was added, and none should be on present evidence: **all SIX distinct "does nothing" clauses in the pool are NEGATIVE sentences** — "didn't move", "has no damage counters", "you can't", "If tails", "you didn't play", "you can't shuffle" — and that is the **grammar of the mechanic**, not an accident of this pool. A card prints "does nothing" precisely to state a REQUIREMENT, and English states a requirement by naming its failure. So `deriveAttackRequirement(text)` reads the printed NEGATIVE clause and returns the POSITIVE board fact required, and `attack.ts` cancels on its ABSENCE. A `not` member would make `BoardCondition` recursive — five consumers (`conditionHolds`, `conditionNote`, `redact.ts`, `cardplay.ts`'s playability descent, `registry.ts`) each owing a recursion arm, and `conditionNote` owing a negated phrasing English does not supply — to buy a spelling nothing in 978 cards asks for. If a POSITIVE clause ever appears under this consequent, THAT is the warrant (D121's rule), and the cheap answer then is a second table of positive-sense rows, still without touching the union. **TWO CONSEQUENTS GET TWO TABLES, sharing the vocabulary but never a lookup:** a clause meaning "80 more damage if X" must not be reachable from a sentence meaning "nothing if X", or a mis-anchored sentence resolves to a plausible wrong answer instead of staying LOUD. **A PRINTED TIMING QUALIFIER CAN BE DISCHARGED BY THE SITE:** Lycanroc's trailing "…before this attack does damage" needed no modelling at all — `opponentActiveDamaged`'s own doc block already commits it to a declaration-time read (that is what "already" means for the D115 bonus family) and the requirement gate sits in front of §8.5, so the qualifier is true by *where the check runs*; an undamaged defender cannot bootstrap the clause with the very hit the clause gates, and the suite pins that non-bootstrap explicitly. `ATTACK_FAILED.reason` widened `"confusion"` → `"confusion" \| "requirement"` — **a widening, so NO `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump** (D124's own distinction: the bump is for a MISSING REQUIRED FIELD on an old record) — and `log.ts` branches on it, or a cancel would render as a confusion that never happened. The family's other **16 printings stay LOUD and are refused on the CLAUSE rather than the shape**, which the suite pins as two separate facts: the 12 coin-flip "Flip a coin. If tails, …" prints are denied `^If` by the flip sentence in front, AND a constructed bare "If tails, this attack does nothing." proves the TABLE refuses it too; the 3 anaphoric "If you can't, …" prints point back at a cost sentence the reader cannot see; Sharpedo `sv06.5-034`'s names a specific card and is multi-sentence. **The coin-flip family is now a better-shaped slice than it was** — D125 built the pre-damage cancel seam it needs, and only the announced flip (an event + `rngState` consumed in front of §8.5, exactly where the confusion flip sits) is missing; take its "If heads, +N damage" half in the same slice. Cost: 1 skeleton regex, 1 two-row Map, 1 exported deriver, 1 reader + 1 gate + 1 `effectSimulated` term in `attack.ts`, 1 enum widening, 1 log branch. New `attackRequirement.test.ts` (**34**) on a new `ATTACK_REQUIREMENT_DECK` with four faithful fixtures (Palafin + Finizen, Lycanroc + Rockruff; the byte-identical `sv03-200` reprint deliberately NOT fixtured), driving real routes throughout — retreat, Switch, Boss's Orders, a real evolve, a real KO+promote chain proving §8.1 still cancels on the following turn, and **Lycanroc's own "Slashing Claw" supplying the damage its "Finishing Fang" reads two turns later**. It also pins the zero-new-vocabulary claim directly, by asserting `deriveAttackDamageBonus`'s cond on the real positive sentences deep-equals `deriveAttackRequirement`'s on the negatives. `promotedClause.test.ts`'s Justice-Kick near-miss case was **kept and BOUND** — its three refusals still hold and two assertions were added that a fourth reader now claims it — rather than deleted; no "unmapped stays LOUD" witness moved, and the three unmapped clauses (Lokix, Lucario, Slither Wing) are untouched. engineVersion 0.75.0 → **0.76.0**; `bun run check` **GREEN exit 0: 167 → 168 files, 2543 → 2577 tests**. |
| D126 | The PRINTED COIN FLIP — one pre-damage flip read from TWO consequents, 31 printings on TWO REGEXES and ZERO vocabulary; the largest single mapping in P3-M5 (P3-M5, engine 0.77.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Flip a coin. If heads, this attack does N more damage."* (**20 printings**, 7 distinct N: 10/20/30/50/60/80/90) and *"Flip a coin. If tails, this attack does nothing."* (**11 printings**) now SIMULATE. **THE HEADLINE IS THE PRICE: two anchored regexes and a two-member union, for 31 printings.** No clause-table row, no `BoardCondition` member, no `DamageCountSource` member, no state field, no registry row, no `continuous.ts`/web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. **WHY IT IS THIS CHEAP, and this is the generalisation to carry: THE CLAUSE IS A FIXED LITERAL.** Every predecessor of the "does N more damage" shape (D115–D124) bought a printed-clause table row and usually a `BoardCondition` member, because its clause named a BOARD FACT. Here the clause is the word *heads*, and **a coin face is not a board fact at all** — no state answers it, so there is nothing for the board vocabulary to spell. `scaledAttackDamage` stays **pure and state-only** on purpose: the face is drawn from `rngState` at resolution. **When the condition is not read off the board, do not try to spell it in the board vocabulary.** **HEADS AND TAILS ARE ONE SLICE because they are ONE MECHANISM** — a single pre-damage flip read from two ends — and `deriveAttackCoinFlip` returns a **2-member union** (`{ kind: "bonusOnHeads"; per } | { kind: "cancelOnTails" }`) rather than two nullable readers, which makes *"at most one flip per attack"* a **type property** instead of a rule `attack.ts` has to keep. Built separately, the flip, its event and its placement would have been designed twice. **PLACEMENT IS D125's RULE APPLIED, NOT RE-DERIVED:** the block sits in `attack.ts` after the D125 requirement gate and in front of the §8.5 pipeline — the §8 step-3 confusion-flip site, one gate later. It could not be an `EffectOp`: a program runs at the function's tail, so a `coinFlipGate` could neither add to a number already computed nor retract damage already dealt. Heads folds `coinBonus` into a new `scaledTotal = scaled + coinBonus` used at **all three** `scaled` sites (the `> 0` guard, the pre-W/R sum, the `DAMAGE_DEALT.scaled` field), so the bonus is **PRE-WEAKNESS**: Litwick on heads into a Fire-weak body is `(10 + 10) × 2 = 40`, not `10 × 2 + 10`. It sits AFTER the D125 gate so an attack already cancelled never burns an `rngState` step — and honestly, **no printing carries both**, so nothing in the suite pins that order; it is the order the rules read in. **THE EVENT WAS REUSED, NOT JOINED BY A SIBLING.** `ATTACK_EFFECT_COIN_FLIP` names a FACT — "a coin was flipped resolving this" — identical here and inside a `coinFlipGate`, and its log copy was already neutralised ("flipped \<face\> for the effect") when Trainers started sharing it, so it reads correctly unchanged. A second type would owe a new log arm and a new wire member to carry information the existing one already carries. `CONFUSION_CHECK` stays separate for the opposite reason: it is a §12 STATUS check, not part of the attack's printed text. `ATTACK_FAILED.reason` widened `"confusion" \| "requirement"` → `+ "coinFlip"` — **a widening, so NO `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump** (D124's distinction: the bump is for a MISSING REQUIRED FIELD on an old record) — and `log.ts` gained a third arm stating the CONSEQUENCE only, since the preceding flip row already named the face. **⚠️ THE `modifierSimulated` TRAP IS REAL AND SILENT:** the 20 heads printings carry a printed `"10+"`-style marker and the coin reader is exactly what consumes that `+`; `effectSimulated` gaining a term is the obvious half, but **omitting the `modifierSimulated` term produces perfectly correct damage on all 20 AND a loud `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` row on every one.** Only a "no skipped row on either face" assertion catches it. The term is narrowed to `coinFlip?.kind === "bonusOnHeads"` because the cancel printings carry a FLAT number. **⚠️ THE DOUBLE-READ RISK:** `deriveAttackEffect` ALREADY reads *"Flip a coin. If heads, your opponent's Active Pokémon is now Paralyzed."* (16 printings) into a `coinFlipGate` program; had `deriveAttackCoinFlip` also claimed it the attack would flip TWICE. The consequent anchor prevents it and the suite asserts both directions **empirically**, not by reading the regex. **THE CENSUS CORRECTED THE PLAN'S OWN NUMBERS** — the resume point carried 16/8 and 12; the anchored re-census says **20/7** and **11**, the difference being entirely EXTENDED prints the `$` refuses that an earlier `%…%` LIKE had folded in. **Re-census before sizing a slice even when a previous session already did.** The long tail stays LOUD and the suite names which property refuses each: `$` refuses the extended prints (Floette `sv01-092`, Conkeldurr V `swsh10.5-040`/`-073`/`-074`, Dragonite ex `sv03-159`, Squawkabilly `sv01-162`); `^Flip a coin\.` refuses a different NUMBER of flips (`Flip a coin until you get tails.` ×9 incl. Bouffalant `sv03-174`'s `more` variant, `Flip 2 coins.` ×18, `Flip 3 coins.` ×10 incl. Bisharp `sv03-149`, `Flip 4 coins.` ×1, `Flip a coin for each …` ×3); the consequent refuses every other single-flip family. Pool facts: the two mapped sentences are the family's first printed text that is **pure ASCII** (no `é`), so the usual `bytes = codePoints + count("Pokémon")` invariant degenerates to `bytes === codePoints` and the suite pins that explicitly; `Flip a coin` is lowercase mid-sentence **exactly once** in 978 cards (Eelektross `sv03-069`); **zero consumers of `ATTACK_FAILED` exist outside `packages/engine/src`**, so the widening is engine-local; and there is **no catalog-wide census test** in the repo, so mapping 31 real printings moved no numeric expectation and the three loud witnesses (Lokix `sv02-021`, Lucario `sv01-114`, Slither Wing `sv06.5-026`) were never touched. Cost: 2 regexes, 1 union, 1 deriver, 1 reader + 1 flip block + 2 `*Simulated` terms + `scaledTotal` in `attack.ts`, 1 enum widening, 1 log arm. New `coinFlipDamage.test.ts` (**27**) on a new `COIN_FLIP_DECK` with two faithful Basics (Tarountula `sv01-018`, Litwick `sv03-036`), both faces reached by the established SEED SWEEP, pinning the full **rngState account** (exactly one `flipCoin` step per attack, verified by recomputing `flipCoin(before.rngState)` — the only assertion that would catch a double flip) and that the three `ATTACK_FAILED` reasons produce three DISTINCT log rows. engineVersion 0.76.0 → **0.77.0**; `bun run check` **GREEN exit 0: 168 → 169 files, 2577 → 2604 tests**. |

This attack does D damage for each heads."* now SIMULATES — **22 printings** (N=2 ×12, N=3 ×9, N=4 ×1; D ∈ {10,20,30,40,50,60,70,80,100}) on **ONE anchored regex, ONE union member and STILL zero vocabulary**. Fixtures: Tandemaus `sv01-160` "Double Hit" ({C}{C}, 30×, 2 flips) and Meowth `sv06.5-048` "Fury Swipes" ({C}{C}, 20×, 3 flips). **THE HEADLINE IS HOW LITTLE CHANGED.** Same reader (`deriveAttackCoinFlip`), same union (`AttackCoinFlip` 2 → 3 members), same site in `attack.ts` (after the D125 requirement gate, in front of §8.5), same `coinBonus` → `scaledTotal` fold, same event. The diff is **a `for` loop, a third union member, one term in `scaledBase` and one in `modifierSimulated`.** **GENERALISATION: once a shape's SITE is right, varying its CARDINALITY is cheap.** The expensive questions were asked already — *where in resolution does this land* (D125) and *what answers this clause* (D126); "how many times" is not in their league, and a slice that only changes cardinality should be priced as cheap even when its printing count is large. **N FLIPS ARE ANNOUNCED AS N ROWS — the one design question D126 did not have to answer.** `ATTACK_EFFECT_COIN_FLIP` carries exactly ONE `CoinFace`, so one event per flip in flip order is the only reading that does not invent an aggregate the event, the wire and the log cannot express. The alternative (a single row carrying a heads COUNT) would owe a new event type, a new wire member and a new log arm to say what a reader can already count off the rows — **and it would throw away the ORDER**, which is precisely what a player watching a three-flip attack wants to see. **IT IS THE MULTIPLY FAMILY, AND THE PRINTED MARKER IS WHAT SAYS SO.** All 22 carry a `"D×"` marker whose digits ARE the per-heads D, so `scaledBase` must drop the printed base exactly as it does for `deriveAttackDamageMultiplier` — Tandemaus on two heads is **60, not 30 + 60**. The word that separates the families is *more*, and the pool holds **two live "more" near-misses** the whole-sentence anchor must refuse: Melmetal `swsh10.5-046` (`Flip 2 coins. This attack does 90 more damage for each heads.`, base `30+`) and Bouffalant `sv03-174` (`Flip a coin until you get tails. This attack does 30 more damage for each heads.`, base `50+`). A substring matcher would score both wrong. **AND YET IT IS NOT A `DamageCountSource` — D126's rule APPLIED, not re-derived:** the count comes from the flips, not from the board; no state answers it, `scaledAttackDamage` stays pure and state-only, and the value belongs in the coin union with its siblings. **`bonusOnHeads` IS NOT COLLAPSED INTO `perHeads` WITH `flips: 1`,** even though the arithmetic coincides — `heads × per` is correct for both and `attack.ts` computes them with ONE expression, so the branch there is over CANCELLATION rather than over the members. What is not shared is the printed marker: `"+"` keeps the base, `"×"` drops it, and one boolean flag would hide the only real difference behind a name that does not mention it. **THE `modifierSimulated` TRAP RECURRED EXACTLY WHERE D126 PREDICTED**, and the fix inverted the term: `"D×"` is a marker the reader consumes, so two of three members own one and the expression became `coinFlip !== null && coinFlip.kind !== "cancelOnTails"` — **naming the EXCEPTION rather than the cases**, which is the more honest statement and keeps a future member from silently defaulting to "unsimulated". **THE GUARDS.** `flips >= 2` because the sentence's *coins* is PLURAL (the one-flip form is printed *"Flip a coin."* and belongs to D126's two patterns, so `"Flip 1 coins."` is neither English nor in the pool — refusing it keeps the grammar and the value in agreement). `flips <= MAX_PRINTED_FLIPS = 10` is a **refusal ceiling, not a rule**: the pool's max is 4, but the digits are read out of INGESTED THIRD-PARTY TEXT and an unbounded `\d+` would let one malformed row spin the resolution loop — **a refusal is LOUD, a hang is not.** `per >= 1` is the guard every scaling arm carries. **ZERO HEADS IS A RESOLVED ATTACK THAT DEALT NOTHING, NOT A CANCELLED ONE** — no `DAMAGE_DEALT` (the `scaledBase + scaledTotal > 0` guard), no `ATTACK_FAILED`, and the turn still ends. That is the sharpest behavioural difference from `cancelOnTails` and it has its own case. Still LOUD after this, both on the leading sentence: **`Flip a coin until you get tails.`** (9 prints — an UNBOUNDED fold, a genuinely different risk) and **`Flip a coin for each {R} Energy attached to this Pokémon.`** (3 prints — a BOARD-COUNTED number of flips, which is where the board vocabulary would finally earn its place in this family). Cost: 1 regex + 1 constant, 1 union member, 1 deriver arm, 1 loop + 2 terms in `attack.ts`. **ZERO new events, ops, `BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members, clause-table rows, state fields, error codes, registry rows or LOG ARMS; no `continuous.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. NO `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` BUMP — no event shape changed at all.** New `multiCoinFlip.test.ts` (**30**) on a new `MULTI_COIN_FLIP_DECK` + one synthetic `fix-colorless-weak-big` (200 HP, ×2 COLORLESS — synthetic in a way no printed card is, because BOTH multi-flip fixtures are Colorless and no existing weak body fields that type). It sweeps seeds to reach **all three** Tandemaus outcomes and **all four** Meowth ones (measured: complete by seed 5 and seed 10 respectively; `SEEDS = 24` inherited from D126 is comfortably past both) and asserts the observed outcome SET exactly, so a regression that stops reaching one fails rather than silently under-testing. It pins the **full rngState account both ways** — the folded state equals `after.rngState` AND the folded FACES equal the emitted rows in order, plus explicit `flips ± 1` non-equality — the four-reader disjointness **empirically in both directions**, no `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` on any swept seed, and the pre-Weakness fold `(0 + 60) × 2 = 120` (with **180** = leaked base and **60** = post-W/R fold named as the wrong answers). engineVersion 0.77.0 → **0.78.0**; `bun run check` **GREEN exit 0: 169 → 170 files, 2604 → 2634 tests**. |
| D128 | The BOARD-COUNTED FLIP COUNT — "Flip a coin for each [{X}] Energy attached to this Pokémon.": the coin family's FIRST board read, a widened `flips` rather than a fourth member, and a bound is owed to the SOURCE of the number (P3-M5, engine 0.79.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Flip a coin for each [{X}] Energy attached to this Pokémon. Census re-run 2026-08-01 over `attacks_json` (978 cards): those three are the WHOLE family, `Flip a coin for each` never counts anything but self-attached Energy, and the only variation is the optional type filter. **THE FLIP COUNT IS A BOARD FACT; THE DAMAGE COUNT IS STILL NOT — D126's rule survives contact with the shape that looked most likely to break it.** `scaledAttackDamage` stays **pure and state-only** and gains **no `DamageCountSource` member**, because the damage still comes from the faces; what the board answers is HOW MANY TIMES TO FLIP, the one number a coin cannot answer. **So the slice is a WIDENED `flips`, not a fourth `AttackCoinFlip` member:** `AttackFlipCount = { kind: "printed"; count } \| { kind: "attachedEnergy"; energy }`, and `perHeads` still means "fold `per` × heads, drop the printed base, emit one row per flip". A fourth member would have duplicated D127's entire fold to vary its bound. **D127's cardinality rule, second sighting and stronger: once a shape's SITE is right, varying its cardinality is cheap even when the count moves from the text to the BOARD.** The fold, the loop, the event sequence and the dropped `"D×"` base are D127's verbatim; the diff is one regex, one union, one deriver arm, one counter and one changed line. **⚠️ THE GENERALISATION TO CARRY: A BOUND IS OWED TO THE SOURCE OF THE NUMBER, NOT TO THE LOOP IT FEEDS.** D127's `MAX_PRINTED_FLIPS = 10` guards the `printed` member ALONE and deliberately does not travel: printed digits are read out of THIRD-PARTY INGESTED TEXT and have no upper bound but that constant, while an attached-Energy count is read off the engine's own state and is bounded by the deck that produced it. A ceiling on the board read would refuse a **legal board** rather than a malformed row — the opposite of what a refusal ceiling is for. (The still-unbuilt `Flip a coin until you get tails.` family is the third case: no bound from the text AND none from the board, so it needs a *termination* argument, not a ceiling.) **⚠️ THE COUNT IS READ AT THE FLIP SITE, NOT AT DECLARATION beside `scaled`** — the count and the flips it bounds are one act, and splitting them would let a future gate between the two silently invalidate the number. Nothing between declaration and the flip can change an attachment today; that is a fact about the current gates, not a property worth depending on. `flipCount(state, seat, attacker, count)` is private to `attack.ts` and reads the attacker off `state`, with the declaration-time Active as the unreachable branch's answer (the `opponentActiveRetreatCost` idiom). **⚠️ `energy: null` IS A DIFFERENT TALLY, NOT A FILTER THAT MATCHES EVERYTHING** — this is the design call the slice turns on. A FILTERED count reads by **PROVISION** (`providesEnergyType`, the D118 rule: an Energy that can pay a `{R}` cost is exactly an Energy a `{R}` sentence counts, so a wildcard Luminous is one for as long as it provides every type). An UNFILTERED count reads by **CARD**: "each Energy attached" counts attached Energy CARDS, so a Special demoted to providing only {C} is still one, and a wildcard providing eleven types is still exactly one. Collapsing null into "the type filter matching everything" would quietly make the second question the first and get Ambipom wrong on any board holding a demoted Special. New `continuous.ts` `countAttachedEnergy(state, pokemon, energy \| null)` sits beside `hasAttachedEnergy` so the two readings of the same printed noun cannot drift; `hasAttachedEnergy` keeps its short-circuiting `.some` rather than being reimplemented on the counter. **BOTH NOTATIONS THROUGH D118's `CLAUSE_ENERGY_TOKENS`, not a local `{R}` lookup.** The pool prints types brace-coded (sv*) and spelled out (swsh10.5); all three printings of THIS sentence happen to need neither notation, but reusing the map costs nothing and admits a "for each Water Energy" reprint by construction instead of silently skipping it. An **unresolvable** token — `{C}`, `{N}`, `Dragon`, junk — is told apart from the UNFILTERED sentence **by the token's presence, not by the lookup's null**, and falls through to the loud `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` path; `{C}` is refused for `ENERGY_TYPE_BY_CODE`'s own reason (Colorless is the conservative provision fallback for every unauthored Special, so a `{C}` filter would match cards nobody meant). **⚠️ A ZERO COUNT MEANS ZERO FLIPS, WHICH IS NOT ZERO HEADS:** no `ATTACK_EFFECT_COIN_FLIP` rows at all and **no `rngState` step consumed**, where D127's zero-HEADS outcome spends its flips and announces every one. D127's witness case ("refuses a BOARD-COUNTED number of flips") was **re-pointed, not deleted** — it now pins the disjointness of the two arms. Cost: 1 regex, 1 exported union, 1 deriver arm, 1 `continuous.ts` counter, 1 private reader + 1 changed line in `attack.ts`. **ZERO new events, ops, `BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members, clause-table rows, state fields, error codes, registry rows or log arms; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. NO `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` BUMP — no event shape changed at all.** New `perEnergyFlip.test.ts` (**37**) on a new `PER_ENERGY_FLIP_DECK` with faithful Torkoal + Bellossom (a Stage 2 IS fieldable by `setActiveFromDeck`, so the untyped case runs on the real card) and the constructed zero-count probe; it sweeps seeds and asserts the observed outcome SET exactly, recomputes every board's whole flip sequence by hand, pins byte-identical `rngState` on the zero board, asserts no `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` on any outcome incl. zero, `(0+80)×2 = 160` pre-Weakness, and a `countAttachedEnergy` block where the three readings **DISAGREE** (a wildcard counts 1 for every type filter and 1 card; a second Special drops every type count to 0 while the card count rises to 2). Mutation-checked: ignoring the filter fails 9, leaking the base 8, hard-coding one flip 7. engineVersion 0.78.0 → **0.79.0**; `bun run check` **GREEN exit 0: 170 → 171 files, 2634 → 2671 tests**, and **zero formatter churn (89 files flagged before and after — the real pre-existing count is 89, not the 57 D113 recorded; the repo has grown since)**. |
| D129 | The UNBOUNDED FOLD — "Flip a coin until you get tails.": the coin family's LAST damaging shape, a TERMINATION ARGUMENT instead of a ceiling, and the flip count that is neither text nor board but the FACES (P3-M5, engine 0.80.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Flip a coin until you get tails. This attack does D [more] damage for each heads."* now SIMULATES — **5 damaging printings on TWO regexes**: the MULTIPLY reading (printed base DROPPED) Growlithe `sv01-030` "Relentless Flames" (`30×`), Rellor `sv02-026` "Ball Roll" (`30×`), Forretress `sv01-139` "Continuous Spin" (`50×`), Chansey `sv01-144` "Egg Rolling" (`60×`), and the ADDITIVE reading (printed base KEPT) Bouffalant `sv03-174` "Damage Rush" (*"…does 30 **more** damage for each heads."*, `50+`). Census re-run 2026-08-01 over `attacks_json` (809 cards carrying attacks, 136 printings / 63 distinct clauses opening with "Flip"): **8** printings say "until you get tails", 5 damaging and read here, 3 running an `EffectOp` per heads and left loudly skipped. **THE FLIP COUNT IS NEITHER THE TEXT NOR THE BOARD — IT IS THE FACES.** `untilTails` is the third `AttackFlipCount` member and the first that is not a number at any point: D127's is known at derivation, D128's at the flip site, this one only once the coin has been drawn. So `flipCount` — a reader returning a COUNT for a `for` loop to consume — **could not express it without flipping to find out how many times to flip**, and became `takeFlips`, returning the **SEQUENCE OF FACES** for all three members. There is no `untilTails` branch at the flip site at all; every member announces its rows and tallies its heads through one path. **⚠️ THE GENERALISATION TO CARRY: WHEN NOTHING BOUNDS A LOOP, OWE A TERMINATION ARGUMENT — NOT A CEILING.** D127's answer (refuse the row at derivation) is for a MALFORMED ROW; "Flip a coin until you get tails." is a legal, fully-specified sentence whose length is a fact about the RNG, so a refusal would refuse legal text and a truncating cap chosen by feel would **silently lie about the game** rather than loudly skip it. The bound is therefore owed to the SOURCE of the faces (D128's provenance rule, third application) and lives in `rng.ts` beside `flipCoin`: `MAX_UNTIL_TAILS_FLIPS = 64`. **The argument is MEASURED, not probabilistic.** mulberry32's pre-states advance by a fixed **odd** stride (`state + 0x6d2b79f5`), so successive flips walk `n·K mod 2³²` and visit every int32 exactly once — the face sequence is ONE fixed cycle with a worst case you can *compute*. Scanned over all **4 294 967 296** steps (plus the wrap: the cycle ends `…HH` and begins `H…`, so no run straddles the seam), **the longest run of consecutive heads is 31**. 64 is twice that, so the cap **cannot fire from any seed** and every sequence ends in tails; the guard is dead code by construction, which is what a guard of this kind should be. To re-derive after changing `nextU32`: scan `n·K` for n in [0, 2³²) and take the longest heads run (~40s of arithmetic). **⚠️ THE CAP IS A PARAMETER SO ITS BRANCH STAYS TESTABLE.** `flipUntilTails(state, cap)` takes the ceiling rather than reading the constant, because a bound set above what the RNG can produce can never be observed by a test that only drives the engine — the suite pins what truncation DOES at cap 1 and cap 2 (all-heads faces, exact step accounting) and separately pins the constant itself. It returns FACES rather than a count precisely so a caller can detect a truncated sequence (all heads, no trailing tails). **⚠️ ONE FLIP COUNT REACHES BOTH DAMAGE MEMBERS, and that is the proof D126's split was load-bearing rather than incidental.** 4 printings are `D×` (`perHeads`, base dropped) and Bouffalant is `50+` (`bonusOnHeads`, base kept) — the same sentence opening, two consequents, told apart by the single word **"more"**, the same word that separates the whole additive scaling family from the multiply one and the same near-miss D127's regex was built to refuse *by name*. So **`bonusOnHeads` grew the same `flips` field D128 gave `perHeads`** (its D126 printings now derive `{ kind: "printed", count: 1 }` — what they always were implicitly): only the SOURCE of the number moved, so D128's widen-don't-add rule applies to the other member unchanged. **The union has now stayed THREE MEMBERS ACROSS FOUR SHAPES** because the flip COUNT and the flip CONSEQUENT are independent axes; a member per (count × consequent) pair would be six members and five copies of one fold. **⚠️ TWO "unmapped stays LOUD" witnesses were RE-POINTED, not deleted** (the family's standing rule): `multiCoinFlip.test.ts`'s "two live *more* printings" keeps **Melmetal `swsh10.5-046`** (`Flip 2 coins. This attack does 90 more damage for each heads.`, `30+`) — now the pool's ONLY unmapped printed-count additive fold, and refused rather than mapped because it is **1 printing** and D121's second-printing warrant is unmet — while Bouffalant moved to a positive assertion; and its "refuses the UNBOUNDED opening" case re-pointed at **Magikarp `sv02-042`** (`Flip 2 coins. If both of them are heads, this attack does 20 more damage.`, 2 printings), the same leading sentence D127 reads with a consequent it does not. Cost: 2 regexes, 1 union member, 1 widened field, 2 deriver arms, 1 rng primitive + 1 constant, `flipCount` → `takeFlips`. ZERO new events, ops, `BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members, clause-table rows, state fields, error codes, registry rows or log arms; no `continuous.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/schema diff; **no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump** — no event shape changed. `untilTailsFlip.test.ts` (39 tests) + the four touched suites; full `bun run check` green, **172 files / 2710 tests**. |
| D130 | The PER-HEADS PROGRAM — "For each heads, discard the top N cards of your opponent's deck.": the coin family's FIRST non-damaging consequent, and the carry from the flip site to the tail is PROGRAM EXPANSION, not a channel (P3-M5, engine 0.81.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Flip N coins. / Flip a coin until you get tails. **For each heads, discard the top M cards of your opponent's deck.**"* now SIMULATES — **2 printings on TWO anchored regexes**: Wugtrio `sv01-057` "Undersea Tunnel" (attack index **1**, `Flip 3 coins.` … top **3**, no `damage` field) and Gyarados `swsh10.5-022` "Wreak Havoc" (index 0, `Flip a coin until you get tails.` … top **2**, no `damage` field).
