The tempting shapes — widen `EffectRecord` (§9.2's uid-array scratch), add a `repeat` op, thread a context object — are all wrong for the same reason: **"For each heads, X" IS X repeated `heads` times, and the tail already runs an `EffectOp[]`.** So the flip block splices `heads` copies of the member's ops into `program` and there is no new channel, no new park state, no serialization question (the expansion is a plain program that parks and resumes like any other). It **APPENDS** (`[...(program ?? []), ...repeated]`) rather than replaces so a registry row can never be silently dropped, and collapses an empty result back to `null` so `heads === 0` takes the existing no-program ending unchanged. **⚠️ THE OPS LIVE ON THE COIN MEMBER, so ONE READER OWNS THE SENTENCE.** `{ kind: "programPerHeads"; flips: AttackFlipCount; ops: EffectOp[] }` is the union's 4th member; the alternative (coin deriver returns the count, `deriveAttackEffect` returns the op) would put two anchored readers on one string with an invariant nobody enforces. `deriveAttackEffect`/`DamageBonus`/`Multiplier`/`Requirement` all still return `null` for both sentences, asserted. **⚠️ NEW OP, REUSED EVENT, NEUTRALISED MESSAGE.** `{ op: "discardOpponentDeckTop"; count: number }` — deterministic, never parks, consumes **no rng** (which is exactly what makes it safe to repeat once per heads), clamped by `slice` so a shallow deck gives up what it has and the event reports the SHORT list. Deliberately no `whose:` and no filter (D104's minimal shape); a future own-deck mill widens the field then. The event is **`DECK_TOP_DISCARDED`, reused unchanged** — its game fact ("these uids went from the top of `seat`'s deck to `seat`'s discard") is exactly ours, with `seat` = the deck's OWNER, which is the actor for Hydreigon's `attachFromTop discardRest` and the **NON-actor** here; naming uids leaks nothing because the discard pile is public, so `redact.ts` still needs no arm. Its **log message was a lie the moment it had a second producer** ("discarded N **looked-at** cards from the top of their deck") and was made provenance-neutral — the D113 precedent exactly: reuse the event, fix the *message*. The Hydreigon case's flavour is already carried by its own preceding `DECK_TOP_REVEALED`/attach rows. **⚠️ DECK-OUT IS NOT THE OP'S BUSINESS.** §14.3 is checked at the DRAW (`flow.ts` `startTurn`), so milling to zero does not end the game on the spot — but attacking ends the turn, so a clamped mill reaches the victim's draw in the SAME event batch and `GAME_OVER reason:"deckOut"` always follows. The claim is therefore pinned on **ORDER** (`DECK_TOP_DISCARDED` < `TURN_ENDED` < `TURN_STARTED` < `GAME_OVER`, no `CARDS_DRAWN`): a build that ended the game inside the op would emit it a full turn early. **⚠️ `modifierSimulated` GREW ITS SECOND EXCEPTION, and it is correctness in principle, not a live bug.** `programPerHeads` folds no damage, so it explains no `+`/`×`; riding the existing `kind !== "cancelOnTails"` test would have suppressed a loud row the engine had not earned. Neither printing carries a `damage` field, so no board can reach the difference — it is written from the rule, not from what a test would notice. `effectSimulated` needed **no** new term (`coinFlip !== null` already covers it) and `scaledBase` keeps the base (this member owns no printed number to duplicate). **⚠️ WITNESSES RE-POINTED, NOT DELETED** (the family's standing rule): `untilTailsFlip.test.ts`'s three-printing loud-skip block becomes **two** — **Masquerain `sv03-007`** (`discard a random card from your opponent's hand` — would be the FIRST `EffectOp` in the engine to consume `state.rngState`) and **Krookodile `sv01-117`** (`discard an Energy from your opponent's Active Pokémon` — `discardEnergy` PARKS, so N heads is N sequential parks across a serialized continuation), both genuinely different machinery and both deliberately out of scope. The end-to-end `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` case moved from Gyarados to Krookodile (fielded faithfully, `{F}` line added) and a new case asserts Gyarados is now POSITIVE. **`UNTIL_TAILS_FLIP_DECK` was re-pointed WITHOUT invalidating D129's measured seed table**: setup's rng depends only on deck length and which indices hold a Basic, so the swap kept 60 cards and indices 12–29 as eighteen consecutive non-Basics (`3 Krookodile + 4 {R} + 3 {F} + 8 {C}` for `4 Gyarados + 6 {R} + 8 {C}`); the split is measured, `{C} = 8` forced by seeds 20 and 29, and every D129 pin passes unedited. Also fixed in passing: the Gyarados fixture claimed a `-30` Grass resistance its D1 row does not have (`resistances_json` is `null`) while its doc said "verbatim off the local D1" — nothing asserted it, which is how it survived. **NOTED FOR A FUTURE SLICE:** seven UNCONDITIONAL opponent-side mills (Chi-Yu ex `sv02-040`/`-234`/`-259`, Garganacl `sv02-123`, Diggersby `sv03-113`, Paldean Clodsire `sv03-128`, Skwovet `sv03-178`) print `Discard the top N cards of your opponent's deck.` and are **already the right shape for this op** — they stay unmapped only because no deriver reads that sentence. Whiscash `sv03-109` mills per attached `{F}`; Gyarados's own "Wild Splash" is the OWN-deck reading the op has no `whose:` for. Cost: 2 regexes, 1 union member, 1 op + 1 executor arm, 2 deriver arms, `const program` → `let`, 1 named boolean, 1 widened event doc, 1 log message. ZERO new events, error codes, registry rows, state fields, `BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members or clause-table rows; no `continuous.ts`/`redact.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/schema diff; **no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump** — no event or state shape changed. `perHeadsProgram.test.ts` (**36 tests**, `SEEDS = 12` chosen by measurement over seeds 0–199: Wugtrio's three-tails outcome first appears at seed 10 and is the binding constraint, and 12 also covers Wugtrio's full `{0,1,2,3}`) + 4 touched suites; full `bun run check` green, **173 files / 2747 tests**. |
| D131 | The UNCONDITIONAL MILL, BOTH DIRECTIONS — "Discard the top [N cards|card] of your [opponent's] deck.": 16 printings on ONE regex and ONE deriver arm, and the widening a PREVIOUS SLICE had already specified (P3-M5, engine 0.82.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Discard the top [{N} cards|card] of your [opponent's] deck."* now SIMULATES — **16 printings / 7 distinct clauses**, the largest single mapping this family has taken and the cheapest per printing. Census re-run 2026-08-01 against the local D1 over the WHOLE effect string (978 cards, 809 carrying attacks): **7 opponent-side** (Chi-Yu ex `sv02-040`/`-234`/`-259` "Jealously Singe", Garganacl `sv02-123` + Diggersby `sv03-113` "Knocking Hammer", Paldean Clodsire `sv03-128` "Muddy Hammer", Skwovet `sv03-178` "Nicked Nibble") and **9 own-side** (Fraxure `sv06.5-045`/`-077` + Haxorus `sv06.5-046` "Dragon Pulse", Gyarados `swsh10.5-022` "Wild Splash", Tyranitar `sv02-135`/`-222` "Dread Mountain", Tyranitar ex `sv03-066`/`-211` "Mountain Hurl", Diglett `sv03-103` "Hole-Diggin' Noggin"). Every one is a STANDALONE single sentence; 9 of the 16 also carry printed damage. **⚠️ THE HEADLINE IS THAT A PREVIOUS SLICE HAD ALREADY WRITTEN THE SPEC.** D130 shipped the mill op OPPONENT-ONLY by D104's minimal shape and wrote into its own doc block that the own-deck reading ("Wild Splash") was a live unmapped sentence, and that *"the first slice that maps it widens this field then, with two readings in hand rather than one guessed at."* This is that slice, executed verbatim off that note with nothing re-derived: `discardOpponentDeckTop` → **`discardDeckTop { whose: "self" | "opponent"; count }`**, and the interpreter arm grew **one ternary** (`op.whose === "self" ? ctx.seat : otherSeat(ctx.seat)`). **The generalisation: when you decline a widening, WRITE DOWN THE CONDITION THAT WOULD WARRANT IT — the note is executable later, and the return is a whole slice that costs a regex.** **⚠️ WIDEN THE FIELD, DO NOT ADD A MEMBER (D128's provenance rule in the small).** The two sentences differ in exactly one word and everything downstream of picking the seat is byte-identical, so a second op member would be two copies of one `slice`. **Gyarados `swsh10.5-022` settles it alone: index 0 mills the OPPONENT (D130, behind an until-tails coin), index 1 mills its OWN deck for 5 — one card, one action, two directions.** The fixture already carried both sentences verbatim (D130 fielded "Wild Splash" as its refused near-miss), so the widening's proof case cost zero new bytes and one flipped assertion. **⚠️ TWO CAPTURES IN ONE REGEX IS NOT A D119 VIOLATION.** D119 ("if TWO things vary it is not one token") governs the clause TABLE, where a row is a lookup KEY and a two-token row is N rows in disguise. Here both varying tokens land in FIELDS of one op (`count`, `whose`), each has a closed reading, and the printed sentence is a single action — so a regex with two captures is simply honest about having two. **⚠️ THE SINGULAR IS A SEPARATE ALTERNATION BRANCH, NOT `(\d+)?`** — "the top card" (7 printings) never carries a digit, "the top N cards" (9) always does, and "the top cards" is neither and reads as nothing. The undefined capture means one, invented at the site: the only place this deriver invents a number rather than reading one. **The branch is about SPELLING and does not filter**: "the top 1 cards" is unprinted but unambiguous and is accepted, because refusing it would cost a digit class existing to reject one string. (An earlier draft of the doc + test claimed the refusal; the test caught the claim, and the claim was corrected rather than the regex.) **⚠️ FIVE REAL CATALOG ROWS ARE REFUSED BY THE ANCHORS ALONE**, and they are this suite's "stays LOUD" witnesses: Wiglett `sv01-056` (`Flip a coin. If heads, discard the top card of your opponent's deck.` — the same action GATED, printed one evolution below Wugtrio, and mapped by nothing today); Wugtrio `sv01-057` / Gyarados `swsh10.5-022` idx 0 (D130's programs — simulated, but by the OTHER reader, which is the boundary pinned: one anchored reader per printed string, never two); Whiscash `sv03-109` (`For each {F} Energy attached to this Pokémon, discard the top card of your opponent's deck.` — a BOARD-COUNTED amount, genuinely unmapped, and the shape D128 would answer); and **Camerupt `sv03-032`** (`Discard the top card of each player's deck. This attack does 100 more damage for each Energy card discarded in this way.` — two decks AND a bonus reading WHAT was discarded; the dangerous one, since a loosened anchor would mill one deck and silently drop the bonus). `deriveAttackDamageBonus` refuses Camerupt too, so the whole row stays loud on BOTH readers — a genuine gap rather than a half-simulation. **⚠️ THE SELF-MILL IS A PRINTED COST, AND §14.3 IS STILL OWED TO THE DRAW.** The op can now empty the ATTACKER's deck (Haxorus pays 3 for 230, Tyranitar 4 for 230), and the rule is unchanged by that: the loss lands at that player's next draw step (`flow.ts` `startTurn`). Attacking ends the turn, so an OPPONENT mill-out surfaces in the SAME event batch (`DECK_TOP_DISCARDED` < `TURN_ENDED` < `TURN_STARTED` < `GAME_OVER`) while a SELF mill-out produces **no `GAME_OVER` at all in that batch** and loses a FULL TURN LATER, after the opponent passes. Both are pinned; the asymmetry is entirely in the rule, not in the op. **⚠️ NOTHING DOWNSTREAM NEEDED A DIFF, AND THAT IS A PROPERTY D113/D130 PAID FOR IN ADVANCE.** `DECK_TOP_DISCARDED` was already provenance-neutral and keyed to the deck's OWNER, so one op emitting on both seats needed **no log copy change and no new event** — the exact payoff of the "reuse the event, fix the message" rule. `redact.ts` still needs no arm (the discard pile is public). **⚠️ A CONTROL BECAME A CASE, and was strengthened rather than deleted:** `retreatLock.test.ts`'s "does not inherit down the attack index" case rides Paldean Clodsire `sv03-128`, whose index-1 "Muddy Hammer" was the *unsimulated* half of a "one card, one derived attack" control. It now derives a DIFFERENT op, so the absent lock is a statement about the declared attack's TEXT rather than about an absence. Cost: 1 regex, 1 deriver arm, 1 renamed + widened `EffectOp` member, 1 ternary. ZERO new events, ops, `AttackCoinFlip`/`AttackFlipCount`/`BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members, clause-table rows, state fields, prompts, error codes, registry rows, redact arms or log arms; no `rng.ts` / `continuous.ts` / `attack.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/schema diff; **no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump** — no event or state shape changed. `deckTopMill.test.ts` (**21 tests**, ONE board and no seed sweep — the sentences take no coin, and the suite asserts that by pinning `rngState` unchanged on both directions) + 1 new fixture (Skwovet `sv03-178`, verbatim) + 1 new deck + 4 touched suites; full `bun run check` green, **174 files / 2768 tests**. `biome format` churn re-measured on a clean tree: **20 files, unchanged**. |
| D132 | The BARE SELF-HEAL — "Heal {N} damage from this Pokémon.": 18 printings on ONE regex and ONE deriver arm, an op that had existed since 0.x, and the discovery that every recorded `biome format` churn number was a `--max-diagnostics` CAP (P3-M5, engine 0.83.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Heal {N} damage from this Pokémon."* now SIMULATES — **18 printings / 4 distinct clauses**, every one a STANDALONE single sentence. Census run 2026-08-01 against the local D1 over the WHOLE effect string (978 cards): **8 at 30** (Horsea `sv06.5-010`/`-067` + Slowpoke `swsh10.5-019` "Hold Still", Slowbro `sv01-043` + Azumarill `sv02-045` "Bubble Drain", Brambleghast `sv02-024` "Absorb Life", Ceruledge `sv02-098` "Life Sucker", Dolliv `sv03-020` "Sunny Wind"), **5 at 20** (Natu `swsh10.5-032` "Nap", Tropius `sv02-007`/`-195` "Leaf Drain", Marill `sv02-044`/`-204` "Bubble Drain"), **4 at 10** (Shroomish `sv01-003` + Smoliv `sv03-019` "Absorb", Bramblin `sv02-023`/`-198` "Blot") and **1 at 50** (Ariados `swsh10.5-007` "Absorb"). **⚠️ THE OP WAS ALREADY BUILT, AND HAD BEEN SINCE 0.x.** `heal { target: "self"; amount }` is one of the oldest members of `EffectOp`, shipped for the COMPOUND printing "This Pokémon is now Asleep. Heal {N} damage from it." (`SELF_SLEEP_HEAL`) and exercised end to end ever since; `interpreter.ts`'s `healSelf` was already correct including its clamp and its silence. **What was missing was never a mechanism — it was a READER for the same action printed without the Sleep clause in front of it**, and the whole slice is a regex, an arm and a guard for eighteen cards. This is D131's lesson pointing the other way: D131 read a note a previous slice left; D132 found that a previous slice had already shipped the *machinery* and only the sentence was unread. **The generalisation: before pricing a shape, ask what an EXISTING arm already emits — the compound form of a sentence is the cheapest possible spec for its bare form.** **⚠️ ONE ANCHORED READER PER PRINTED STRING, PROVED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** `SELF_HEAL = /^Heal (\d+) damage from this Pokémon\.$/` starts at `^Heal`; `SELF_SLEEP_HEAL` never does, so neither anchor can claim the other's string and the invariant holds **by construction rather than by arm ordering**. The suite asserts the compound's second op is **byte-identical** (`toEqual`) to the bare anchor's for the same N — which turns "one action, two sentences" into a checkable claim. **⚠️ THE GUARD IS THE FAMILY'S; THE CLAMP IS THE INTERPRETER'S.** `amount >= 1` refuses a printed "Heal 0 damage" (a silent no-op reporting a simulated effect is strictly worse than a loud unsimulated one), but there is deliberately **NO ceiling and NO clamp in the deriver** — `healSelf` already takes `Math.min(amount, damage)`, so the printed number is a MAXIMUM and the board decides. A **whiff is SILENT**: an undamaged Active emits no `HEALED` row at all, and the attack is still SIMULATED. "The op ran and had nothing to do" and "the sentence was never read" are different facts and must not share a log row — both are pinned. **⚠️ TWELVE OF THE EIGHTEEN CARRY PRINTED DAMAGE**, which the tail placement (D125) handles with no new thought: ops run AFTER the §8.5 pipeline, so Ceruledge's "50 to the defender, then heal 30 on itself" resolves in printed order for free. The other six print no `damage` field at all, so the heal is the entire visible result — **both halves are pinned, because a build that folded the heal into the damage pipeline would pass the first and fail the second**. The direction is asserted from the side that must NOT move (the defender is never healed). **⚠️ THE `heal` MEMBER WAS THE ONLY ONE IN THE WHOLE `EffectOp` UNION WITH NO DOC COMMENT** — it now has one, and `target` staying a one-member literal union is recorded as deliberate (no pool printing heals the DEFENDER; "this Pokémon" is resolved at `ctx.seat` by the interpreter, not carried in the op). **⚠️ A CONTROL BECAME A STRONGER CASE, not a deletion:** `clauseTable.test.ts`'s "Ceruledge's index-0 heal is its own index-keyed negative" asserted `deriveAttackEffect(lifeSucker)` was `null`; it now asserts the exact `heal` op, so the two SCALING derivers staying silent is a statement about **which reader owns which sentence** rather than about an absence. The `sv02-098` fixture doc block (which called Life Sucker "UNBUILT … a standing marker for the bare-heal sentence family") was rewritten. **⚠️ FIVE REAL CATALOG ROWS ARE REFUSED BY THE ANCHOR ALONE** and are this suite's "stays LOUD" witnesses: Nacli `sv02-121` (`Heal 20 damage from 1 of your Pokémon.` — `healChosen` EXISTS and the sentence is unread), Steenee `sv03-017` (`Heal 30 damage from each of your Pokémon.` — `healEach` EXISTS, likewise), Blissey `swsh10.5-052` (`Heal all damage from 1 of your Benched Pokémon.`), Fuecoco `sv02-035` (`Flip a coin. If heads, heal 30 damage from this Pokémon.` — `deriveAttackCoinFlip` refuses it too, so the row stays loud on BOTH readers: a genuine gap, not a half-simulation) and Iron Moth `sv06.5-009` (`Heal from this Pokémon the same amount of damage you did to your opponent's Active Pokémon.`). **⚠️ THE CHURN NUMBER WAS NEVER A MEASUREMENT.** Successive sessions recorded `biome format` churn as 89 (D128), 57 (D113) and 20 files (D131) and each told the next to "re-measure rather than quote". The cause is now known: **`biome format` truncates its diagnostics at `--max-diagnostics`, default 20**, so every one of those figures was a CAP or a differently-capped count, not a count of dirty files. Measured to CONVERGENCE (identical at `--max-diagnostics` 500/1000/3000): **88 files repo-wide, 83 excluding the gitignored `.claude/worktrees/` copies.** Always pass an explicit high `--max-diagnostics` before quoting a churn number. None of this slice's five touched files is in that set. Cost: 1 regex, 1 deriver arm, 1 test fixture (Natu `swsh10.5-032`, verbatim off the D1), 1 test deck, 1 test file, 1 doc block on the undocumented member, 1 flipped assertion. ZERO new events, ops, `EffectOp`/`AttackCoinFlip`/`AttackFlipCount`/`BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members, clause-table rows, state fields, prompts, error codes, registry rows, redact arms or log arms; **NO INTERPRETER DIFF**; no `rng.ts`/`continuous.ts`/`attack.ts`/`events.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff; **no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump**. `selfHeal.test.ts` (**17 tests**, ONE board, no seed sweep — the sentence takes no coin and the suite pins `rngState` unchanged) + 1 touched suite; full `bun run check` green, **175 files / 2785 tests**. |
| D133 | The OWN-BOARD HEAL — "Heal {N} damage from each of your Pokémon.": 7 printings on ONE regex and ONE deriver arm, D132's inventory rule applied to D132's OWN remainder list, and the reading ("each of your Pokémon" = Active + Bench) is the only substantive content (P3-M5, engine 0.84.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Heal {N} damage from each of your Pokémon."* now SIMULATES — **7 printings / 2 distinct clauses** (N ∈ {20, 30}), every one a STANDALONE single sentence. Census run 2026-08-01 against the local D1 over the WHOLE effect string (978 cards, `attacks_json LIKE '%from each of your%'`): **5 at 30** (Skeledirge ex `sv02-037`/`-233`/`-258`/`-272` "Vitality Song", **WITH a printed 50**, + Steenee `sv03-017` "Aromatherapy", no damage) and **2 at 20** (Cresselia `sv06.5-021`/`-071` "Healing Pirouette", no damage). **⚠️ NO NEW SWEEP WAS NEEDED — THIS SLICE WAS READ STRAIGHT OFF D132'S REMAINDER LIST**, which had already ranked it "the CHEAPEST un-taken thing in the engine", named all 7 printings, and flagged the one thing to verify (does `healEach` cover the Active?). It cost exactly what that note forecast. **⚠️ D132's INVENTORY RULE, APPLIED TO ITS OWN LEFTOVERS.** `healEach { amount }` has shipped since 0.x, produced by Garganacl `sv02-123`'s between-turns "Blessed Salt" Ability; what was missing was a READER for the same action printed as an ATTACK. **NO INTERPRETER DIFF, for the second slice running** — and the test asserts the derived op is byte-identical to the program the Garganacl registry row carries, which is what makes "one action, two readers" checkable rather than descriptive. **⚠️ THE READING IS THE SLICE, NOT THE REGEX. "Each of your Pokémon" is the ACTIVE *and* the whole Bench** (§1.1 — the Active is one of your Pokémon; only the printed word "Benched" excludes it, D120's zone rule). `healEach` was already written that way (`side.active` then `side.bench` in index order), but it had only ever been reached from a CHECKUP trigger, where the Active is nobody in particular; from an ATTACK the Active is the ATTACKER, which is precisely the case a build that healed "the rest of the board" would get wrong. So it is asserted end to end rather than inherited from the op's doc comment — including the degenerate **empty-Bench** board, where the Active alone IS "each". **⚠️ THE CLAMP IS PER POKÉMON, AND THE BOARD IS BUILT TO SHOW IT:** {100, 80, 10} damage against a printed 30 heals {30, 30, 10} and emits three rows reporting exactly that. A shared clamp, or a clamp read once off the Active, passes every one-body board and fails only here. The whiff is silent PER POKÉMON too — an undamaged body BETWEEN two damaged ones emits NO row and does not truncate the sequence, so the count of HEALED rows is the count of Pokémon that MOVED, not the count on the board. **⚠️ THE SEAT SCOPE IS THE `healEachAll` BOUNDARY, ASSERTED FROM BOTH SIDES:** the Picnic Basket sentence ("each Pokémon (both yours and your opponent's)") is refused at the deriver, and the end-to-end case DAMAGES the opponent's Active and Bench so they can be asserted unhealed — invisible on an undamaged opponent, which is why every earlier heal board would have passed a seat-blind implementation. **⚠️ THE ANCHOR'S REAL JOB IS SAGUARO** `sv02-187`/`-255`/`-270`: "Choose up to 2 of your Pokémon and heal 50 damage from each of them." — the mapped words, mid-sentence, behind a choice. A reader without `^` takes its tail and heals the whole board unconditionally. It is a Supporter and never routes through this deriver in production; the guard is asserted anyway, because **a guard that depends on which reader a card happens to reach is not a guard**. **⚠️ NEW TEST SURGERY, AND IT IS A REAL TRAP: `clearBench`.** `setActiveFromDeck` DISPLACES the Active it replaces onto the bench rather than removing it, so a board built by two or three successive Active surgeries silently carries one benched stranger per surgery. Invisible to every suite that only asserts the Active (most of them), and WRONG for any suite whose claim is about the whole SIDE — a board heal, a spread, a bench count. The first three test failures of this slice were all that, not the engine. Cost: **1 regex, 1 deriver arm, 2 test fixtures (Skeledirge ex `sv02-037`, Cresselia `sv06.5-021`), 1 test deck, 1 test surgery helper, 1 test file, 1 widened `EffectOp` doc block, 2 re-pointed witnesses in `selfHeal.test.ts`**. ZERO new events, ops, `EffectOp`/`AttackCoinFlip`/`AttackFlipCount`/`BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members, clause-table rows, state fields, prompts, error codes, registry rows, redact arms or log arms; **NO INTERPRETER DIFF**; no `rng.ts`/`continuous.ts`/`attack.ts`/`events.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff; **no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump**. `boardHeal.test.ts` (**20 tests**, ONE board, no seed sweep) + 1 touched suite; full `bun run check` green, **176 files / 2805 tests**. |
| D134 | The FLIP-GATED OP family — "Flip a coin. If heads, <action>.": 3 printings / 2 clauses on TWO anchored regexes and TWO deriver arms, both consequents and the gate ALREADY BUILT, and a one-printing sentence is warranted by its SHAPE rather than by its printing count (P3-M5, engine 0.85.0) | 2026-08-01 | *"Flip a coin. If heads, heal {N} damage from this Pokémon."* (Fuecoco `sv02-035`/`-201` "Spacing Out", **2 printings**) and *"Flip a coin. If heads, discard the top card of your opponent's deck."* (Wiglett `sv01-056` "Dig a Little", **1**) now SIMULATE. Census run 2026-08-01 against the local D1 over the WHOLE effect string (978 cards, `attacks_json LIKE '%Flip a coin. If heads,%'` — **76 printings / 29 distinct clauses**, the whole gated neighbourhood in one query, counted through the derivers themselves: 45 mapped / 31 unmapped after this slice): these are the only two whose consequent this reader already had an op for. **Neither prints a `damage` field**, so the gated action is the ENTIRE visible result of the declaration and a tails face moves nothing anywhere. **⚠️ D132's INVENTORY RULE FOR THE THIRD SLICE RUNNING, and the cheapest form it has taken.** `heal { target: "self" }` has shipped since 0.x, `discardDeckTop` since D130, and `coinFlipGate` since 0.x with TWO members already reading it (`FLIP_DEFENDER_NOW`, 16 printings; `FLIP_OPPONENT_ACTIVE_DISCARD`, 4). What was missing was two READERS. **NO INTERPRETER DIFF, for the third slice running** — and the suite asserts the derived program's `then` is byte-identical to what the BARE anchor derives for the same clause, in both directions, which is what makes "one action, two printings" checkable rather than descriptive. **⚠️ THE ONE JUDGEMENT CALL IS SHIPPING THE TWO TOGETHER, AND IT IS THE SLICE'S GENERAL LESSON.** Fuecoco is 2 printings and clears D121's warrant alone; Wiglett is **ONE** and does not. Taken together the SHAPE is 3 printings / 2 clauses and the engine already reads two other instances of it, so Wiglett is a fourth member of a family rather than a bespoke row for one card. **THE RULE: when a one-printing sentence is an INSTANCE of a shape the engine already reads, the warrant is owed by the SHAPE, not by the printing — count members of the family, not copies of the card.** (The converse still holds: a one-printing sentence that introduces a shape still owes D121's two.) **⚠️ LITERAL ARMS, NOT A D120 TEMPLATE, and this is the precondition FAILING rather than a preference.** The token that varies between the two anchors is the WHOLE CONSEQUENT — an open-ended vocabulary of everything an `EffectOp` can express — where D120 licenses a template only for a token with a CLOSED reading (the five status words, the nine energy codes). The suite asserts it as a SET INTERSECTION: the two consequents share not one word after "If heads, ". So the gate prefix is spelled out once per consequent and `effects.ts` now carries four copies of it on purpose. **⚠️ THE MILL ARM IS DELIBERATELY NARROWER THAN `DECK_TOP_MILL`**, which reads both a plural count and either deck: exactly one gated printing exists and it is the SINGULAR, OPPONENT-side one, so widening would invent three readings the ingest has never produced — the same guess this family refuses everywhere else. Widen it when a printing appears, with the readings in hand (D130→D131 is the precedent for how). **⚠️ THE TAILS FACE IS THE HALF THAT NEEDED TESTING, and this is the first heal-or-mill suite that takes a SEED SWEEP** (8 seeds per case, collecting both faces, asserting both were seen so no pair passes vacuously). THREE states are distinguishable and all three are real: tails (the branch never ran), heads-with-nothing-to-do (it ran and whiffed — an undamaged attacker heals nothing), and SKIPPED (the sentence was never read). **A TAILS IS NOT A SKIPPED EFFECT** — no `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED` on either face — and **the coin is TAKEN on both**, so `rngState` advances even when nothing happens; a build that checked the face before flipping would desynchronise the next coin in an online match. **⚠️ A FIXTURE HP TRAP, and it cost the slice its only three failures: the attacker carries its own damage BEFORE it declares.** Fuecoco is 90 HP; a damage figure at or above that is a body the attack epilogue sweeps off the board mid-case, so every later assertion is about a promoted stranger. Own-board damage figures must be read against the FIXTURE's printed HP, which the defender-side figures every other suite uses never had to be. Cost: **2 regexes, 2 deriver arms, 2 test fixtures (Fuecoco `sv02-035`, Wiglett `sv01-056`), 1 test deck, 1 test file (24 tests), 2 widened `EffectOp` doc blocks, 3 re-pointed witnesses (`selfHeal`, `boardHeal`, `deckTopMill` — none deleted, and each re-pointed case now makes a STRONGER claim than its null did)**. ZERO new events, ops, `EffectOp`/`AttackCoinFlip`/`AttackFlipCount`/`BoardCondition`/`DamageCountSource` members, clause-table rows, state fields, prompts, error codes, registry rows, redact arms or log arms; **NO INTERPRETER DIFF**; no `rng.ts`/`continuous.ts`/`attack.ts`/`events.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff; **no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump**. `flipGatedOp.test.ts` + 3 touched suites; full `bun run check` green exit 0, **177 files / 2831 tests**. |

ANY-ZONE (5 printings / 3 clauses): Nacli `sv02-121`/`-220` "Salt Coating" (20), Smoliv `sv01-021` "Nutrients" (30), Vespiquen ex `sv03-096`/`-212` "Healing Pheromone" (60). BENCH-ONLY (5 / 4): Combee `sv03-008` "Share" (20), Chansey `swsh10.5-051` "Delicious Egg" (30), Tropius `sv01-007` "Fresh-Picked Fruit" (60), Blissey `swsh10.5-052` "Enriching Egg" + Arboliva `sv03-021` "Healing Fruit" ("all"). **NOT ONE of the ten carries a printed `damage` field**, so the heal is the entire visible result every time. **After this slice the heal neighbourhood is CLOSED** — every "Heal …" sentence the pool prints as an attack has a reader except Iron Moth `sv06.5-009`'s damage-echo and Floette `sv01-092`'s compound gate. **⚠️ THE HEADLINE IS THAT THE MECHANISM QUESTION THIS SLICE WAS PRICED ON DOES NOT EXIST.** D132, D133 and D134 each carried the item forward with the same note — *"the first PARKING op the ATTACK deriver emits; check how D125's tail placement interacts with a park before pricing it"* — which made it the expensive item on three consecutive lists. It is false, and had been for many slices: `damageChosen` (the derived snipes) and `discardEnergy` (the derived self-discard cost) already park from an attack tail, through the SAME `runProgram` + `settleProgram` a Trainer uses, differing only by `{ resumeTail: true }`. A park writes an `effect:choose` phase, emits `EFFECT_PENDING`, and LEAVES `attackEpilogue` on `state.pending`, so the KO sweep, the prizes and §5.3's turn end all wait for the choice and then run in order. **ZERO `attack.ts`/`flow.ts`/`cardplay.ts` diff.** **THE GENERALISATION IS ABOUT THE NOTE RATHER THAN THE CODE: A FORECAST OF COST IS NOT A MEASUREMENT OF IT** — the note was written by a slice that never looked, copied forward verbatim three times, and gained authority from repetition alone; the check took one read. **Verify a forecast before letting it re-rank a list** — a wrong estimate is worse than none, because it MOVES work rather than merely leaving it unsized. **⚠️ THE ONLY NEW VOCABULARY IS `healChosen.zone?: "bench"`** — the printed word "Benched" and nothing else (D120): absent, the candidates are ALL your Pokémon because §1.1 makes the Active one of them; present, the Active is dropped. **A FIELD, NOT A SECOND OP MEMBER** (D131's widen-don't-add rule, applied to the op that already carries `upTo` the same way) — everything downstream of picking the candidate set is byte-identical, so the interpreter diff is ONE TERNARY over `ownBenchRefs`, a helper `switchActive` and `gust` have used since 0.x. The zone reaches BOTH arms from one place including `upTo` (which no printing combines with it): a field that silently meant nothing on one arm is a trap for the first author who combines them, and `healUpToNote` takes the zone word so a heading can never disagree with its candidates. **⚠️ ONE REGEX, TWO CAPTURES, ONE OP — D131's shape, and NOT a D120 template.** Both varying tokens land in FIELDS of one op rather than rows of a lookup table, so D119 does not govern; a template needs a closed token AND a table to look it up in, and there is no table. **"all" is a SPELLING OF THE AMOUNT, not a separate sentence** — `healChosen.amount` has taken `number \| "all"` since 0.x, so Blissey/Arboliva cost one alternation branch. **The cross product is deliberately UNFILTERED**: "Heal all damage from 1 of your Pokémon." is unprinted and this anchor accepts it, the same call D131 made for "the top 1 cards" — a refusal would be a claim about the INGEST rather than about the game. **⚠️ AN EMPTY BENCH IS A WHIFF, NOT A SKIP.** `parkOrForce` has three endings and all three are reachable from these ten printings: zero candidates resolves SILENTLY, exactly one is applied INLINE with no prompt, two or more park. None emits `ATTACK_EFFECT_SKIPPED`, and not by accident — `attack.ts`'s `effectSimulated` is `program !== null \|\| …`, so a derived program means the sentence WAS read whatever it then did (D134's three-state distinction on a different axis). **⚠️ A CORRECTION TO D134's REMAINDER LIST**, which had the Bench half at 4 printings: it is **5**. Arboliva `sv03-021` prints the "all" clause as its ATTACK ("Healing Fruit") and had no registry row. **⚠️ AND THE `ARBOLIVA` REGISTRY ROW IS A DIFFERENT CARD, NOT A DIFFERENT PRINTING** — mis-stated while authoring, corrected against the D1: that row is keyed **`sv01-023`**, a 150 HP Arboliva from another set whose only attack is "Solar Beam" and whose `onEvolve` Ability "Enriching Oil" heals "1 of your Pokémon" with NO zone word, while the printing mapped here is **`sv03-021`**, 140 HP, with NO Ability at all. `programFor("sv03-021")` is undefined ENTIRELY and `sv01-023`'s row is untouched. **Two cards, one name** — key every registry claim by ID and check it, because the wrong reading had a registry row shadowing a deriver it never meets. **Witnesses RE-POINTED, none deleted:** `selfHeal.test.ts` and `boardHeal.test.ts` each listed all three chosen-heal clauses as "stays LOUD" near-misses, and each now makes a SHAPE claim instead of a null. **Five of `selfHeal`'s seven near-misses now land on a neighbouring anchor** — what a family being finished looks like from the inside — so **Floette `sv01-092` was ADDED** as a live unmapped witness ("Flip a coin. If heads, this attack does 30 more damage, and heal 30 damage from this Pokémon.": this anchor's exact action, lowercase, at the tail of a gate that also carries a pre-damage bonus one op cannot ride). Cost: **1 regex, 1 deriver arm, 1 `EffectOp` FIELD, 1 interpreter ternary + 1 note parameter, 3 test fixtures (Nacli `sv02-121`, Tropius `sv01-007`, and `fix-allheal` — Blissey/Arboliva's real "all" sentence on an invented BASIC body, because both real printings are evolutions), 1 test deck (`CHOSEN_HEAL_DECK`), 2 re-pointed witnesses**. `chosenHeal.test.ts` (**25 tests**) + 1 test deck (`CHOSEN_HEAL_DECK`). The suite covers the arm from both ends: all seven clauses table-driven, the `zone` key asserted ABSENT (not `undefined`) on the any-zone derivations, op identity against Potion's authored `healChosen`, zero registry rows, and end to end — the three `parkOrForce` endings, a DAMAGED Active excluded from a bench-only `prompt.candidates`, the `resumeTail` park→`resolveEffect`→epilogue round trip (`TURN_ENDED` strictly after `HEALED`), the clamp, the silent whiff, and `rngState` unchanged throughout. | D136 | The FIRST CODE REVIEW of D96–D135 — three findings on 40 unreviewed decisions, and TWO OF THE THREE WERE PINNED AS INTENDED BY THE SUITES THEMSELVES (P3-M5, engine unchanged) | 2026-08-02 | `/code-review` over `git diff origin/backend...HEAD` — **25 commits, 50 files, ~33k lines, D112–D135** — the first review this branch has had since D96, run at the user's trigger (it is user-triggered; no loop session can launch it, and every one of the 24 autoloop sessions ended by saying so). **Three findings, one medium and two low; nothing structural came back, and the list of things the reviewer chased and CLEARED is the more informative half** — the retreat-block lifetime across all four turn orderings, all three bench→Active routes stamping `promotedTurn` with no stale-flag path, `gust`/`switchOwn` both routing through `switchInto`, the deliberately-inverted `ATTACK_REQUIREMENT_CLAUSES` polarity, the `phaseViewOf` import raising no runtime cycle, disjointness of the four effect-text readers, and `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION = 2` correctly retiring pre-`promotedTurn` records. It also **re-derived D129's termination argument independently** by scanning the full 2³² mulberry32 cycle: longest heads run **31**, so `MAX_UNTIL_TAILS_FLIPS = 64` is genuinely unreachable. **⚠️ FINDING 1 (medium) — THE MILL LOG ROW CREDITED THE VICTIM.** `DECK_TOP_DISCARDED` files under `event.seat` = the deck's OWNER (correct, and deliberate since D131) but read in the ACTIVE VOICE: rows render after their seat's name, so P1's Chi-Yu ex produced "**P2** discarded 2 cards from the top of their deck" — the milling credited to the player who lost the cards. **D130 SAW HALF OF THIS AND FIXED THE WRONG HALF**: it noted the message "was a lie the moment it had a second producer", dropped the false word "looked-at" and called the result provenance-neutral — but neutral wording addresses PROVENANCE, and the VOICE is what carries the ACTOR. Fixed by making the verb PASSIVE (`had {n} cards discarded from the top of their deck`), not by re-seating the row. Passive over `ENERGY_DISCARDED`'s separate `actor` field because it costs no event field and this row never names the actor anyway — the producing `ATTACK_DECLARED`/`ABILITY_TRIGGERED` row sits directly above it. Tri Howl's own-deck row also reads passive, which is mildly stilted for a self-discard and is the correct trade: the alternative is WRONG on the mill. **⚠️ FINDING 2 (low) — THREE REGEXES MISSED THE `['’]` CLASS, AND TWO SUITES HAD PINNED THE GAP AS INTENDED.** `ATTACK_COIN_MILL_PRINTED`, `ATTACK_COIN_MILL_UNTIL_TAILS` (D130) and `OPPONENT_ACTIVE_TYPE_CLAUSE` (D120) spelled `opponent's` straight-only while every sibling reading the SAME noun phrase (`DECK_TOP_MILL`, `FLIP_DECK_TOP_MILL`, `DEFENDER_NOW`) carries the class that `effects.ts` documents as existing *"so a re-ingest changing only punctuation cannot silently un-derive"* the sentence. **The ASYMMETRY is the defect, not the spelling**: a catalog re-ingest normalising to U+2019 would keep D131's unconditional mills deriving while D130's two dropped to the loud path — a regression that reads as "two cards broke" with the punctuation nowhere in sight. **⚠️ AND THE TESTS ASSERTED THE BUG.** `perHeadsProgram.test.ts` listed both curly variants as near-misses that must be REFUSED, commenting that they "would silently drop both cards onto the loud path with no number to notice it by" — the risk named, then pinned as correct. `typeNameClause.test.ts` pinned Wonder Flash's BYTES and observed that a curly apostrophe "would silently un-map the clause". **Both suites documented the hazard instead of catching it, which is what a near-miss list does when nobody asks whether the near-miss is actually a MISS.** The strings moved out of the refusal lists into POSITIVE assertions of **equality with the straight form** (not merely non-null: the apostrophe carries no meaning, so both spellings must yield the SAME member). The byte-pins STAY — they say what the catalog holds today; the new tests say the reader does not depend on it, and pinning only the first mistakes a property of the current ingest for a property of the engine. **⚠️ FINDING 3 (low, latent) — THE TRUNCATION GUARD HAD NO READER, AND CLOSING IT COST THE ENGINE'S FIRST `throw`.** `rng.ts` returns FACES rather than a count *"precisely so a caller can detect the truncation"*, and no caller did: every consumer tallies heads, so a truncated all-heads sequence would quietly score **64 heads** — of damage on one printing, of mill iterations on the other. Guard placed in `takeFlips` (the single choke point for the `untilTails` arm, covering both consumers and any future one) rather than at the flip site the review named. **THE ENGINE HAS ZERO OTHER THROWS AND IS TOTAL BY DESIGN**, so this is a real convention break, taken because there is NO HONEST VALUE TO RETURN: a "flip until tails" that has not seen tails has no heads count, refusing the attack would invent game semantics the rules do not describe, and scoring the truncation corrupts the match record. Unreachable by construction (31 < 64), so it cannot fire today — it fires only if someone replaces `nextU32` with something degenerate, and a crash at the flip site is how they should find out rather than through a match that paid 64 heads. **DELIBERATELY UNTESTED**: driving it needs `vi.mock` on the RNG, which the engine suite has never used and which would break a stronger convention than the throw does; `rng.ts`'s own truncation branch stays reachable through its `cap` parameter and is covered there. **⚠️ NOT DONE, AND THE ONE THING A NEXT SLICE SHOULD KNOW: THE LITERAL CLAUSE-TABLE KEYS HAVE THE SAME EXPOSURE.** ~8 rows (`"your opponent's Active Pokémon is Poisoned"` and its neighbours) are exact `Map` lookups with straight-only apostrophes, so the same re-ingest drops them too. The fix is NOT a character class — a Map key cannot carry one — it is a normalisation pass at lookup, which is a design call rather than a patch, and it was left out rather than expanded into silently. **THE GENERALISATION, AND IT IS ABOUT THE LOOP RATHER THAN THE CODE: A TEST SUITE WRITTEN BY THE SAME SESSION THAT WROTE THE CODE INHERITS ITS BLIND SPOT.** Both low findings were places where the author saw the hazard, wrote it down, and asserted the wrong side of it; the tests passed for 40 decisions because they encoded the belief rather than the requirement. That is precisely the class of error a fresh reader catches and an authoring session cannot, and it is the argument for running `/code-review` on a CADENCE rather than at the end. Engine version UNCHANGED (no behaviour a card can reach moved), no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump, no web/API/DB/schema diff. Full `bun run check` green exit 0, **178 files / 2859 tests** (was 178 / 2857). |
| D137 | The APOSTROPHE FOLD — the LITERAL CLAUSE-TABLE half of D136's hardening: a Map key cannot carry `['’]`, so the fold moves off the KEY and onto the LOOKUP; 7 exposed rows closed on ONE helper, and the census found TWO MORE straight-only regexes D136's review had missed (P3-M5, engine unchanged) | 2026-08-02 | D136's one deliberate leftover, and the cheapest item on its resume point. That review gave three regexes the `['’]` class every sibling on the same noun phrase already carried, and wrote down that the LITERAL clause tables have the identical exposure — a catalog re-ingest normalising straight apostrophes to U+2019 would silently un-derive them — but **A MAP KEY CANNOT CARRY A CHARACTER CLASS**: it is one exact string of bytes or it is nothing. So the hardening had to move off the KEY and onto the LOOKUP, which is a design call rather than a patch and is why D136 left it. **⚠️ THE CENSUS FIRST, AND IT WAS NOT THE ~8 ROWS THE RESUME POINT ESTIMATED.** Grepped every literal `Map`/`Record` lookup on printed text in `packages/engine/src`: **FOUR TABLES, FIVE CALL SITES, and exactly SEVEN exposed rows of the twenty-one that hold printed clauses** — `CONDITIONAL_DAMAGE_CLAUSES` 5 of 19 (`your opponent's Active Pokémon` × "already has any damage counters on it" / "is an Evolution Pokémon" / "is Poisoned" / "is affected by a Special Condition" / "is a Pokémon ex or Pokémon V"), `ATTACK_REQUIREMENT_CLAUSES` **2 of 2** (Palafin's CONTRACTION "didn't" and Lycanroc's POSSESSIVE, so the whole table), and `CLAUSE_ENERGY_TOKENS` (19 rows, 2 sites) + `CLAUSE_POKEMON_TYPES` (11) exposed at ZERO because their vocabularies are closed and schema-derived. The one exact lookup NOT routed through the fold is `deriveAttackEffect`'s `ENERGY_TYPE_BY_CODE`/`ENERGY_TYPE_BY_NAME` pair, indexed with a capture from `TYPED_SELF_DISCARD` — a regex whose alternation IS those two key sets, so an apostrophe cannot reach them by construction. **⚠️ AND THE CENSUS TURNED UP TWO MORE STRAIGHT-ONLY REGEXES, WHICH IS THE SLICE'S REAL FINDING.** `RETREAT_COST_SCALE` and `RETREAT_COST_MULTIPLY` (D91-era, Heracross `sv01-002` / Spidops ex `sv01-019` / Sharpedo `sv03-047` / Stoutland `sv03-172`) spell *"your opponent's Active Pokémon's Retreat Cost"* — **the pool's only sentence carrying TWO possessives**, and D136 found neither. Its search had been for `opponent's`-shaped siblings and these two matched the shape; what it missed is that a fix which classes one slot and not the other reads exactly like a whole fix until a re-ingest lands. Both slots now carry `['’]`. **The lesson is that "find every X" is only as good as the QUERY, and a census run for one purpose is the cheapest place to re-run the previous one's** — this slice was scoped to Map keys and found regexes because it looked at every apostrophe in the file rather than at every table. **⚠️ WHERE THE FOLD LIVES: in `literalClauseRow(table, clause)`, the ONE function every exact lookup on printed text now goes through — NOT at the clause split, and NOT duplicated per lookup helper.** There is deliberately no choke point above the two helpers: D125 made `boardConditionForClause` and `deriveAttackRequirement` share no lookup, because a clause meaning "does 120 more damage if X" must never be reachable from a sentence meaning "does nothing if X", and that disjointness is worth more than the convenience of one entry point. Taking the table AS AN ARGUMENT keeps both properties at once — two vocabularies, one fold, written once — and it is the placement a THIRD consequent inherits for free rather than having to remember. The two token maps are routed through it too although their closed vocabularies can never hold an apostrophe: **the rule is the SITE, not the need** ("every exact lookup on printed text", not "every one that needs it today"), which is precisely how a table added by a later slice gets the hardening without anyone thinking about it. **⚠️ THE FOLD IS A FALLBACK, NOT A NORMALISATION PASS**, and that ordering is the whole safety argument: the clause AS PRINTED is tried first and always wins, and only on a MISS is U+2019 folded to U+0027 and the table asked again. So the change can turn a miss into a hit and can NEVER turn a hit into a different hit — no clause any row already owns is re-interpreted — and a future row keyed with a curly apostrophe stays reachable by its own bytes instead of being shadowed. An `includes` guard in front of the retry states the same thing in code: a clause that misses for any other reason misses exactly once and stays LOUD. **⚠️ APOSTROPHE ONLY — NO LOWERCASING, NO TRIMMING, NO é NORMALISATION, AND THE SCOPE-OUT IS PART OF THE DECISION.** The tables commit to a real U+00E9 in `Pokémon` and to a matcher with no `/i`, and both commitments are load-bearing (`Poisoned`, `Special Condition`, `Benched`, `Active`, `Evolution` are capitalised as printed and told from ordinary words by nothing else). A wider fold would owe a **row-by-row argument that no two distinct printed clauses COLLIDE under it**, which nobody has made; the apostrophe needs no such survey because U+2019 and U+0027 are the same punctuation mark in two encodings and no printed clause is distinguished from another by which one it uses. The negative block of the suite is what stops a later session "improving" this into a general normaliser. **⚠️ THE TEMPLATE/REGEX PASS IS DELIBERATELY NOT ROUTED THROUGH THE FOLD.** Every anchored pattern carries `['’]` in its own source and matches the RAW clause; folding before them would be harmless but redundant, and folding INSTEAD of the class would move a documented, greppable property of each regex into a caller the regex cannot see. **Two mechanisms, one per pass, each visible at the site where it applies** — and the suite pins that separation, so a session deleting a class "because the fold handles it" goes red. **⚠️ THE POOL CLAIM RE-VERIFIED**, since `effects.ts` asserts it in two doc blocks: the local D1 (`apps/api/.wrangler/state/v3/d1`, 978 cards) holds **ZERO U+2019 in `effect`, `attacks_json`, `abilities_json` and `name`**, against **359 cards carrying the straight form in `attacks_json`**. The KEYS stay ASCII on purpose — they record what the catalog prints, not what the lookup will tolerate. **⚠️ THE SUITE'S CENSUS IS DISCOVERED, NOT LISTED**, which is the shape worth copying: it sweeps `FIXTURE_POOL` for every distinct printed effect carrying an apostrophe that ANY of the five readers resolves (**32 sentences at D137**, pinned), rewrites each to U+2019 and asserts **EQUALITY WITH THE STRAIGHT FORM ON ALL FIVE READERS** — D136's shape, never merely "the curly one is non-null", because a non-null check passes on a reader that folded the clause into some other row. So a row, regex or template added by a later slice is swept the day its FIXTURE lands rather than the day somebody remembers this file. The seven literal rows are ALSO listed explicitly, and the disjointness of the two consequents is re-asserted in the curly spelling — a fold written as one merged table instead of one function taking a table would have quietly joined them. Mutation-checked both ways: disabling the retry fails 3 tests, reverting the retreat-cost class fails 2. Cost: **1 helper (7 lines) + 2 constants, 5 call sites re-pointed, 2 regexes classed, 3 doc blocks amended, 1 test file (13 tests)**. ZERO new events, ops, union members, clause-table rows, state fields, prompts, error codes, registry rows, redact arms or log arms; NO interpreter/`attack.ts`/`rng.ts`/`continuous.ts` diff; no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. **Engine version UNCHANGED and no `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump** — no behaviour a card in today's catalog can reach moved, because today's catalog has no U+2019 to fold; this is a property of the READER, bought against an ingest that has not happened yet. Full `bun run check` green exit 0, **179 files / 2872 tests** (was 178 / 2859). |

So the member names the MECHANISM — the enum's first mechanism member since `"confusion"` (poison/burn/confusion are mechanisms, ability/moved/attack are provenances; the enum was always mixed and this joins the older group). **⚠️ "SHOULD A REAL ABILITY AND A TOOL RENDER DIFFERENTLY AT ALL" — ANSWERED `NO`, ON STRUCTURAL GROUNDS RATHER THAN ON COST.** The engine's own aggregation makes them **indistinguishable at the read site by construction** (`passivesOf` hands `attack.ts` a bare `number`; no provenance survives the addition), so the site could not label by kind even if the product wanted it to — asserted, not argued: every authored `damageAttacker` record is checked to carry nothing but `amount`/`requiresTool`. And what the reader needs is *why their attacker suddenly has damage on it*, which is the mechanism; **which slot on which card granted it is not even well-defined** once two cards contribute. **⚠️ AND IT GENERALISES, WHICH IS WHY THE AXIS WAS WORTH ARGUING.** CENSUS (local D1, 2026-08-02, whole effect string, 978 cards, **a NEW census — the counter-PUT one is closed and was not re-run**): "…damage counters on the Attacking Pokémon" appears on **8 printings** — **3 Ability** (Cacnea `sv01-005`, Cacturne `sv01-006`, Stunfisk `sv03-112`, all authored), **2 TOOL** (Rocky Helmet `sv01-193` authored; **Vengeful Punch `sv03-197`** unauthored — same mechanism on a **KO** condition) and **3 ATTACK** (Lycanroc ex ×2 "Scary Fangs" 10 counters, Mabosstiff `sv02-143` "Comeuppance" — counters **equal to the damage done**; both §11 blocks with a DURATION, loud today). **A `"tool"` member would have been wrong before it shipped**, and each of the five unmapped printings joins this label with **NO new member** when it lands — the test of whether the axis was right. **NOT `"recoil"`**: the event already uses that word for `"self"` (an attacker damaging ITSELF with its own printed attack — Skeledirge "Blazing Shout"), the opposite direction and the attacker's own VOICE, so the two are **pinned apart** rather than left to collide. **⚠️ THE ROW STAYS SYSTEM-VOICED, for a reason of its own on top of D138's/D139's:** `seat` here is the ATTACKER's (it owns the DAMAGED Pokémon, per the event's contract) while the **CAUSER is the DEFENDER**, so an active-voice row would credit the attacker with damaging itself — **the mirror image of D136's finding 1**. And unlike every `"ability"` placement there is **no `ABILITY_TRIGGERED` row above naming a source** (it is a passive, and a Tool has no Ability to trigger), so **this row is the entire explanation a reader gets**, which raises the bar on the label being true rather than lowering it. **⚠️ THE GUARD IS SWEPT, NOT LISTED (D140's move) — and its absence is why this lived three versions.** The old "guard" was the ACCIDENT that whoever wrote the literal was thinking of Cacnea, and the suite then pinned the WRONG SIDE of it as intended — **the D136 "the suite asserts the bug" pattern for a THIRD time**. `counterattack.test.ts` DISCOVERS the family from the printed catalog text in `FIXTURE_POOL`, asserts the **text census equals the authored-program census** in both directions, asserts the family **SPANS both `Pokemon` and `Trainer`** (the fact the hardcoded label denied — a pool of only Pokémon *could* honestly say "Ability"), and then **DRIVES every member it found**, each wearing Rocky Helmet so a `requiresTool` producer fires instead of passing vacuously. A fifth producer's fixture is swept the day it lands. **⚠️ NO `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` BUMP, AND THE QUESTION WAS ASKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED (D140's obligation, discharged in the negative).** This site is **not an `EffectOp` at all** — an inline event push; nothing parks on it and no `source` value is stored in `GameState`. `MatchRecord` persists the **RENDERED** `SeatLogEntry[]`, so an in-flight match keeps its already written "Ability:" rows as TEXT and the record stays fully readable; the enum widening is the `ATTACK_FAILED.reason` precedent. **⚠️ A FIFTH SITE SWEPT FOR AND NOT FOUND, WITH THE REASONING WRITTEN DOWN**: the only other hardcoded `COUNTERS_PLACED` provenance left is `attachEnergyFrom`'s `bonusCounters` (Gardevoir ex "Psychic Embrace"), and it is **NOT exposed today** — ONE producer, a real Ability, and its `seat` **IS** `ctx.seat` (the actor's own), so D136's voice defect is impossible by construction there; it becomes a question only if a non-Ability ever reaches that op. Everything else is already parameterised (`damageActive`, `damageChosen`) or mechanism-named (`poison`, `burn`, `confusion`, `self`, `moved`). **Cost: 1 enum member, 1 log arm, 1 literal, 1 test deck (`COUNTERATTACK_DECK` — all four producers on one deck so the sweep can drive every one), `counterattack.test.ts` (16 tests), 3 moved assertions across 2 suites, 1 added `log.test.ts` arm pin, 1 wrong id corrected in D140's own doc block (`sv01-172` → `sv01-193`).** **Mutation-checked four ways**: restoring the hardcoded `"ability"` fails **9** tests across 3 files, wording the new arm "Ability: " fails **3**, turning `passivesOf`'s SUM into a MAX fails **3**, and rendering the row in ACTIVE voice fails **3**. engineVersion 0.89.0 → **0.90.0** (a log label IS behaviour a player sees, and this one changes in every match containing a Rocky Helmet); full `bun run check` **GREEN exit 0, 183 files / 2960 tests** (was 182 / 2944). ZERO new ops, prompts, parks, events, clause-table rows, state fields, error codes, registry rows or redact arms; no `effects.ts`/`interpreter.ts`/`continuous.ts`/`registry.ts`/`flow.ts` diff and no web/API/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff. |
| D142 | The GATED PREVENT clause — *"Flip a coin. If heads, during your opponent's next turn, prevent all damage [FROM AND EFFECTS OF] attacks done to this Pokémon."*: **13 printings across TWO READINGS**, the family's first CONTINUOUS-EFFECT MECHANISM, and the lifetime is a TURN STAMP because the clock D112 reused would lift it a turn EARLY (P3-M5, engine 0.91.0, MATCH_RECORD_VERSION 3 → 4) | 2026-08-02 | The biggest un-taken count in D134's gated census now SIMULATES on **TWO anchored regexes, TWO `deriveAttackEffect` arms, ONE new `EffectOp` (`preventDamage { effects?: true }`), ONE new turn-stamped `InPlayPokemon` field and ONE new `EffectContext` field**. Every previous slice in this tail bought a SENTENCE; this one buys a **LIFETIME**, and three other printed shapes become rows on top of it. **⚠️ THE LIST'S COUNT HELD AND ITS DESCRIPTION DID NOT — the seventh list running to carry something wrong, and the first whose miss is in the WORDS rather than the numbers.** "13 printings / 2 spellings" is exactly right (11 + 2, re-verified against the local D1, 2026-08-02, 978 cards, whole effect string). But **they are not two spellings of one sentence, they are two different READINGS**: eleven say "prevent all damage FROM AND EFFECTS OF attacks done to this Pokémon", two say "prevent all damage done to this Pokémon **by attacks**", and the second stops damage and **nothing else**. A slice that folded them into one anchor would have shipped a block refusing a Poison the card does not refuse. **AND THE COUNT IS A FLOOR, NOT A TOTAL**: D134's census was `LIKE '%Flip a coin. If heads,%'`, so it structurally could not see the **SIX ungated / otherwise-gated printings of the same family** — 3 "…by attacks from **Basic** Pokémon" (Fletchling `sv01-150`, Bombirdier `sv02-153`/`-246`), 1 "…from **Ancient** Pokémon" (Iron Moth `sv06.5-009` idx 1), 1 **KO-gated** (Grimmsnarl `sv02-148` "Accelerate") and 1 **two-consequent coin** (Squawkabilly `sv01-162` "Fly") — **19 printings in all**. Each stays LOUD, each is written down beside the property that refuses it, and the generalisation is: **a census run for one purpose is a FLOOR for every other purpose.** **⚠️ THE §11 READING, AND THE CATALOG PRINTS IT RATHER THAN THE RULEBOOK.** What splits the two halves is the parenthetical on Palafin `sv03-145`: **"(Damage is not an effect.)"** — found in the pool while censusing, not reasoned from intuition. **IN, damage half (BOTH readings):** the §8.5 main hit and every attack-dealt damage op, at the four sites Mimikyu "Safeguard" is honoured at. **IN, effects half (the WIDER reading only):** §12 Special Conditions on the body, the §11 retreat rider, an Energy discarded off it, and — the sharpest — damage **COUNTERS PUT** on it, because this engine has ruled since `damageActive` (D138/D139) that a placed counter is not attack damage (it skips Weakness, Resistance and every reduction passive), so it falls **entirely** on the effects side and a NARROW block does not stop it. **OUT:** anything an attack does to a **different** Pokémon (one Spread Shot is prevented on the shielded Active and lands 20 on every benched body in the SAME declaration); anything done to the **player** rather than to a Pokémon (a mill, a hand disruption); the attacker's own recoil; and **everything that is not an ATTACK** — a Trainer, an activated or triggered Ability, a §13 Checkup tick, all of which can land inside the window and none of which the printed sentence mentions. **⚠️ THE STATE IS A TURN STAMP, AND THE REASON IS NOT D124's.** D124 chose a stamp to avoid a boundary clear it would be easy to forget; here **the boundary clear that already exists is actively WRONG**. D112's retreat block reuses §13.4's paralysis clock — cleared in `runCheckup` for `endedSeat` — which works because that block sits on the **DEFENDER's** body. This one sits on the **INSTALLER's own**, so the very first Checkup after the attack has `endedSeat` = the holder's seat and that clock **lifts the block a full turn EARLY, before its window has opened at all**. So `InPlayPokemon.attackBlock: { turn: number; effects: boolean } | null` stamps **`state.turn + 1`** — an attack ENDS the turn (§5.3), so the next turn is the opponent's *by construction* — and it expires by arithmetic with nothing anywhere to clear it. **Measured: reusing the paralysis clock fails 12 tests; stamping the installing turn instead of the next one fails the same 12.** The stale stamp is deliberately left on the card afterwards: it answers nothing, and not clearing it is the whole point. **⚠️ "EFFECTS OF ATTACKS" IS A PROVENANCE THE TARGET CANNOT ANSWER, so it rides `EffectContext.invokedBy?: "attack"` — set by `attack.ts` and by nothing else. THIS REFINES D139 RATHER THAN REVERSING IT.** D139 explicitly declined a context field and put the provenance on the OP (`damageActive`/`damageChosen` still carry `source`), because a **LOG LABEL** is a property of the row an op emits. What rides on the CONTEXT is a **RULE that six ops must answer identically**, and the only site that knows the answer is the one that started the program: written per op it would be one fact in six places with six chances to go stale. D139's second objection — that `ctx` is serialized into every park — is exactly what makes it right here: an attack effect that PARKS and resolves three actions later is still an attack's effect, and the continuation carries that across the wire for free. The two provenances are **pinned to agree** on every producer in the pool. Without the check Crushing Hammer and Boss's Orders stop working inside the window; the sharpest witness is **Trevenant's between-turns Ability reaching the SAME `damageActive` op an attack reaches**, at the Checkup that ends the window turn — where the turn stamp cannot help and only the provenance can. **⚠️ `effects` IS A FIELD — D131's widen-don't-add rule applied and PASSING (the D139 case, not the D138 one):** same install site, same holder, same stamp, same event, same expiry, same three early clears, and the suite asserts the two derived programs are **byte-identical once the one field is stripped**. OPTIONAL, and the polarity is deliberate (D135's `zone` read from the other end): the printed words WIDEN the block, so a producer that forgets it blocks **less** rather than more, and an over-blocking default would silently make an opponent's whole attack do nothing. **NOT widened to a `damage` flag**, with the warranting condition written down: Palafin `sv03-145`'s effects-WITHOUT-damage reading, on ONE printing and a different sentence. **⚠️ THE EARLY CLEARS ARE §10's, AND WHICH ONE IS REACHABLE IS INVERTED FROM D112's.** Both endings null the field (`clearOnLeavingActive`, `switchInto`, `placeEvolution`). The **EVOLVE** clear is **unreachable by construction** — installing ends the turn, the window is the opponent's, and nobody evolves on someone else's turn — and is written anyway because §10 sheds the effects of ATTACKS, with a surgery pinning it. The **SWITCH** clear is reachable in the sharpest possible way: a **Boss's Orders played INSIDE the window** drags the shielded body off the spot and the protection goes with it, pinned by a following Spread Shot landing 20 on the very body that was immune a moment earlier. **⚠️ THE ONLY WAY TO KNOCK OUT A SHIELDED POKÉMON is a counter PUT through the NARROW reading** — the wide one refuses the placement, the damage half refuses every hit — which is how the "KO'd while blocked" ordering is driven end to end (`COUNTERS_PLACED` < `KNOCKED_OUT` < `PRIZES_OWED` to the attacker). **⚠️ TWO NEW EVENTS, AND THE VOICE IS THE MIRROR OF D141's.** `ATTACK_BLOCK_APPLIED { seat, uid, effects }` renders in the **ACTIVE** voice — `seat` owns the shielded Pokémon AND that Pokémon is the ACTOR's own, the exact mirror of `RETREAT_BLOCKED` (whose seat owns the victim) and of D141 (whose causer is the other player) — and there is deliberately **no `…_ENDED` twin**, because a stamp expires with nothing to announce and no boundary walk to announce it from; the early endings are already told by their own `POKEMON_SWITCHED`/`POKEMON_EVOLVED` row (D112's own call). `ATTACK_EFFECT_PREVENTED { seat, uid }` fires **once per refused op**, because a silently-skipped attack clause reads to a player exactly like a bug. The DAMAGE half needs no event: `DAMAGE_DEALT.prevented` has named that FACT since Safeguard (0.58.0) and is identical whichever prevention did it. **⚠️ `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` 3 → 4, AND IT IS D124's CASE RATHER THAN D140's.** No `EffectOp` moved; what moved is a **new REQUIRED field on `InPlayPokemon`**, so every Pokémon in play in an old record lacks it — and it would read back **benignly** (`undefined.turn` is never `state.turn`, and no such block could have been installed by a deploy with no op to install one), which is exactly the soft landing that constant exists to refuse. D124 refused it on the identical shape; refusing it again keeps the rule a rule, and the next durated field inherits the answer. The other persisted shape touched — `EffectContext.invokedBy`, which rides `EffectContinuation` into `phase.cont` — is **OPTIONAL and therefore a WIDENING** (D125's distinction), asked about explicitly rather than folded in. **Cost: 1 op member + impl, 1 state field + type, 1 `continuous.ts` reader (`attackBlockOf`), 2 events, 2 log arms, 2 regexes, 2 deriver arms, 1 `EffectContext` field, 6 guarded effect sites, 4 damage sites, 3 early clears, `preventBlock.test.ts` (32 tests) on its own `PREVENT_BLOCK_DECK`, 2 fixtures (Scyther `sv03-004` wide + printed 10, Togedemaru `sv03-151` narrow + no printed damage), `clauseApostrophe`'s DISCOVERED census 36 → 38, 1 added `log.test.ts` arm pin, 1 moved `match.test.ts` version pin.** **⚠️ THE GUARD IS SWEPT, NOT LISTED (D140's move):** the suite DISCOVERS every op kind any attack in `FIXTURE_POOL` can produce — walking `coinFlipGate` bodies, since this whole family hides inside one — and asserts the §11 classification is **TOTAL over the discovered set in both directions**, so a slice that teaches the deriver a new op fails here until it has said which side of the reading that op falls on. **Mutation-checked SEVEN ways**: reusing D112's clock fails **12**, stamping the installing turn fails **12**, dropping the main-hit block fails **5**, ignoring `effects` fails **5**, dropping `invokedBy` fails **2**, skipping the leaves-the-Active clear fails **1**, system-voicing the install row fails **1**. engineVersion 0.90.0 → **0.91.0**; full `bun run check` **GREEN exit 0, 184 files / 2993 tests** (was 183 / 2960). ZERO new prompts, parks, clause-table rows, `BoardCondition` members, error codes, registry rows or redact arms; no `rng.ts`/`cardplay.ts`/`registry.ts` diff and no web/DB/`@luminous/schema` diff beyond the one `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` constant. |
| D143 | P3-M5 — the SELF-LOCK: `During your next turn, this Pokémon can't attack.` — **22 printings across TWO sentences** on TWO anchored regexes, ONE bare `EffectOp`, a SECOND turn-stamped `InPlayPokemon` field and the first `ATTACK_PREVENTED` rejection (engine 0.92.0, MATCH_RECORD_VERSION 4 → 5) | **Taken as item 1 of D142's remainder list, and the slice that shows what that list was FOR: the duration mechanism D142 bought is reused with NO new concept, and the only new thinking is ONE NUMBER.** **⚠️ THE LIST SAID "2 PRINTINGS" AND THE ANSWER IS 22 — AN EIGHTH CONSECUTIVE LIST CARRYING SOMETHING WRONG, AND THE FIRST WHOSE MISS MAKES THE ITEM TEN TIMES BIGGER.** Every CATALOG claim held exactly (both ids `sv03-029`/`-199`, the attack name "Nine-Tailed Dance", **INDEX 1** on both — checked, not assumed, D139's habit — 9 counters, `{R}{R}`, NO printed `damage`, and the printed string verbatim). What the list did not say is that the compound's SECOND SENTENCE is printed BARE, as the whole effect, on **TWENTY more printings**: Miraidon ex ×4, Koraidon ex ×4, Dragonite VSTAR ×2, Alomomola, Clawitzer, Steelix, Kilowattrel, Tinkatuff, Victini ex, Crabominable, Umbreon, Probopass, Golbat. One extra `test` arm over one extra regex took the slice from 2 to 22 — **and D142's own duration table could not see it**, because that table was built from `During your opponent's next turn` and this sentence says *your* (it lists "an attack lock (4 + 1)", which is the OPPONENT-side printing and the buff). **D142's rule — a census run for one purpose is a FLOOR for every other — applied to D142's own census, one slice later.** **⚠️ THE "+2" WAS VERIFIED BY DRIVING IT, AND IT IS THE WHOLE SLICE.** Declaring an attack ENDS the turn (§5.3), so the turn after the installing one is the OPPONENT's and the one after that is the installer's own: D142's block prints *"during your OPPONENT's next turn"* and stamps `+ 1`; this prints *"during YOUR next turn"* and stamps **`+ 2`**. **Every other durated printing in the pool says the opponent's**, so `+ 1` is precisely what a session copying the neighbour writes — and it fails QUIETLY IN PLAY rather than loudly, because a `+ 1` lock is live only on a turn its holder could not have acted on anyway and the card's entire printed drawback silently disappears. Driven: install on turn 2 → stamp **4** → turn 3 (the opponent's) untouched → turn 4 refuses **BOTH** of the holder's attacks → turn 6 attacks again. **Measured: `+ 1` fails 14 tests.** **⚠️ A SECOND STAMPED FIELD, NOT A WIDENING OF THE FIRST — AND THE REASON IS THAT THE TWO WINDOWS ARE DIFFERENT NUMBERS ON THE SAME BODY.** `InPlayPokemon.attackLockedTurn: number | null` sits beside `attackBlock` because one `turn` could hold at most one of them, **asserted on a real board rather than in prose**: a case installs the lock for real, surgeries D142's block onto the same Pokémon, and drives turn 3 (damage prevented, lock inert) and turn 4 (block expired by arithmetic, holder refused). **Folding the lock into `attackBlock.turn` fails 8 tests.** Everything else is INHERITED unchanged — the stamp convention, the read-through-`continuous.ts` shape (`attackLocked`), the three §10 early clears, D124's no-defensive-`undefined` rule. **⚠️ IT NEEDS NO PROVENANCE CHANNEL, WHICH IS THE SHARPEST STRUCTURAL DIFFERENCE FROM D142** and a fact worth generalising: that block had to invent `EffectContext.invokedBy` because *"effects of ATTACKS"* is a claim about what INVOKED an effect and the target cannot answer it, whereas this field is read at `attack.ts`'s §8 gate and at the HUD's payability projection — **both by construction about declaring an ATTACK** — so there is exactly one kind of thing it can refuse. A durated effect does not automatically owe a provenance; only one that gates an OP does. **⚠️ IT GATES A DECLARATION, SO IT IS A REJECTION AND NOT AN EVENT.** New error code `ATTACK_PREVENTED`, `RETREAT_PREVENTED`'s exact mirror: §12's Asleep gate refuses the declaration while §12's Confusion flips at resolution, and *"can't attack"* is the first kind — **nothing is spent and the turn does NOT end**, which is the only reading a player can act on (an `ATTACK_FAILED` would burn their whole turn on a button the server had already decided to refuse). **That makes the projections LOAD-BEARING rather than cosmetic**, and is why this slice owes a REDACT ARM and a WEB DIFF most in this family do not: `redactedAttacksOf`'s `banned` and GameHud's `disabled` are the only things between a locked player and a button the server rejects. **Dropping the projection line fails 1 test; dropping the §8 gate fails 9.** **⚠️ THE §10 EARLY CLEARS ARE D142's WITH THE REACHABILITY INVERTED, AND EVERY ONE IS NOW A LINE OF PLAY.** D142's evolve clear was unreachable by construction (its window is the opponent's turn, and nobody evolves on someone else's); this window is the HOLDER's own, so **evolving frees a locked Pokémon to attack the same turn** — driven end to end with no surgery, which is what the slice's one invented fixture (`fix-mola-stage1`) exists for — **retreating out and Switching back in** frees it too (three legal actions on one turn), and a **Boss's Orders** un-locks a body the opponent was trying to keep off the board. Each clear fails **2** tests when skipped. **⚠️ AND THE THREE CLEAR SITES ARE SWEPT RATHER THAN LISTED (D140/D142's move), WHICH IS ALSO THE GENERALISATION EVIDENCE**: one case encumbers a Pokémon with every §10-sheddable field and asserts all three routes — `clearOnLeavingActive`, `placeEvolution` (turn.ts) and `switchInto` (interpreter.ts) — shed the SAME set, because those are three hand-written object literals in two files with nothing making them agree. **⚠️ IT LOCKS THE POKÉMON, NOT THE ATTACK**, and the pool prints the other reading EIGHT times (*"this Pokémon can't use {AttackName}"* — Munkidori ex ×3, Lucario `sv01-114`, Radiant Charizard `swsh10.5-011`, Radiant Blastoise `swsh10.5-018`, Skarmory `sv03-142`, Greedent ex `sv03-179`); all eight stay LOUD and the difference is pinned FROM THE INSIDE — Alomomola's index-0 "Surf" is refused by the lock its index-1 "Aqua Slash" installed. **⚠️ NO `target` FIELD, AND THE WARRANT FOR ONE ALREADY EXISTS — WHICH IS WHY THE REFUSAL IS ARGUED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The pool prints the DEFENDER-side lock on 4 printings across 2 sentences (Eiscue ex `sv03-042`/`-210`/`-222` "Scalding Block", Houndoom ex `sv03-134` "Evil Claw"), and it is **NOT a one-token widening**: the defender-side window is `state.turn + 1` and this one is `+ 2`, so a `target` field is TWO axes rather than one. Map one of them and the field is earned with two readings in hand (D130 → D131's precedent, D138's no-fields-at-all call). **⚠️ AND ONE THING THE ITEM CALLED FREE WAS NOT — THE THIRD "GUARD THAT IS AN ACCIDENT" IN FIVE SLICES.** Sentence one was priced as a one-token widening of D140's anchor, and the op (`damageChosen { target: "opponentAny" }`) really does exist — but **NO PUT-COUNTER SNIPE HAD EVER OFFERED THE ACTIVE**: every previous producer was Bench-only, so `placeSnipe`'s Active branch assumed `deals` and ran the full §8.5 pipeline, documented in a comment and asserted nowhere (the D139 → D140 shape again). Routing a PLACEMENT through it would DOUBLE the counters on a Weakness and CUT them on a Resistance, against this engine's standing ruling that a placed counter is not attack damage (D138/D139). The fix **REUSES `damageActive`** rather than writing a second flat-placement path — that function is already this arm to the byte, D142's effects-half guard included — which is D131's widen-don't-add rule applied to a FUNCTION instead of a field. **Routing the placement through `snipeActive` fails 2 tests; setting `deals` fails 4.** **⚠️ TWO SENTENCES, ONE ANCHOR, TWO OPS IN PRINTED ORDER** — `DEFENDER_POISON_CANT_RETREAT`'s shape (D125's two-consequent question, same answer). Matching sentence one as a PREFIX would place the counters and silently drop the drawback, shipping an attack the catalog does not print, which is strictly worse than leaving the card LOUD; **dropping the second op fails 6 tests and reversing the order fails 6**. The order is also the only one that reads right in a log, since sentence one can Knock the target Out. **⚠️ `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` 4 → 5 — D142's CASE AGAIN, ON A SECOND STAMPED FIELD, AND THE REPEAT IS THE POINT.** No `EffectOp` moved (a new union member is a WIDENING, `preventAttack` never parks, and `damageChosen` gained no field); what moved is once more a REQUIRED field on `InPlayPokemon`, which would read back BENIGNLY (`undefined === 4` is false, so an old record resolves to "not locked" — even correctly). Three bumps now on the identical shape (D124's `promotedTurn`, D142's `attackBlock`, this), **and the next one is already visible**: a third durated field (the `takes {N} less damage` family) would bump a fourth time, which is the strongest argument on record for folding the stamps into ONE installed-effects value — after which further durated effects become VALUES in a list rather than KEYS in a type and cost NO bump at all. **⚠️ THE GENERALISATION QUESTION, ANSWERED WITH THREE READINGS IN HAND AS THE LIST ASKED: PARALLEL FIELDS STILL WIN TODAY, AND THE TRIGGER IS NAMED.** The two built readings share almost NOTHING beyond the stamp — different windows (`+1` vs `+2`), different holders-of-consequence, different read sites (four damage sites and six effect ops vs one action gate and two projections), different refusal shapes (a nulled result vs an illegal action), one needs a provenance channel and one cannot use one. A record of installed effects would therefore buy exactly TWO things — the three §10 clear sites stop being hand-written literals, and the `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` bump happens once instead of per field — at the cost of a discriminated union, a lookup at every read site and a persisted shape change; **on two readings that is a loss, and the sweep test written here covers the one hazard it would remove.** The trigger to write down: **a THIRD field makes it a win**, and item 4 (14 printings, a NUMBER rather than a boolean, read at the SAME four damage sites as `attackBlock` one line below it) is that third — so the generalisation should be taken WITH it and not before. Cost: 1 op member (no fields), 1 op impl, 1 state field, 1 `continuous.ts` reader, 1 error code, 1 event, 1 log arm, 1 redact line, 1 GameHud line, 2 regexes, 2 deriver arms, 1 rewritten `placeSnipe` branch, 3 early clears, `attackLock.test.ts` (**32 tests**) on its own `ATTACK_LOCK_DECK` (**seed-free** — neither sentence flips, pinned by an unchanged `rngState` across a whole park-and-resolve), 3 fixtures (Alomomola `sv01-048`, Ninetales `sv03-029`, `fix-mola-stage1`), **3 re-pointed stays-LOUD witnesses** (`counterPut`, `counterBenchPut`, `counterMove` all listed the Ninetales sentence; none deleted), `clauseApostrophe`'s DISCOVERED census **38 → 40**, 1 added `log.test.ts` arm pin, 1 added `preventBlock.test.ts` classification row, 1 moved `match.test.ts` version pin, 1 moved `setup.test.ts` shape pin. **Mutation-checked FOURTEEN ways** (largest: `+ 1` fails **14**, dropping the §8 gate **9**, sharing `attackBlock.turn` **8**, dropping the compound's second op **6**, reversing its op order **6**, `deals: true` **4**; then 2 each for the three §10 clears, the `snipeActive` misroute, the dropped `['’]` class and a never-expiring lock, and 1 each for the projection line and a system-voiced row). engineVersion 0.91.0 → **0.92.0**; full `bun run check` **GREEN exit 0, 185 files / 3026 tests** (was 184 / 2993). What is new is that the resolution lives **inside** `attackBlockOf` with `attacker` a REQUIRED (nullable) parameter, so a read site cannot honour the turn stamp and forget the filter.
